**Owed to the book, and this batch paid none of it:** thirty-one on the chandler's note and not one of its three counts of weeks; three marks to a man of Ashtide; seven pence to this yard's own shipwright; eight marks and a day of a boat to this harbor's own bellwright; eight shillings and tenpence to a woman of this harbor; forty marks standing whole to a boy of twenty-two, with the three nots of the eighth of June; forty marks first after food to a person of no office; **eighteen** wages of four marks; the plate at the fifth seam, in eleven feet in this mole's own foundation stone, seen and not measured and not touched, **THE NOTE AGAINST IT OPEN;** the case of five bells at about five and a half fathoms inside four hundred yards on nobody's schedule, with the limit four people of this coast wrote and signed; the delayed note's eleven logged occasions and two with no call in them and a hand's width of a second that has not moved; the lead plug and the undertaking of the second of July; the three custody items and the live question no paper in this harbor or in that office's copying room answers; the run under this town; the compartment, which is spent; the first echo's witness; Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found, one line; the two objects of nobody's; the answer inside the delayed note, reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint; **THE NEW-VOLUME QUESTION, WHICH THIS BATCH DID NOT ASK AND DID NOT ANSWER AND DID NOT REFER TO;** **AND THE QUESTION THIS BATCH LEFT STANDING, WHICH IS WHAT A YARD OWES FOR A LINE IT PRINTED IN GOOD FAITH AND CANNOT GET BACK;** and the volume's final chapter image, which is the close's work and is not rounded up by anybody.

**Owed to the yard, and this batch added two things to it without answering either:** **what a place that is not a hold is, and who would keep it, and what it costs, and where the money comes from in a harbor where the mole-head light is not on because a light costs oil** — and the entry of the fourteenth of January stands and was not improved on and was not answered in these twenty days. **AND THE NEW ONE: the yard printed a line in a printed sheet in August that says where every copy of that sheet is, and four places on this coast have read that sheet and each of them has read it as a different thing, and a man of about thirty two days down this coast is a man of this yard in forty people's hearing because of it and is not one, and there is no office on this coast the holder can go and ask whether that is allowed, and he has not got a second page to put that on.**

**NOT OWED AND NOT TO BE RESTARTED BY A LATER CHAPTER:** the reason a refusal to hold is read as an intention to keep, which is paid and is a standing position; **A PAGE HAS NO DOOR IN IT, WHICH IS PAID, IS THE MOVEMENT'S WORKING RULE, AND IS NOT TO BE IMPROVED ON;** the fact that a name came off a list in another town; the fact that a corner of a sheet and a half has gone soft, which is PAID AND IRREVERSIBLE; **THE FINDING THAT A LINE OF A PAGE IS A RECORD OF WHERE THE PERSON WAS STANDING;** **THE FINDING THAT A LINE SAYING WHERE EVERY COPY OF A PAGE IS IS THE OPPOSITE OF A HOLD AND NOT A HOLD, WHICH IS PAID AND IS NOT TO BE IMPROVED ON AND IS NOT A CHAIN OF CUSTODY AND IS NOT AGED BY ANY LOCK;** **THE FINDING THAT A LIST OF NAMES IS A LIST OF WHO CAN BE ASKED WHAT, WHICH IS PAID, IS THE MOVEMENT'S TURN, IS SAID BY A MAN WHO IS NOT OF THIS PARTY AND WAS NOT ASKED, AND IS NOT TO BE IMPROVED ON;** **THE FOUR NAMES AND NOT A FIFTH;** **AND THE FACT THAT THE PRACTICE THIS YARD HAS BEEN DEMANDING HAD A NAME, WHICH IS PAID AND IS NOT TO BE SAID IN A MARKET, IN A ROOM, OR IN THIS BOOK AGAIN.**

**AND THE THING THAT IS NOT OWED AND IS THE WHOLE OF THE NEXT BATCH: A CHAIN OF CUSTODY IS A JURISDICTION AND IT IS MOVEMENT 5'S, AND IT DOES NOT EXIST BEFORE MOVEMENT 5 PUTS IT IN. NO CHAPTER OF MOVEMENTS 1 TO 4 DESCRIBES ONE, NONE OF THESE TEN DOES, AND A CHAPTER OF MOVEMENT 5 MAY NOT DATE IT EARLIER THAN THE DAY IT IS MADE. WHAT MOVEMENT 4 HAS INSTEAD IS FIVE PLACES ON THIS COAST HOLDING A COPY OF A PAGE THIS YARD PRINTED, FOUR READINGS OF IT, ALL FOUR CORRECT, AND A QUESTION LEFT STANDING THAT THE NEXT BATCH OWES AN ANSWER TO IN THE SECOND COLUMN AND NOT IN A SPEECH.**

---

## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 230

**Movement 3 is complete and its turn is paid. Movement 4 is the next writer batch. The volume is not closed and the close is not owed.**

### The movement's beats, in order, as they stand on the page

1. **221 — where the four come from.** No record is opened in this chapter and the chapter says why: **a page is not read on the day it arrives, and a man who has carried a page eleven days in a boat's locker and a woman who has kept one forty years in a house and a man who has kept a book of his own since he was seventeen do not get a page read at them in a doorway.** The chapter's work is the reading of two second-column lines in this yard's own book: the line of places kept by more than one community for longer than any book in that office goes back, and the line of a record of a chain of vessels kept by nine communities with a date against each and a place against each. **The bare fact of the nine was already in that book and is not new in this volume, and the chapter's work is that somebody reads the line and asks what the nine are, and the answer is that this yard does not know and has never been to any of them.** The four arrive by four routes and the four routes are the chapter: **one is shown** (a man of sixty-four of the first lay, a joiner from a place four days up this coast, with a book of his own begun at seventeen, shown standing up at the boards); **one is produced** (a woman of seventy-one who has kept a page in a house for forty years and has never been asked for it, and who walks up the mole and says so at the door); **one is ordinary traffic** (a man of about thirty-four who sells lime and chalk, who found a folded sheet in a crate and carried it up the hill on nothing); **and one is in a boat's locker**, said in nine words at the head of the dock by the man of the second lay on his way to get the pump. **No new institution, no new antagonist, and no office handed one over.** The cost of the chapter is entered in the second column: this party went to four households on two days and asked to see a page, and that is the same act at a smaller size as the refusal of the tenth of December, and four men in this town can now be asked whether this party came, and be told that it did.
2. **222 — the first of the four, and what it is a record of.** The thirty-seventh Monday since the first of May, the wall bare, the counter not restarted, the cistern charged on the rota day and walked on none, and that is the first of five this month. **A working day on the Monday, on a hirer's own account, with the pump and the man his.** The oilcloth comes off at about two and the first of the four is one sheet of ruled paper with the fold at the left edge a tear and not a stitch, torn out of something that was never bound, carried four hundred miles in a boat's locker, and it is **a page that is not a copy of anything and is not a document anybody sent anywhere and is simply the page a place has at the inside of a bar.** The ship's hand reads it and the holder does not read it for an hour and a quarter, and the chapter is the other one listening. The line that matters is a night in which **the light on the point was burning and two boats came in on it about two in the morning and lay there till it was light.** The read-back runs. The cost of the chapter is a day of the ship's hand lost on the Sunday night, in the second column, with no name in it and no reason in it that is checkable.
3. **223 — the second, and the price of a Tuesday.** **A wage of four marks did not go in and no envelope was made and the number of weeks fallen due is THIRTEEN, all thirteen in the same breath, and the reason is the reason of the twenty-ninth of December past and is not improved on.** A working day on the Wednesday at four hundred and eighty pence. On the Tuesday the woman's page says at about half past two that she is going to be asked about the ninth and has known it since about half past seven that morning. **They go down the hill to a house on the north side of this town and the woman of seventy-one says her grandmother's line: the ninth, and a hard night, and there was no light on the point, and the man that keeps it had the lamp out the week before and the oil was not carried, and the two boats that came in were in before it was light and were not in on the light.** Both are true, neither is resolved, and the woman will not say which is wrong and neither will the holder. **THE FINDING: there are two pages, there is a night, there is a point with a light on it or there is not, and neither page says where the person was standing when it was written.** The second page also carries a sale of the right to take what came ashore, in words and not in figures, and the words do not add up to the figures. **THE COST: this yard asked a woman of seventy-one one question in her own front room, and that is the first time in forty years anybody has asked that house anything, and the question is going to be in three other front rooms by Friday, and this yard cannot unask it and is not going to try.**
4. **224 — the third, and the cost of a man whose trade is not the yard's.** **The cistern is charged on the Thursday and walked on none, and that is the second of five.** A man of sixty-four comes up the hill with his book and puts the **fourth page of the ninth leaf** on the bench and shuts the book and will not let it go, and reads the line himself because he has read it forty times and wants to hear somebody else in the room while he does it: **on a Tuesday in a bad month a man of that place, aged thirty-one, went out in a boat of about sixty tons and did not come back, and there was a lantern in his pocket and a boot knife and two shillings and sixpence, and his mother held his things and his brother held the boat, and the boat came back and he did not.** He has carried that page nine years in a book and in nine years he has never once asked himself whether he was allowed to; what he has looked at is whether anybody has noticed. **The holder cannot tell him.** The cost: **a two-day stair, booked for the Thursday, done on the Friday and the Saturday, and a day of a joiner's trade at about three marks entered by him in his own book against the Thursday and entered by the yard because a man in this town can go and ask him, and entered also as not in this yard's money and not going to be.**
5. **225 — the fourth, which is the ninth of the nine.** **No room, no market, no clerk, no man with a seal, and no office in that shed of any kind, and the reason is on the page.** The sheet comes out from under the pump in two pieces because the fold has gone through. It is a copy, and the yard knows it is a copy because the writing is even all the way down the page and because the ink on the upper two thirds is a different make from the ink on the lower third. **At the head of it, in a hand bigger than the rest, is a name, and the name is written out on this page and NOWHERE ELSE IN THE WHOLE OF VOLUME 05, and it is a name of an agreement and not of a body.** Under it: an agreement made at a place, between nine, concerning a page. Four of the lines are legible and two are not: that a page about a place is the place's, and that it is not to be carried out of the place it is about, and that a copy is not a page and has none of a page's standing, and that no one of the nine is to be asked what another of the nine keeps. **At the foot, in a second ink and a later hand of that same place, a line that is not one of the four: that a page was carried out of that place in a year nobody wrote down and was brought back in another year and has been kept ever since in a house that is not the house it was kept in before. IT DOES NOT SAY THE CARRYING WAS WRONG. THE SHEET DOES NOT SAY THE AGREEMENT HELD AND THE YARD IS NOT GOING TO SAY IT EITHER.** The name is said out loud twice to two people in a shed and is not written in this yard's book; it goes in as the name at the head of the fourth of the four and not as the name.
6. **226 — the reading.** The thirty-eighth Monday, bare. **The cistern charged and walked on none, the third of five.** The man of sixty-four brings the leaf at eight and takes the book away and comes back at four on the Tuesday and will not be in the room. **The woman of seventy-one sends word and will not be in the room and will not be told afterwards and does not want a copy, and gives the reason, that there is a piece of paper on that bench that says a copy is not a page, and that she has never been asked anything about it and is not going to start.** **The four are read side by side from about two until about four on the Monday afternoon, at the top of that bench, in that shed, in about two hours, by two people, and there was no clerk in that room and no man with a seal in that room and no market anywhere near it.** The four are incompatible and all four are true and the incompatibility is the finding: two pages about one night and a light, a page about a man who went out, and a page about an agreement. **A sentence that would settle the light was written four times in the holder's head and not put on the page, because it does not say that one of the two is a lie.** A boat of about sixty tons is in two of them and this yard is not going to make them into one. **THE FIFTH THING, which is about the shape of the four and is not a conclusion and is not an answer to anything: THERE IS NOT ONE DATE ON ANY OF THE FOUR. A pencil was out and four dates could have been invented and they would have looked right, and nothing was put on the page.** A wage of four marks did not go in on the Tuesday and the number of weeks fallen due is FOURTEEN, all fourteen in the same breath. Three of the four went home on the Tuesday afternoon. The fourth stayed, because the man who sells lime said Saturday.
7. **227 — the reversal.** The cistern charged on the Thursday and walked on none, the fourth of five. **THE YARD DISCOVERS THAT ITS OWN FIRST COLUMN IS A LINE OF CUSTODY WITH A NAMED PERSON AT THE END OF EVERY LINE, AND THAT IT IS THE FIRST THING ABOUT ITS OWN METHOD IT HAS NOT BEEN ABLE TO SEE BECAUSE IT WAS INSIDE IT.** Every line of that column since the first of May has a day at the end of it and a name at the end of it, and four lines have a day and nothing else, and they are the four lines immediately above the named line of the twentieth of July and they are still four, still nameless and not struck out. **THE SHIPWRIGHT ASKS FOR THE FOUR TO BE STRUCK OUT AND IS REFUSED, IN THE SAME WORDS IT IS REFUSED FOR A LINE IN ANY COLUMN: a line comes out of a column when it is wrong, and that is the only ground any column in this yard has ever had.** The holder would strike them out if he could put a name at the end of them. **The other half of the reversal: a party which has spent four volumes insisting that a record a man can check is the only kind worth having arrives, in one week, at a record nobody in this harbor can check, and cannot say whether that is better or worse.** So they go down to a counter off the fish market, buy a sheet of paper at a penny, and ask. **A copyist of fifty-eight refuses the question and gives the nearest thing to it she has, which is about her and not about their page: a record a man can check is worth what the man is who checks it and a record nobody can check is worth what the person who keeps it is worth, and there is no figure for either of them, and she has met about nine people in thirty-one years she would trust with a page and about four hundred she would not, and the nine are not better than the four hundred.** **SHE IS NOT TOLD WHAT A SECOND COLUMN IS AND SHE DOES NOT ASK, and the reason is on the page: the moment a man tells a woman what a column is he is selling her a thing, and she told this party to their faces that she can tell the difference between a thing a man is selling and a thing a man is showing.** She says the other thing she has not looked at in thirty-one years is still coming and will be worse.
8. **228 — the ninth-line mark, and the other five of the nine.** A working day and nobody argued about anything. **THE NINE-LINE MARK CLOSES HERE AND ONLY AS A FACT: it is a builder's mark, nine short lines joined at one point, cut in a plate by a man building the thing the plate is on, of a construction older than the calendar this coast keeps; it is NOT a claim seal and NOT a mark of anybody consenting to anything; and THIS PARTY HAS COUNTED A ROW OF NINE ONCE AND IS NOT GOING TO COUNT IT TWICE, and the reason is that a man who counts a thing once has said what he saw and a man who counts it twice has started a pattern.** No office in this country can read it and that goes in the second column, and the reason against the line is that the holder has it from two men who build stone and neither of them is an office. **Four nines are four different things and neither may be converted into either: fifty-four fastenings are not the mark; the nine places in six books are not the nine communities; the nine records are not the nine places; and the point with the light is on the list of nine places and the light in the second of the four pages has nothing to do with it.** The other five of the nine are named as places and not as records: **this harbor's own weir, the point with the light, the next town down this coast, the hard at Ashtide, and Lowfen.** And on the Saturday morning the man who sells lime comes back for the fourth of the four and takes it down the hill in his coat and not in the barrow, because the barrow has got chalk in it, and the bench under the pump is bare for the first time since the ninth of December.
9. **229 — the cost, and the turn.** The thirty-ninth Monday, bare, **the cistern charged on the Monday and walked on none, and that is the fifth and last charge this month that the party has entered walked on none of.** The far bench is occupied from about ten until about four and neither brother goes over. **THE COST: on the Sunday morning the two of them lay out the five pieces of paper this yard holds to look for one word, and the sheet and a half with no signature and no date on it came apart in the ship's hand along the fold that had gone soft, about three inches of it, and cannot be put back. The reason she gave on the fourteenth of January for not lifting it off the bench was true and was not the reason: the reason was that lifting it meant deciding what to do with it, and as long as it was on the bench under a pump it was a thing that had not been dealt with. THEY DEALT WITH IT.** The shape of the cost is named: **it is not a person who has read a page and cannot unread it; it is a sheet that has been on a bench in this town for fourteen days and has gone home down that coast in the inside of a man's coat to a place two days down it, and four people are going to be asked about it this week and not one of them by this yard, and not one of the four is going to be told what two people said out loud about a light on a point, and that is the cost and it is not going to be put back. THE EARLIER FORM OF IT — *A PAGE IN A SHED IN ANOTHER TOWN FOR NINE DAYS* AND *FOUR PEOPLE WHO KEEP THAT PLACE ARE GOING TO BE TOLD* — IS WITHDRAWN: THE SHEET WAS IN A SHED IN THIS TOWN, THE OTHER TOWN IS WHERE IT BELONGS, IT WAS THERE FOURTEEN DAYS, AND THIS PARTY HAS TOLD NOBODY ANYTHING AND IS NOT GOING TO.** **THE TURN: the record-keeping this yard has been demanding of everybody else was once somebody's ordinary practice, and the ordinary of it is that nobody had to be asked; the practice had a name; the name is on one sheet in this country that this yard has ever been shown and is on none of the five pieces of paper this yard holds; and the holder is not going to say that the name being on one sheet and not on five is a fact about the world, because it is a fact about this yard's drawer.** A man of the first lay said in a market in December that the practice is six centuries old; an office in this country has kept records for three hundred years; the two are different figures and neither is going into the other's sentence.
10. **230 — the batch closes.** **Neither of these two days is a rota day and no cistern is charged in this chapter, and that is not a fault: the rota runs out of days before this book does, and the next one is the fourth of February and is past the end of it.** A wage of four marks did not go in and the number of weeks fallen due stands at FIFTEEN, all fifteen in the same breath. A working day on the Wednesday at four hundred and eighty pence, the last day of work this yard books in this book. The read-back runs. **THE QUESTION LEFT STANDING, IN THE SECOND COLUMN WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE: WHAT IS A COPY. Not what it is worth. The fourth of the four says a copy is not a page and has none of a page's standing, and that is the only sentence anybody has said to this yard in five volumes that this yard cannot argue with, and this yard has got a copy of a record of the twenty-fourth of November in a drawer and about sixty copies of its own first column on this coast, and nobody in four volumes has ever said out loud what a copy is.** The cost of the night is a printer: a man of forty-four in a town of about two thousand up this coast sent a letter on the twentieth of December with one question in it, and this yard has the answer in a drawer and is not going to send it, and the reason is that a printer in that town keeps a list of everything that comes into his shop that anybody means to keep. **AND THE BATCH'S LAST LINE IS A FACT AND NOT A RESOLUTION: the bench under the pump is bare, it is the only bare thing in that shed, the shape of that bench is the shape of a sheet and a half that is not on it, and the copy of a record of the twenty-fourth of November is nine feet away in a drawer in that room, and that is a fact a man can check on any day of a week, and nobody in that shed improved on it.** The writ runs to midnight on the first of March, the yard is a party of one and a half until that day, and **there are twenty-five days between the third of February and the first of March, and that figure is a figure and not a plan.**

- **Calder and Pernel Saye.** One scene, on Thursday the twenty-eighth, and it is the closest this yard has come to asking a trade a question about something other than a price. **She refused the question and gave the nearest thing to it she has, which was about her and not about their page, and she said so before she said it.** She was not told what a second column is and did not ask. **She has not been paid anything but a penny for a sheet of paper, and the penny went into the box.**
- **Calder and the man of sixty-four.** **The newest and the most unpaid of the batch's costs.** He showed a book he has never shown anybody, carried a page of it out of the place that page is about, from a house four days up this coast, for nine years, asked a yard of one and a half whether he had been carrying it right, and was told no, because nobody knows. **NO MILEAGE FOR HIM IS GIVEN ON THE PAGE AND THE FIGURE OF *NINE HUNDRED MILES* IS WITHDRAWN; THE FIGURE OF *FOUR HUNDRED MILES* ON THE PAGE BELONGS TO THE FIRST OF THE FOUR, OUT OF A BOAT'S LOCKER, IN CHAPTER 222, AND IS NOT HIS.** **He is going to keep carrying it and said so, and gave the reason, that he is sixty-four and it is the only thing he has that is not in a house where it does not belong.** He lost a day of a joiner's trade to a shed on this mole and it is in his own book. **He has not been thanked and has not asked for anything.**

- **THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN THIS BATCH AND WAS NOT AVAILABLE TO BE.** The body is charged: **a night of the said Sunday the seventeenth of January was lost to it and the day is entered as a day this yard did not work, in the second column, with a reason against the line and no name in it.** **The four lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four, are still nameless, and are not struck out, and a request to strike a line out of a column was made once in this batch by the ship's hand against the first column and was refused in the same words it is refused for a line in any column.**

- **The wage column stands at twenty-six lines and one hundred and four marks, the fifth of March the oldest, and there are twenty envelopes on a bench, and FIFTEEN weeks have now fallen due with no envelope standing against them. - **The slate wall came down bare three times, being the thirty-seventh, thirty-eighth and thirty-ninth Mondays since the first of May, on the eighteenth and twenty-fifth of January and the first of February, all with the clerk keeping his own page, and no sitting was held on any of them.**
- **The cistern was charged at no figure on five rota days and this party walked none of them: the eighteenth and twenty-first and twenty-fifth and twenty-eighth days of January and the first day of February. THE WALK IS NIL AND IT STAYS NIL, AND THAT IS THE SEVENTEENTH OF THE CHARGES THIS PARTY HAS ENTERED WALKED ON NONE OF SINCE THE THIRD OF AUGUST, AND THE NEXT ROTA DAY IS THE FOURTH OF FEBRUARY AND IS PAST THE END OF THIS BOOK.**
- **The far bench is entered in all ten of the chapters, covering all twenty of the days, at two instances to the chapter except in 229, which carries the occupied line and the unoccupied line together in the first column. THE FIGURES *NINE OF THE TEN CHAPTERS* AND *EIGHTEEN OF THE TWENTY DAYS* ARE WITHDRAWN: EVERY CHAPTER CARRIES THE ENTRY.**

The party went past both of them once in the twenty days and touched neither.**
- **A copy of a said record was not produced to a court in a city on the fourth of January, which came and went before this batch, and nothing happened, and this party asked no office for anything in aid of it, sent nothing to a city, did not write to a man in a coat, and has not been to the counter of that circuit office since the twenty-fourth of November 2026, and none of that changed in these twenty days.**
- **FOUR PIECES OF PAPER ARE IN THIS YARD'S HANDS AND A FIFTH IS GONE.** The copy of a record of the twenty-fourth of November; the acknowledgment of the twenty-ninth; the half sheet of a court in a city of the fourth of December whose date has passed and nothing happened; the printer's letter of the twentieth of December with one question in it that has not been answered; **and the sheet and a half with no signature and no date on it, which came up that hill on foot on the ninth of December, sat on a bench under a pump for fifty-three days, and came apart in the ship's hand on the thirty-first of January and cannot be bought, cannot be got and cannot be put back.** The chandler's note is at thirty-one and was not said out loud in the eight weeks ended on the third of February. Eight shillings and tenpence to a woman of this harbor with her name on the figure. ### What is owed and what is not

**Owed to the book, and this batch paid none of it:** thirty-one on the chandler's note and not one of its three counts of weeks; three marks to a man of Ashtide; seven pence to this yard's own shipwright; eight marks and a day of a boat to this harbor's own bellwright; eight shillings and tenpence to a woman of this harbor; forty marks standing whole to a boy of twenty-two, with the three nots of the eighth of June; forty marks first after food to a person of no office; fifteen wages of four marks; the plate at the fifth seam, in eleven feet in this mole's own foundation stone, seen and not measured and not touched, **THE NOTE AGAINST IT OPEN**; the case of five bells at about five and a half fathoms inside four hundred yards on nobody's schedule, with the limit four people of this coast wrote and signed; the delayed note's eleven logged occasions and two with no call in them and a hand's width of a second that has not moved; the lead plug and the undertaking of the second of July; the three custody items and the live question no paper in this harbor or in that office's copying room answers; the run under this town; the compartment, which is spent; the first echo's witness; Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found, one line; the two objects of nobody's; the answer inside the delayed note, reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint; **THE NEW-VOLUME QUESTION, WHICH THIS BATCH DID NOT ASK AND DID NOT ANSWER AND DID NOT REFER TO;** **AND THE QUESTION THIS BATCH LEFT STANDING, WHICH IS WHAT A COPY IS;** and the volume's final chapter image, which is the close's work and is not rounded up by anybody.

**Owed to the yard, and this batch added one thing to it without answering it:** **what a place that is not a hold is, and who would keep it, and what it costs, and where the money comes from in a harbor where the mole-head light is not on because a light costs oil.** The entry of the fourteenth of January stands and was not improved on and was not answered in these twenty days. **AND A SECOND THING, WHICH IS NEW ON THE FACE OF THE BOOK: a record nobody can check has been on this yard's bench for nine days, and the yard bought one sheet of paper to find out what a copy is worth, and does not know, and the copyist told it in her own words that there is no figure for either of them.**

**NOT OWED AND NOT TO BE RESTARTED BY A LATER CHAPTER:** the reason a refusal to hold is read as an intention to keep, which is paid and is a standing position; the fact that a man with a firebox and a key is going out of it in the spring; the fact that a name came off a list in another town; the fact that a corner of a sheet and a half has gone soft, which is now PAID AND IRREVERSIBLE; **THE FINDING THAT A LINE OF A PAGE IS A RECORD OF WHERE THE PERSON WAS STANDING, WHICH IS PAID AND IS NOT TO BE IMPROVED ON AND IS NOT THE COMPACT AND IS NOT A CONCLUSION;** **THE FOUR NINES, WHICH ARE FOUR DIFFERENT THINGS AND MAY NOT BE CONVERTED INTO ONE ANOTHER;** **AND THE FACT THAT THE PRACTICE THIS YARD HAS BEEN DEMANDING HAD A NAME AND THE NAME IS ON ONE SHEET IN THIS COUNTRY, WHICH IS PAID AND IS NOT TO BE SAID IN A MARKET, IN A ROOM, OR IN THIS BOOK AGAIN.**

**AND THE THING THAT IS NOT OWED AND IS THE WHOLE OF THE NEXT BATCH: A CHAIN OF CUSTODY IS A JURISDICTION AND IT IS MOVEMENT 5'S. THIS BATCH FOUND ONE INSIDE THIS YARD'S OWN FIRST COLUMN AND DESCRIBED IT AND DID NOT EXTEND IT: a page in a drawer in five towns, an agreement about who may read a page, and a person who is answerable to nobody and removable by nobody, are all still SPENT BEFORE MOVEMENT 5 PUTS THEM IN, AND NO CHAPTER OF MOVEMENTS 1 TO 4 MAY DESCRIBE ONE.** What this batch has instead is four pages that came up a hill by four ordinary routes, three of which went back down it, and a bench under a pump with nothing on it.

## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 220

**Movement 2 is complete and its turn is paid. Movement 3, the volume's midpoint, is the next writer batch. The volume is not closed and the close is not owed.**

### The movement's beats, in order, as they stand on the page

1. **211 — the days after a turn.** The compressor is looked at for the first time since the twentieth of October and the gland is the chapter: the part is on the machine, it holds, and there is no hose. **The yard declines to price a foot of hose and gives the reason, that a compressor in a harbor with no hose is a thing that makes noise.** The working question is answered on the page: a day at a seam is a pump, a line, a man on the pump and a slack water, and this yard has the line and two of them and a writ and has not got a pump, and the harbor's gear has no boat of its own. **The decision is the yard will take the next day that comes up the hill**, and the reason is that a yard which is findable has to be seen working and there is no way to be findable and be quiet about it.
2. **212 — the last Tuesday of the year.** The thirty-fourth Monday comes down bare. The cistern is charged and walked on none. **A man who was refused on the twenty-fifth comes back on the twenty-eighth and is told yes, in a doorway, and the reason is given out loud: on the Friday the holder could not say what he was doing on the Saturday morning and on the Monday he can say, and that is the whole of the change and it is not humility.** The day is worked. **On the Tuesday three marks come in and four marks do not go out, on the same column, on the same day, and the number of weeks fallen due is ten, all ten in the same breath, and the reason the wage is not paid is entered once, in the holder's own left hand, in the first column: a wage is a week and not a payment, and one week out of ten leaves a bag that pays one week and no other, and this yard has a page for a figure and does not want one.**
3. **213 — a thing that has to be built out of nothing.** Nineteen lines in a shipwright's hand, a list and not a plan, and every item on it a figure this yard cannot pay or gear it does not own, the seventh of them a hand. **The day's cost is entered against that day before the day is spent, and the day is spent, and what came of it is nineteen lines and a pencil and no agreement.** On the last afternoon a man says out loud at the head of the dock that this was the last day of the year and that a man ought to be able to say what he had done in it, and **neither of them answers him and neither of them says the day of the month, and the entry says that a day on which a thing is said in a street is a day on which a thing was said in a street.** Item eleven is said out loud in this yard's own book as a thing this yard cannot put in front of anybody. **No buoy went into any water and no stage of any kind was taken.**
4. **214 — the boy in a June berth that nobody in that crew has gone to see.** No day of the chapter is a scene in the berth. **The yard does not go, does not write, does not send anybody, does not ask anybody who has seen him what he said, and enters that with the reason of the fourth of October past.** A day of about three marks was offered and could not be taken and the figure is entered against no day at all. **The cost falls on a boat of about forty tons one hand short since the middle of July, out of one part owner's share: four marks, one shilling and eightpence, being six hundred and sixty pence, not coming back, and nobody going to ask this yard for it.** And a man of the third lay says the thing she was not going to ask him: **four men in that crew and not one of them has gone to see the boy who was in that boat in June, and he is one of the four.**
5. **215 — the fourth of January.** The thirty-fifth Monday comes down bare. **On the Sunday the month's being out is said out loud in a shed and answered with a sentence about the length of a month, and entered, and that is the only thing anybody in this batch says about a month or a year. ON THE FOURTH THE DATE ON THE HALF SHEET ARRIVED AND NOTHING HAPPENED, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO IN THOSE WORDS: no court sat, no clerk came up the hill, nobody was charged with anything, nobody was fined, nobody was refused anything, nobody was examined.** The reason is the reason of the seventh of December, and the half of it that is in the shed and not in a market is that the copy could be produced tomorrow and nothing whatever would happen to anybody and a clerk would have a piece of paper with this yard's name at the head of it.
6. **216 — the coast road's own figures, the refused licence, and item eleven.** **Eleven weeks fallen due, all eleven in the same breath.** A page off a printed sheet out of a press in a city is lying on the boards in this harbor under a stone and carries two figures for the bar of gravel side by side in the same weight of type, and **neither is withdrawn, the Kettle was not looked at, the refused licence stands unreversed and the thirty marks were not paid and are not owed to anybody and cannot be applied to anything.** A man of fifty-eight offers his own figure for that bar and **is refused, in the street, and the refusal is a refusal and not a request, and the figure is not in this yard's book.** The cost of the chapter is that **this yard's own method went out loud in a street in about nine people's hearing for the first time in four volumes.**
7. **217 — a hand at a counter, and the trade priced again.** The cistern is charged and walked on none. **The question is not about paper: what a yard of one and a half with about three marks in it can build, what a man has to know to keep a thing on the water, and how many days a year a thing on the water takes out of a working harbor.** A copyist prices what she can price and says plainly what she cannot, and the answer is that **a thing on the water is not a building, a building is made once and a thing on the water is a bill, and nobody can produce the number of times anybody is going to look at it.** She is not told what a second column is and does not ask, and no money of this yard went into that room. On the Friday the day is worked **on a pump a man brought up a hill, so that the three marks is for the hands and the line and the tide and not one penny of it is for a machine.**
8. **218 — what a refusal to hold is read as. THE BATCH'S CLIMAX.** **A man of sixty-eight offers a shelf in a dry room with a firebox and a lock above a chandler's, for nothing, in good faith, and is refused in the open with the reason given in the open, and the price of giving the reason is named before it is given.** The reason is that a shelf in a man's room is a favor and a favor is a thing with a man at the end of it. **And the no is read as an intention to keep, by two men of the first lay who were standing there, and the yard cannot correct it without putting a name on something, and this time the thing it would have to name is not four people on a weir and not a reason — it is that the only way to be read correctly is to have somewhere to put it that is not a hold, and this yard has not got one and is not going to build one in a fortnight. The three sentences a man can say in a street are given and every one of them is wrong. THE GAP IS NOT CLOSED AND THE CHAPTER SAYS ON THE PAGE THAT IT IS NOT CLOSED, AND THAT THE ANSWER IS A HOLE, AND A HOLE IN A STREET FILLS ITSELF.**
9. **219 — the cost of a day, again, and a different shape.** The thirty-sixth Monday comes down bare, the cistern is charged and walked on none, **the far bench is occupied from about ten until about four and neither brother goes over.** The day is worked. **The cost has a shape of its own and it is none of the five: a man of thirty-one took his own name off a list in a book in the next town down this coast, a woman in an office wrote it off with a pen in about four seconds, a man is not asked at that place again, that is not a day and not a wage and cannot be put back by anybody, and about six marks is behind it. THE NAME IS IN THE SPEAKER'S MOUTH AND IS NOT IN THIS YARD'S BOOK, AND THE REASON IS ENTERED AGAINST THE LINE.** Twelve weeks fallen due, all twelve in the same breath.
10. **220 — the turn.** The cistern is charged and walked on none and the far bench is entered as not occupied. **A corner of the sheet and a half is gone soft about the width of two fingers, and the yard did not lift it off the bench and gave the reason on the page: there is nowhere in this harbor to lift it on to.** The cost of the three weeks is read out in one breath and every item in it is a figure. **And then the third entry goes in the first column, in the holder's own left hand, with the date and the hour on it: a refusal to hold is read as an intention to keep; the only way to be read correctly is to have somewhere to put it that is not a hold; this yard has not got one, knows what one would cost, and is not going to pretend it knows the cost of a thing it has not built. A question is left standing that this party is not answering in a market, in a room, or in this book. THE BATCH'S LAST LINE IS A FACT AND NOT A RESOLUTION AND NOBODY IN THAT SHED IMPROVED ON IT.**

She said the day could not be taken, on a mole's steps, and nobody knows why but the two of them and the second column. She said the gap between a refusal and a correction cannot be closed and would fill itself. **She has not been thanked and is not going to ask, and he has not thanked her and is not going to and did not.**
- **Calder and the man of the first lay with the lock-up.** A new and unnamed relationship, and it is the batch's turn standing on its own two feet: a man who offered the best thing this harbor has and is going out of it in the spring, and a yard which refused him in a street and is not going to be asked again. **He is not a friend of this party and has not been asked to be and is not on any ladder.**
- **Calder and Ors Kest.** Unchanged. **He is in no room in this batch and was not asked for anything, because he said on the tenth of December past that he was not going to do that twice inside a month without saying so out loud first, and it is inside a month. - **Calder and Bevin.** **Unchanged and not in a room.** Off the stone since the fifteenth of May, forty marks standing whole, the three nots standing, the head of the third column keeping his hand. **The batch came closest to him it ever has and it did it by not going: a boat of the third lay is a hand short because of him, four marks and a change and eightpence came out of a man's share because of him, and this yard did not go, did not write, did not send anybody, and did not ask anybody who has seen him what he said. - **Calder and Alden.** **Do not move.** A man of sixty-one at a far bench, occupied on one day of these twenty and entered as not occupied on six others, not asked for anything on any day, asking nothing on any day, no fragment, no report talk, no second story, **and Calder Marr did not go over to him on any of the twenty days. - **Calder and the two men of the first lay.** **Three days out of five weeks, and no fourth out of nothing.** The yard went up on none of the three, has got neither name, has asked neither of them anything, and is not going up that hill for it. **And the reason against all three lines is the reason of the seventeenth of December: a correction is the reason, and the reason does not go into a street.**

NO STAGE OF ANY KIND WAS TAKEN, AND THE BELLWRIGHT STAGE WAS NOT TAKEN, NOT NAMED, AND NONE OF ITS THREE COSTS DISCHARGED: the book of hours of the thirtieth and thirty-first of October stands at sixteen lines and is not extended, the nineteen lines are a list of materials and not a stage, a thing on the water is entered as a bill and not as an instrument, and this yard has not got a hand to go and look at one and says so on the page. NO BUOY WENT INTO ANY WATER AND NO KEEPLIGHT WAS EXTENDED. No person of this yard went in any water on any day of the twenty, no finding of worked glass was made, nothing was raised and no return of any kind was made. The word *seam* on a page of this batch is the mole's own second and fifth seams and a day of work at a seam, and is not the perception.** The standing fact is unchanged: a seam identifies difference and not truth.

### The bodies and the dated positions

- **THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN THIS BATCH AND WAS NOT AVAILABLE TO BE.** The body is charged: **a day of about three marks was offered on Friday the first of January and could not be taken, the figure is entered against that day and against no day at all, and the reason is in the second column with no name in it and is not going to have one.** **The four lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four, are still nameless, and are not struck out, and a request to strike a line out of a column is refused in the same words it is refused for a line in any column.**
- **The bag is sixteen marks, six shillings and four pence, being two thousand six hundred and thirty-six pence, which is four days at the face of the second seam since the sixth of December and one penny out since the eighteenth of that month. NOTHING WENT OUT OF THIS YARD IN THESE TWENTY DAYS. The figure the bag is short of the wage went the wrong way twice and is one thousand nine hundred and ninety-six pence.**
- **The wage column stands at twenty-six lines and one hundred and four marks, the fifth of March the oldest, and there are twenty envelopes on a bench, and TWELVE weeks have now fallen due with no envelope standing against them. Three wages of four marks were not paid in these twenty days. The reason was entered once, on the twenty-ninth of December, and is not improved on.**
- **The slate wall came down bare three times, being the thirty-fourth, thirty-fifth and thirty-sixth Mondays since the first of May, on the twenty-eighth of December, the fourth of January and the eleventh of January, all with the clerk keeping his own page, and no sitting was held on any of them.**
- **The cistern was charged at no figure on six rota days and this party walked none of them. The walk is nil and it stays nil, and the charge of the ninth of November past stands entered with no figure against it.**
- **The far bench is entered in six of the ten chapters, covering twelve of the twenty days, and occupied on one of them, being the eleventh of January. It is not claimed on the other eight days, and every not-occupied line carries no figure of what came before it.**
- **The two objects of nobody's are about two hundred and fifteen days up the crown of the mole, about nine feet apart, under two tarpaulins, one with a corner off it since the seventeenth of June and not lashed back, and neither has moved. The party went past both of them twice and touched neither.**
- **A copy of a said record was to be produced to a court in a city on the fourth of January and was not produced, and nothing happened, and this party asked no office for anything in aid of it, sent nothing to a city, did not write to a man in a coat, and has not been to the counter of that circuit office since the twenty-fourth of November 2026.**
- **Five pieces of paper are in this yard's hands and none has been answered: the copy of a record, the acknowledgment, the form of the court, the form of an unnamed office in a city which is going soft on a bench under a pump, and a printer's letter with one question in it. The chandler's note is at thirty-one and was not said out loud in these twenty days. Nothing in a tin since the twenty-eighth of April. Three marks to a man of Ashtide, unmoved and unasked about. Seven pence to this yard's own shipwright, unpaid. Eight marks and a day of a boat to this harbor's own bellwright, unargued, his chest unopened and his number unprinted. Eight shillings and tenpence to a woman of this harbor, with her name on the figure. Forty marks standing whole to a boy of twenty-two, the three nots standing, the third column unfilled and unappointed. Forty marks first after food to a person of no office, untouched and unoffered.**

### What is owed and what is not

**Owed to the book, and this batch paid none of it:** thirty-one on the chandler's note and not one of its three counts of weeks; three marks to a man of Ashtide; seven pence to this yard's own shipwright; eight marks and a day of a boat to this harbor's own bellwright; eight shillings and tenpence to a woman of this harbor; forty marks standing whole to a boy of twenty-two, with the three nots of the eighth of June; forty marks first after food to a person of no office; twelve wages of four marks; the plate at the fifth seam, which stands at eleven feet in this mole's own foundation stone, seen and not measured and not touched, **THE NOTE AGAINST IT OPEN**; the case of five bells at about five and a half fathoms inside four hundred yards on nobody's schedule, with the limit four people of this coast wrote and signed; the delayed note's eleven logged occasions and two with no call in them and a hand's width of a second that has not moved; the lead plug and the undertaking of the second of July; the three custody items and the live question no paper in this harbor or in that office's copying room answers; the run under this town; the compartment, which is spent; the first echo's witness; Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found, one line; the two objects of nobody's; the answer inside the delayed note, reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint; the new-volume question; and the volume's final chapter image, which is the close's work and is not rounded up by anybody.

**Owed to the yard, and this batch put a figure on it for the first time in four volumes:** **what a place that is not a hold is, and who would keep it, and what it costs, and where the money comes from in a harbor where the mole-head light is not on because a light costs oil.** **THE ENTRY SAYS THIS PARTY IS NOT GOING TO ANSWER THAT IN A MARKET, IN A ROOM, OR IN THIS BOOK, AND IT IS MOVEMENT 3'S AND LATER MOVEMENTS' WORK.**

**NOT OWED AND NOT TO BE RESTARTED BY A LATER CHAPTER:** the reason a refusal to hold is read as an intention to keep, which is paid; the fact that a man with a firebox and a key is going out of it in the spring, which is paid; the fact that a name came off a list in another town, which is paid; the fact that a corner of a sheet and a half has gone soft, which is paid and irreversible; and the fact that this yard has not got a place to put a page in, which is a standing position and not an event.

**AND THE THING THAT IS NOT OWED AND IS THE WHOLE OF THE NEXT BATCH:** **a chain of custody is a jurisdiction and it is new, and it does not exist before Movement 5 puts it in, and no chapter of Movements 1 to 4 may describe one. Nothing in these twenty days is one.** What this batch has is a drawer in a shed with four pieces of paper in it and a store room above a chandler's with a firebox and a lock, and the yard refused the second of them in a street.

## The Volume 03 close and its audit — as AUDITED, 27 September 2026, and the Volume 04 hand-off

**`reviews/volume-03/volume-03-close.md` is the audit and is the home of every ruling below. Nothing in this section is new canon; it is the audit's account of the canon, and where it corrects a figure it says so on its face.**

- **VOLUME 03 IS CLOSED. Every clause of `outline/volume-03.md` is PAID, PART, RULED or NOT ATTEMPTED with the reason, and nothing is owed to Volume 03 and nothing is owed to a chapter that has not been written. THE VOLUME MEASURES 154,738 WORDS ACROSS FIFTY CHAPTERS, in five batches of 29,905 / 30,356 / 33,738 / 30,916 / 29,823, measured with `wc -w` on 27 September 2026 and re-measured after the audit's eleven repairs.**
- **THE CENTRAL PRESSURE IS RULED RESOLVED AND RESOLVED IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION TO THE OPENING, AND IT WAS THE PUBLICATION AND NOT THE CUTTER.** The cutter did not erase the map; it emptied itself in the open over a market wall. **The volume's own argument — that a route is public or it is nothing — is not the argument the outline asked for and is a better one, and the outline is not amended.** The cost of the map was the map's only advantage, and the cost is a sentence that cannot be said again.
- **THE CLIMAX IS RULED DISCHARGED IN A DIFFERENT FORM AND THE CHAPTER'S FORM IS STRONGER.** There was no claim to surrender and no salvage to abandon: the purpose was entered in the first column in advance as *to get four persons off her and to nothing else*. **The case was in his hands and he let go of a line round his own forearm when a boy of seventeen went off a foredeck, and the loss is irreversible, no other finding is substituted for it, and the cost fell on a wage of four marks that was not paid, on three marks owing to a man of Ashtide, and on the sentence.**
- **THE BELL-ARCHIVE IS RULED THE VOLUME'S BEST PIECE OF CONSTRUCTION AND THE AUDIT AGREES.** The community is one of the nine the volume's own books name: **the Sill, this harbor's own weir, whose sill stone is under four feet of stone at the south end of this mole and a foot and four inches above the third of the six joints, and four of that community's people came up the mole's steps on the tenth of August.** The archive is five bells under an after hatch, not a set, a line cut into the lip of each, read out aloud rather than written down, in a hall, in front of the people it is about.
- **THE CONCRETE RESOLUTION IS PAID IN ALL FOUR OF THE OUTLINE'S ELEMENTS, IN THE OUTLINE'S ORDER, AND NONE WAS WAIVED.** A longer leash with a name on it: a man in a coat named Amos Trewhella came up a hill on the twelfth of August with a paper, **and the second season of the writ is still not granted and the writ still runs to the first of March.** A boy who cannot do the thing he was kept for, in the yard's own hand, dated the first of August. A conservator who is not on the crew and is owed forty marks. A question about who changed a rule, asked in a market and not answered, with the not-answering on the page.
- **THE FINAL CHAPTER IMAGE IS PAID IN ALL NINE ELEMENTS AND THE AUDIT AGREES WITH THE CHAPTERS ON BOTH OF THE TWO THE OUTLINE LEFT UNSETTLED.** The nine communities are carried as nine **places** and the thing is called nothing, and the word *Harbors* is not on the page anywhere; and *under a naval paper* is settled in the prose by a sheet that goes up on the outside of whatever is there, and the shipwright refuses to pretend a sheet under a naval paper is a sheet on its own.
- **OF THE TWENTY-EIGHT LOCKS, NONE IS BREACHED. ONE IS AMENDED (L5). TWO ARE HELD IN ANOTHER FORM AND IN A STRONGER ONE (L8, the bell; and the chart and its notation). TWENTY-FIVE HOLD.**

### The one ruling that is the most important thing in the audit — LOCK L5

**LOCK L5 IS AMENDED. THE CLAUSE STANDS. THE ENUMERATION IS SUPERSEDED BY THE CHAPTERS. THERE ARE FOUR RETURNS IN VOLUME 03 AND THERE WAS NO FIFTH, AND THE FOURTH IS CANON.** Return 1, Thursday the fourth of May, two days late. Return 2, Wednesday the tenth of May, on the day. Return 3, Saturday the seventeenth of June, on the day. Return 4, **Friday the fourteenth of July, on the day, out of a finding of Monday the tenth** — the day of the finding is not counted and the fourth day after the tenth is the fourteenth, and the counting rule was spoken out loud on the page and the party entered that it was not going to try to move the date. **All four were caught by the first limb of the clause by itself, and the party has not needed the second limb and does not know that it would.**

**NOTHING IS RECALLED, NOTHING IS CORRECTED, NOTHING IS UN-SAID AND NOTHING IS RE-SENT.** A return states a fact and not a conclusion, and a document already in a circuit office's file cannot be withdrawn by a party deciding to amend its own bookkeeping. **The clerk's own page has said four in his own hand since Friday the fourteenth of July, because he keeps his own page and because he said what a box holds. THE RECORD IS ALREADY TRUE. A VOLUME'S INTERNAL BOOKKEEPING IS NOT A RECORD, AND A LOCK IS NOT A DOCUMENT, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF THE RULING.** The amended form is written into `outline/volume-04.md` lock L5 and governs every later volume.

### The other thirteen rulings, each dated 27 September 2026

1. **The condition's onset is the FOURTEENTH of October 2025 and not the thirteenth, and the three things on that date are three different things and may never be fused: the dive was the night of the THIRTEENTH; the onset is the FOURTEENTH; and the order and the seizure were the FOURTEENTH at half past six in the morning.**
2. **The join's physical facts, re-derived:** six joints at about four fathoms on the second seam's own face, landward of the third seam's mark, in a straight line, **a string with two pins in it coming to three hundred and fifty-one feet**, fouling of a quarter of an inch, a quarter, three inches and a quarter, three inches and a quarter, nine inches, and three inches and a quarter, in that order from the root end of the crevice to the seaward end, and **three thicknesses of fouling is three and the party does not know how many more and is not going to arrange them into a figure.** **The nine fastenings are not a mark of nine joined lines and the nine places are not the Nine Harbors, and neither may be converted into either.**
3. **The nine places as the page carries them**, and **the three with no joint are still owed a placement in a later volume as a fact and not as an identification, and the party does not know why the three are there and is not going to guess.**
4. **What a chip of worked glass carries is STILL OPEN BY DESIGN and stays unread.** **The six marks and two days and the yard's inability to pay are both on the page, and a later volume that reads the chip must have somebody paid six marks and two days and the party must not have paid them.**
5. **The figure on the bar of gravel:** the yard's is two and three-quarters, made once on the second of May; **Rowan Trewin's is two and a half, made four times in nineteen years; both are in a press in a city and neither is withdrawn and this volume does not decide which is right**, and the man who is wrong about one thing the yard cannot do one single thing about is the yard itself.
6. **The glass storm's two written occurrences stand in two hands on two pages that are not on top of each other, and no cause is named in any volume.**
7. **The delayed note: eleven and two stand, neither is advanced, the answer is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint, and the delay is a hand's width of a second with nobody having said why.**
8. **The coast road's four figures, the pilot's two, the Kettle not looked at, and the yard's refused licence: the road is published and cannot be un-published and is not resolved in Volume 04 either.**
9. **The custody items: three, digits never quoted, the municipal claim on the third stands, and the live question — what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material — may not be answered by a clerk in this volume or any other.**
10. **The ninth seam's end of the first of September falls seventeen days after the volume closes and is Volume 04's first movement, and an office whose instruments nobody contests has now entered three things in its own book and every one of them cost somebody who was not in the room.**
11. **THE ATTRIBUTION OF THE VOLUME'S CLOSING SENTENCE. NO FISHER IN VOLUME 02 IS RECORDED AS HAVING SAID IT.** It is Calder Marr's in a room in Volume 02, Chapter 97, and the same shape is Ors Kest's in a room in Chapter 58, **and neither man is a fisher.** **The outline's clause is DISCHARGED AND NOT VARED: Volume 03 gave it to three people in three mouths, put a herring skipper in the last of them, in a market, on the last page but one, and the sentence's origin is settled at Volume 02, Chapter 97, in a hearing room.**
12. **THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF JULY 2026 IS A FRIDAY.** Two state files and one prompt called it a Tuesday. **The chapters are right and the state files are corrected in place, and the warp is taken on Friday the twenty-eighth of July in Chapter 141, on the page, in two places.**
13. **THE FOUR ITEMS BATCH 0005 FLAGGED AND DID NOT APPLY ARE DISPOSED OF:** the two *everything in her* clauses are open by design and are not Volume 04's; **the five bells may be supplied a way at a price and the price must fall on somebody who is not the yard, and no harbor authority, no office in Veyr and no person with a seal may supply one**; **the relief lands on the fifteenth of August and the posting begins on the first of September and the fortnight between them is the only thing about the posting that is not decided;** and the attribution is at item 11 above.

### The hand-off to Volume 04 — six open threads and no more

1. **Mara Venn's break with the Registry, which is Volume 4 and is a DIFFERENT EVENT from her leaving this crew. No chapter of Volume 03 fused them and no chapter may fuse them now.**
2. **The third column, the boy, and the pencil.** Off the stone since the fifteenth of May, forty marks standing whole, **and the yard's own book says in its own hand and with a date that he cannot do the thing he was kept for, and that entry binds a later volume as much as this one.** The third column was not filled on the eighth of August and nobody has been appointed.
3. **The ninth-line mark, which closes in Volume 5 and not before.**
4. **The question about who changed the rule**, asked in a market in this harbor on the thirteenth of August and not answered.
5. **The second season of the writ and the first of March, and the first of September, and a wage that was not paid on the fifteenth of August and three marks owing to a man of Ashtide.**
6. **The five bells at about five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards, and the four people of the Sill who own them and who have no boat, and the fact that no instrument in this harbor reaches that water, no money in this harbor reaches that water, and no trade on this coast whose work it is.**

**Carried forward in `outline/volume-04.md` and named there and NOT in this six, so that the hand-over stays at six:** the second-column bare fact and the spent naming of the twentieth of July; the lead plug, the bellwright and the undertaking; the coast road, the pilot and the Kettle; the delayed note's eleven and two; the custody items and the live question no paper in this harbor answers; the plate at the fifth seam and the refused plan of the seventeenth of January; the chart and its bell notation in a vessel's keeping; the two objects of nobody's on the mole's crown and the marking on one of them; the three places with no joint; the shard of worked glass nobody can read; the first echo's witness; Neave Tarrant; **and the harbor's poverty, which is not fixed for anything and must not be fixed in Volume 04 either.**

### The eleven mechanical repairs, listed

**Four weekday faults against lock L1** — Ch 106 (*Monday the ninth to Sunday the fifteenth* of May → *Tuesday the ninth to Monday the fifteenth*); Ch 115 (*the Thursday the fourth of June* → *the Sunday the fourth*, with six words added saying it was not a rota day, because the chapter's own seventh line says the boat came in on the Sunday the fourth); Ch 119 (*not on the Thursday the nineteenth* → *not on the rota day, the nineteenth*, and the fourth of June moved off the rota, which it was not on); **Ch 143 (*on Tuesday the fourth of May* and *on Monday the eleventh of May* → *Thursday* both, which is what the grid gives and what makes them a weekly pattern in the four days before the boy went off the stone).**

**Six wrong letters** — Ch 115 *harbourmaster* → *harbormaster*; Ch 120 ×4 and Ch 130 ×1, *licence* → *license*. **A search of all fifty chapters for the whole British-spelling class now returns nothing at all.**

**Two duplicated paragraphs**, against the `AGENTS.md` Quality Gate — the sixty identical words describing a return's jacket in Ch 121 and Ch 134, and the twenty-two-word cistern line opening Ch 136 and Ch 141. **In each pair the first instance is left exactly as it is and stands as the device, and the second is varied in the yard's own idiom with no fact added and no fact removed. A byte-comparison of every paragraph of twelve words or more across the fifty chapters now returns ZERO duplicates.** Thirty-six exact repeated sentences remain and every one is the volume's own device — the two-column read-out, the six conditions read at the boat in the same words because the lock requires it, two Crown notices quoted verbatim, and twelve stock sentences of the house about a town that does not change. None is repaired and the reason is recorded at § 21d of the audit.

### The four state-file figures corrected by the audit

**Batch 0003 was given as 33,731 words with Chapter 123 at 3,835 and is 33,738 with Chapter 123 at 3,842** — the batch's count was taken before the last of that batch's review repairs was applied and was never recomputed, which is the same class of error the Volume 02 audit found in its own Batch 0002 figure. **Batch 0002 is 30,356 and not 30,345; Batch 0004 is 30,916 and not 30,890; Batch 0005 is 29,823 and not 29,814**, the three having moved by the audit's own repairs. **The volume total had never been measured and is 154,738.** All four are corrected in place with the reason on their face, in this file, in `state/chapter-summaries.md` and in `state/continuity.md`.

## THE VOLUME 04 CLOSE AND ITS AUDIT — as AUDITED, 27 September 2026, and the Volume 05 hand-off

**`reviews/volume-04/volume-04-close.md` is the audit and is the home of every ruling below. NOTHING IN THIS SECTION IS NEW CANON; IT IS THE AUDIT'S ACCOUNT OF THE CANON, AND WHERE IT CORRECTS A FIGURE IT SAYS SO ON ITS FACE.**

- **VOLUME 04 IS CLOSED. Every clause of `outline/volume-04.md` is PAID, PART, RULED or NOT ATTEMPTED with the reason, and nothing is owed to Volume 04 and nothing is owed to a chapter that has not been written. THE VOLUME MEASURES 174,906 WORDS ACROSS FIFTY CHAPTERS, in five batches of 31,731 / 29,302 / 35,190 / 37,052 / 41,631, measured with `wc -w` including headings on 27 September 2026 and re-measured after this audit's nineteen mechanical repairs. THE FIVE FIGURES SUM TO THE SIXTH.**
- **NINETEEN MECHANICAL REPAIRS WERE APPLIED IN PLACE IN ELEVEN CHAPTERS — 151, 158, 162, 163, 183, 184, 192, 195, 196, 199 and 200 — and every one is listed individually at its § 21a with the lock it breaks and the fix. THREE ARE WEEKDAY WORDS, ONE IS A LINE THAT CARRIED TWO WRONG DATES, FOUR ARE THE DATE OF THE SEIZURE, TWO ARE THE DATE OF A FAILED EXAMINATION, TWO ARE THE DATE ON WHICH FOUR PEOPLE OF THIS COAST WROTE A LINE, TWO ARE THE ARRANGEMENT OF PAPER ON A MARKET WALL, ONE IS A BYTE-IDENTICAL PARAGRAPH AND FOUR ARE BRITISH SPELLINGS. NO SCENE, TURN, COST OR PLANNED BEAT WAS TOUCHED.**
- **THE CENTRAL PRESSURE IS RULED RESOLVED, AND IT RESOLVED IN THE PUBLICATION'S CONSEQUENCES AND NOT IN A CONVICTION. Nothing was decided, nobody was charged, and a yard which spent two volumes saying that a record a man can check is the only kind worth having came out of a room less able to be checked, by its own method, in public, with a clerk's own entry against it. THE VOLUME'S OWN ARGUMENT — THAT A REASON DOES NOT TRAVEL — IS NOT THE ARGUMENT THE OUTLINE ASKED FOR AND IS A BETTER ONE, AND THE OUTLINE IS NOT AMENDED.**
- **THE MIDPOINT IS PAID AND RATIFIED AS DELIVERED**, in the chapter's own narrower form: the join went on a notice of the tenth of September and not on the yard's own work, the clerk of the circuit office could see the two inks himself and entered the fact before the man who put the join there, the man said *I do not know* three times and would not say it a fourth time in a different way, the market of the eighteenth of July was read back to him in his own words by a stranger in a flat voice, and the third column was given up to a clerk because the office cannot make a boy come. **THE VOLUME ANSWERS THE HARDER OF ITS TWO QUESTIONS: THE PROBLEM WITH A SECOND COLUMN IS NOT THAT A REASON DOES NOT TRAVEL, IT IS THAT A YARD WITH SOMETHING TO BE CAREFUL ABOUT IS THE ONLY KIND OF YARD THAT GETS A SECOND COLUMN AT ALL.**
- **THE CLIMAX IS PAID IN ALL FOUR OF THE OUTLINE'S ELEMENTS, AND FOUR RULINGS ARE RECORDED ON THE PAGE.** **THE MANIFEST IN THE COPY RECORD IS THE *ASTER GANNET*'S OWN** — the same manifest `bible/world.md:195` and `outline/volume-04.md`'s reveal discipline mean when they forbid proving who altered it, and the clauses are otherwise vacuous — **AND THEREFORE THE COMPARTMENT IS STILL NOT NAMED, THE THREE GANNET DOCUMENTS ARE STILL THREE, AND NO CHAPTER OF THE FIFTY RESTATES ANY STEP OF THE COMPARTMENT'S FINDING IN EITHER THE TWO-STEP OR THE THREE-STEP FORM. THE COST IS CALDER'S OWN ACCOUNT OF HIS OWN EARS IN A ROOM IN FRONT OF A CLERK, ENTERED AGAINST HIMSELF IN THE FIRST COLUMN BEFORE ANYBODY ELSE COULD ENTER IT. THE ITEM STAYS IN THE CUSTODY OF THE CIRCUIT OFFICE, THE PARTY DOES NOT APPLY, THE HOLDER WANTS IT AND SAYS SO IN FRONT OF A CLERK, AND NO DIGIT OF ANY OF THE THREE NUMBERS IS ON ANY PAGE. THE ACKNOWLEDGMENT WENT INTO A MARKET AT A PENNY A SHEET AND NOT ONE PENNY OF IT CAME OUT OF THIS YARD.**
- **THE CONCRETE RESOLUTION IS PAID IN FOUR OF THE OUTLINE'S FIVE ELEMENTS AND RULED IN A DIFFERENT FORM ON THE FIFTH: MARA VENN HAS HELD NO OFFICE SINCE THE FOURTH OF JANUARY AND IS NOT RESIGNING ANYTHING, BECAUSE THERE IS NOTHING TO RESIGN FROM. THE VOLUME PAID A BREAK IN A DIFFERENT FORM — A LETTER OF THE ELEVENTH OF OCTOBER REFUSING TO BE CITED, QUOTED, PRODUCED OR ASKED AGAIN, A FINDING UNSIGNED AT HER OWN ASKING, A LINE PRICED ON THE TWENTY-NINTH OF SEPTEMBER THAT WAS AN UNDERTAKING AND NOT MONEY, A LETTER OF THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF NOVEMBER PRICING AN ASK AND REFUSING IT, AND FORTY MARKS FIRST AFTER FOOD — AND IT PAID NEITHER A LEAVING NOR A RE-ASSOCIATION.**
- **THE SECOND BELL ELIGIBILITY IS PAID AND RULED ON THREE POINTS WITHOUT SOFTNESS. (a) A MECHANISM OF THIS SHAPE IS NEW TO THE PAGE AND IS NOT PRE-EMPTED: CHAPTERS 1 TO 150 SAY ALMOST NOTHING ABOUT THE SECOND BELL, NO OFFICE THAT EXAMINES IS EVER NAMED, NO EXAMINATION IS EVER SAT, AND THE WORD *NOT EXAMINED* IS ON NO PAGE BEFORE CHAPTER 200. (b) THE LOSS IS THE PARTY'S OWN DOING AND THE YARD ENTERED IT IN ITS OWN LEFT HAND BEFORE ANY FIGURE OF ANY KIND AND SAID IT WAS NOT GOING TO BLAME THE WRIT. (c) NO LATER CHAPTER MAY MOVE IT ONTO THE WRIT, MAY SAY THE COURT REFUSED HIM ANYTHING, MAY SAY THE COURT FOUND AGAINST HIM, OR MAY SAY HE HAS SAT THE EXAMINATION SINCE SEPTEMBER 2025 — AND THE FORM SAYS THE OPPOSITE OF ALL FOUR AND THE FORM IS THE AUTHORITY. THE ONE NEW CANON IS THAT HE WAS PUT TO THE EXAMINATION ONCE, IN SEPTEMBER 2025, AND DID NOT SATISFY IT.**
- **THE NEW-VOLUME QUESTION IS OPENED IN A MARKET ON WEDNESDAY THE SECOND OF DECEMBER IN ABOUT FORTY PEOPLE'S HEARING AND NOT ANSWERED, AND THE AUDIT RULES THAT NO OFFICE IN VEYR IS SHOWN A DOCUMENT THAT WOULD ANSWER IT, THAT THE NINE HARBORS ARE NOT NAMED, THAT THE OFFICE OF RETURNING MEMORY IS NOT NAMED AND IS NOT NAMED IN VOLUME 05 EITHER, THAT THE NINE-LINE MARK IS NOT EXPLAINED, AND THAT THE QUIETENING AND THE CROWN SEAL ARE NOT NAMED.**
- **THE FINAL CHAPTER IMAGE IS NOT PAID AND IS NOT ROUNDED UP. FOUR OF ITS ELEMENTS ARE ABSENT ON PURPOSE: NO PAGE WENT UP ON ANY WALL IN THIS HARBOR IN THE THREE WEEKS ENDED ON THE FIFTH OF DECEMBER AND NONE CAME OFF; NO PERSON WENT OUT IN A BOAT IN A CLOSING IMAGE; THE THIRD COLUMN IS EMPTY AND NOBODY HAS BEEN APPOINTED TO IT; AND THE DELAY WAS NOT MEASURED, NOT ADVANCED AND NOT WRITTEN DOWN WITH A DATE AGAINST IT. THE FIVE ELEMENTS THAT ARE PAID ARE THE HARBOR'S STANDING AND THE CHANDLER'S NOTE, AND THE NOTE IS NOT RE-WRITTEN. THE FINAL CHAPTER IMAGE IS THE VOLUME CLOSE'S WORK AND NOT A BATCH'S, AND IT IS NOT SMUGGLED INTO ANY BATCH PROMPT.**
- **OF THE THIRTY LOCKS, NONE IS BREACHED. ONE IS AMENDED AND ITS AMENDMENT STANDS (L1). ONE IS NOT PAID AND IS CARRIED OPEN (L7). SIX ARE HELD IN A FORM NARROWER OR BROADER THAN THE LOCK ASKED FOR AND EACH IS RULED. TWENTY-TWO HOLD EXACTLY.**

### The one ruling that is the most important thing in the audit — LOCK L7, THE PLATE NOTE

**LOCK L7 IS NOT PAID. IT IS PART. IT IS CARRIED TO VOLUME 05 OPEN. IT IS NOT WAIVED AND IT IS NOT RE-OPENED TO GET A DIFFERENT ANSWER, AND THE REASON IS NOT THE ONE THE CLOSE PROMPT GAVE.**

`outline/volume-04.md` L7 said: *in Volume 04 the plate note CLOSES WITH A REMOVED FILE PAGE — a document, in an office's hand — AND NOT WITH A CONFESSION FROM THE FAR BENCH.* **The plate is in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched, in twenty-five chapters; the note stands open in twenty-five chapters; no chapter reached it.** **THE DOCUMENT WAS NOT WITHHELD. IT WAS PRODUCED, QUOTED, DESCRIBED AND SUMMARISED IN CHAPTERS 175, 176 AND 180, WHERE THE REGISTRY'S OWN FORM'S OWN LINE READS *Reason entered by this office: risk management*, AND IT WENT TO MOVEMENT 3's TURN AND TO MARA VENN's BREAK AND NOT TO THE PLATE.** **A DOCUMENT PRODUCED ONCE IN THIS SERIES IS SPENT. IT IS NOT PRODUCED AGAIN IN VOLUME 05 OR ANY LATER VOLUME FOR ANY PURPOSE.** See `outline/volume-05.md` lock L7.

### The two-calendar fault, ruled

**CHAPTERS 151 TO 170 RUN ON ONE GRID AND CHAPTERS 171 TO 200 RUN ON ANOTHER, THREE DAYS OUT FROM IT, AND THE CHANGE FALLS BETWEEN THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER AND THE FIRST OF OCTOBER 2026. THE AMENDMENT AT THE FOOT OF `outline/volume-04.md`'s LOCK L1 STANDS, IS BINDING, AND IS NOT RE-OPENED. SIX SINGLE-CHAPTER DATE FAULTS ARE REPAIRED AND THE FAULT IS NOT.**

**THE REPAIR QUESTION AND ITS ANSWER: it is NOT repairable inside the text of Volume 04, and the only textual repair is a re-staging of twenty chapters costing one sitting of two days, six wage Tuesdays, six slate-wall wipes, a rota on six days, a cistern run on six days, forty days of standing inventory and a fortnight of read-out, and it would be a different book.** **THE THIRD OPTION IS CHOSEN: THE ALMANAC IS NOT THE RULE OF THIS BOOK, THE CHAPTERS DECIDE EVERY DATE, AND A PLANNING FILE MAY NOT PRINT A DAY TABLE A CHAPTER CAN CONTRADICT.** The other two options are ruled out with their costs: a printed erratum costs the book's own method, because a webnovel has no front matter a reader of Chapter 173 would see; a line in the yard's own book costs the first column, because a checkable line about an uncheckable fact is the one thing this yard has refused to enter for four volumes. **THE SLATE WALL'S COUNTER SURVIVES THE FAULT INTACT — sixteen wipes, one running number, no gap, no repeat, from the fourteenth of August to the thirtieth of November — AND THAT IS WHY A RE-BASING WOULD ALSO HAVE TO RE-DERIVE A FIGURE A READER CAN CHECK.** `outline/volume-05.md` locks L1 and L1a carry this forward, and L1 PRINTS NO TABLE.

THE SHEET ON THAT WALL IS THE ONE OF THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF AUGUST, THE RETURN IS UNDER IT, NO NAVAL PAPER WAS EVER NAILED OVER IT, THE TWO NAVAL NOTICES CAME DOWN IN AUGUST AND WERE NOT TAKEN DOWN BY THIS YARD, AND THERE IS NO SHEET OF THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST ON THAT WALL BECAUSE IT WAS ONE OF THE FOUR THAT CAME OFF ON THE SIXTEENTH. 2. **There were four returns and no fifth, all four caught by the first limb by itself, the day of a finding not counted, the counting rule spoken out loud in a room, and no finding of worked glass made on any day of the fifty chapters. NOTHING IS RECALLED, NOTHING IS CORRECTED, NOTHING IS UN-SAID AND NOTHING IS RE-SENT.**
3. **The delayed note: eleven logged occasions and two with no call in them stand, neither count was advanced by arithmetic or by a measurement, no occasion was entered in any of the fifty chapters, the delay is a hand's width of a second and has not moved, nobody in this harbor has ever said why, this party is not going to be the first, and the answer is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint. Volume 03's forward permission — that a Volume 04 chapter *might* write the figure again on a flat calm with a slate and a stopwatch — WAS NOT EXERCISED BY ANY CHAPTER.**
