A PUBLISHED FIGURE IS NOW ON A LIST OF THINGS THIS YARD HAS DECIDED IT CANNOT PUT IN FRONT OF ANYBODY. ON THE SEVENTH OF JULY THE YARD'S ARGUMENT FOR A WALL WAS THAT SOMEBODY CAN BE ASKED A QUESTION ABOUT IT, AND ON THE TWENTY-SECOND OF NOVEMBER ITS OWN SHIPWRIGHT WROTE THE TWO FIGURES DOWN AS A THING THAT MUST NOT GO INTO A ROOM, AND THE METHOD WON.**
6. **The custody items: three, no digit of any of the three numbers on any page, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request, and the live question — what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material — is still unanswered and was not put to anybody, and the Court of Wreck Claims' form does not answer it and may not be read as having answered it.**
7. **THE WORD *CUSTODY* NOW OCCURS IN THIS VOLUME WHERE IT DID NOT IN MOVEMENT 4: four times in Chapter 195 and six times in Chapter 196, because the volume's subject is a record about an item entered in a custody room. No digit is quoted and the room is not asked for on any day.**
8. **The bell, the bellwright and the lead plug: the going out of the second of May is done and is spent and was not repeated inside four years, the standing sentence of the third of May stands unreversed and is carried in the closing position in its own terms, the bellwright was not asked in any of the fifty chapters and is in no chapter of the last two batches as a person, no digit of anything he said on the second of July is printed, the chest was not opened and was not brought down the hill, the eight marks and a day of a boat stand owing since the twenty-third of May, and the undertaking of the second of July binds the yard and not the office and not the market and not the boy.**
9. **The boy, the third column and the pencil: off the stone since the fifteenth of May and not replaced, forty marks standing whole and not reduced, the three nots standing, the head of the column keeping his hand and a request to scrape it out refused, the column not filled on any day of the volume with nobody appointed to it, and the volume closed on it empty. A COLUMN NOBODY FILLS IS A COLUMN SOMEBODY ELSE FILLS, AND THE SOMEBODY ELSE IS A CLERK WITH A PEN, AND A CLERK WITH A PEN IS NOT A BAD THING AND IS NOT A VILLAIN AND IS THE ONLY WAY A RECORD GETS MADE AT ALL, AND THE DAY HE FILLS IT IS THE DAY THIS YARD STOPS BEING ABLE TO SAY THAT THE ONE HONEST COLUMN IN IT IS HIS.**
10. **Alden Marr: no confession, no fragment, no report talk, no second story, and Calder did not go over to him on any day. THE FAR-BENCH FIGURE FOR THE WHOLE FIFTY CHAPTERS IS TWENTY-SIX DAYS, COUNTED FROM THE PROSE AND WRITTEN FROM THE COUNT, in five batches of seven, three, six, six and four.**
11. **The onset is the fourteenth of October 2025 and not the thirteenth, the dive was the night of the thirteenth, and the order and the seizure were the morning of the fourteenth at half past six, and those are three different things and may never be fused.**
12. **THE SENTENCE'S ATTRIBUTION: it is *both of those are true and neither of them cancels the other*, a shape of sentence and not one sentence, first recorded as Calder Marr's in a hearing room in Morrow Quay on the first of May in Volume 02 Chapter 97, no fisher in Volume 02 is recorded as having said it, and in VOLUME 04 IT WAS SAID SIX TIMES, IN CHAPTERS 157, 169, 180, 185, 186 AND 196, BY FOUR PEOPLE, IN FOUR PLACES, ABOUT FIVE DIFFERENT THINGS, AND IMPROVED ON BY NOBODY. No character remarked on how often it has been said and no fisher claimed to have invented it.**
13. **The book of hours and the Bellwright stage: sixteen lines over three nights in the last week of October — nine pumps, four boats out, three boats in — is the first thing about a listening buoy and is entered, no chapter turned it into a stage, the stage was not taken, and its costs of maintenance, public exposure and dependence on a trained crew are NOT DISCHARGED.**
14. **The five bells and the four people of the Sill: the case is at about five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards, it is not raised, offered, argued or promised, on the second of December a man of the third lay asked in a market whether it was coming up and was told it is on nobody's schedule, the four are alive with no boat and no wages and about nine marks between them, their limit stands and is not in any record, and the holder has not asked twice to have it lifted.**
15. **THE TWO OBJECTS OF NOBODY'S: neither has moved, one tarpaulin still has a corner off it since the seventeenth of June, and THE DAY-COUNT IS **NOT MONOTONIC** — it falls four days at the Chapter 180/181 boundary and is not one day a day anywhere in Chapters 181 to 190. THE STATE FILES' CLAIM OF MONOTONICITY IS WITHDRAWN IN PLACE WITH THE REASON ON ITS FACE, AND NO CHAPTER FIGURE IS MOVED, BECAUSE THE REPAIR IS A RE-BASING OF ABOUT TWENTY FIGURES IN TEN CHAPTERS OF TWO REVIEWED BATCHES AND THAT IS NOT A MECHANICAL REPAIR. THE VOLUME'S CLOSING FIGURE OF A HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-FIVE DAYS STANDS, BECAUSE IT IS ON THE PAGE IN CHAPTER 200.**

### The hand-over to Volume 05 — six open threads and no more

1. **Who altered the manifest, and what an office thought it was guarding against when it decided what it was allowed to lose.** The copy shows a line was altered and says on its own face that it does not show by whose hand, at whose instance or with what intent. The new-volume question was asked in a market in this harbor on Wednesday the second of December and not answered, and the reason is on the page. **This is the one open thread that is a document and a question about an institution, and it is the spine of Volume 05.**
2. **The third column, the boy, and the pencil.** Off the stone since the fifteenth of May, forty marks standing whole, the three nots standing, the head of the column keeping the boy's hand, the column empty and unfilled since the first of August, nobody appointed, and the party on the record as not knowing where he is and not asking.
3. **The nine-line mark, which closes in Volume 5 and not before.** Not referred to, not named, not explained in any of the fifty chapters, and the nine places in six books are not the same thing and were not converted into it.
4. **The two dates in a city: a copy of the said record is to be produced to the Court of Wreck Claims on the fourth day of January, and the writ runs to midnight on the first of March with the second season a named absence.** This yard has asked no office for anything in aid of the first, has sent nothing to a city, and has not been to the counter of that circuit office since the twenty-fourth of November.
5. **The five bells at about five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards, on nobody's schedule, and the four people who own them and who hold a limit this yard has not asked twice to have lifted.**
6. **Mara Venn's forty marks, first after food, unpaid, and the letter of the twenty-seventh of November in which she priced an ask and refused it and told this yard not to ask again this winter** — and the answer is not a purchase, not a forgiveness and not a repair, and the yard did not tell her about the copy and is not going to.

**AND WHAT A VOLUME 05 WRITER INHERITS FROM THREAD 6, STATED SO IT CANNOT BE MISSED: a woman of no office, thirty, owed forty marks first after food and six marks and two days as a price, who has refused in writing to be cited, quoted, produced or asked again; who has told this yard not to ask her again this winter; whose price for the November letter was that it be kept, that what she wrote go in the second column with a reason against the line, and that not one word of it go in a market. A Volume 05 chapter may not ask her for anything before the winter is out, may not put her in a room, may not fuse the refusal with the copy, may not reduce either figure, and may not make the forty marks a purchase, a forgiveness or a repair.**

## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 210

**VOLUME 05 MOVEMENT 1 IS WRITTEN, CHAPTERS 201 TO 210, TWENTY CONSECUTIVE DAYS FROM SUNDAY THE SIXTH TO FRIDAY THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF DECEMBER 2026. ITS TURN IS PAID. NOTHING IS RESOLVED AND NOTHING IS WON, AND THE BATCH'S LAST LINE IS A FACT AND NOT A RESOLUTION.** In twenty days a yard of one and a half was handed a form with no signature and no date on it by a man who did not give his name, heard the Crown's position in one sentence, asked a reeve in a street what his office thought of a thing and got an answer and a price that fell on the reeve's own office, worked a day at a seam, put its own eleven-line list beside a form and found that the list is the answer and that the answer is a list it cannot put in an answer, said something in a street that was read as an intention to keep and could not be taken back, paid a penny at a copyist's counter for two figures it could not get any other way, was told the price of a man's day by a man with a lighter before anybody asked for it, and had a printer's letter up a hill with one question in it and put it in a drawer, and then understood on the twenty-fifth, in its own left hand, what it has been asked to be.

### The movement's own beats, ten of ten, in the order the volume outline gives them

1. **THE FORM CAME UP THIS HILL ON WEDNESDAY THE NINTH OF DECEMBER**, a sheet and a half, brought on foot by a man in a coat who stayed unnamed and who gave the reason a clerk of the circuit office gave at the same bench on the twenty-third of November: a man's name at the foot of a thing is a thing a man in a city can hold for four years. **There is no signature anywhere and no date at the head, none in the body and none at the foot, and at the foot there is the name of an office, and that office is not in this yard's book and is not named in this book.** It proposes that this yard hold a copy of a record for the coast and be named in an entry as the person who holds it. It says the party is not to be examined, licensed, enrolled, paid, or asked to find anything, and is asked one question. **It says nothing about what follows an answer of no, and the date of the answer is the only date on it, and the date of the answer is in this yard's keeping.** The man said he is not the man who directs it, said he did not know what picking up a sheet off a bench is, and went down the mole and nobody followed him. **The Crown's position reached this harbor in one sentence and one sentence only: testimony is safe in one place and nowhere else, and this harbor is not that one, and that is not an opinion, it is what three hundred years of an office is. Nobody said it was a lie and nobody said the man was anything at all.**
2. **THE HARBOR AUTHORITY AGREED, IN THE SENSE THAT A THING KEPT IN MORROW QUAY IS A THING KEPT IN MORROW QUAY**, said in the open air at the foot of the mole's steps in about eleven people's hearing, and the reason it gave is that no office on this coast can get at a copy of a record kept in this place, and that a man in a city who wants a thing in a fortnight will not send a carriage and will write to this harbor authority first, because that is the name a man writes first when he does not know any other. **He named what his office cannot do — it cannot certify a copy of a record, cannot hold a record for the coast, cannot enter it in a spent ledger whose ninth acknowledgement is spent and whose tenth may not be entered, cannot renew the writ of the first of May and is the only office that can, cannot overrule a seizure and cannot certify a glass witness — and said he was not going to do the rest of it and pretend afterwards that it was part of it. He entered the whole thing on a page of his own and not in the ledger, because a page of his own is not a record and cannot be produced on demand. THE PRICE WAS NAMED BEFORE HE SAID YES AND IT FELL ON HIS OWN OFFICE, WHICH IS NOW A NAME IN THIS BUSINESS, and every party who comes up that hill about anything at all is going to be told at the foot of those steps that this office has an opinion about a copy of a record, for years, including the ones who have nothing to do with this party. HE IS STILL NOT A FRIEND OF THIS PARTY AND SAID IT AGAIN.**
3. **ORDINARY WORK DID NOT STOP.** A day of about four hours and a quarter at the face of the second seam on the twelfth, landward of the third seam's mark, on a hirer's own account, with nobody in the water, a hand lead's line over the side and back four times, the gross on the board twelve and the yard's being three, the book written in the ship's hand, the slate going into the book in her own writing and the holder reading it back after her. **Entered as a day not earned whole and not as a day at a figure, because the yard is a party of one and a half until the first of March and every day it books in this month is a day it books with a man in it. The compressor's gland and the hose after it, and this harbor has no hose.**
4. **THE ELEVEN-LINE LIST WAS TESTED BESIDE THE OFFER AND THE TWO DO NOT AGREE, and that is the batch's discovery.** The ship's hand took the sheet of the twenty-second of November down and read the eleven aloud and put the form beside it with about four inches of bare wood between them. **A place a copy of a record is kept in is a room, and the eleven lines are a list of what does not go into a room, and every one of them is a thing this yard would be right to keep out of a room, and so the eleven lines are the answer to that form — and the answer to that form is eleven lines this yard cannot put in an answer, because an answer carries the name of the party, the place and the date, and the limit of the four of the Sill is a reason, and a reason does not go into a record, and this yard holds a writ and a writ is a channel and a man with a writ is the thing that limit was written against. The limit was read out once, in a shed, and it is that the account is not to be read out, is not to go into a book that leaves this harbor, is not to be said to a man with a seal, and not one name to any office in any city. NOBODY'S NAME WENT NEAR A PIECE OF PAPER ABOUT A FORM.** Item ten has happened and came back no. Item eleven, the two figures for the bar of gravel and the Kettle, has not been moved off that list in three weeks and is not going to be moved in December.
5. **THE YARD SAID SOMETHING THAT WAS NOT A YES IN A STREET AND IT WAS READ AS AN INTENTION TO KEEP, AND IT COULD NOT BE CORRECTED.** At the head of this dock on the sixteenth, in about eleven people's hearing, asked by a man of the third lay who said he was not asking it for anybody, the holder gave the whole shape of the sheet and said in the open that the answer will be in his own hand and is not an office's and is not signed by a clerk and has no day on the sheet to be late, and that he could not say on that day whether it was a yes or a no, and that he would not be in a position to say in three weeks either. **Two men of the first lay were saying on the Thursday that this yard had not said no, and one of them said the word *hold* twice, and the yard did not go up and correct either of them. THE REASON IT COULD NOT CORRECT IT IS IN THE SECOND COLUMN AND IS THAT A CORRECTION IS THE REASON, AND THE REASON IS THE FOUR OF THE SILL, AND IT DOES NOT GO INTO A STREET. The chapter's two columns are the reason against the line and the fact that a reason does not travel, and the gap between them was not closed and says so on the page. THE SAME TWO MEN WERE SAYING A THING ABOUT THIS YARD AT THE HEAD OF THAT DOCK AGAIN ON SATURDAY THE NINETEENTH, WHICH IS ENTERED IN CHAPTER 208 IN THE PROSE AND IN THE FIRST COLUMN AS THE SECOND OF TWO DAYS, AND THE YARD DID NOT GO UP ON THAT DAY EITHER. THE MARKET OFFICE WAS NOT USED AND COULD NOT HAVE BEEN: it is open on a Tuesday and a Friday morning and this was a Wednesday and a Thursday.**
6. **A COUNTER OFF THE FISH MARKET PRICED TWO THINGS AND THE TWO PRICES ARE NOT THE SAME PRICE.** No page of the copy of the record was read aloud there and nobody in that room heard one line of it. **A page that anybody in nine places could check is nine people who each went and got it, and nine men's time, and a man's time in a working port in December is a day at about three marks — so the price of a checkable page is the whole of a day's work at a seam. A page that nobody can check is a sheet and a shed and no money at all, and is the one that cannot be shown to anybody. And a paragraph is a trade and a trade travels, and the moment a reason goes on a page it is a paragraph, and a reason cannot be kept in a paragraph.** She also said, about sheets in general and not about this yard's, that a sheet with an office's name at the foot of it and no man's name on it anywhere is the easiest thing in the world for a copyist to sell, because there is nobody on it for her to ask where it came from. **The yard bought a sheet of her paper at a penny and it was for its own use and not for a copy of anything, and the reason against the line is that this party cannot put a price in a record out of a person's mouth.**
7. **THE COST OF A DAY WAS ENTERED BEFORE IT WAS ASKED FOR.** A man of fifty-five with a lighter told the ship's hand at the boards on the twentieth that he hired a man on the Saturday evening for the Monday, paid him two marks and his victuals out of his own pocket, and the man did not come. **The man's reason, in his words: if everything a yard says in this town goes into a book in a city then a day's work of mine goes into one too, and I have got a girl at a school in a town four days up this coast and I am not having her name read out in a town I have never been to. The two men of the first lay who were saying it on the Saturday evening were saying it like a thing that had been settled, AND IT WAS THE SECOND OF TWO DAYS ON WHICH THE SAME TWO MEN WERE SAYING A THING ABOUT THIS YARD, THE FIRST BEING THURSDAY THE SEVENTEENTH, AND CHAPTER 208 ENTERS THAT IN ITS PROSE AND IN ITS FIRST COLUMN WITH THE REASON AGAINST BOTH LINES, AND THE ENTRY SAYS THAT THE YARD WENT UP ON NEITHER DAY, THAT THE FIRST OF THE TWO IS OUT OF ITS OWN EYES AND THE SECOND IS OUT OF WHAT A MAN OF THE SECOND LAY SAID AT THE BOARDS, AND THAT IT HAS NOT GOT EITHER NAME AND IS NOT GOING UP THE HILL FOR IT. IT IS NOT THE THIRD LAY'S FOUR DAYS AND IT IS NOT A MAN OF THE FIRST LAY'S MONDAY AND IT IS NOT THE TIDE GANG'S THURSDAY MORNING, AND ITS SHAPE IS A MAN WHO WAS PAID AND DID NOT COME, WHICH NEITHER OF THE OTHER TWO IS.** The yard did not offer the two marks and entered why on the Sunday, and the reason is that a yard which pays a man's day for a thing it said in a street has bought the street, and the yard does not want it and cannot afford it, and the first of those three is the one that is on the page.
8. **A PRINTER'S LETTER CAME UP THE HILL ON THE TWENTY-THIRD WITH ONE QUESTION IN IT, and it is not a threat and not a favor and the printer says so himself.** A sheet bought at a penny at a counter in this market on the sixth of December went up a coast road in a box of fish with a man who sells ice, a printer kept it because his trade keeps things, and on the ninth a man came to that counter and bought type and nothing else and asked whether the printer could say where a copy of a record was. **His one question is where the copy of the record is, and the yard did not answer it, and the reason is in the first column and not the second, and it is that a reply is a document with this yard's name at the foot of it.**
9. **THE BATCH'S TURN.** **The instrument the yard built to make itself credible is the instrument that is going to make it locatable, and the yard built it on purpose on the seventh of July and nobody made it do it.** The acknowledgment of the twenty-ninth of November — which this yard did not write, did not pay for, did not ask for and did not allow, and which a copyist of fifty-eight took up in a market of her own account and told them to keep away from — **is the one place in this country where a man in a city can look to find out where this yard is, and it did the whole of the work in nine days.** A sheet with an office's name at the foot of it and no man's name on it can be bought at a penny by anybody and resold to anybody and there is nobody on the paper to ask. The copy of the record is in this shed and the yard wants it, and that is the reason a line in a printer's book has a town on it and no address. **Both of those are true and neither of them cancels the other**, and the shape was said once in this batch and by nobody else.
10. **THE RESOLUTION'S REMAINING ITEMS ARE ON THE PAGE, in the outline's order, and each was paid by saying it out loud or by entering it rather than by winning anything:** a boy off the stone and not replaced with a first-column line and a date on it; a chandler's note not re-written and not said a fourth time and not said once in twenty days; a longer leash with a name on it, and the name is an office and not a man; a reeve who put one more thing on a page of his own at his own office's cost and is still not a friend; five bells at about five and a half fathoms that are not raised and are not on anybody's schedule; and the market question, not answered, with the refusal on the page in the yard's own hand with the reason against it, **referred to once, in a first column, with its date, its asker, his age and his tonnage, and with the answer in the words already on the page and not improved on, and the yard entered as not going to answer it in a book either. NO COMPACT IS NAMED. NO OFFICE ANSWERS IT. NO DOCUMENT ANSWERS IT. NOTHING IN THIS BATCH ANSWERS IT IN ANY DIRECTION. THE VOLUME IS NOT CLOSED.**

### The relationship pressure at the end of Chapter 210

**Calder and Mara. Nothing moved and nothing was asked.** She is four days up this coast, of no office, not on the crew, not a holder, not on the four names, owed forty marks first after food and six marks and two days as a price. **She was not written to in three weeks, no letter of hers came up this hill, she is not in a room, and the undertaking of the twenty-ninth of September — that this party does not ask her for anything again, not at a figure, not at nothing, not for a line, not for a name, not in a room and not in a shed — stands and was not tested because nothing was asked.** Her price of the twenty-seventh of November was that the letter be kept, that what she wrote go in the second column with a reason against the line, and that not one word of it go in a market: **all three of those were kept.** The not-telling of the seventh of August is six entries and a sixth occasion not to, and it is not paid, and it was not fused with the form, with the letter, with the printer or with the refused day, and it bought nothing.

**Calder and Kest. The reeve spent one more thing and it was not a favor and not a rescue.** He said in the street that a thing kept in Morrow Quay is a thing kept in Morrow Quay, named six things his office cannot do, put the whole of it on a page of his own and not in the ledger, and paid for it with his own office's findability. **He refused the tenth acknowledgement out loud again, said he is not a friend of this party and never said he was, and the yard agrees with him and did not improve on the entry of the nineteenth of November. Neither man got what he wanted.**

**Calder and Bevin. Unchanged and not in a room.** The boy is not in any of these ten chapters as a person. The three nots stand. Forty marks stands whole. ## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 200 — RETAINED IN FULL AS THE BATCH 0005 RECORD, AND SUPERSEDED ON EVERY POINT THE VOLUME 04 AUDIT RULED ON ABOVE

**VOLUME 04 MOVEMENT 5 IS WRITTEN. ITS CLIMAX IS PAID, AND IT IS PAID AS A COPY OF A RECORD AND NOT AS AN OBJECT, AND THE VOLUME'S FINAL CHAPTER IMAGE IS NOT REACHED AND IS NOT ATTEMPTED.** In twenty days a yard of two and half people applied in writing for a copy of a record at a cost of a penny, was given a form with an office's name on it and no date on it, was given a copy off a counter at the end of a day and read it in a shed, put a page and a half into a room over a tide-gang store on two days, had its own best man asked in front of a clerk whether he could vouch for the water and heard him say that he cannot, agreed in that room that the object is not coming up this hill and said out loud in the same room that he wanted it, was refused a part payment on forty marks by a letter and paid the price, stood in a market and let a copyist read a form of an office about it at a penny a sheet, refused two questions in a market and gave the reason, said the chandler's note is not re-written for the third time in seven months, and lost the eligibility it wanted before the writ on a half-sheet out of a court, and the name at the head of that half-sheet is an office and not a man.

### The movement's own beats, ten of ten, in the order the volume outline gives them

1. **THE PARTY APPLIED.** One sheet, the holder's own left hand, dated Monday the sixteenth of November, asking the circuit office of this place for a copy of one record of that office, being the record of the entry of one item of recovered transparent mineral in the custody room of that office; and it says on the face of it that it asks for a copy of a record and not for the object. A penny of the ordinary post came out of the bag. **The reason it applies now, when it entered in June and again on the eighteenth of September that it would not, is in the second column with a reason against the line, and the reason is not that the old reason has gone soft: it is that a copy of a record carries on its own face the limits the record itself carries, and a limit printed on the face of a sheet is the one kind of limit that does not arrive in a city as a bare line with nothing behind it. The party has also entered, on the same line, that it is not saying no to it in four years and is not saying yes either, and that the release of such a copy is not in this yard's keeping and has never been in it.**
2. **THE APPLICATION WAS ANSWERED AND THE COPY CAME INTO THE YARD'S HANDS.** A clerk came up the hill on the twenty-third with a form which says a party may apply at its own cost, which grants no copy and makes none and releases nothing, and that **no date attaches**; and that at the foot, where a signature would be, there is the name of an office and no man's name, and the clerk gave the reason, that a clerk's name at the foot of it would let a man in a city say in four years that a clerk of that circuit decided a copy should be made, and that that is not what happened. The holder went down to the counter on the afternoon of the twenty-fourth and came back up with one sheet, and a clerk told him three things about a thing leaving a counter, one of which is that after the first of December nobody in this harbor is going to ask that question again. **The copy has six heads and about a page and a half on the face of it and the yard can check none of it, and the yard said so out loud in the shed, and the two lengths are a page and a half and the rest of this book's life.**
3. **THE CHOICE WAS MADE AND IT WAS THE ONE THAT COSTS.** Three instruments on a bench on the morning of the eighteenth: a page in a shed, worth what two men in one room are worth; a page in a market, read by about four hundred people and taken down by any man with a knife; a page in a room in a city, which goes on a record and cannot be got off. **The market is the only one of the three that arrives with its reasons on it, and it is the one the party did not use, and the reason is that a reason on a market wall is a reason in a market, and four people of this coast wrote a line and signed it. The party chose the room and entered that it chose the instrument that costs it.**
4. **THE COPY WAS READ OUT IN A ROOM, WHOLE, WITH ITS LIMITS ON IT, BY THE SHIP'S HAND AND NOT BY THE HOLDER**, in the hearing room over the tide-gang store on Thursday the twenty-sixth, in about eleven people's hearing, and the clerk read it all back and entered that the answer came from her and not from him, which is the second time that has been entered in that room. **And in the same sitting the man who is the best reader of joins in this harbor was asked, by a clerk who had written the question in his own page first, whether he could say what was in the water off the south end of this mole on any day this year, and he said he could give a figure, an hour, a state of the tide and the name of the man who hauled the line, and that he cannot tell the room what was under that water and never has been able to, and that he said a thing in a market on the eighteenth of July that was wrong in three parts out of four and has not taken it back and is not going to be asked to and is not refusing to be asked to, and that he cannot vouch for the water with his own ears, and that he is not going to get out of it by being right about something. The clerk entered all of it. Nobody in that room said a single thing.**
5. **THE ITEM STAYS IN THE CUSTODY OF THE CIRCUIT OFFICE AND THE PARTY DOES NOT APPLY FOR IT, AND THE HOLDER ACCEPTED THAT IN A ROOM AND SAID HE WANTED IT.** He said that if it came up this hill that afternoon it would be worth more to this yard than every mark that has gone through its hands since the first of May and that he would not know what to do with it, and that four people of this coast wrote a line in October about what gets said and what does not, and that this yard is not going to use a piece of glass to be the reason it does not ask. **The shape of sentence was said once, in that room, about the item not being theirs and the yard wanting it and not asking, and improved on by nobody.**
6. **AN OFFICE ACKNOWLEDGED THE DISCREPANCY IN A FORM THAT GOES ON A PAGE, AND A WOMAN WITH COPIES OF IT AT A PENNY A SHEET READ IT OUT IN A MARKET.** Pernel Saye, fifty-eight, took it up in the market on Monday the thirtieth of her own account and unasked, said that a sheet of that office is a public sheet and is the only kind worth the candle, said in thirty-one years of copying she has never copied one of that office's for nothing and is not going to start, said that the fourth paragraph is the part that was put in on purpose, made twenty-one copies at about nine minutes a sheet, and read the whole thing out at the head of the fish dock on Tuesday the first of December in about forty people's hearing in about nine minutes. **She was not paid by this yard and this yard did not ask her, did not send anybody and did not stand near her while she did it. A man of the first lay bought four copies at four pence. Two men of the third lay read the whole of it standing up and gave it back without buying a sheet. THE WORDS ON THE BOARD ARE THE OFFICE'S WORDS AND THE YARD COULD NOT HAVE IMPROVED THEM BY ONE.**
7. **MARA VENN PRICED A THING AND THE ANSWER WAS NO, AND THE NO IS NOT A PUNISHMENT AND NOT A REPAIR.** See the relationship section below.
8. **CALDER MARR IS NOT ELIGIBLE TO BE EXAMINED FOR THE FULL SECOND BELL LICENSE, AND IT IS NOT THE WRIT.** See the section below.
9. **A QUESTION WAS ASKED IN A MARKET ABOUT THE ACT OF AN OFFICE AGREEING TO SOMETHING, AND IT WAS NOT ANSWED, AND THE REFUSAL IS IN THE YARD'S OWN HAND WITH THE REASON AGAINST THE LINE, AND THE REASON IS ABOUT A PERSON OF NO OFFICE AND NOT ABOUT THE OFFICE.** See the section below.
10. **THE CONCRETE RESOLUTION'S REMAINING ITEMS ARE ON THE PAGE, in the outline's order, and each was paid by saying it out loud rather than by winning anything:** a boy off the stone and not replaced with a first-column line and a date on it; a chandler's note not re-written and said out loud at the head of this dock for the third time in seven months; a longer leash with a name on it and the name an office; a reeve with nothing left to put in his book who is not a friend; five bells at about five and a half fathoms that are not raised and are not on anybody's schedule; and the market question, not answered, with the refusal on the page. **THE NEW-VOLUME QUESTION IS OPEN AND NOT ANSWERED. THE VOLUME'S FINAL CHAPTER IMAGE IS NOT REACHED: no page went up on any wall in this harbor in three weeks and none came off, the third column is empty and nobody is appointed to it, the delay was not measured and nothing was written down with a date against it, and nobody went out in a boat in a closing image. THE VOLUME IS NOT CLOSED.**

### The relationship pressure at the end of Chapter 200

**Calder and Mara. HE ASKED HER FOR SOMETHING AND SHE PRICED IT AND THE ANSWER WAS NO, AND IT IS NOT FUSED WITH ANYTHING.** He wrote to her on the sixteenth of November, one question, in his own left hand, dated, and the question was **whether this party might offer her part payment on the forty marks out of a day of work and take the rest when there is any.** It asked for nothing else. It did not ask her to come to this harbor, to be in a room, for a line, for what any record says, or for anything else, and it said that if the answer is no then it is no and the yard will enter it with the date and will not ask a second time. She answered on the twenty-seventh, one sheet, in her own hand, **price first and answer second, and the price is that he keep the letter, put what she has written in the second column with a reason against the line, and put not one word of it in a market** — and the reason she gives is that in about four years a man in a city is going to read that a yard of Morrow Quay went to a woman it owed forty marks to and asked whether it might pay her part of it and was refused, and that the said reading is going to be true of a man like him and is not going to be true of him, and that there is nothing she can write from where she sits that will make the difference. **The answer is no, and the reason is that a part payment is a receipt, and a receipt is a document, and a document is how a person of no office becomes a thing a person with an office can use, which is the sentence she said to him in a room on the twenty-ninth of September and which he did not argue with then.** She also wrote that the six marks and two days has not moved and is not going to be reduced by anybody including by her, because it is her price and not a debt, and that the two have never been one thing and that this is the first time anybody has tried to make them one. **The letter says not one word about the copy, the room, the sitting or the sheet, and the yard had not told her about any of them and is not going to, and the yard entered that. The not-telling of the seventh of August is now a SIXTH OCCASION NOT TO, entered on the sixteenth of November on a line of its own, with the reason of the fourth of October given again and the undertaking not to invent a second one, and the ship's hand said in the shed that it is not going on the same line as the answer to a question about a part payment, and that a price is not a confession and a refusal is not a repair. The forty marks stands first after food, unpaid, not one shilling of it paid, not one shilling of it offered.**

**Calder and Kest. HE WAS ASKED FOR A ROOM AND A DAY AND NOT FOR AN INSTRUMENT, AND HE NAMED THE PRICE OUT LOUD BEFORE HE SAID YES, AND THE PRICE FELL ON A MAN OF THE TIDE GANG WHOSE NAME HE REFUSED TO GIVE.** The holder asked in the open at the foot of the mole's steps on the nineteenth of November, in about eleven people's hearing, and said first that he was not asking for an instrument, that there are eight acknowledgements in that ledger and the ninth was entered on the twenty-eighth of August at this party's asking and is spent and a tenth may not be entered, and that this party did not ask for the ninth again and did not ask for the tenth, and that a room is not an instrument and a day is not an instrument. He said he was not asking that office to seat anybody, **that the power to seat one of two disputants belongs to the inquiry and not to the writ of the first of May, and that the two instruments are not the same instrument.** Kest said the room is over the tide-gang store and a sitting in it shuts the store, and that one man of the tide gang of this harbor loses his Thursday morning, and that the tide gang is four men short and will be four men short at the first of March, and that he did not name the man because the moment he names him he is a man in this business. Then he said three further things: that his ledger is spent and there is no line he can enter this week that would mean anything to anybody in the first of March; that his office cannot pay for a clerk's day and has had nothing in its repair account since the middle of February and the clerk will enter what goes in that room in his own hours and the office is going to owe him a day, and the debt is entered against the office and not against the party; and **that he is not a friend of this party, has never said he was, and is not going to begin on a Thursday in November, and that if the party needs a man in this harbor to be on its side then it has counted him twice and should take one of them out.** The holder entered all of it and said the party will take none of them out and agrees with him.

**Calder and Bevin. Unchanged and not in a room.** The boy is not in any of these ten chapters as a person. The three nots stand. Forty marks stands whole. The head of the third column keeps his hand. **The third column was not filled on any day of this batch and nobody was appointed to it, and the party's first-column entry of the seventeenth of November and the read-out of the fourth of December both say so.**

**Calder and Alden. Do not move, and the figure is counted from the prose first and written from the count. HE WAS AT THE FAR BENCH ON FOUR DAYS OF THIS BATCH AND ON NO OTHER: Monday the sixteenth and Thursday the nineteenth of November, Wednesday the twenty-fifth of November, and Thursday the third of December.** Not asked for anything on any of the four and asking nothing on any of the four, and Calder Marr did not go over to him on any of the four. **The entry of the nineteenth of November says he was not at that bench on the Wednesday at any hour; the entry of the twenty-fifth says he was not at it on the Tuesday at any hour; the entry of the third of December says he was not at it on the Wednesday at any hour. The read-out of the fourth of December lists the same four days and says that neither brother went over to the other on any of the four. EITHER BROTHER CAN CHECK IT ON ANY DAY OF A WEEK.** No fragment, no report talk, no second story, no confession. The tin cup of the eighteenth of October is not repeated and is not improved on.

**Calder and Nessa. The pair this volume changes most and neither of them says so, and this batch is where the second half of it is.** She read a page and a half out of a room in six heads and read out what was not under each head as well as what was in it, and she read the sixth head last and slowest because it is the head that is about nothing at all, and the clerk entered that the answer came from the ship's hand. She argued in a shed for the room and against the market and gave the reason and would not give it twice. She put an eleven-line list on the bench of what is not going into that room and made the holder stop her at the ninth item and add a tenth, which is a letter about a person of no office. She was charged two shillings on the twenty-first for a strapping coming off, and she said the bone knitted straight and that a wrist does not turn all the way round in a cold month for two years and that the only thing that makes it worse is a boat on a cold tide with a fall in the hand, and that she does not want it in her own book. She entered in her own hand that until the first of March this yard is a party of one and a half and not two and that every day it books in this month is a day it books with a man in it. **He has not thanked her and is not going to and is not asking.**

### The power state at the end of Chapter 200

**NO STAGE OF ANY KIND IS TAKEN IN MOVEMENT 5 AND NONE MAY BE. The Faultreader stage is spent, is not re-taken, is not improved, and the word is on no page of these ten chapters.** A search of the ten for *seam* and *faultreader* returns only the phrase *the fifth seam*, which is the plate at the fifth seam and not a perception. **NO PERSON OF THIS YARD WENT IN ANY WATER ON ANY DAY OF THIS BATCH AND NO FINDING OF WORKED GLASS WAS MADE AND NOTHING WAS RAISED.** The book of hours of the thirtieth and thirty-first of October stands at sixteen lines and was not extended, and its costs of maintenance, public exposure and dependence on a trained crew are NOT discharged and were not touched.

**THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN THIS BATCH.** It is carried as a bare fact in the second column of Chapter 195, that on the sixth day of October a crew of two and a half had one member with a bone cracked about two inches above her left wrist and a figure of three shillings and sixpence against it and a quarter of a year out of the left side, and no reason and no name. **The four lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four, are still nameless, are not struck out, and the count of four stands in the first column in a ship's hand with no name against it. THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 04 WAS ALLOWED WAS SPENT IN CHAPTER 164 AND IS NOT AVAILABLE AGAIN IN THIS VOLUME OR ANY OTHER.**

### The bodies and the dated positions at the end of Chapter 200

- **The money, re-derived from the chapters and checkable in pence: 262p on the evening of the tenth of November, 261p on the sixteenth after a penny of postage, 261p on the seventeenth, 901p on the twentieth, 877p on the twenty-first, 237p on the twenty-fourth, 237p on the first of December, 717p on the fourth. A mark is a hundred and sixty pence and a shilling is twelve, so a wage of four marks is six hundred and forty, a day that brought four marks is six hundred and forty, a day that brought three marks is four hundred and eighty, two shillings is twenty-four, and a penny is a penny. THE BATCH CLOSES FOUR HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FIVE PENCE HEAVIER THAN IT OPENED AND THE WHOLE OF THE DIFFERENCE IS TWO DAYS OF WORK ON A HIRER'S OWN ACCOUNT, TWO SHILLINGS TO A BONESETTER, ONE WAGE AND A PENNY OF POSTAGE, AND NO FIGURE IN THIS HARBOR IS THE PRICE OF ANY OF IT. NO FIGURE IN THE BAG AT ANY POINT IN THIS BATCH EXCEEDS SIX MARKS AND EIGHT SHILLINGS.**
- **The wage column: twenty-five lines and one hundred marks at the open; TWENTY-SIX LINES AND ONE HUNDRED AND FOUR MARKS on the twenty-fourth with the wage PAID into an envelope in the holder's own left hand with the week on the outside of it, in daylight, in front of about nine people; unchanged at twenty-six and a hundred and four on the first of December with the wage NOT PAID and no envelope made. The fifth of March the oldest throughout. Envelopes: nineteen, twenty, twenty.**
- **The number of weeks fallen due this year with no envelope standing against them is FOUR at the open, FIVE after the seventeenth of November, and SIX after the first of December, and all six are given in the same breath in every place the figure appears: the fifteenth of August, the fifth of September, the sixth of October, the third of November, the seventeenth of November, and the first of December instant.** The bag was three hundred and seventy-nine pence short of the wage on the seventeenth and four hundred and three pence short of it on the first of December, and both figures are on the page.
- **The whole of what came into this yard in this batch is seven marks and forty-eight pence, being two days at the second seam's own face on a hirer's own account, and the whole of what went out is six marks and twenty-five pence, being a penny of postage, a wage of four marks, and two shillings to a bonesetter. NO OFFICE IN THIS HARBOR PAID THIS YARD A MARK AND NONE IS GOING TO.**
- Thirty-one on the chandler's note, **not re-written, and said out loud at the head of this dock for the third time in seven months** on the third of December in about eleven people's hearing, with the three counts of weeks unmoved at twenty-two, thirteen and eleven and the reason given again and not improved on, being that re-writing a note with nothing in the bag is not a payment but a figure, and that he would rather owe a man thirty-one on a page he has not re-written than nineteen on a page the two of them did together in a shop. Nothing in a tin since the twenty-eighth of April. Three marks owing to a man of Ashtide for the eighth of August, unmoved, and he was not named and was not asked anything and nobody was sent for him. Seven pence owing to this yard's own shipwright, unpaid, not paid out of a bonesetter's figure. **Eight marks and a day of a boat owing to this harbor's own bellwright since the twenty-third of May, unargued, and he was not asked anything this month, no digit of anything he said on the second of July is printed, and the chest was not opened and was not brought down the hill.** Eight shillings and tenpence owing to a woman of this harbor, with her name on the figure. Forty marks standing whole to a boy of twenty-two. Forty marks to a person of no office, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October.
- **The hearing room was used on two days, the twenty-sixth and the twenty-seventh of November. The market was used on three, the first, the second and the fourth of December, and a copyist's counter off the fish market on the thirtieth of November and the first. THE HEARING ROOM AND THE MARKET WALL ARE NEVER THE SAME DAY AND IT IS VERIFIED A SIXTH TIME. NO SHEET WENT UP ON ANY WALL IN THIS HARBOR IN THE THREE WEEKS AND NONE CAME OFF, and that is entered in the first column, and the volume's final chapter image is not on any of them and was not attempted.**
- **The hearing room's slate wall came down bare three times in this batch and no sitting was held on any of them: Monday the sixteenth of November, the twenty-eighth Mondays since the first of May; Monday the twenty-third, the twenty-ninth; Monday the thirtieth, the thirtieth. THE COUNTER DID NOT RESTART. The clerk kept his own page and did not mention it, and there is a day and a room in the said clerk's own page for the Thursday of that week and nothing on the wall.**
- **The cistern behind the engine house was charged at no figure by four men this yard does not know on the six rota days of this batch — the sixteenth, nineteenth, twenty-third and twenty-sixth of November and the thirtieth of November and the third of December — and this party walked none of them, and the walk is nil and stays nil. THE CHARGE OF THE NINTH OF NOVEMBER STANDS ENTERED WITH NO FIGURE AGAINST IT, AND THE PARTY IS NOT GOING TO STAND IN A STREET AND SAY WHAT THOSE SIX AND THAT ONE MAKE BETWEEN THEM, BECAUSE IT DID NOT SEE THE DAYS THOSE MEN COME ON.**
- The two objects of nobody's are about a hundred and seventy-five days up the crown of that mole at the close of this batch, about nine feet apart, under two tarpaulins, one with a corner off it since the seventeenth of June and not lashed back, and neither has moved. **The sequence across the ten chapters is 156, 159, 161, 163, 165, 167, 169, 171, 173, 175 and it is monotonic, and no chapter gives the same figure twice and no figure was counted twice. THE 171 IS A REPAIR AND THE 170 IT WAS IS WITHDRAWN: Chapter 198 runs to Tuesday the first of December and is the only one of the ten that had keyed its count to its first day, and eight of the other nine key theirs to the last day, so the figure that answered to the thirtieth of November is now the one that answers to the first of December, which is what the rest of the run does and what makes the ten figures arithmetic. The full account is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0005 (Volume 04) review repairs*.**
- The notice of the thirty-first: four clauses, not lifted, not narrowed, not re-served, not altered, and **no person, no boat and no lead of this yard went inside its first clause on any day of this batch.** The plate at the fifth seam is in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched, and **the note against it STANDS OPEN, and no better letter was written about it in three weeks.** The custody room was not asked for, no digit of any of the three numbers is in this yard's book, and **the question that no paper in this harbor or in that office's copying room answers was not put to anybody in these three weeks.** The glass storm is not referred to. The run under this town is not referred to. The compartment is not named and no step of its finding is restated. The lead plug is not asked about, not printed, not opened and not brought down the hill. The coast road is in type in a city beside a pilot's figure, neither is withdrawn, **this volume does not decide which of them is right**, and the Kettle was not looked at.
- **THE READ-OUT DEVICE WAS USED ONCE IN THIS BATCH, at the head of this dock on the evening of Friday the fourth of December, in about eleven people's hearing, in about nine minutes, and not one line of the second column was read out in it. THE COPYIST'S READING OF AN OFFICE'S FORM IN A MARKET ON THE FIRST OF DECEMBER IS NOT THIS DEVICE AND THE ENTRY OF THE FIRST OF DECEMBER SAYS SO ON ITS FACE. THE READ-BACK — the ship's hand reading the day's entries back from the top of the bench and the holder reading them back after her, nobody else in the shed — RUNS IN FOUR OF THE TEN CHAPTERS, 191, 193, 195 AND 200, and the clerk reading an entry back in a doorway or a room in Chapters 194 and 196 is his own trade's practice and is not the shed device. **THE FIGURE OF THREE, BEING 191, 195 AND 200, IS WITHDRAWN AND NOT SILENTLY REPLACED. `chapter-0193.md:17` carries it — the count and the times went onto a slate in the ship's hand, the slate went into the book in her own writing, and the holder read it back after her — and a plain search of the ten chapters for *read it back*, *read them back* and *read back after her* returns 191, 193, 195 and 200 and nothing else. THIS IS THE THIRD TIME THIS COUNT HAS BEEN WRONG IN THIS VOLUME, AFTER ONE IN BATCH 0004 AND THREE IN BATCH 0003, and the full account is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0005 (Volume 04) review repairs*.**
- **THE CALENDAR OF THIS BATCH IS TAKEN FROM CHAPTER 190 AND FROM THE CHAPTERS AFTER IT, AS LOCK L1 AS AMENDED BINDS, AND NO CHAPTER RESTATES A WEEKDAY FOR ANY DAY BEFORE THE ELEVENTH OF NOVEMBER AND NO CHAPTER STATES A YEAR. THE TEN CHAPTERS RUN ON MONDAY THE SIXTEENTH TO SATURDAY THE FIFTH OF DECEMBER, TWENTY-ONE DAYS, WITH NO DAY IN A CHAPTER TWICE AND NO COST PAID TWICE.**

### The eleven days' new-volume question, and the form that has taken the Second Bell

**A QUESTION WAS ASKED IN A MARKET IN THIS HARBOR ON WEDNESDAY THE SECOND OF DECEMBER, IN ABOUT FORTY PEOPLE'S HEARING, BY A MAN OF THE FIRST LAY WHO IS FIFTY-ONE AND IS MASTER OF A COASTER OF ABOUT A HUNDRED AND NINETY TONS OF HIS OWN, AND IT WAS NOT ANSWERED, AND THE NEW-VOLUME QUESTION IS THE SECOND OF THE TWO THINGS HE ASKED AND IT IS THE ONE THIS VOLUME OPENS.** His second question was what an office in this country that has kept records for three hundred years thought it was guarding against at the point at which it decided what it was allowed to lose. He gave the reasoning out loud: that an office that keeps records that long has had to decide what it was allowed to lose; that that is not an accusation but arithmetic; that somebody, in some room, in some year nobody is alive in, decided what this coast was going to be without; and that a town like this one is the last place on the coast anybody can ask, because there is nothing in it worth a clerk's time and everybody in it knows it. **The holder answered that this yard does not know, and that this yard said the same out loud in that market on the thirteenth of August in about forty people's hearing, and that it is not going to say it a second time about the same thing, and that the sheet of the fifteenth of August carries the answer and the reason on its face in about nine words. He then gave out of the yard's own book a second-column line with no name in it, being a bare fact and nothing else, and said he was not going to call a fact an answer in a market. NO OFFICE IN VEYR IS SHOWN A DOCUMENT THAT WOULD ANSWER IT, NO COMPACT IS NAMED, NO MINISTRY IS NAMED, NO OFFICE OR PERSON ANSWERS IT IN A ROOM, AND THE PHRASE *RISK MANAGEMENT* IS ON NO PAGE OF THIS BATCH.**

**AND ON FRIDAY THE FOURTH OF DECEMBER A HALF-SHEET CAME UP THIS HILL IN THE ORDINARY POST WITH THE NAME OF A COURT IN A CITY AT THE HEAD OF IT AND THE NAME OF THAT COURT AT THE FOOT AND NO SIGNATURE ANYWHERE, AND IT SAYS FIVE THINGS AND THEY ARE THE WHOLE OF IT.** That the matter of the ASTER GANNET stands before that court and is not closed by a finding, and that a person entered in that matter as an interested party is not examined for the full Second Bell license while the matter stands. That the holder of the said writ is so entered **by the making of a copy of a record, the entry being on the face of that copy, and the entry is dated the twenty-fourth day of November instant** — which is the day the copy came off the counter of the circuit office, in consequence of an application this party made in its own left hand on the sixteenth at a cost of a penny. That the said person was put to the examination once before and did not satisfy it, and that that court has before it no other matter concerning him and knows nothing whatever of his trade. **That this is not a suspension, not a penalty and not a finding against anybody, and that a person who is not examined is not a person who has been refused.** And that the writ of the first of May instant is not before that court, is not a Second Bell license, does not lead to one and renews nothing, that the court has not considered it and takes no notice of it, and that nothing in that paper touches, grants, refuses or deals with the second season of that writ.

**AND THE HOLDER ENTERED IT IN THE FIRST COLUMN IN HIS OWN LEFT HAND AND BEFORE ANY FIGURE OF ANY KIND: that he was put to the said examination in the month of September two years back and did not satisfy it, and that the writ of the first of May instant was granted eight months afterwards, and that he has not gone near that examination since, and that this yard is not going to say in a street in the month of January that the writ did this. THE REASON AGAINST THE LINE IS THAT A WRIT IS A MUNICIPAL INSTRUMENT OF THIS HARBOR AND A FORM OF A COURT IN A CITY IS NOT, AND THAT THE YARD HAS HAD A THING TAKEN OFF IT THIS MONTH THAT IT WANTED BEFORE IT HAD THE WRIT, AND THAT THE YARD WILL NOT BE THE PARTY THAT PUTS THOSE TWO THINGS TOGETHER BECAUSE A YARD THAT IS LOOKING FOR SOMEBODY ELSE TO BLAME IS A YARD THAT HAS BEGUN TO BELIEVE IN A WRIT. THE WHOLE OF THE RELEASE WAS THE PARTY'S OWN DOING, AND THE PARTY IS NOT GOING TO CALL THE COST SOMEBODY ELSE'S.**

**AND THE PAPER ENDS WITH A DATE: a copy of the said record is to be produced to that court on the fourth day of January, and a party which does not produce it is not fined, because a court does not fine a poor man, and the matter simply goes on. THE LONGER LEASH HAS A NAME ON IT AND THE NAME IS AN OFFICE AND NOT A MAN THIS TIME, AND THIS PARTY HAS NOT ASKED ANY OFFICE FOR ANYTHING IN AID OF IT, HAS NOT SENT TO A CITY, HAS NOT WRITTEN TO A MAN IN A COAT WHOSE NAME IS ON NO PAGE OF THIS BOOK, AND HAS NOT BEEN TO THE COUNTER OF THAT CIRCUIT OFFICE SINCE THE TWENTY-FOURTH.**

### What is owed and what is not, and the one queued phase

**THE VOLUME'S FINAL CHAPTER IMAGE IS NOT REACHED AND WAS NOT ATTEMPTED, AND THE VOLUME IS THEREFORE NOT CLOSED.** Five of its elements are absent from these ten chapters on purpose: no page went up on a market wall where the two naval notices used to be; no person went out in a boat as a closing image; the boy is not on the stone and the third column has no name in it at all and nobody has been appointed to it; the delay is a hand's width of a second and was not measured, not advanced and not written down with a date against it; and the chandler's note is not re-written and no sheet is on any wall. **The one queued phase is `workspace/volume-04/close/PROMPT.md`, which is the volume close and not a batch, and it exists. Nothing else was created: no later batch prompt, no Volume 05 outline prompt, no `.done` marker and no `.retired` marker. `state/phase-ledger.json` was not read as evidence of anything, not edited, and no marker was written to compensate for it; phase status is the controller's alone.**

## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 190 — SUPERSEDED BY THE CHAPTER 200 SECTION AND BY THE VOLUME 04 AUDIT ABOVE, AND RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0004 RECORD

**VOLUME 04 MOVEMENT 4 IS WRITTEN. ITS REVERSAL IS PAID, AND IT IS PAID IN THE VOLUME'S OWN TERMS AND NOT IN A DOCUMENT'S.** In twenty days a yard of two and a half people was told a day was to be named, was served a paper naming it, read its own book out loud in a room for three hours and fifty minutes, put a join on a notice in front of a clerk, could not say what it meant three times, had a market of July read back to it in its own words by a stranger in a flat voice, came out of the room less believed than it went in, entered that against itself in the first column with the reason against the line, cracked a bone above a wrist on a wharf, paid a bonesetter three shillings and sixpence, refused a wharf day of about three marks, did not pay a wage on Tuesday the third of November for the first time this year that the figure went to four weeks, and closed the month forty-two pence lighter than it opened it, and the whole of the forty-two pence is a bonesetter's bill.

### The midpoint, in one paragraph

**THE CREW'S BOOK OF THIS YARD WAS READ OUT IN A ROOM IN MORROW QUAY ON WEDNESDAY THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF OCTOBER, WHOLE AND UNSHORTENED, FROM THE FIRST OF MAY TO THE LAST PAGE, IN THE ORDER IT IS IN, IN THE HOLDER'S OWN VOICE, FOR THREE HOURS AND FIFTY MINUTES, AND THE CLERK ASKED TWICE FOR A THING TO BE READ A SECOND TIME AND BOTH TIMES IT WAS READ A SECOND TIME.** The two columns were read out. The third column, fourteen lines at the head of a page in the right hand of a boy of twenty-two dated from the second of May, was not read by the holder; the holder gave it up in a sentence in the room and did not take it back, and the clerk read it in a flat voice and said in the room that this office cannot make that boy come and cannot make anybody ask him, and entered that the three nots of the eighth of June stand and that this office is not going to act on them. **THE SECOND COLUMN'S FIVE CHECKLESS LINES WERE READ OUT AS LINES, AND NOT ONE REASON AGAINST ANY OF THEM WENT IN, AND THE CLERK SAID WHY IN HIS OWN TRADE'S WORDS AND NOT AS A FAVOUR: a record has a column and the column is the width of my page, and if I take four words out of a man's paragraph then in about four years a man in a city thinks the man wrote four words, and he did not.** No copy of any record was made in that room and none was given to any party and none was asked for.

### The active pressures going into Volume 04 Movement 5

1. **A SEAM WAS PUT ON A DOCUMENT IN A ROOM AND IT TOLD THE MAN WHO PUT IT THERE ONE THING.** On Thursday the twenty-ninth of October the holder stated that the notice served on this party on the tenth of September has been in two hands at two times, and gave four grounds — the colour of the ink in the first three paragraphs and the sixth against the fourth, the fold of the sheet at that paragraph, the opening and shutting of the paper at the fold, and the depth at which the fourth paragraph begins — and said in the same breath that it does not say which hand is right, or why, or whose the two hands were. **AND THE CLERK OF THE CIRCUIT OFFICE THEN SAID THAT HE HAD HIMSELF HAD THAT PAPER SINCE THE TENTH OF SEPTEMBER AND COULD SEE THE TWO INKS AND HAD NOT NEEDED THE HOLDER OF THE WRIT OF THE FIRST OF MAY TO COME UP A HILL AND POINT AT THEM, AND ENTERED THE FACT IN THAT ORDER, THE CLERK FIRST AND THE HOLDER SECOND, AND GAVE HIS REASON, WHICH IS THAT IF THE FACT IS LEFT OUT OF THE RECORD THEN IN ABOUT FOUR YEARS THE ONLY THING IN THE BUNDLE ANYBODY IS PROUD OF IS A THING A MAN OF THIS HARBOR PUT THERE.** He was asked what it means, having written the question in his own page first, and answered *I do not know* three times and would not say it a fourth time in a different way, and the clerk entered all of it. **A SEAM IDENTIFIES DIFFERENCE AND NOT TRUTH AND IT WAS USED THE THIRD TIME IN THIS VOLUME AND IT MADE HIM LESS CREDIBLE AND NOT MORE, WHICH IS WHAT THE VOLUME OUTLINE SAID IT WOULD DO.**
2. **A SENTENCE OF THE EIGHTEENTH OF JULY IS IN A RECORD IN A CITY AND HE DID NOT DENY IT.** The clerk read the entry of the second column of the eighteenth of July out loud in a flat voice, in the holder's own words, and then read the line of the twenty-third of July out of the first column, that the party did not stop him, that it entered the whole of what was said and has not shortened it, and that it was wrong in three parts out of four and has not taken it back. **AND THE HOLDER SAID IN THE ROOM THAT A PAGE IN A BOOK AND A SENTENCE IN A STREET ARE NOT THE SAME INSTRUMENT, AND THE CLERK ENTERED THAT AND ENTERED THAT THEY ARE BOTH GOING TO BE IN THE SAME BUNDLE IN ABOUT FOUR YEARS AND THAT HE IS NOT THE MAN WHO DECIDES THAT THEY GO IN SEPARATE ROOMS. AND THE HOLDER SAID HE SAID FIVE THINGS IN THAT MARKET AND THAT FOUR OF THEM ARE IN HIS BOOK AND THAT THE FIFTH HE CANNOT PRODUCE, AND SAID SO IN THE ROOM INSTEAD OF NOT SAYING IT.**
3. **A MAN OF THIS YARD WAS RIGHT ABOUT A THING AND WRONG ABOUT HIMSELF IN THE SAME ROOM, AND THE YARD ENTERED IT IN THE FIRST COLUMN WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE ON THE TENTH OF NOVEMBER.** And on Sunday the first of November, at the head of this dock, **Gwil Lasker, thirty-six, of the first lay, a boat of about a hundred and twenty tons, said out loud that he was not saying this yard was wrong and that a man in a city is going to read two lines on one page and take the first off and leave the second on, and gave his name before he was asked for it.** The figure of two and three-quarters on the flood and two and a quarter on the ebb was not withdrawn and is not going to be, the pilot's two and a half was not withdrawn, and neither can check the other and this volume does not decide which is right. **The error he put to his own account in that street is his own and not the holder's: at twenty-two he took a sounding off that bar off a master's own figure at that master's own asking, was a foot and a half out in a slack water, did not tell him, and the said master had it himself inside a month off a man out of a city, and neither of them has said a word about it since.**
4. **THE SECOND COLUMN IS NOW THE MOST DANGEROUS COLUMN IN THIS HARBOR, AND THE PARTY SAYS SO OUT LOUD, ON THE THIRTIETH OF OCTOBER, IN THE SHED.** A party that spent two volumes saying that the second column is the only place a man is allowed to say *I do not know* and mean it came to a room in which saying it went on a page in a city, and the limit four people of this coast wrote themselves and signed — that the account is not read out, is not put in a book that leaves this harbor, is not said to a man with a seal, and not one name to any office in any city — **is not in that record, because a limit is a reason and reasons do not go into a record, and cannot be put there, because this yard holds a writ and a writ is a channel and a man with a writ is the thing that limit was written against.**
5. **THE THIRD COLUMN WAS READ BY A STRANGER AND IS NOT FILLED AND NOBODY IS APPOINTED.** **NOBODY ASKED IN THE ROOM WHO FILLS IT — the clerk read the fourteen lines in a flat voice, said that his office cannot make the boy come and cannot make anybody ask him, entered that, and asked nothing further about it. The *who fills it* argument is in the shed on Wednesday the fourth of November, and she said no twice, gave the reason in her own words, and would not give it again.** The reason is in the first column of the seventeenth of October in the ship's hand. The head of the column keeps the boy's hand. A request to scrape it out is refused in the same words it is refused for a line in any column. **A column nobody fills is a column somebody else fills, and the somebody else is a clerk with a pen, and a clerk with a pen is not a bad thing and is not a villain and is the only way a record gets made at all.**
6. **AN INJURY TO A WORKING CREW MEMBER WITH A REAL BILL, AND A BAD DAY, AND BOTH ARE PAID, AND NEITHER IS PAID BY A DIVE.** On Friday the twenty-third of October a copper union on a wharf's discharge let go under pressure and the pump's handle came round through the quarter-turn that is in it and struck **Nessa Pike's** left forearm across the bone about two inches above the wrist at about half past two in the afternoon, and she said before anybody had said anything that it was the bone and not the joint. **Her figure is three shillings and sixpence, charged and paid on the twenty-fourth, and a quarter of a year out of the left side of a shipwright, and both of those are on one line in the first column in her own hand and in his, with the reason that a bone is not a bill and a bill is not a bone and a man who has the two in one line will end up arguing about the wrong one.** A day of work at the second seam on the sixth of November went slower than a day with three on it, and a wharf day of about three marks offered on Sunday the eighth for Tuesday the tenth was refused, and the cost of that refusal is a day not earned and is entered because a day refused is a day not earned.
7. **THE VOLUME'S UNPAID WAGE IS NOW FOUR WEEKS AND THE FIGURE IS IN PENCE.** Tuesday the twenty-seventh of October was paid, the third of November was not paid and no envelope was made, and the tenth of November was paid, and the bag held eight shillings and sixpence against that one, which is one hundred and two pence, so the wage it could not pay was **five hundred and thirty-eight pence** short. **The column runs twenty-three lines and ninety-two marks, twenty-four and ninety-six, twenty-four and ninety-six, twenty-five and a hundred, with the fifth of March the oldest throughout. Eighteen envelopes on a bench, then eighteen, then nineteen.**
8. **THE QUESTION ON THE TABLE IS A DOCUMENT THE PARTY HAS NEVER SEEN, AND IT IS MOVEMENT 5's, AND IT IS NOT OBTAINED HERE.** A copy of a record about an object, with the limits the record itself carries printed on the face of the copy, which would show that a manifest was altered and would not show who altered it, and which the copy itself would say so on its own face, and which a man of the first lay of this harbor cannot check. **The party entered on the tenth of November that it is not applying for one this month, and why, and that it is not saying no to it in four years and is not saying yes either, and that the release of that copy is not in this yard's keeping and has never been in it.** No copy was made, released, printed, quoted, summarized or described in any of these ten chapters.
9. **HE STILL HAS NOT TOLD HER, AND THE NOT-TELLING IS FIVE ENTRIES NOW AND IS NOT PAID.** The decision on the third of October, the not-telling and the reason on the fourth, her line of the seventeenth of October that she was not going to ask him, a fourth occasion in a room on the twenty-eighth and twenty-ninth, and a fifth entry on the tenth of November with the reason again and the undertaking not to invent a second one. **It is not a confession, not a relief, not a price, not a trade, and it is not going to be one in this volume, and the reason has not been improved on in five entries and is not improved on in this one.**
10. **MARA VENN IS NOT IN A ROOM, IS NOT ASKED, IS NOT PAID, IS NOT FORGIVEN, AND DID NOT COME BACK.** On Thursday the fifth of November a man of the first lay, about fifty-five, stood at the wall outside the market office and read the reason on the page of her unsigned finding out loud and asked this yard for her name, and this yard said no in about four minutes and gave the whole of it. **HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT IN JULY THE SAME ANSWER HAD SOUNDED LIKE A YARD BEING CAREFUL AND THAT ON THE FIFTH OF NOVEMBER IT SOUNDS LIKE A WALL, AND THAT HE WOULD RATHER HAVE THE PAGE IN A CITY THAN THE WALL, AND THAT HE WOULD RATHER HAVE IT FROM THIS YARD THAN FROM ANYBODY ELSE, AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO PRETEND HE WOULD RATHER HAVE A YARD THAN A WALL BECAUSE HE HAS BEEN IN FOUR ROOMS IN THIS HARBOR THIS MONTH. AND THE HOLDER AGREED WITH HIM IN ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE'S HEARING AND ENTERED AGAINST THIS PARTY THAT HE HAS NOT GOT A REASON FOR THAT PREFERENCE WHICH IS WORTH ANYTHING.** Forty marks first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, not one mark of it paid, and a day of work at a seam that brought five marks is not a payment of it and is not going to be entered as one.
11. **THE HARBOR IS POORER ON THE TENTH OF NOVEMBER THAN IT WAS ON THE TWENTIETH OF OCTOBER, AND THE WHOLE OF THE DIFFERENCE IS THREE SHILLINGS AND SIXPENCE.** A mark and eight shillings and sixpence in the bag, which is two hundred and sixty-two pence, against three hundred and four pence on the evening of the twentieth. One herring berth of two out. The north berth out. The light not on. The tide gang four men short. The fire party's hose dead until the spring. The hard at Ashtide unanswered. **The cistern behind the engine house was charged twenty-four times since the third of August up to the fifth of November and this party walked none of the twenty-four, and it was charged again on the ninth of November by men this yard does not know and that charge is entered with no figure against it, **and the read-out at the head of the dock says the twenty-four and then says out loud that the party is not going to stand in a street and tell anybody whether that makes it twenty-five, because it did not see the days those men come on**.**
12. **THE REEDE'S LEDGER IS SPENT AND A MAN OF THE SECOND LAY IS OUT OF A TUESDAY.** Fenn Ardle, forty-three, with a boat of about seventy tons and a boy of seventeen on the second hand, stood on the mole's steps on Monday the twenty-sixth of October because that office put him there for the two days of the sitting and told him so on the Friday and not on the Monday morning, and he is out of a Tuesday on the water on the twenty-seventh, and the harbor authority has not entered that day's loss because its ledger is spent and cannot, and **a yard that has been told in the open air by the officer of that office that his ledger is spent does not go back up those steps with the same request, and this party went up the steps to ask a man a question and did not make a request of any office.**

**Calder and Mara. THEY HAVE NOT SPOKEN SINCE THE TWELFTH OF OCTOBER AND HE ASKED HER FOR NOTHING ON THE FIFTH OF NOVEMBER AND SHE CAME UP NO HILL.** He did not tell her, and there has been a fifth occasion not to. She did not give the line, and her price has not moved, and the six marks and two days were not offered, not reduced and are not going to be. Her finding is on a market wall unsigned at her own asking with the reason on the page, and no chapter of this batch signed it, took it down, softened it or improved on it. **She is not on the crew, is not of the yard, is not a holder, is not on the four names, is owed forty marks first after food, and the reporting clause on the writ of the first of May does not reach her and never did and a room in a city said so on the record on the sixteenth of October and this batch does not soften that line and does not repeat it as a scene.**

**Calder and Kest. ### The power state at the end of Chapter 190

**NO STAGE OF ANY KIND IS TAKEN IN MOVEMENT 4 AND NONE MAY BE.** The Faultreader stage is spent, is not re-taken, is not improved, and **the word is on no page of these ten chapters.** One seam was put, on one document, in a room, at the midpoint, and it said one thing: that a sheet of paper has been in two hands at two times. It did not say which is right, or why, or by whom; it did not prove a motive, convict anybody or settle an argument. **HE WENT IN NO WATER IN THIS BATCH, AND NO PERSON OF THIS YARD WAS IN ANY WATER ON ANY DAY OF IT, AND THE INJURY WAS NOT PAID BY A DIVE.** He began to learn the trade of a listening buoy on the thirtieth of October, to about the depth of a book of hours: three nights on the mole's steps writing down every pump that went off and every boat that went out and every boat that came in, with the hour against each, sixteen lines, and no reason against any of them. **The ship's hand told him in the same scene that the rest of it needs a crew that keeps the hours, and that he has not got the one he would need and neither has she, and that she is not going to let him have the other thing.**

**THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN THIS BATCH.** It is carried once, in the second column of Chapter 189, as a bare fact with a reason against it and no name in it: a man stood in a room for two days and read out of his own book for three hours and fifty minutes, and on the Wednesday night he did not sleep and on the Thursday night he did, and the party entered both halves of that and is not going to name what it is in a shed. **The four lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four, are still nameless, are not struck out, and the count of four stands in the first column in a ship's hand with no name against it. THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 04 WAS ALLOWED WAS SPENT IN CHAPTER 164 AND IS NOT AVAILABLE AGAIN.**

THE READING OF THE CREW'S BOOK IN A ROOM IS NOT A USE OF THAT DEVICE: it is a clerk's duty and a party's undertaking and it is the volume's midpoint, and the two are different instruments and the state files say so. THE READ-BACK — the ship's hand reading the day's entries back from the top of the bench and the holder reading them back after her, nobody else in the shed — RUNS IN FOUR OF THE TEN CHAPTERS, 181, 185, 188 AND 190, **and the figure of one that stood here is withdrawn and was wrong**, and a plain string search for *read it back*, *read them back* and *read back after her* across the ten chapters returns `chapter-0181.md:77`, `chapter-0185.md:49`, `chapter-0188.md:63` and `chapter-0190.md:55` and nothing else. **THE CLERK READING AN ENTRY BACK IN A DOORWAY OR A ROOM IN CHAPTERS 181 AND 184 IS HIS OWN TRADE'S PRACTICE AND IS NOT THIS DEVICE.****
