## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 180 — SUPERSEDED BY THE CHAPTER 190 SECTION ABOVE, AND RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0003 RECORD

**VOLUME 04 MOVEMENT 3 IS WRITTEN AND ITS TURN HAS LANDED AND IS SPENT.** In twenty days a yard of two and a half people published a refusal in a letterbox for four pence, sent a man of the first lay down a hill once with the truth, and entered that the second time he says he came is his own account and not the yard's, did not pay a wage for the third Tuesday running, found out that a woman of no office had been cited in a file four hundred yards from its own shed, heard the two words an office uses about its own records out of a copyist's counterfoil at a penny a sheet, was told by that same woman in a doorway on the twelfth of October that the line it had asked her for on the twenty-ninth of September is in her hand in a city anyway and is worth nothing to anybody without a seal, went into a hearing room on the sixteenth and had the truth about a person put into a record in a city in its own eleven words, and closed the month four pence lighter than it opened it with nothing on a market wall and a day of work booked for a Friday it has not reached.**

**THE TURN IS PAID IN THE VOLUME'S OWN TERMS AND IN ITS OWN FORMS, TWICE, AND IT IS SPENT. On Friday the ninth of October a conservator of thirty was handed, at a copying counter in a room off the fish market, a withdrawal counterfoil about four inches by three, in the hand of the circuit office of this harbor, on which the Registry records that it agreed to take leaf 62 out of its own file of the ASTER GANNET on the application of a commissioned officer of the Crown Navy by letter, and that the reason it entered against it is *risk management*, and that the leaf is not to be produced and a copy is held. On Thursday the fifteenth a paper served on this party in a doorway states in a document that the same file is before the inquiry, that one leaf is called for and is not produced, and that the reason of the non-production is on a form of the Registry's own dated the second of September which is not to be produced to this party. THE YARD CAN NOW PROVE ON PAPER THAT A PAGE IS GONE AND CANNOT CHECK WHAT WAS ON IT, AND THE PROOF IS A FORM THAT THE OFFICE SAYS EXISTS AND WILL NOT PRODUCE. ALL THREE RESERVED THINGS ARE SPENT AND MOVEMENTS 4 AND 5 MAY NOT SPEND THEM AGAIN.**

### The active pressures going into Volume 04 Movement 4

1. **THE READING OF THE YARD'S OWN CREW'S BOOK IN A ROOM HAS NEVER HAPPENED AND IS COMING.** A copy of the whole of it has been in a file in a city since the ninth of September and cannot be recalled. It has been produced whole three times — twice in September and once on the sixteenth of October — and not one line of it has been read out of it in any room, and no copy of any record has been made in any room, and on the sixteenth a clerk read a schedule into the record instead. **The party entered in the first column on the twentieth of October that it knows this is coming, is not going to soften a line and is not going to leave one out, and that the week of it is not in this party's keeping and is not going to be.** **IT IS CHAPTERS 181 AND LATER AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT.**
2. **THE SECOND COLUMN HAS FIVE LINES THAT NOBODY IN THIS HARBOR CAN CHECK, AND THE HOLDER CANNOT BRING UP ONE OF THEM.** A line of the fourteenth of August is in his own left hand and he can remember the first four words of it — *a man in a boat* — and not the rest of it, and he entered that on the fourth of October against himself with a reason, the reason itself being what happened to him on the Saturday before. **He entered the same day that he did not know whether the not-reading of that column in a room was a rule or an answer, on the sixteenth of October he did not read it out again, and he entered that he still does not know, and on the twentieth he entered that he is not going to improve on that entry either.**
3. **A PARTY WITH FOUR THINGS AND NOT ONE OF THEM ABLE TO BE PUT IN FRONT OF ANYBODY.** A number off a copying job sheet. A counterfoil in a box under a counter and a fair copy in a woman's own book, both four hundred yards from this shed and neither of them the page. A file on a second floor it has not asked for and is not going to. And a woman it cannot pay. **The shipwright said that out loud in a doorway on the twelfth and it is on the page and it is not going to be improved on in a market.**
4. **ELEVEN WORDS IN A RECORD IN A CITY SAYING THERE IS A WOMAN IN THIS HARBOR THE YARD OF MORROW QUAY WILL NOT NAME**, entered by the holder in his own left hand, read back to him by the clerk of the sitting, and entered again. It cannot be got off a page. **The entry of the eighteenth of September that this party does not put a name on a thing it cannot check is the authority for it and is not improved on, and about four hundred people can read it in a fortnight.**
5. **LEAF 88 EXISTS AND NOBODY HAS SEEN IT AND TWO PEOPLE IN THIS TOWN CAN PROVE IT EXISTS.** A copy of a conservator's own page is to be read with it, and she never wrote it, and an office wrote it, and the copy of her page was made so that it could be. **The yard is not going to be told what is on it and is not going to ask and is not going to guess, and the not-knowing is the whole of what the batch paid for it.**
6. **HE STILL HAS NOT TOLD HER, AND THE NOT-TELLING IS FOUR ENTRIES ACROSS FOUR CHAPTERS AND IS NOT PAID.** A decision to tell her was entered on Saturday the third of October, the day he read his own book from eight in the morning until the light went and could not bring up one line of his own second column, and on Sunday the fourth he entered that he had not told her and the reason, and the reason is that on the third he found out he could not remember one line of his own second column, and a man who has found that out is not a man who is going to be able to tell a woman on a mole's steps that he kept something back from her for six weeks and be believed about it. **On the seventeenth of October she said in the shed that she is not going to ask him and had him enter on one line that she did not ask, with a reason against the line that is about him and not about her.** It is not a confession, not a relief, not a price and not a trade, and it is not going to be one in this volume.
7. **SHE LEFT THE REGISTRY AND SHE DID NOT JOIN THIS CREW AND NEITHER IS A SACRIFICE TO THE OTHER.** A letter of four lines in the ordinary post on the eleventh of October, at a penny out of her own pocket, saying that no form and no sheet and no notation and no hand and no date of hers is to be used to support an account she did not write and has not read, and that she is not resigning anything because there is nothing to resign from. **She is not coming to the market office of this town on a Tuesday or a Friday morning, and on the thirteenth a man came into that room and asked it a question about a form with her hand on it and about nine people in that room could not answer him, and the yard heard about it on the Wednesday and did not tell him where she is.**
8. **THE THIRD COLUMN HAS NOT BEEN FILLED SINCE THE FIRST OF AUGUST AND THE SHIPWRIGHT HAS NINE SENTENCES IN HER HEAD AND HAS SAID OUT LOUD WHY SHE IS NOT WRITING THEM** — that the column is a page where a man wrote down what he said when he was not in the boat, and that if those nine sentences went in it this yard would be holding a page about what a woman said to it in a doorway, and that in about four years there is going to be a page in a city that says what a yard of Morrow Quay knew in October and anybody can read it. **Nobody is appointed to that column, the head of it keeps a boy's hand, forty marks stand whole, and a request to scrape it out is refused in the same words it is refused for a line in any column.**
9. **A REFUSAL, IN A LETTERBOX, IN THE HOLDER'S OWN HAND, FOR FOUR PENCE, and a man of fifty-one who read it in his own wheelhouse and did not know what to do with it for four days, and a man of fifty-one who wrote to a city and would not put his word under a line about water and would not take a shilling for it, and a man of fifty who was sent down this hill once with the truth and nothing else, and who says he came up twice and asked in a doorway on the Saturday as well, which is his account and not the yard's.** Four words in a file in a city say that a yard of Morrow Quay declines to certify a figure and will not be held to it, and the yard cannot get at them, and the refused license from June is carried as a cost and is not re-decided, and about four hundred feet of a woman's warp is on the bottom of this harbor.
10. **A HARMONY OF WORK AND ITS COST: two days at the second seam's own face at five marks and at three, a wage of four NOT paid on Tuesday the sixth of October, a day of about three marks on a wharf refused by the yard itself for the Monday because a crew of two and a half cannot lose a Monday in the week before a sitting, a day that was there to be booked on the seventh and was not booked because the third lay is short and the tide gang is four short and one herring berth of two is out, and a day booked for Friday the twenty-third at a hirer's own figure with the board not yet written and the money not yet in.**
11. **THE PLATE NOTE STANDS OPEN AND AN OFFICE'S PAPER IS THE ONLY MECHANISM.** The party entered on the eighth of October, against itself and with the hour against it, that it does not know whether the removal of a leaf from a file in a second-floor room is the same thing as the change of politics in an office it has been waiting for since the seventeenth of January, and that it is not going to guess, and that a number in a book is not a change of politics in an office and is not going to be made into one by anybody in that shed. **THE NOTE IS NOT CLOSED AND IS NOT REQUIRED TO CLOSE IN MOVEMENT 4, AND NO CHAPTER MAY REACH THE PLATE, REOPEN THE EIGHT-CLAUSE PLAN OR WRITE A BETTER LETTER.**
12. **A HARBOR THAT IS POORER ON THE TWENTIETH OF OCTOBER THAN IT WAS ON THE FIRST**, and a bag a mark and twelve shillings against a mark and twelve shillings and fourpence, and the whole of the difference being one letter's postage. One herring berth out. The north berth out. The light not on. The tide gang four men short. A hard at Ashtide unanswered. A fire party's hose dead. Nineteen winters in a list in a city. **THE CISTERN BEHIND THE ENGINE HOUSE HAS BEEN CHARGED NINETEEN TIMES SINCE THE THIRD OF AUGUST AND THIS PARTY HAS WALKED NONE OF THE NINETEEN, and the run reads fourteen on the first of October, fifteen on the fifth, sixteen on the eighth, seventeen on the twelfth, eighteen on the fifteenth and nineteen on the nineteenth, and the fifteenth is the one charge of the month this party knows of at second hand, from a man of the third lay at the head of the dock on the seventeenth.**

### The relationship pressure at the end of Chapter 180

**Calder and Mara. THEY HAVE SPOKEN ONCE SINCE THE TWENTY-NINTH OF SEPTEMBER AND HE ASKED FOR ONE THING AND WAS REFUSED AND SHE CAME UP THE HILL ON THE TWELFTH OF OCTOBER AND GAVE HIM NOTHING AND ASKED FOR NOTHING.** On the ninth she found out at a copying counter, in a room open to anybody, that the Registry had taken her own page out of its own file. On the twelfth she came up the hill unasked and unsent, said the whole of it in about nine minutes, and told them that the first of her four grounds of the eleventh of November is about a plate in eleven feet in this mole. **SHE REFUSED THE LINE HE ASKED FOR ON THE TWENTY-NINTH OF SEPTEMBER, AGAIN, ON A MOLE, AND GAVE THE SAME REASON AND THE PRICE HAS NOT MOVED, AND A LINE OF HERS IN A BOOK OF THE YARD WOULD BE A DOCUMENT AND THE REGISTRY'S PAGE IS A DOCUMENT AND ONLY ONE OF THE TWO HAS A SEAL ON IT.** He did not thank her, did not say he was sorry, and did not ask her one question, and entered all three with reasons, and the reasons are about her. **HE DID NOT TELL HER ABOUT THE SEVENTH OF AUGUST.** **SHE IS NOT ON THE CREW, IS NOT OF THE YARD, IS NOT A HOLDER, IS NOT ON THE FOUR NAMES, IS OWED FORTY MARKS FIRST AFTER FOOD UNPAID SINCE THE FIFTEENTH OF OCTOBER, AND IS NOT COMING TO THE MARKET OFFICE ON A TUESDAY OR A FRIDAY MORNING. THE REPORTING CLAUSE DOES NOT REACH HER AND NEVER DID AND A ROOM IN A CITY HAS SAID SO ON THE RECORD.**

**Calder and Kest. He is in no chapter of this batch as a person and was not asked for anything.** The yard entered in the first column on the fourth of October that it considered going to the harbor authority for one thing — a record of the harbor's own ground for the week of the eighth of August — and did not go, and gave the reason: the ninth is the last entry in that ledger, a tenth may not be entered, the price of the ninth fell on a man of the first lay out of a Monday and has not been paid, and a yard that has been told in the open air that its ledger is spent does not go back up the same steps with the same request. **Entered as a decision and not as a virtue and nobody is going to thank this party for it.**

**Calder and Bevin. Unchanged and not in a room.** The boy is not in any chapter of this batch as a person. The three nots stand. Forty marks stand whole. **Calder and Alden. Do not move. THE FAR BENCH ON SIX DAYS OF THIS BATCH — THE SECOND, FIFTH, EIGHTH, TWELFTH, EIGHTEENTH AND NINETEENTH OF OCTOBER — and on no other day in it, not asked for anything on any of the six and asking nothing on any of the six, and Calder Marr does not go over to him on any of the six, entered in the first column on the twentieth with the six days and the reason: neither brother has said why and neither brother is going to. **THE FIGURE OF SEVEN AND THE DAYS OF THE FIRST AND THE SIXTEENTH ARE WITHDRAWN AND NAMED AS WITHDRAWN: no chapter of this batch places him on the first or the sixteenth, Chapter 171 places him on the second, and the withdrawn figure and its days are recorded at `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0003 (Volume 04) review repairs*. ON THE EIGHTEENTH AT ABOUT ELEVEN IN THE MORNING THE HOLDER PUT A TIN CUP OF WATER ON THE END OF THAT BENCH AND WENT BACK TO HIS OWN BENCH AND SAID NOTHING, AND THE MAN DID NOT TURN ROUND AND DID NOT DRINK IT AND DID NOT REFUSE IT AND SAID NO WORD, AND THE CUP WAS STILL THERE ON THE MONDAY MORNING AND WAS NOT THERE AT FOUR IN THE AFTERNOON, AND THE HOLDER ENTERED THAT HE HAS DONE THAT ONCE AND IS NOT GOING TO DO IT AGAIN, AND WHY.**

**Calder and Nessa. The pair this volume changes most and neither of them says so. She said in a shed on the twentieth of October that a wall has nothing in it you can be made to answer for and a room has a person in it and a clerk with a pen, and he said the other half of it out loud, and the other half is that what comes out of such a room goes on a page in a city and cannot be got off, and that he has been treating the second of those as if it were the first since the seventh of July. She has nine sentences in her head and did not write them. She has not been thanked and is not going to ask. He is going into a room in November with a book in a bag and she is going into it with him and the two of them are going to be asked in front of each other about a thing she wrote and a thing he wrote.**

### The power state at the end of Chapter 180

**NO STAGE OF ANY KIND IS TAKEN IN MOVEMENT 3 AND NONE MAY BE.** The Faultreader stage is spent, is not re-taken, is not improved, and **the word is on no page of these ten chapters.** He reads no seam, on a document or on anything else, and no seam settles anything, proves anything, convicts anybody or tells him what happened. **He goes in no water in this batch: two days of work on the second seam's own face with nobody in the water, a hand lead's line over the side and back four times, and no pot of air bought and none charged to any day.**

**THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN THIS BATCH.** The bare fact is carried twice, in the second column, with a reason against each line, and without a name. **The four lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four, are still nameless, and are not struck out, and the count of four stands in the first column in a ship's hand with no name against it. THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 04 WAS ALLOWED WAS SPENT IN CHAPTER 164 AND IS NOT AVAILABLE AGAIN.**

### The bodies and the dated positions at the end of Chapter 180

- **No air in this harbor except what this yard buys at its own two-mark rate, and nothing was bought in this batch.** No delivery hose; the longest thing of hose about nine fathoms and it is not this yard's; a compressor's gland fitted since the nineteenth of May, unpaid, on a page that has not been re-written, and of no use whatever because the thing after the gland is the hose.
- **The ground of every day of work in this batch is the second seam's own face, landward of the third seam's mark, on a hirer's own account, with nobody in the water. No person, no boat and no lead of this yard was inside the first clause of the notice of the thirty-first on any day of this batch, and the eighth-clause plan of the seventeenth of January was not reopened, and the plate at the fifth seam was not reached, not measured, not touched and not asked about.**
- **A wage at four marks a week: the sixth of October NOT PAID, the thirteenth PAID, the twentieth PAID. The column stands at twenty-three lines and ninety-two marks with the fifth of March the oldest, and seventeen envelopes are on a bench, and three weeks fallen due this year have no envelope standing against them being the fifteenth of August, the fifth of September and the sixth of October.**
- **A mark and twelve shillings in a bag at the close, against a mark and twelve shillings and fourpence on the evening of the thirtieth of September, and the whole of the difference being the postage of one letter in the ordinary post, which is the only money this yard has spent on itself in twenty days. Thirty-one on the chandler's note, not re-written, its three counts unmoved since the fourth of January. Nothing in a tin. Seven pence to the yard's own shipwright and not paid, and not the four pence. Three marks to a man of Ashtide for the eighth of August, unmoved, and he is not named in that book again. Eight marks and a day of a boat to the bellwright, unargued, and he was not asked anything in this month. Eight shillings and tenpence to a woman of this harbor. Forty marks to a boy of twenty-two. Forty marks to a person of no office, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October.**
- **The two objects of nobody's are on the mole's crown about a hundred and forty-nine days, about nine feet apart, under two tarpaulins, one of them with a corner off it since the seventeenth of June and not lashed back, and NEITHER OBJECT HAS MOVED.**
- **The delayed note: eleven logged occasions and two of them with no call in them, unchanged. No occasion was entered in this batch, no measurement was taken, and the delay is a hand's width of a second and has not moved and nobody in this harbor has ever said why and this party is not going to be the first.**
- **The bell is off the south end in eight and a half feet and is not going out and is not going to go out inside four years. The bellwright was not asked in this batch, is in no chapter of it as a person, no digit of anything he said is printed, and the chest was not opened and was not brought down the hill.**
- **The plate note is open and says so. The removed file page, the document in which an office accepts a removal and the phrase *risk management* are ALL SPENT IN THIS BATCH, and once spent they are not spent again in Movements 4 or 5.**
- **THE CUSTODY ROOM WAS NOT ASKED FOR, nothing came out of it, no digit of any number in it is in this yard's book, and the live question that no paper in this harbor or in that office's copying room answers was not put to anybody.**
- **THE HEARING ROOM WAS USED ONCE, ON FRIDAY THE SIXTEENTH OF OCTOBER, FOR ABOUT FORTY MINUTES. THE MARKET WALL WAS NOT TOUCHED ON ANY DAY OF THIS MONTH. THE MARKET OFFICE WAS USED ONCE, ON TUESDAY THE THIRTEENTH. NO TWO OF THE THREE ROOMS SHARE A DAY, AND IT IS VERIFIED A FOURTH TIME.**
- **THE READ-OUT DEVICE WAS NOT USED IN THIS BATCH AND ITS ONE USE PER BATCH IS CARRIED FORWARD. THE READ-BACK RAN IN SEVEN OF THE TEN CHAPTERS, 172, 173, 175, 176, 177, 179 AND 180, and is not a use of the device and may be used on any night without spending anything.**

## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 170 — SUPERSEDED BY THE CHAPTER 190 SECTION ABOVE, AND RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0002 RECORD

**VOLUME 04 MOVEMENT 2 IS WRITTEN AND ITS TURN HAS LANDED.** In twenty days a yard of two and a half people could not pay a wage, worked two days at a seam at three marks and five, told a man of the second lay in a doorway that a day of work could not be done because this harbor has no hose, went into a hearing room at ten in the morning and produced its whole crew's book a second time and read not one line of it aloud, was directed onward to a sitting in the week following the twelfth of October, had a man of the third lay come up a hill in the rain to say where a boy was and is not and was not asked one question, had its refusal of a certification turn into a statement in a city in four days that it had never made, and went into a market office on a Friday morning and was told, in about nine minutes and in eleven people's hearing, that the price of a line with a conservator's name at the foot of it is not money and is an undertaking this yard cannot give, and the answer is still no.**

**THE TURN IS PAID IN THE VOLUME'S OWN TERMS: a refusal to certify is read as a claim, and the only way to be read correctly is to be certified by somebody the yard cannot pay, and it prices the thing in the only currency it has, and says no.**

**And the cost of the fortnight is arithmetic and it closes checkable: a mark and twelve shillings and fourpence in a bag on the eleventh of September and a mark and twelve shillings and fourpence in it on the thirtieth, and in between the two of those figures two days of work at a seam, two wages of four marks paid, two wages of four marks not paid, and the two that were not paid are the week of Tuesday the fifteenth of August and the week of Tuesday the fifth of September, and neither of them has moved and neither is going to be paid this month.**

### The active pressures going into Volume 04 Movement 3

1. **A REFUSAL IS IN A FILE IN A CITY WITH FOUR WORDS IN IT THAT THIS YARD NEVER SAID.** *The yard of Morrow Quay, whose sheet is set in type in this city, gives the bar of that harbor at two and three-quarter fathoms on the flood; declines to certify the figure; and will not be held to it.* **The yard cannot get at the file, will not write to the city, and has entered that a party which has published itself is not a party that gets to be sorry in a street and mean it.** This is the movement's turn and it is on the page.
2. **THE ONLY WAY TO BE READ CORRECTLY IS A NAME, AND THE ONLY PERSON ON THIS COAST WHO CAN PUT A TRADE AT THE FOOT OF A LINE IS NOT ON THIS CREW.** Wil Tranter said it out loud in a doorway on the twenty-seventh: a man with a page cannot act on a refusal, he can act on a name, and there are three people on this coast who can put a trade under a line and this yard has none of them.
3. **A PRICE THAT IS NOT SIX MARKS AND TWO DAYS.** An undertaking, in the yard's own first column, in the holder's own left hand, with a date on it, that this party does not ask her for anything again — not at a figure, not at nothing, not for a line, not for a name, not in a room and not in a shed. **The reason she gave is on the page in her own words: a conservator this party cannot pay is a conservator this party has to keep asking, and asking her to be useful is asking her to be cheap.** The yard could not give it and did not offer it and has entered that it cannot.
4. **HE WENT IN.** On the eighth of September he stood on the steps of the market office for eighty minutes and did not go in. On the twenty-ninth he went in and asked for something, and was refused, and did not thank her and did not say he was sorry, and put the whole of it in the book including the fact that he came in with something to ask for. **He has still not told her that he wrote down the sentence of the seventh of August in the second column on the eighth, six weeks after she told him not to, and the not-telling stands.**
5. **A DIRECTIONS SITTING ON SATURDAY THE SIXTEENTH OF SEPTEMBER, AND A SITTING TO BE APPOINTED FOR THE WEEK FOLLOWING THE TWELFTH OF OCTOBER.** The inquiry is not over. **A copy of the yard's whole crew's book is in a file in a city and has not been read out in any room, and the reading of it in a room is the volume's midpoint and is in Chapters 181 and later. The second column of that book has four or five lines in it that nobody in this harbor can check, and a shipwright said out loud on the crown on Wednesday the thirteenth that if those lines are read out in a room then in about four years they are the only thing in forty-seven weeks that this yard knew and would not say.**
6. **A DAY OF WORK WAS REFUSED IN A DOORWAY BECAUSE THIS HARBOR HAS NO HOSE.** Five marks of compressor's gland on a creditor's page since the nineteenth of May, unpaid, sound, and of no use whatever because the thing after the gland is the hose; the longest thing of hose in this harbor about nine fathoms and it belongs to a man of the second lay. **A man of the second lay booked a day at his own figure for the Friday following and was told at eight in the morning on the Friday that the work could not be done, and the booking was refused and no money changed hands either way, and the yard entered the whole of it with the hour against it.** A poor crew does not get an inquiry's costs waived.
7. **THE WAGE COLUMN HAS MOVED TWICE AND STANDS AT TWENTY LINES AND EIGHTY MARKS**, the fifth of March the oldest, and **FIFTEEN ENVELOPES ARE ON A BENCH AND TWO WEEKS FALLEN DUE THIS YEAR HAVE NO ENVELOPE STANDING AGAINST THEM, being the fifteenth of August and the fifth of September, and the two figures are different figures and neither of them is the other.** The next wage is four marks on Tuesday the third of October, which is three days off at the close of this batch.
8. **THE SECOND COLUMN OF THE YARD'S OWN BOOK HAS ONE NAMED LINE IN IT AGAIN, AND IT IS THE ONLY NAMING VOLUME 04 HAS, AND IT IS NOT AVAILABLE AGAIN.** Chapter 164, Sunday the seventeenth of September, on land, in a doorway, with a man of the third lay: the holder cannot always tell which of two things he said in the last minute was the one he had decided to say, and it is a tide-sickness, and it has been got since the fourteenth of October of last year. **THE FOUR LINES ABOVE THE NAMED LINE OF THE TWENTIETH OF JULY ARE STILL FOUR, ARE STILL NAMELESS, AND ARE NOT STRUCK OUT, and the count of four stands in the first column in a ship's hand with no name against it.**
9. **AN ORDINARY MAN CAME UP A HILL IN THE RAIN AND SAID WHERE A BOY WAS AND IS NOT, AND WAS NOT ASKED ONE QUESTION.** A fore berth in June, from about the sixth to about the twenty-first, paid out of the man's own pocket and not out of a wage, and he is not in it and has not been since the twenty-first of June. **The three nots of the eighth of June stand. Nobody wrote to the boy, nobody went to see him, nobody asked anybody who has seen him what he said, and the yard entered that it wanted to say thank you and did not say it, and why.**
10. **THE THIRD COLUMN HAS NOT BEEN FILLED SINCE THE FIRST OF AUGUST** and there is a line in the first column about it, and nobody has been appointed, and a column nobody fills is a column somebody else fills, and **the shipwright has now said out loud, in the first column, that she did not fill it, and why, and that a column kept by a shipwright in a shed on a Sunday is a column about a shipwright.**
11. **THE NINTH SEAM'S MOORING IS IN SERVICE AND IT IS HARBOR GROUND AND IT IS NOT THIS YARD'S.** A man of the first lay asked on the twentieth whether it would take a boat of about ninety tons, and the yard said it would not answer for the mooring, gave him what its own book holds, took nothing, said it was not going to be thanked and had not been, and told him to go and ask the tide gang, **and the tide gang would carry her on a slack water for a day and a half, and a day and a half of the gang was the whole of the price and the tide gang is four men short of what it was.**
12. **THE COAST ROAD'S OWN FIGURE IS SET IN TYPE IN A CITY WITH THE PILOT'S FIGURE BESIDE IT AND NEITHER OF THEM CAN CHECK THE OTHER.** A man of about two hundred tons bought a printed sheet off a stall for a penny on the morning of the twenty-second and read it twice on the boat coming up. **The yard refused to tell him which figure is right, entered that it is not going to decide it in a shed, and did not argue either half of the sentence that is on the sheet. The Kettle was not looked at. The refused license stands. Five miles of that coast has not been looked at and is not going to be this season, and that is on the face of the sheet in the yard's own words.**
13. **THE PRIVATE OFFER OF MONEY IS A SEASON OLD AND IS CARRIED, NOT RE-DECIDED.** Thirty marks in coin, refused in nine sentences in June, on the back of a paper folded into the back of the writ of the first of May, brought out on the twenty-third of September for the only reason that puts a man's hand on it, which is a bag with twelve shillings and fourpence in it. **About four hundred feet of Sella Brigg's own warp came up off the bottom of this harbor on the twenty-eighth of July for twenty-six marks and a day, she was paid by the man of Ashtide, and this yard owes her eight shillings and tenpence, undiminished, unpaid, entered with her name on it at her own asking and not on any wall.** The refusal is a cost and is not a decision.
14. **A REPUBLIC HEAD OF A WALL AND A PRINTED SHEET, AND A PARTY THAT WROTE ON A WALL ON THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF AUGUST THAT A ROAD NOBODY ELSE CAN USE IS NOT A ROAD AND A ROAD EVERYBODY CAN USE IS NOT THIS YARD'S** — and a man with a brush saying *you put it on a wall and told sixty boats where the copies were*, and the yard saying *a printed sheet is the same instrument as a wall with one more step between it and the man who is not going to be able to be brought up in front of it*, entered in the second column and not the first.
15. **A HARBOR THAT IS POORER ON THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER THAN IT WAS ON THE ELEVENTH**, and poorer than it was on the fifteenth of August, by two unpaid wages and a day of work refused for want of a hose and a man of the first lay out of a Monday in August and a refusal that cannot be corrected by anybody but its author. **The cistern is on a rota of a Monday and a Thursday and was charged on eight days in September at no figure and this party walked it on none of the eight.** One herring berth out. The light not on. The tide gang four short. A hard at Ashtide unanswered since the ninth of November. Nineteen winters in a list in a city.

### The relationship pressure at the end of Chapter 170

**Calder and Mara.** They had not spoken since the seventh of July and he had not written. On the eighth of September he stood on her steps for eighty minutes and did not go in. **On the twenty-ninth of September he went in, at about ten past ten, in a room open to anybody, and asked her for one line in her own trade's words with her name and her price on it. She priced it before she answered it. The price is an undertaking he cannot give. The answer is no, and it is no at a figure and no at nothing, and she said in eleven people's hearing that she has not done a thing at nothing since the fourth of August and said in front of nine people on that day that she would not do one again.** He said he is not going to thank her and is not going to say he is sorry and is not going to pretend he is going to pay her. **He still has not told her he wrote the sentence of the seventh of August down in the second column on the eighth.** She is not on the crew, is not of the yard, is not a holder, is not on the four names, and the reporting clause does not reach her and never did. **Her break with the Registry is not in this batch, is a different event from her leaving this crew, is Movement 3's beat, and no chapter of this batch shows or hints at it or has a character remark that she is not where she was.**

**Calder and Kest. He is in no chapter of this batch as a person and was not asked for anything.** The yard entered in the first column on the twelfth of September that it did not go to the harbor authority and did not ask that office for anything, and why: the ninth is the last entry in that ledger and a tenth may not be entered, the officer said out loud on the twenty-seventh of August that his office has been asked twice, has paid twice and cannot pay a third time, and a yard that has been told that does not go back up the same steps with the same request. **It is entered as a decision and not as a virtue and nobody is going to thank this party for it.** The price of the ninth, a Monday out of a man of the first lay, is unpaid and unthanked and is not going to be said sorry about in a market.

**Calder and Bevin. Unchanged and not in a room.** The boy is not in any chapter of this batch as a person except as a berth in June in the mouth of a man of the third lay who was not asked one question. The three nots stand. Forty marks stand whole. The head of the third column keeps his hand. **The third column has not been filled since the first of August.**

**Calder and Alden. Do not move.** **The far bench on FIVE DAYS of this batch and not on six or seven: the eleventh, the twelfth, the seventeenth, the eighteenth and the twenty-fourth of September, and on no other day in it,** not asked for anything, asking nothing, and Calder Marr does not go over, entered in the first column on the eighteenth with the reason: neither brother has said why and neither brother is going to.

**Calder and Nessa. The pair this volume changes most and neither of them says so.** She won the argument about the second column in nine words in May and again on the thirteenth of September, on the crown, in nine people's hearing, in about nine minutes: reasons are long, reasons do not go into a copy, and if the second column is read out in a room then in four years its five checkless lines are the only thing in forty-seven weeks this yard knew and would not say. She counted the envelopes and made him count them once. She said the thirty marks were for four hundred feet of a woman's warp, out loud, once. She refused to let the volume's sentence be improved on in a shed. **She did not fill the third column and said why in the first column. They have not discussed the mooring and neither of them has.** He still has not thanked her and she is not going to ask.

### The power state at the end of Chapter 170

**NO STAGE OF ANY KIND IS TAKEN IN MOVEMENT 2 AND NONE MAY BE.** The Faultreader stage is spent, is not re-taken, is not improved, and **the word is on no page of these ten chapters.** He reads no seam, on a document or on anything else, and no seam settles anything, proves anything, convicts anybody or tells him what happened. **He goes in no water in this batch: two days of work on the second seam's own face with nobody in the water, a hand lead's line over the side and back four times, and no pot of air bought and none charged to any day.**

**THE CONDITION IS NAMED ONCE IN THIS BATCH, IN CHAPTER 164, ON LAND, IN A DOORWAY, IN THE SECOND COLUMN, IN THE HOLDER'S OWN LEFT HAND, WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE AND NOT EARNED BY PUTTING HIM IN THE WATER.** The Volume 03 naming is spent and is not available again. **The one naming Volume 04 was allowed was used once and is spent.**

## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 160 — SUPERSEDED BY THE CHAPTER 170 SECTION ABOVE, AND RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0001 RECORD

**VOLUME 04 MOVEMENT 1 IS WRITTEN. THE BATCH MIDPOINT IS PAID AND THE BATCH CLIMAX IS PAID, AND THE VOLUME'S CENTRAL PRESSURE IS ON THE PAGE.** (Retained as the Batch 0001 record; superseded for working purposes by the Chapter 170 section above.) In twenty-six days a yard of two and a half people found its own sheet of the fifteenth of August gone off a market wall in one morning in a boat with two naval notices, got the date and the room out of a four-line form that does not mention the writ's second season, sold two days of ordinary harbor work, paid a wage on a Tuesday for the first time in three weeks and did not pay a shilling of a debt to a man of another crew, took a man of fifty-four's figure and put it on a wall beside its own, published a refusal it had received rather than a fact it had established, spent a reeve's last entry on a day of work inside a notice and charged it to a man of the first lay, watched two strangers put a mooring back into service because they had read a date off a wall, stood on the steps of a market office for eighty minutes and did not go in, put a whole crew's book on a table in a hearing room and read nothing out of it, and was served on Sunday the tenth with a notice that hands the harbor authority the power to seat one of two disputants — which is the reporting clause's own second limb, applied to this party, by a form this party did not write and cannot un-write.**

**A DATE AND A ROOM ARE ENTERED IN ADVANCE AND THE DAY IS A DAY IN THE WEEK AFTER THE ELEVENTH OF SEPTEMBER. The yard will be at the sitting, will not be represented, and will answer what is asked and not what it would have chosen, and it has entered that this is a decision and not a virtue and is not going to be thanked for it and is not asking. The third column was not filled on the Sunday night, which is the second day since the second of May that it has not been filled, and nobody has been appointed, and a line in the second column says that the holder does not know whether he is going to read the second column out in a room, with a reason against it and no name in it.**

**A COPY OF THE YARD'S WHOLE CREW'S BOOK IS IN A FILE IN A CITY, and that is the first time in four volumes that a copy of this yard's own record has left this harbor, and not one line of it was read aloud in the room it was produced in, and a man in a coat said out loud at the table that he is not going to open the copy in that room and is going to read it in a city, and the yard cannot stop him and has entered that it does not know what he will be done with it.**

**And the third column of a woman of thirty is empty, and a sentence she told him on the seventh of August not to write down is in the second column tonight with a reason against it and no name in it, and he has not told her he wrote it.**

### The active pressures going into Volume 04 Movement 2

1. **THE INQUIRY HAS A DATE, A ROOM AND A CONSTRUCTION, AND THE CONSTRUCTION IS THE PARTY'S OWN.** Where a finding is disputed the harbor authority may seat one of two disputants and take such finding as is given, without prejudice to any claim. The party built that in April, agreed to it in good faith, printed the clause on a market wall in July in forty copies in about sixty boats, and is now the thing on the table instead of the person reading the table. **This is the volume's central pressure and it is paid.**
2. **THE BOOK IS IN A CITY.** A copy of it, whole, in a file, in another man's hand, and it contains the day a man lost the count of his own basket and the day a case went down, and both were entered by the yard against itself at the time, and the yard cannot un-enter them and did not have to enter them to be hurt by them.
3. **THE READING OF THAT BOOK IN A ROOM IS COMING AND IS NOT SPENT.** It is the volume's midpoint and it is in Chapters 181 and later. The yard has entered that it knows it is coming, has entered that it is not going to soften a line and not going to leave one out, and has entered in the second column that it does not know whether the not-reading on the ninth was a rule or a fear.
4. **A MAN WHO IS THE BEST READER OF JOINS IN THIS HARBOR HAS THREE WEEKS AND NO IDEA WHAT HE WILL SAY.** He is not going to be told, and no seam is put on a document in this batch, and nothing in this batch tells him anything.
5. **THE DRAFTING OF THE FORM IS FROM SOMEBODY WHO HAS READ A MARKET WALL OF THIS HARBOR, and the yard does not know who and has entered that it does not know and is not going to guess.**
6. **A PROVISIONAL PUBLIC COPY RECORD EXISTS AND IS NOT OBTAINED.** A copy of a record about an object, with the limits the record itself carries printed on the face of the copy. The yard may ask for a copy of the copy at its own cost. The request is entered; the copy is not made, is not released, and no date attaches to either. **Nothing of it is on a wall, in a book, in a market, or in a room, and its release is the volume's climax.**
7. **THE YARD'S OWN FOURTH RETURN IS THE ONLY PAPER ON THE MARKET WALL AND IT IS STILL THERE**, under one sheet of the yard's own making since the twenty-fifth of August, still readable from the side of it in the right wind. **A copy of the record of that return has been asked for by letter in the ordinary post and refused, and the refusal is on a market wall in a market with a date on it.**
8. **A REPUBLIC HEAD OF A WALL THAT TELLS A MAN WHERE THE COPIES ARE.** The pilot said so out loud on the mole's steps and the yard heard him, did not agree, said so out loud in a market on the sheet's own face, and is not taking it down. **A road nobody else can use is not a road and a road everybody can use is not this yard's, and both halves are on a wall and neither is going away.**
9. **A NINTH ACKNOWLEDGEMENT IS SPENT AND A TENTH MAY NOT BE ENTERED, AND THE PRICE FELL ON A MAN OF THE FIRST LAY.** Corby Vance is out of a Monday and a day's work because this yard asked, and the yard has entered his name and the figure and has not paid it, is not going to thank him, is not going to send for him, and is not going to say sorry in a market.
10. **THE WAGE COLUMN HAS MOVED TWICE AND STANDS AT EIGHTEEN LINES AND SEVENTY-TWO MARKS**, the fifth of March the oldest and staying there, and **thirteen envelopes on a bench and one week fallen due this year with no envelope standing against it, being the week of Tuesday the fifteenth of August**, and the two figures are different figures and neither of them is the other.
11. **THREE MARKS ARE OWING TO A MAN OF ASHTIDE AND NOT A SHILLING OF IT IS PAID**, with the reason in the first column and the date on it, and he was named once in this volume and is not to be named again.
12. **A REASON A PARTY CANNOT PAY IS BEING USED IN A ROOM BY AN OFFICE, SIX THINGS AND A PRICE, and the party is standing on the steps outside and is not going in and is not going to be the reason any of it is asked.**
13. **A HARBOR THAT IS POORER ON THE TENTH OF SEPTEMBER THAN IT WAS ON THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST** and is not going to be made better: one herring berth out, a day of a man of the first lay lost, a light not on, a cistern on a rota, a tide gang four short, a hard at Ashtide unanswered, nineteen winters in a list in a city, forty marks to a boy, forty marks to a woman, eight shillings and tenpence to each of two women, eight marks and a day of a boat to a bellwright, a chandler's note at thirty-one and not re-written, and a bag with a mark and twelve shillings and fourpence in it.

### The relationship pressure at the end of Chapter 160

**Calder and Mara.** They have not spoken since the seventh of July and he has not written. On the eighth of September he stood on the steps of the market office for eighty minutes while a man in a coat asked her six questions she could answer and a seventh she could not, and he did not go in, and he did not ask her afterwards what she said, and both of those are entered in the first column with a date and with a reason, and a request to strike the line out of the column is refused in the same words it is refused for a line in any column. **He then wrote down the sentence she told him on the seventh of August not to write down, and he has not told her, and the not-telling is entered.** She is owed forty marks first after food since the fifteenth of October, the yard has not offered her six marks and two days and has not been asked to and has entered that it is going to say no if it ever is. **She is not on the crew, is not of the yard, is not a holder, is not on the four names, and the reporting clause does not reach her and never did.**

**Calder and Kest.** The reeve has spent the last thing in his ledger and says so, and names the price in the open air before he grants it, and refuses to let the yard thank him because a thank-you in a market is the only true thing this harbor will be able to say about this party in four years and he is not going to give it away. **He is not a friend and the volume does not pretend he was right about everything.** He said the same thirteen words about the plate in August that he said in July and the yard did not ask a second question.

**Calder and Bevin.** Unchanged and not in a room. The third column was not filled on the tenth of September, the second day since the second of May it has not been filled, and nobody has been appointed. The head of the column keeps his hand and a request to scrape it out is refused. Forty marks stand whole.

**Calder and Alden.** Do not move. The far bench on six days in this batch, not asked, asking nothing, Calder Marr does not go over, one line in the first column that either brother can check.

**Calder and Nessa.** She won the argument in a shed on the Sunday the twentieth and did it in nine words — *a column is not a defense of anything and was never one*. She is the person holding the pencil. **She has not been thanked for anything in this batch and neither has he, and the two of them did not discuss the mooring and that is the closest they have come in four volumes.**

### The power state at the end of Chapter 160

**NO STAGE OF ANY KIND IS TAKEN IN MOVEMENT 1 AND NONE MAY BE.** The Faultreader stage is spent, is not re-taken, is not improved, and **the word is not on any page of these ten chapters.** No seam is put on a document in this batch and no seam settles anything. **He is not told what a seam means in any room and he is not put in one.**

**THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN THIS BATCH**, which the batch's hard rules permitted at most once and only if a chapter earned it, and no chapter earned it. The bare fact is carried twice in the second column with a reason against each line and without a name: the not-reading of the crew's book in a hearing room on the ninth, and the not-knowing about reading the second column out in a room on the tenth. The four lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four, are still nameless, and are not struck out. The count of four stands in the first column in a ship's hand with no name against it.

### The bodies and the dated positions at the end of Chapter 160

- **No air in this harbor except what this yard buys at its own two-mark rate, and nothing was bought in this batch.** No delivery hose; a compressor's gland fitted since the nineteenth of May, unpaid, on a page that has not been re-written, at five marks, and useless because the thing after the gland is the hose. A hand lead's line was used on the second seam's own face on the twenty-first of August and not past the outer marks, and the reason is on the page.
- **The ground of every day of work in this batch is the second seam's own face landward of the third seam's mark, on a hirer's own account, and once the mole's toe inside the first clause of the notice on the twenty-eighth, with the ninth acknowledgement on the page and the reeve's own cost. No person, no boat and no lead of this yard was inside the first clause on any other day of this batch, and the eighth-clause plan of the seventeenth of January was not reopened and the plate at the fifth seam was not reached, was not measured, was not touched, and was not asked about.**
- **A wage at four marks a week: the twenty-second of August PAID, the twenty-ninth of August PAID, and the fifteenth of August of Volume 03 still not paid with the envelope not made.** The column stands at eighteen lines and seventy-two marks with the fifth of March the oldest.

## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 150 — SUPERSEDED BY THE CHAPTER 160 SECTION ABOVE, AND RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0005 RECORD

**VOLUME 03 MOVEMENT 5 IS WRITTEN AND IT WAS THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX AND ITS RESOLUTION.** In twenty days a yard of two and a half people read a Crown notice nailed over its own return on a market wall, watched a working vessel of a community of this coast go on the ground inside the four hundred yards with four people in her, **wrote down the sentence it had published on the fifth of July and repeated on the twenty-sixth and entered the breaking of it and the whole of the cost in its own first column on the seventh of August, in advance, at nine at night — and went past the outer marks on the eighth on its own account with no instrument of any office, with two pots of air bought at the harbor authority's own compressor at this yard's own two-mark rate and a day of another crew's boat at six marks of his own naming, part paid. HE SAVED THE VESSEL'S LIVING CREW AND LOST THE BELL-ARCHIVE, and the two are the same choice and neither of them cancels the other.**

**THE COST WAS PAID AND IT WAS PAID BY SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT THE YARD: A WAGE OF FOUR MARKS WAS NOT PAID ON TUESDAY THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST AND THE ENVELOPE WAS NOT MADE, and the money that would have paid it is the money that was spent on a boat, and the yard is four marks short and has entered that in the first column with the hour against it; and three marks is owing to a man of Ashtide for the eighth, with a date on it; and a community's only local copy of its own account of itself is at the bottom of the sea about a mile and a half off this harbor in about five and a half fathoms, and the four people it belongs to are alive and have no boat, and the page that went up on the market wall on the fifteenth says where the bells are and does not pretend anybody is coming for them.**

**Captain Veyra Holt is relieved of the *Vigil* and of the command of this coast from the fifteenth of August and posted from the first of September to the making up of the coast's wreck lists, in a city, at a table. She does not come to this harbor, has not been in it, and appears in no room. The posting came on a paper read in the hearing room over the tide-gang store on Saturday the twelfth of August by a man in a coat of an office in a city whose name the clerk wrote down and this yard has not repeated; **the channel is the writ, was ruled on the fifth of July, was recited on the twenty-sixth and was applied on the twelfth, and the yard entered that the man was let up the stairs BECAUSE of that standing arrangement.** The second season of the writ is not granted, not refused and not dealt with by that paper; the writ runs to the first of March; its ground is not widened; its four names are four. The yard checked the posting against its own entry of the fifth of July and entered that the check holds: it is not a command in this harbor, not an inspection of the harbor authority, not a commission, and puts nobody past the outer marks or the imperial line.**

**Four people of the Sill testified on a mole's steps in about forty people's hearing on the tenth of August, in their own words, with a limit they wrote themselves and signed: a bell-archive is not a thing that is read out, it is a thing that is struck, in a hall, in front of the people it is about, and there is nobody in this town it is about. SOMEBODY BEARS IT: the four bear it, and the holder bears it, and the yard has entered in its own hand and with a date that he wanted it lifted, could not lift it, did not ask twice, and is not going to. A question was asked in a market on Sunday the thirteenth of August in about forty people's hearing — WHO CHANGED THE RULE — and it was not answered, and the not-answering is in the first column in the yard's own hand with the reason against it, and the reason is this yard's decision of the fifth of July not to put a name on a thing it cannot check. A herring skipper of fifty-one with nineteen winters in a naval file said, in the same market, that both of those are true and neither of them cancels the other, and would not say it twice, and made the holder read it back twice, and it is in the book.**

**And on Tuesday the fifteenth of August at about eleven in the morning, in a market, in daylight, a page went up on the market wall of Morrow Quay under a naval paper, on the same nails, on the outside of whatever was there — the *Vigil's* notice of the fourth of August being over the *Sallow Reach's* printed sheet, which was over the sheet of the seventh of June, which was over the yard's tenth thing, and the yard's own fourth return of the twenty-second of July having been under four papers since the twenty-eighth of July and still readable from the side of it in the right wind. IT CARRIES THE PLACE OF EVERY COPY OF IT, THE NINE PLACES AS THIS YARD IS ABLE TO STATE THEM WITH A DATE AGAINST EACH AND THE WORD *HARBORS* NOT ON IT ANYWHERE, AND THE TWO COLUMNS WITH A REASON AGAINST EVERY LINE OF THE SECOND.**

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page: NOTHING in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, nothing in a tin, and three marks owing to a man of Ashtide, on Tuesday the fifteenth of August — against nothing in the same bag on Wednesday the twenty-sixth of July.** The chain is **0, 3, 3, 7, 3, 7, 7, 11, 0, 0** across the days money moves. Four days of work at about three or four marks each, the whole of it borrowed from a week that was not doing them. On the eighth of August: the wage, four marks; two pots of the office's air, four marks; the master of the *Sallow Reach*, six marks named out loud, three paid at the mole and three not — **which empties the bag exactly.** Three wage weeks fall in the batch, the first, eighth and fifteenth of August. **The first and the eighth went into envelopes; the fifteenth was NOT PAID.** The column goes **fifteen lines and sixty marks – sixteen lines and sixty-four marks** and stops, the fifth of March the oldest and staying there, and eleven envelopes on a bench and not one of them has been anywhere in twelve weeks. The note is not re-written, its three counts of weeks do not move, five marks of compressor's gland stand on it unpaid and useless, and the tin is empty. **The whole of the money that has come into this yard since the twenty-second of July is fourteen marks and the whole of what went out is eighteen. No office in this harbor has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May and none is going to.**

### The active pressures going into the Volume 03 close

1. **THE VOLUME IS WRITTEN AND THE CLOSE IS SHORT.** Fifty chapters, five movements, the climax paid, the resolution paid, the new-volume question opened and not answered, the final image on the page. **The close is an audit and a hand-over and must not open a Volume 04 thread by accident.**
2. **LOCK L5 IS STILL FLAGGED AND THE AUDIT MUST RULE ON IT.** It enumerates three returns and says there are three and only three. There are four on the page, the fourth made on Friday the fourteenth of July on the day, out of a finding of Monday the tenth, and Batch 0005 added no fifth.
3. **THE BELL-ARCHIVE IS LOST AND THE OWNERS ARE ALIVE AND POORER.** Five bells at about five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards. No instrument in this harbor reaches that water, no money in this harbor reaches that water, and no trade on this coast whose work it is. A later volume may supply a way, at a price, and the price will have to fall on somebody.
4. **THE YARD BROKE ITS OWN PUBLISHED SENTENCE AND ENTERED IT IN ADVANCE, AND THE SENTENCE CANNOT BE SAID AGAIN.** A yard whose saying is worth nothing in any figure it puts on any wall in any year after this one. This is a standing condition and not an event.
5. **A WAGE OF FOUR MARKS IS UNPAID AND THE WAGE COLUMN HAS STOPPED AT SIXTEEN LINES AND SIXTY-FOUR MARKS.** Not the yard's forty marks, which are not going to be paid either way. This one is new, it is a working man's four marks for six days, and anybody can come up the hill and ask.
6. **THREE MARKS ARE OWING TO PERRY OLLETT OF THE *SALLOW REACH*** for the eighth of August, a debt to a man of another crew, in the first column, with a date on it, not in any column of the book.
7. **THE PUBLIC THING IS DONE AND IS NOW ON A WALL UNDER A NAVAL PAPER.** The nine places, the place of every copy, the two columns, the limit, the five bells, the broken sentence. About sixty boats. One copy in a city going to press. **A Crown claim was enforced against a working boat over a market wall, which the volume's reveal discipline allows and which is now on the page.**
8. **THE QUESTION IS ASKED AND NOT ANSWERED,** and the not-answering is on the page in the yard's own hand with the reason against it, and the reason is a rule the yard set itself on the fifth of July.
9. **A BOY WHO CANNOT DO THE THING HE WAS KEPT FOR**, in the yard's own hand with a date on it, on the first of August, and the three nots standing, and the head of the third column keeping his hand, and the final chapter's pencil in Nessa Pike's hand. **He was not paid, resolved, repaired or reduced in this batch and the forty marks stand whole.**
10. **A CONSERVATOR OF NO OFFICE IS NOT ON THE CREW AND IS STILL IN THIS HARBOR,** and is in the market office on Tuesday and Friday mornings because that office is open to anybody, and was asked for nothing by this party, did one thing at nothing **once** and said so in front of nine people, gave six things about the bells and could not give a seventh, and is owed forty marks first after food unpaid since the fifteenth of October. **Her break with the Registry is Volume 4 and is a different event and no chapter of Volume 03 fuses them.**
11. **A REEVE WITH NOTHING LEFT TO PUT IN HIS BOOK,** who said on a mole's steps on the sixth of August that there is nothing in his book that will put a boat of his past the outer marks and nothing in the holder's either, and that this is not a failure of either book, it is the design. Not a friend, not a hero, his ledger spent.
12. **A HARBOR THAT IS WORSE OFF ON THE FIFTEENTH OF AUGUST THAN IT WAS ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF JULY** and is not going to be made better by anything: one herring berth short, a mooring out of service with an end of it seventeen days off, a third lay that has lost four days and then four more of its moorings in the ordinary way, the tide gang four men short, the fire party's hose dead, the mole-head light not on, no boat of this office's own since the twenty-eighth of April, the hard at Ashtide unanswered since the ninth of November, and nineteen winters in a file, in a letter and in a list, and a chart sold for them.
13. **A MAN OF FIFTY-FOUR OUT OF A STORM BELT IS STILL COMING IN THE AUTUMN FOR NOTHING,** with his own figure for a bar of two and a half where this yard's figure is two and three-quarters, and no instrument in this harbor that can call him and none that can stop him, and the yard did not go and look and has entered that it did not.
14. **THE PLANNED ENDING IS UNTOUCHED.** No new antagonist, no new institution, no new final enemy. The ninth-line mark, the Quietening, the Crown Seal, the Office of Returning Memory, the nine final routes, the compartment, the custody items, the lead plug's number, the run under this town, the coast road, the first echo's witness and the plate at the fifth seam are all exactly where Volume 02 left them.

### The relationship pressure at the end of Chapter 150

**Calder and Mara are two people who have disagreed in public about whether to publish a dangerous witness, and the disagreement is not repaired and is not settled by him giving her the evidence.** In this batch she was not asked for anything by him at all. **A woman of the second lay asked her a question in the market office on Friday the fourth and she answered at nothing once, said so in front of nine people, said the yard did not ask and would not have got a different figure, and said the not-saying costs her and not the boat.** He learned in Volume 03 that a conservator I cannot pay is a conservator I have to keep asking, and asking her to be useful is asking her to be cheap; **in this batch he kept to the first half and the standing arrangement held, and on the seventh of August she told him not to write down one sentence and he did not write it down, and she told him that if she is ever the one holding the pencil in that yard he can check whether she kept it.** **She is the person holding the pencil in Chapter 150.**

**Calder and Bevin are unchanged and the batch did not put him in a room.** He is in no chapter of it as a person. The three nots are recited in four chapters and none is broken. Forty marks standing whole. Eleven envelopes on a bench. **And the first column of the yard's own book now says, in the holder's own left hand and with a date, that he cannot do the thing he was kept for, and that the party is not going to ask him whether he could, and that a column in that book is not a wage.** The head of the third column carries his hand and a request to scrape it out is refused.

**Calder and Kest are at the end and the end is now on the page.** The reeve has spent three times and has nothing left to put in his book, and has said out loud on a mole's steps that there is no instrument in it that will put a boat past the outer marks. **He is not a friend and the volume does not pretend he was right about everything; the holder did not thank him for the second entry, entered that he was not going to use it, and said that in four years a man reading that wall will read it as a reeve's price. The plate was not mentioned, not asked about, and not reached.**

**Calder and Nessa are the pair this batch changed most and neither of them says so.** She won the argument about being quiet in a market. She read back the sentence that broke a published one, in a shed, at ten past nine, and read it back after him. **She did not say the reader thing a fourth time and the reason is on the page. She did not pull the cutoff in nineteen minutes and has entered both what she did and what she would have done at twenty-five. She told him not to write a sentence down and he did not write it down. And the third column was not filled on the eighth of August and nobody appointed anybody to fill it, and she is holding the pencil on the fifteenth.**

**Calder and Alden do not move.** The far bench on the twenty-seventh of July, the thirty-first and the first, and the seventh of August; not asked for anything on any of the four days, asking nothing, **and Calder Marr does not go over to him on any of them, and the yard has entered that he did not and that neither brother has said why and neither brother is going to.**

### The power state at the end of Chapter 150

**NO STAGE OF ANY KIND IS TAKEN IN MOVEMENT 5 AND NONE MAY BE.** The Faultreader stage was taken in Movement 4, is spent, and is not taken again. **He read one seam in this batch — mendings under mendings in the *Hearthwake's* garboard, in six feet of water in the dark, on the first call down of the eighth of August — and it identified difference and nothing else, and THIS TIME HE DID NOT SUPPLY THE REST, and the reason is in the second column and the reason is that a man who has been wrong in public in a market once about the word *chain* does not get to be the man who supplies the rest of a sentence about a boat with people in her.** He lost about a second and a half over a shout he could not place, and that second and a half is the price of the volume's climax and it is in the yard's own book in the second column with a reason and **without the word.**

**THE ONE NAMING OF THE CONDITION WAS SPENT IN CHAPTER 137, ON THE TWENTIETH OF JULY, IN THE YARD'S OWN BOOK, IN THE HOLDER'S OWN LEFT HAND, IN THE SECOND COLUMN, WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE, AND IT IS NOT AVAILABLE AGAIN IN THIS VOLUME OR IN ANY OTHER.** The bare fact is on the page twice in this batch, in the second column, with a reason, and without a name. **The four lines above it are still nameless, are not struck out, and a request to strike a line out of a column is refused. The count of four stands in the first column in a shipwright's hand with no name against it.**

**And he is not repaired and the volume does not let him off.** He goes in the water four times in this batch, inside limits he set himself at the boat and that nobody raised, with the six read aloud in order and the cutoff hers and the word *Enough*, and he comes back with a hand that cannot tell a shout from a deck and with a decision he made by letting go.

### The bodies and the dated positions at the end of Chapter 150

- **No air in this harbor except six pots bought this season at this yard's own two-mark rate off the harbor authority's own compressor on that office's own hose, and two of them were in a boat past the outer marks on the eighth of August. No delivery hose; the longest thing of hose about nine fathoms; a compressor's gland fitted since the nineteenth of May, unpaid, on a creditor's page at five marks, and useless because the thing after the gland is the hose. A hand lead's line made in this yard's own net loft on the second of May, used on the twenty-seventh, the thirty-first and the seventh of August, and not used past the outer marks.**
- **The ground of every day of work this batch is the second seam's own face landward of the third seam's mark, and the dump, and the dump is ground the writ of the first of May names in its own ground. The ground of the eighth and ninth of August is inside the four hundred yards and about three miles inside the imperial line, and no person, boat or lead of this yard went anywhere near the imperial line, and the full Second Bell license is not granted to anybody in this harbor, is not approached, and was not approached. There are eight acknowledgements in that office's ledger, both of this year's are spent, and this batch entered none, asked for none, and put nothing inside the first clause on any day.**
- **A wage at four marks a week: the first and the eighth of August PAID, the fifteenth NOT PAID.** The column is sixteen lines and sixty-four marks with the fifth of March the oldest. Eleven envelopes on a bench and not one of them has been anywhere in twelve weeks. **The forty marks of the claim stand whole.**
- **The bell is on the harbor's own ground in eight and a half feet off the south end**, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was**, a rope bought on the eleventh of January, **a going out on the second of May that is done and is spent and was not repeated, and eight marks and a day of a boat owing since the twenty-third of May and unpaid and unargued and not asked about in this batch, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May standing unreversed and being the whole of the last image.**
- **The lead plug is in a chest in a shed with a lock the yard owns. The bellwright was not asked again and is in no chapter of this batch as a person, no digit of what he said on the second of July is printed in any of them, the chest was not opened and was not brought down the hill, and it is named once, in the read-out, as a thing that was not opened. This is not a virtue and is not put forward as one: a yard that has undertaken not to ask does not ask, and the work did not need him.**
- **The delayed note: eleven logged occasions and two of them with no call in them, unchanged, and nothing entered in this batch and nothing measured. The delay is a hand's width of a second and it has not moved and was not taken and was not written down, and nobody in this yard has said why and nobody in this harbor has ever said why and this party is not going to be the first.**
- **Two things of nobody's are still living in the open air on the mole's crown above the third seam's mark, about nine feet apart, under two tarpaulins, one of them with a corner off it since the seventeenth of June and not lashed back, and NEITHER OBJECT HAS MOVED IN NINETY DAYS, and the marking on one of them is not identified and is not going to be.** Six joints four fathoms under that same stone, nine fastenings in about seven feet of each, three thicknesses of fouling, a chip of worked glass put into a packing on purpose, and a line of six places three hundred and fifty-one feet long which this party has declined to call a chain.
- **THE FIVE BELLS are at about five and a half fathoms inside the four hundred yards, under the after hatch of a vessel claimed whole by an office in a city, and they are the only local copy of a community's account of itself, and their four people are alive at the top of a room in this harbor with no boat, no wages and about nine marks between them.**
- **The crew's book has three columns.** The third was filled on the first of August at about four hours' lateness and on the fifteenth at about an hour's, the shortest since the fourth of April, **and was not filled at all on the eighth**, and the entry says why and says that the party does not know who will fill it next and has not appointed anybody.
- **The market wall of Morrow Quay now carries, in the order it was put on: the yard's tenth thing; the sheet of the seventh of June with a woman's name and a figure and a date on it; the *Sallow Reach's* printed sheet of the twenty-seventh of July; the *Vigil's* notice of the twenty-eighth of July; the *Vigil's* second notice of the fourth of August; and, UNDER the naval paper, the yard's own sheet of the fifteenth of August — with the yard's own fourth return of the twenty-second of July beneath the whole of it and still readable from the side of it in the right wind.** The hearing room was used once, on Saturday the twelfth of August, and no sitting has been held in it since.
