## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 140 — SUPERSEDED BY THE CHAPTER 150 SECTION ABOVE, AND RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0004 RECORD

**VOLUME 03 MOVEMENT 4 IS WRITTEN AND IT WAS THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT.** In twenty days a yard of two and a half men put a hand lead's line into a crevice four fathoms down on the second seam's own face and snagged it on a piece of metal about the length of a hand, and worked it up by hand in a westerly, and came back four days later to the same place with a ring and brought up a lump the size of two fists and a chip of green glass the length of a thumbnail out of the tallow of the lead, **and then made a fourth return on the day, because the clause says every finding of worked glass in four days and the day of the finding is not counted and the fourth day after the tenth of July is the fourteenth.** A conservator of no office who had been in this harbor about a fortnight and whom nobody knew priced herself out loud before she touched anything, proved six things about that joint with a lens and a pair of calipers of her own — two ends cut to each other, packing and fastenings of one age, both over three inches of fouling on the seaward side and none on the landward, the packing pressed in from seaward, nine fastenings in about seven feet with three chisel blows a head and the same three blows on all nine, and a chip of the same worked glass put into the packing on purpose — **and could not give a seventh, and the seventh was who, and her reason is that a body does not leave anything on a thing that a trade can read.** A man in the water put his hand flat on the cut and **read a seam for the first time in his life**: the two cuts in that joint are of different ages, and it came into his hand with no picture and no sound, and it told him there were mendings under mendings and it told him nothing whatever about when or in what order or by whose hand — **and he supplied all three, and said out loud in a market on Tuesday the eighteenth at about half past three, in about eleven people's hearing, that the six joints are a chain, that they were made once in one time by one set of hands from the north end down, and that whatever they are for it is not to keep two boats off the bottom in a blow.** Fouling took it apart in two days: the two thinnest readings are the two at the root end, which is the north end, and he had told eleven people the newest was the first; and a quarter of an inch and three inches and a quarter is not a morning but at least three different days in three different seasons; and there is a joint with nine inches on it, which is the oldest thing in the crevice and is the fifth of the six and the second from the seaward end, and he does not know what that means and said he was not going to say. **It cost a day bought for seventeen and half of it done, a hired diver of another boat who went down because he was told to and is not doing it because of Tuesday, a woman of the second lay who paid twelve marks at the mole for half a day and came up the hill to say the tide was there at seven and at two, and the woman of this yard who is the reason there is a reader and had to read the wrong down and then read a word about her partner's ear down after it.** And on the twentieth of July, in the shed, at a quarter past nine, with a man of the *Grey Widow* awake on a net loft floor and nobody pretending otherwise, **he said the word for it out loud for the first time in seven months and wrote it into the second column of the yard's own book in his own left hand with a name in it, and there is a name in that line and there is no name in any of the four above it, and he broke his own line of the second of June, and the reason against the line is that a man who can lose a quarter of a second out of an ordinary conversation in a shed is a man whose account of a seam under four fathoms is a thing that can be asked about, and the party has decided it can be asked about in this book, on this page, on this date, by anybody who comes up this hill, and the only difference between that and a man with a seal is that it can be argued with.** On the twenty-first the party went into the market office for the first time in five months and read four books out of a press under a stone slab that is unlocked to anybody on a Tuesday and a Friday morning, **and found a builder's day-book of the port of Ashtide with six places in it that are the six joints and a builder's name cut into a stone at the mole head, and a weir-book with a fisher's own storm in it and the third joint a foot and four inches under that weir's own sill stone, and a poor-book with twenty-two children put out of a village and nine of them named and the village not on this coast, and a tide-gang's column of figures that stops — and laid a string on the shed floor and the six joints came to three hundred and fifty-one feet with nothing in the string's way, and there are nine places and six of them stand over a hole in a stone wall and three of them do not, and the party has entered the three and has entered that it does not know why and is not going to guess.** And it declined the word. Nine places is nine places. A line is a line. And the word *chain*, said on the Tuesday by a man who was wrong in three parts of the same sentence, is disowned in the yard's own book. **And on Saturday the twenty-second, in daylight, in a market, the yard nailed its own fourth return up on the market wall over the rival's printed sheet, with no scale on it and no price on it and nowhere on it to write a letter to, and a shilling changed hands at the foot of the dock for the rival's and not for the yard's, and a conservator of no office said out loud in the open air in forty people's hearing that he was a man who had run out of money and a man who had not, and that she was not taking either half back, and her objection went onto the sheet unsigned with the reason, and his answer went on beside it in fifteen words: both, and I will not be able to tell you which was right in October.** And on Sunday the twenty-third at the top of the mole's steps she said, in her own words, before anybody asked her, that a yard that cannot pay her asks her whether she can be useful, that she has been useful four times since the twenty-seventh of January without being asked once or paid once, that six marks and two days is her price and does not come down, and that a trade that says yes to nothing is not a trade — **and then he told her all of it, the day-book and the weir and the poor-book and the nine places and three hundred and fifty-one feet, and she called it the best thing anybody has found out in this harbor in five months and then said it was worth nothing to her, because she is not being paid for it, and a thing a person is given because she is owed money is not a gift and not a trade and not a finding.** And the yard said its own half out loud, in Calder Marr's own words, on a mole's steps: **a conservator I cannot pay is a conservator I have to keep asking, and asking her to be useful is asking her to be cheap, and I have been doing the second thing since the twenty-seventh of January and calling it the first.** And on the twenty-sixth a reeve entered in his own book, at his own cost, that the mole's footing from the third to the twelfth seam is to be examined on the second of August and the third lay closed for the four days of it, **with the repair account of that office empty since the middle of February and the money not coming and the entry made nevertheless, and a man of the third lay said four days out loud in a market and did not ask for anything, and the party asked the reeve on the steps whether any of it was about the plate at the fifth seam and was told, in thirteen words, that it is not and he will not say what it is about.** And at about half past six a man of the third lay said a boat's name out loud in the street — *Hearthwake*, working, with people in her, off the outer marks since before the ninth of July, one of the nine, the tide gang walking past her daily and told nothing — **and the yard wrote it down where anybody can check it, which is a page in a book in a shed on a hill, and did not go, and sent nobody, and asked that man no question about her, and said out loud the fourth thing it entered on its own account on the fifth of July, word for word: the outer marks stand where the writ says they are and the party is not going past them this season and has not tried and is not going to, and that is not nothing and it is not enough and both of those are on the page.**

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page: NOTHING in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, and nothing in a tin, on Wednesday the twenty-sixth of July — against FOUR marks in the same bag on Thursday the sixth.** The chain is **4, 2, 6, 2, 7, 1, 4, 0** across the seven days money moves. A pot of air at the yard's own two-mark rate goes out on the seventh and another on the eighteenth. A day at the second seam on a hirer's own account brings four in on the tenth. A day on **Edric Pringle's own account** brings five in on the seventeenth — gross seventeen, the eighth two, the share two, victuals two, two hired hands at three apiece, twelve out of seventeen — **and that is two marks less than the same day of work brought on the twenty-fourth of June, and the two figures are written side by side on the mole's steps in front of everybody without saying which one the yard hoped for.** A day on a hirer's own account, nobody in the water, brings three in on the twenty-first. **Three wage weeks fall in the batch — the eleventh, eighteenth and twenty-fifth of July — and each is four marks out of the bag into an envelope with the week written on it in Calder Marr's own left hand, because there is nobody else in that shed who writes, and the envelopes are in the bench chest and not among the five rolled in oilcloth, and there are nine of them now and not one of them has been anywhere, and the reason is on the page and it is the same reason it has been since the second of May: a wage is not a reason to go to a door.** The column goes **twelve lines and forty-eight marks – thirteen and fifty-two – fourteen and fifty-six**, with the fifth of March the oldest and staying there. The note is not re-written, its three counts of weeks do not move, five marks of compressor's gland stand on it unpaid and useless, and the tin is empty. **The whole of the money that has come into this yard since the twenty-fourth of June is twenty-two marks, and the eight marks that were in the bag on the fifteenth of June went out in June as three weeks of a wage and a pot of air, and this month four weeks of a wage and two pots of air went out, and there is nothing in the bag, and no office in this harbor has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May and none is going to.** A shilling changed hands at the foot of the dock on the twenty-second for the rival's printed sheet and it is not this yard's income and is not in the bag and will not be.

### The active pressures going into Volume 03 Movement 5 — SUPERSEDED, RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0004 RECORD

1. **THE CLIMAX IS OWED AND IT IS NOW DATED IN THREE PLACES. The *Vigil* moves on the crew while Calder Marr is trying to raise the *Hearthwake*; he saves the vessel's living crew and loses the chance to carry her full bell-archive to the surface; the two are the same choice and neither cancels the other. A boat's name is in a market in this harbor, in a shed book, and the outer marks and the bar of four hundred yards off them and the writ's first of March and a Crown attachment on a market wall are three things a writer can lean on. THIS BATCH DID NOT BEGIN IT, DID NOT PUT THE *VIGIL* IN THE WATER, AND PUT NO PERSON, A BOAT OR A LEAD OF THIS YARD PAST THE OUTER MARKS.**
2. **THE PUBLIC FACT CANNOT BE MADE PRIVATE AGAIN.** The yard's own fourth return is on a market wall with no price on it; a printer in a city was told three lines in advance in the ordinary post and cannot be stopped; a rival says out loud that he will be standing in front of it on Monday with a better sheet. **The only advantage this yard ever had, which was a thing nobody else knew, is gone, and it is gone because the party published it on purpose and because a man of fifty told it that a wall with nobody able to be asked a question about it is a wall worth nothing.**
3. **A FINDING THAT CANNOT BE READ.** There is a chip of worked glass in a packing four fathoms under a mole, put there on purpose by somebody who expected somebody to come, and the party has declared on the page that nobody in this harbor can read it and that the trade which can costs six marks and two days and none of them has six marks. **It is the volume's unsent witness and no chapter of Volume 03 has read it.**
4. **Six joints, nine places, three hundred and fifty-one feet, three thicknesses of fouling and three places with no joint, and a figure nobody can name.** The formation is legible. What it means is not. **The party has refused the word, refused the name, and refused to join the account about the sea to the finding about the glass.**
5. **A man who read one seam and got it wrong in a market.** The Faultreader stage is taken and it is spent in the only way the volume's argument allows: **it identified difference and not truth, he supplied the truth from his own head, and the fouling proved him wrong in three parts out of four in front of eleven people and a hired diver of another boat and a woman who paid for the day.** Whether he reads another one, and whether a second one is better, is not owed by this batch.
6. **A condition that has a name in it now, forever.** Five entries in five months, four nameless and one carrying the word *a tide-sickness*, in a book a clerk and a court could one day ask for. **He broke his own line to do it and gave the reason, and the reason is that it can be argued with.**
7. **A conservator who is not on the crew and is not in the Registry's eyes.** She said no in her own words before anybody asked. She took her calipers. She did not leave the harbor and is to be found on a Friday morning in a room open to anybody, and the party did not arrange that. **Her forty marks are unpaid and she put the figure of nine months on the page herself.**
8. **A reeve who has spent twice and a third lay that has lost four days of the best of its season to an examination with no money behind it.** Neither man got what he wanted and the plate is still in eleven feet seen and not measured and not touched, and the plate has been referred to twice in the whole volume, the second time in thirteen words.
9. **A girl of twenty-two's June, forty marks of claim standing whole, a third column this yard intends to keep and cannot yet fill and has now priced at four marks a week, and nine envelopes on a bench and not one of them anywhere.** None of it is paid, reduced, repaired or resolved, and this batch may not and did not.
10. **A cistern that has held since the nineteenth of June and a third lay that is closed four days, and a mole-head light that is not on, and a hard at Ashtide unanswered since the ninth of November, and nineteen winters in a naval file, and a harbor that is worse off on the twenty-sixth of July than it was on the sixth and is not going to be made better by anything.**
11. **A man of fifty-four with a different figure for the bar, coming in the autumn for nothing, with no instrument in this harbor that can call him and none that can stop him.** And a question out of a city, three quarters answered, one not, and a sheet going to press in a city in July that will be wrong about one thing this yard cannot do one single thing about.

### The relationship pressure — SUPERSEDED, RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0004 RECORD

**Calder and Mara are two people who have disagreed in public about whether to publish a dangerous witness, and she is off the crew, and the disagreement is not repaired and is not settled by him giving her the evidence.** He gave her all of it — the day-book, the weir, the poor-book, the step book, the nine places, three hundred and fifty-one feet — and she called it the best thing anybody has found out in this harbor in five months and then said it was worth nothing to her, because she is not being paid for it. **She is owed forty marks, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, not reachable by the writ, not an assistant, not asked to be useful, and not one mark of it paid out of anything that has come into this yard since the eighth of May, and she put the figure of nine months on the page in her own hand and asked for it to be said out loud.** She did not break with the Registry and no chapter fuses the two; that is Volume 4 and a different event. She did not leave this harbor and is in Chapter 140. He learned the thing in his own words on a mole's steps: **a conservator I cannot pay is a conservator I have to keep asking, and asking her to be useful is asking her to be cheap.** And she is the reason a man who cannot pay her at the rate she is owed is a man who has to keep asking, and that is the arrangement now.

**Calder and Bevin are unchanged and the batch did not touch him.** He is in no chapter of it. The three nots are recited in four chapters. Forty marks standing whole. A wage going into an envelope. Nine envelopes on a bench.

**Calder and Kest are at the end and a step past it.** The reeve has now entered two things in his own book in five months that a person in this harbor can use and one of them cost a third lay four days with no money behind it, and the clerk on the steps of the fourteenth of July said out loud that the only reason nobody can dispute this finding is a reeve who sold a herring skipper's winter for a chart, and that in four years that will be the only reason anybody could not take the thing out of that mole, **and it is a reeve's price and not a party's.** The plate was asked about on the mole's steps on the twenty-sixth and answered in thirteen words and the party entered that it does not know what the morning's entry was about and has not asked.

**Calder and Nessa are at the point where she is right six times in twenty days and the sixth one was a word about his ear that she had to read back.** She wrote the count of four in the first column with no name on it. She priced a hand and said the reader thing once in a sentence that meant the same thing. She won the argument about the word *joint* with a better rule than his. She did not stop a man from saying a false thing in a market and gave the reason she did not stop him. She read a woman's sentence back and did not improve on it. And she made the market office door into a standing arrangement and entered that the party did not arrange it.

**Calder and Alden do not move.** The far bench on three days across two chapters, not asked for anything, asking nothing, neither brother going over to him, one first-column line that either brother can check by asking the other.

### The power state — SUPERSEDED BY THE CHAPTER 150 SECTION ABOVE, RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0004 RECORD

**TAKEN, IN THIS MOVEMENT, LATE, BADLY AND AT A PRICE: THE FAULTREADER STAGE, AND NO OTHER. No Bellwright, Keelwright, Storm-Reader, Chorus Diver or Openhand stage is taken and none may be.**

**What it gained, in one sentence and no more: the perception that two cuts in one joint can be of different ages, and that the difference between a cut made once and a cut made on top of a cut that was there first comes into a hand without a picture and without a sound.** It is not a rank, it was conferred by nobody, it is not named as a stage on the page, and it puts no crew behind a man.

**What it cost, all three, on the page:** **concentration a paying job needs** — a day bought for seventeen at a woman's own rate and half of it done, and the boat off that face two hours and a quarter before the flood, because the man in the water could not say whether the basket had gone up four times or three and he said three and she said four; **conflicting aftertaste** — a bad day on all three days he was in the water in company, and ordinary talk in a shed going on as more than one talk at once, and an answer given to a question a quarter of a second after the answer that answered it; **and the fact that a seam identifies difference and not truth** — it told him there were mendings under mendings and told him nothing about when, in what order or by whose hand, and he supplied all three and said them out loud in a market in about eleven people's hearing.

**THE ONE NAMING OF THE CONDITION WAS SPENT IN CHAPTER 137, ON THURSDAY THE TWENTIETH OF JULY, IN THE YARD'S OWN BOOK, IN THE HOLDER'S OWN LEFT HAND, IN THE SECOND COLUMN, WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE, AND IT IS NOT AVAILABLE AGAIN IN THIS VOLUME OR IN ANY OTHER.** The four lines above it are still nameless and are not struck out and a request to strike one out is refused. The count of four is in the first column in a shipwright's hand with no name against it.

**And he is worse at it afterwards than he was before, which is what the volume promised.** He goes in the water five times in this batch, inside limits of nineteen, seventeen and seventeen minutes that he set himself at the boat and that nobody raised, with a seated named weight, an independent line and a cutoff that is hers and the word is *Enough*, and **he enters nothing.**

### The bodies and the dated positions at the end of Chapter 140 — SUPERSEDED, RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0004 RECORD

- **No air in this harbor except two pots bought this month at this yard's own two-mark rate and carried on this office's own hose, no delivery hose, the longest thing of hose in it about nine fathoms, and a compressor's gland fitted to the yard's own pump since the nineteenth of May, unpaid, on a creditor's page at five marks, and of no use whatever because the thing after the gland is the hose.** The hand lead's line made in the net loft on the second of May was used five times in this batch, on the eighth, the tenth, the seventeenth, the eighteenth and the twenty-first.
- **The ground of every day of work this month is entered in the first column, and all of it is the second seam's own face landward of the third seam's mark, and the ground of the third, fourth, fifth and sixth joints is landward of the mark, and a man in a city can check every one of those days against a notice and the notice does not reach them, and the yard has said so out loud four times this month and is not going to stop saying it.** The ninth seam's mooring is out of service and has an end of it entered against it, the first of September. **The footing of this mole from the third seam to the twelfth is entered for examination on the second of August, at the cost of an office with an empty repair account, closing the third lay's moorings for four days, and the price lands on the third lay and was said out loud in a market in forty people and is not going to fall on this party.** The north berth is out. The third lay is full. The tide gang is four men short. **The harbor authority's cistern was charged on the tenth, the thirteenth, the twentieth and the twenty-fourth — the ninth, tenth, eleventh and twelfth walks — at no figure, by people this office cannot pay, and NOT on Monday the seventeenth, and the reason is on the page in the yard's own hand, and it is that the gate takes four hours with three men and the same three men were on the second seam's face from seven in the morning until about two.** It has not gone dry since the nineteenth of June. The mole-head light is not on. The repair account is empty. The harbor authority has had no boat of its own since the evening of the twenty-eighth of April. The *Kittiwake* is on the hard with no cradle. The hard at Ashtide is unanswered. Nineteen winters are in a naval file and in four lines of a letter.

- **The bell is on the harbor's own ground in eight and a half feet off the south end**, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame at all and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was**, a new rope bought on the eleventh of January, **a going out on the second of May that is done and is spent and was not repeated and is not going to be repeated, and eight marks and a day of a boat owing to that man since the twenty-third of May and unpaid and unargued and not paid in this batch, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May standing unreversed.** **ITS NOTATION IS IN A NAVAL FILE AND IS NOT ON A WALL IN THIS HARBOR AND CANNOT BE TAKEN DOWN.**
- **The lead plug. Thumb and a half, a piece of chain, a clean half-inch drilled hole, eleven inches under a bell in eight and a half feet, taken out on the twenty-seventh of February, described four times by four men that do not agree, in a chest in a shed with a lock the yard owns, with a boy's writing on the lid. IT WAS ASKED IN A ROOM ON SUNDAY THE SECOND OF JULY AND ANSWERED ONCE, AND NO STROKE OF IT IS IN ANY BOOK IN THIS HARBOR, AND THE YARD HAS UNDERTAKEN IN ITS OWN BOOK NOT TO ASK AGAIN, AND THAT UNDERTAKING BINDS THE YARD AND NOT THE OFFICE AND NOT THE MARKET AND NOT THE BOY. HE WAS NOT ASKED AGAIN IN BATCH 0004 AND IS NOT IN IT, AND THE CHEST WAS NOT OPENED AND WAS NOT BROUGHT DOWN THE HILL, AND NO DIGIT OF WHAT HE SAID IS PRINTED IN ANY CHAPTER OF IT.**
- **The delayed note: eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, unchanged. Two occasions logged with no call in them, unchanged. NO OCCASION IS ENTERED IN BATCH 0004, and the mole head's handbell went on the third of July and nothing was taken and nothing was written down as a figure.**
- **Two things of nobody's are still living in the open air on the mole's crown above the third seam's mark, about nine feet apart, under two tarpaulins, weighted with a fender and forty feet of the harbor authority's own mooring rope lent at nothing and entered at nothing, and one of the tarpaulins has had a corner off it since the seventeenth of June and has not been lashed back because lashing it back takes three men and a morning. NEITHER OBJECT HAS MOVED. The lump of green glass and the shard of a foot and a half are nine feet above the whole of what this month was about, and the party found that out on the ninth of July and entered it as a fact about the party and not as anything else.**
- **Six joints four fathoms under that stone, nine fastenings in about seven feet of each, three thicknesses of fouling, a chip of worked glass in the packing on purpose, and a chain that is not called a chain of three hundred and fifty-one feet.**

- **The coast road, the worked panel in a master's keeping, the conservator's earlier unsigned finding, the run under this town, the compartment, the custody items and the first echo's witness are all carried forward and all unresolvable inside this volume. And the coast road's figures are printed in a market and going to press in a city, and the man of fifty-four with a different figure is still coming in the autumn.**
- **The concealment is hidden and the batch closes with it hidden.** Nobody asked the boy, nobody asked the conservator, and the boy is in no chapter of it and is not to be written to.
- **Captain Holt's posting after her defeat is EXPLICITLY STILL OPEN AND IS RECORDED AS STILL OPEN IN `state/open-threads.md`.** Her defeat is in Movement 5. **What Chapters 130 and 140 foreclose on the page, in the yard's own hand, is what a posting may not be, and the party put the same sentence in a market on the twenty-sixth of July when a man gave it a boat's name. THE CHANNEL IS THE WRIT AND WAS RULED ON THE FIFTH OF JULY AND APPLIED ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH.**

## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 130 — SUPERSEDED BY THE CHAPTER 140 SECTION ABOVE, AND RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0003 RECORD

**VOLUME 03 MOVEMENT 3 IS WRITTEN.** In twenty-one days a yard of two and a half men made a third return on the day, went out on a mole in a north-easterly and could not say in which of two directions a man's voice had come from, and wrote it down in the second column with a reason against it, for the third time in five months, with nobody asking; got its own boat back into the water off a stone crown; worked two days at the second seam and could not afford a third; said out loud on a mole's steps that it could neither produce the harbor authority's chart nor refuse it for anybody, because the chart is not the yard's; read the eleven names of its own public record out loud on a mole in nine people's hearing and would not ask the four it has never reached; watched a reeve sell a chart and pay nineteen winters of a herring skipper's dues for it in a room with a clerk in it, and said nothing, and wrote down that it had said nothing; let a man from Ashtide nail a printed sheet over its own on the market wall, and put the whole of the reason out loud on a mole's steps in nine people: that a slip with eleven names on it had been on that wall since the third of May for a shilling and was the only reason a stranger could print a sheet anybody could write to; heard a woman say in a market that she was going to take her own rope to a man with a compressor; heard a coast office say, for the first time since the first of March, that it does not moor a fleet, and that it has taken three lines out of this harbor's own account of what it cannot moor and put them in a list; answered three quarters of a stranger's question out of its own book and refused, in writing and in public, to make itself able to answer the fourth; asked a man of fifty one question in a room in a boat, exactly as he said he would be asked, and did not write down one stroke of what he said; and declined to put a sheet on a wall it could have put a sheet on.

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page: FOUR marks in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, and nothing in a tin, on Thursday the sixth of July — against eight marks in the same bag on Thursday the fifteenth of June.** The chain is **8, 6, 2, 9, 5, 8, 4.** A pot of air at the yard's own two-mark rate goes out on the nineteenth. A day at the second seam on the *Anna Beth's* own account brings seven in on the twenty-fourth, and the yard's seven is three more than the four this yard costed the same day of work to on the mole's steps on the thirty-first of May, because the pot of air went out of the bag on the Friday and the ring, the shackle, the swivel and the four feet of warp all came on the ring and nothing else came off the day. A day at the second seam on a hirer's own account brings three in on the first of July. **Three wage weeks fall in the batch — the twentieth and twenty-seventh of June and the fourth of July — and each is four marks out of the bag into an envelope with the week written on it in Calder Marr's own left hand, because there is nobody else in that shed who writes, and the envelopes are in the bench chest and not among the five rolled in oilcloth, and there are six of them now and not one of them has been anywhere.** The column goes **eight lines and thirty-two marks – nine and thirty-six – ten and forty – eleven and forty-four, with the fifth of March the oldest and staying there.** The note is not re-written, its three counts of weeks do not move, and five marks of compressor's gland stand on it unpaid and useless. **The whole of the money that came into this yard in twenty-one days is ten marks, of which twelve went out as three weeks of a wage and two went out for a pot of air, and no office in this harbor has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May and none is going to.** A shipwright additionally bought a rival crew's printed sheet for four marks **out of her own hand**, and that four is not the yard's money and is not in any column and is not going to be repaid.

### The active pressures going into Volume 03 Movement 4 — SUPERSEDED, RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0003 RECORD

1. **A physical join, found by labor and not by an argument, and the faultreader stage taken late and badly.** Movement 4 is the volume's midpoint and it is owed both. **A place where a keel-line ran between two things that were never made to be joined, with a material, a repair, a fouling and a figure; a witness who was not coerced and a joiner who was; and a man reading a seam for the first time and getting it wrong in front of people who trusted him.** It is not the compartment, it is not the coast road, and it is not the shard of the tenth of June, which is on a mole's crown under a tarpaulin and is not going to be moved for the sake of a chapter.
2. **THE ONE NAMING OF THE CONDITION IS OWED TO MOVEMENT 4 AND IS NOT SPENT.** It is on the page four times as a bare fact with a reason against it and a count against the fourth, and for the first time in another person's hand, and no chapter of Movement 3 named it.
3. **A printed sheet in a market with a scale and a price and the yard's own figures and the yard's own eleven names on it, and four of the eleven have answered a stranger and two of those have stopped writing, and this yard did not ask which four.** The rival is not a villain, has no office and no following, and is not going to go away, and the market wall is now three deep.
4. **A woman with about four hundred feet of her own warp on the bottom of this harbor, who has said out loud that she is going to ask a man of Ashtide, and the yard has no standing with her and did not try, and the yard has also said out loud that the refusal of the seventh of June is the reason that man can do the job and the yard cannot.** And she has worked out about the thirty marks herself and told the yard so in a market.
5. **A question from a city, three quarters answered, one not, and the reason in the first line of the answer, and a printed sheet going to press in July that will be wrong about one thing and that this yard cannot do one single thing about and has refused to make itself able to.**
6. **A chart out of this harbor and a notation out of this harbor with it, and nineteen winters in a naval file, and a letter of four lines in a man's coat, and a list in a city being made up in the first week of July out of three lines this harbor wrote itself in May for nothing.**
7. **A number in a lead plug that was said out loud once in a room on Sunday the second of July and is written nowhere at all, and a yard that has undertaken in its own book not to ask about it again.** The undertaking binds the yard and not the office and not the market and not the boy.
8. **A ninth seam's mooring with an end of it entered at last, the first of September, in an office whose instruments are not being contested, and a cistern that has held since the nineteenth of June, and a wharfinger who has said out loud in a market that a cistern that holds is a hole with a lid on it and not a town.** The light is not on, the north berth is out, the third lay is full, the hard at Ashtide is unanswered and nineteen winters are in a file, and **none of it is fixed and none of it is going to be.**
9. **The autumn visit of a man of fifty-four with a different figure for the bar, who is coming for nothing, and no instrument in this harbor that can call him and none that can stop him.**
10. **A crew of two and a half, a third column filled five times since the second of May and read back by the man who wrote it, a shipwright who has said twice that a reader must be hired and has now said she will not say it a third time, and a first of July four weeks and a day gone.**
11. **Labor.** Four marks; thirty-one on the note; eleven lines and forty-four marks; six envelopes on a bench and none of them anywhere; forty marks of claim standing whole; forty marks to a conservator with no office, first after food, not one mark of it paid since the eighth of May; eight shillings and tenpence to each of two women; nineteen winters; eight marks and a day of a boat to a bellwright owing since the twenty-third of May; no air and no hose, and a gland fitted and unpaid, and a crew of Ashtide at the outer end of the second lay with a compressor of her own and forty fathoms of her own hose; the *Kittiwake* on the hard; no boat of this office's own since the twenty-eighth of April; the tide gang four men short; the fire party's hose dead; the repair account empty; eleven copies and a list of the eleven on a wall; Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found, one line in the book, and this yard has not gone.

### The relationship pressure — SUPERSEDED, RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0003 RECORD

**Calder and Mara are two people who have refused each other in public for five months and have worked, and she is in no room in this batch and has not been asked for anything.** She is owed forty marks, first after food, unpaid since the fifteenth of October, not reachable by the writ, not an assistant, not asked to be useful, and **not one mark of it has been paid out of any money that has come into this yard since the eighth of May.** **She is owed a scene in Movement 4 and the sentence that ends her time on the crew, in her own words and before anybody asks it**, and her break with the Registry is Volume 4 and is a different event and a chapter may not fuse the two. He has not asked her for anything since the twenty-seventh of January.

**Calder and Bevin are at the end of the closest and most dangerous thing in this book and the batch did not resolve it and may not.** The boy is not in the batch at all. The three nots stand. **The one use made of him in twenty-one days is that his sentence on the lid of a chest, written at twenty-two with a hand that was going, is the reason a man of fifty will not let a number be written down.** Forty marks of claim standing whole, a wage of four a week going into an envelope, six envelopes on a bench and none of them anywhere.

**The bad-wheel thread is now two men and it is a colder pressure than it was, and it is in his own trade's mouth.** They may never be merged: **Coll Ferrow, thirty-one, of the *Kestrel's Sister*, has a bad wheel in that boat and it is that boat's; and the man of fifty-one of the *Grey Widow* has a bad wheel of his own, is better on a lead than anybody in this yard, is not on one, has asked not to be told and has not been asked.** Neither of them is the man Chapter 127 is about. **That man is Harl Dace, forty-eight, master of the *Grey Widow*, whose own figure of the bar of gravel is the fourth question a man in a city asked and whom this yard refused to ask, and who has a slate and a name on the yard's own wall and no wheel of any kind in this harbor.** On Friday the thirtieth a shipwright said out loud in a shed that a printed sheet is wrong about one thing, and refused to say which, and would not let either of them write it down, and gave the reason: **writing a man's name next to a doubt is asking him about himself.** That is the first time the yard's rule about him has had a use that was not about the yard, **and the state files had that use attached to the wrong man of the two and it is corrected here and in `state/continuity.md`.**

**Calder and Nessa are at the point where she is right about four things in ten days and nobody thanks her for any of them and she has stopped needing to say so.** She made the officer read a date as a date. She found the eleven names on a stranger's sheet. She bought a printed sheet for four marks out of her own hand and will not be repaid. She wrote the fourth sound entry in her own hand and put no name against it and gave the reason. **And on Monday the twenty-sixth she refused to say a third time that a reader must be hired, and said that a thing said three times in a shed is a complaint and she does not make complaints, she makes entries.**

**Calder and Kest are at the end of the pattern and the reeve's spending is done and what he bought is named.** He entered his ninth thing in a ledger in his own hand and it is the first one he ever bought, and the price was somebody else's winter, and the man it was paid out of was one day at sea. **The yard was in the room, produced everything, was asked for nothing, entered nothing, and has said out loud that it is not a party to the paper, and there is a first-column line saying so and a second-column line from the shipwright saying she does not know what it makes him.** The plate was referred to once, in nine words, and not reopened.

**Calder and Alden do not move.** The far bench, in two chapters of this batch, not asked for anything and asking nothing, and a single first-column line that either brother can check by asking the other.

### The power state — SUPERSEDED, RETAINED AS THE BATCH 0003 RECORD

**Unchanged in rank.** Line Diver, with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice and not used since the eighth of December, and the lift at the end of a childhood call gone and not restorable. **No dive of his own in this batch, no entry of anything, and no Bellwright, Keelwright, Storm-Reader, Chorus Diver or Openhand stage is taken and none may be. THE CONDITION IS NOT NAMED IN THIS BATCH.** **It is on the page as the bare fact twice, in the second column, with a reason against each line, no cause against either, no name in either, and nobody asked — and with a count of four entered against the fourth.** **On Monday the third of July, for the first time in five months, he said it out loud to a person, and a shipwright wrote it in her own hand, and he did not tell her what to write, and she put no name against the line and said why: the day a man of this yard writes that he could not tell which of two sounds it was with his name on it, in about two years there is a page in that book that says what a man of this yard can and cannot hear, and that page will be read by somebody in Veyr who is looking for exactly that.** **THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 03 IS ALLOWED IS THEREFORE NOT SPENT AND IS OWED TO MOVEMENT 4, WHERE THE VOLUME GETS A SEAM WRONG IN FRONT OF PEOPLE WHO TRUSTED HIM. NO CHAPTER OF MOVEMENT 4 MAY TREAT IT AS SPENT AND NO CHAPTER MAY LET IT VANISH SILENTLY: IT IS ON THE PAGE FOUR TIMES, IT HAS A COUNT, AND THE FOURTH OF THOSE IS IN ANOTHER PERSON'S HAND.** What the batch paid is method: a repeat of four casts against a repeat of four casts five weeks earlier that agreed and was entered as a fact and not a proof, six conditions read out in order at a boat with a named tender who is not a diver and a cutoff that is hers, a stated reason entered in the yard's own book with the clerk's absence entered rather than filled in, a third column filled five times with the lateness entered each time and a day-and-a-half lateness entered as the longest and not made into a practice, **and one decision not to write down a figure a yard cannot check, which is the first time in this volume that the yard has had a number in its hand and refused it.**

### The bodies and the dated positions at the end of Chapter 130

- **No air in this harbor except a pot bought on the nineteenth of June, no delivery hose, and a compressor's gland fitted to the yard's own pump since the nineteenth of May, unpaid, on a creditor's page at five marks, and of no use whatever because the thing after the gland is the hose.** The hand lead's line made in the net loft on the second of May is right, was said to be right in about nine words on the twenty-fourth of May, and has been used four times since the second of June — on the second of June, the tenth, the twenty-fourth and the first of July — of which three are in this batch. **Nothing was cast with it on the third of July.**
- **The ninth seam's mooring is out of service and, for the first time since the first of May, HAS AN END ENTERED AGAINST IT: THE FIRST OF SEPTEMBER, entered in the harbor authority's own book on Monday the third of July, by the reeve's own hand, and entered because that office's instruments are not being contested.** It is the first thing that office has entered in five months that a person in this harbor can use. **The notice of the thirty-first of October is not lifted, not narrowed and not altered, and no acknowledgement was entered for a mooring, and no person, boat or lead of this yard went inside the first clause on any day of this batch.** The north berth is out. The third lay is full. **The tide gang is four men short and Petch's stand-off ended on the tenth of May and the mole steps are not going to be cleared in June. The harbor authority's cistern was charged on Monday the nineteenth, Monday the twenty-sixth, Thursday the twenty-ninth, Monday the third and Thursday the sixth — eight times in three weeks, at no figure, by people this office cannot pay, and NOT on Thursday the twenty-second, and the reason is on the page — and it has not gone dry since the nineteenth of June, and the harbor authority has had no boat of its own since the evening of the twenty-eighth of April.** The mole-head light is not on and will not be. The repair account is empty since the middle of February and is not going to be filled. The *Kittiwake* is on the hard with no cradle. The hard at Ashtide is unanswered. Nineteen winters are in a naval file and in four lines of a letter.
- **A wage at four marks a week: the twentieth and twenty-seventh of June and the fourth of July PAID, in the holder's own left hand, into six envelopes on a bench. The column is eleven lines and forty-four marks with the fifth of March the oldest. The forty marks of the claim stand whole.**
- **The bell is on the harbor's own ground in eight and a half feet off the south end**, fouled, a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame at all and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was**, a new rope bought on the eleventh of January, a delay of a hand's width of a second that has not moved, **a going out on the second of May that is done and is spent and was not repeated, and eight marks and a day of a boat owing to that man since the twenty-third of May and unpaid and unargued and not paid in this batch, and the standing sentence said in a street on the third of May standing unreversed.** **ITS GROUND GEAR AND MOORING CARRY, IN A REEVE'S OWN HAND OF THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF DECEMBER, A NOTATION THAT ANY PERSON PROPOSING TO WORK, RECOVER, LIFT OR MOVE THAT BELL IS TO BE GIVEN THE ACCOUNT OF THE HERRING FLEET'S DUES FIRST, AND THAT NOTATION WENT OUT OF THIS HARBOR ON THURSDAY THE TWENTY-SECOND OF JUNE ON THE CHART, AND THERE IS NO SECOND COPY OF IT BECAUSE THE NOTATION IS ON THE CHART AND THE CHART IS THE PAPER. THE BELL READS NOTHING, SETTLES NOTHING AND SAYS NO WORD, AND IT IS NOT IN A BOAT IN THIS BATCH.**
- **The lead plug. Thumb and a half, a piece of chain, a clean half-inch drilled hole, eleven inches under a bell at eight and a half feet, taken out on the twenty-seventh of February, carried four months, put on a bench on the first of May on purpose, described four times by four men that do not agree, in a chest in a shed with a lock the yard owns, with a boy's writing on the lid saying there is nothing in it that anybody in this harbor can check. IT WAS ASKED IN A ROOM ON SUNDAY THE SECOND OF JULY AND ANSWERED ONCE, AND NO STROKE OF IT IS IN ANY BOOK IN THIS HARBOR, AND THE YARD HAS UNDERTAKEN IN ITS OWN BOOK NOT TO ASK AGAIN, AND THAT UNDERTAKING BINDS THE YARD AND NOT THE OFFICE AND NOT THE MARKET AND NOT THE BOY. THE CHEST WAS NOT OPENED.**
- **The delayed note: eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, unchanged. Two occasions logged with no call in them, unchanged. NO OCCASION IS ENTERED IN THIS BATCH**, and on Monday the third of July the mole head's handbell went off the crown at about eleven and a person of this party could not say in which of two directions its answer came, and it was not taken and it was not written down as a figure.
- **Two things of nobody's are living in the open air on the mole's crown above the third seam's mark, about nine feet apart, under two tarpaulins, weighted since the twelfth of June with a fender and forty feet of the harbor authority's own mooring rope lent at nothing and entered at nothing, and the tarpaulins were put back on on the seventeenth of June and neither object has moved since the tenth.** One is a lump of green glass about a hand's length with eleven scallops in about four inches, wetter than it was on the sixth of May, worth nothing to anybody the party has been able to find out about, and the subject of the second return made on the tenth. The other is a shard of about a foot and a half with nine scallops in about three inches and a mark on one face that no person in that boat identified and that this party is not going to identify. **Neither is in any office's book, neither carries a seal, neither has a number anywhere, and neither is connected to the other or to anything else, and the number of scallops is not being counted.**
- **The crew's book has three columns**, the third created on the second of May, filled on the eighteenth and the twenty-sixth of June and the fourth of July with the lateness entered each time, with nobody in the yard who will read a position back, **and a line written at the head of the third column on the second of July with nothing under it.**
- **The coast road, the worked panel in a master's keeping, the conservator's unsigned finding, the run under this town, the compartment, the custody items and the first echo's witness** are all carried forward and all unresolvable inside this movement. **And the coast road's figures are now printed in a market in this harbor and are going to press in a city in July, and the man of fifty-four with a different figure is still coming in the autumn.**
- **The concealment is hidden and the batch closes with it hidden.** Nobody asked the boy and nobody asked the conservator, and the yard's refusal to make itself able to answer a question about a lead line and a slate is on the page and is a refusal about a lead line and a slate.

## Volume 04, Batch 0001 summary (Chapters 151–160) — Movement 1, the notice and the seat. **THE BATCH MIDPOINT AND THE BATCH CLIMAX ARE PAID.**

**31,731 words across ten chapters as re-measured after this batch's review repairs, and 31,019 before them**, measured with `wc -w` including headings (Ch151 3,074 · Ch152 3,582 · Ch153 2,690 · Ch154 2,806 · Ch155 4,666 · Ch156 3,164 · Ch157 2,669 · Ch158 2,995 · Ch159 2,932 · Ch160 3,153), against the 2,200–3,200 guideline. **Every chapter is above the floor. Seven are inside the guideline and three are past it — Chapter 152 by 382 and Chapter 155 by 1,466 — and Chapter 155 is one complete market office and one complete market wall in one morning with the read-out in the middle of it, which is the one place in this book where a figure is worth anything and the Volume 03 audit's ruling is that it is allowed to run long. Chapter 152 is one complete morning in a doorway and one complete evening in a shed.**

**THE READ-OUT IS THE DEVICE AND IT IS USED ONCE IN THIS BATCH**, on Friday the twenty-fifth of August at about eleven in the morning, in about forty people's hearing, at a market wall: the holder reads a **sheet** of his own out twice, gets one of the six builder's figures wrong the second time, corrects himself off the sheet in his hand without going back to the top, and the correction takes about a second and a half and nobody in that market says anything about it, and the yard enters it against itself in the second column with a reason and **without a name of any kind in it.** **The two-column head-of-dock read-out is NOT used anywhere in this batch**, and that is deliberate and is the batch's own rule: the sheet is the instrument, and the yard has spent five months learning the difference between a book a party keeps and a record an office keeps. **AND THE READ-BACK IN THE SHED IS NOT THE READ-OUT, WHICH A REVIEWER PASS HAD TO CORRECT IN THIS FILE AND IN THE OTHER THREE: the ship's hand reading an entry back and the holder reading it back after her, nobody else in the shed, at nine at night, is this yard's standing practice since the second of May and it runs in eight of the ten chapters — 151, 152, 153, 155, 156, 158, 159 and 160 — and Chapter 157 opens with a read-out of the yard's own book in a shed. None of that spends the device.**

**The hearing room is used ONCE, on Saturday the ninth of September. The market wall is published on TWICE, on Wednesday the sixteenth and Friday the twenty-fifth. The two are never the same day, and this is verified twice in the batch.** No day is in a chapter twice and no cost is paid twice.

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page, and a reviewer pass found that it did not: NOTHING in a bag on the morning of Wednesday the sixteenth of August, and A MARK AND TWELVE SHILLINGS AND FOURPENCE in it on the evening of Sunday the tenth of September. A mark is a hundred and sixty pence and a shilling is twelve, so a shilling out of one mark leaves a hundred and forty-eight pence and not nineteen of them.** The chain is **nothing · 1 · 1 · 12s 4d · 5m 12s 4d · 1m 12s 4d** across the days money moves: a day at the second seam on a hirer's own account on Monday the twenty-first, gross seventeen and the yard's five, and the wage of four out of it on Tuesday the twenty-second, which leaves one; **seven pence of postage on the letter of Sunday the twentieth, out of the shipwright's own pocket and not out of the bag, because the bag held nothing, and owed by this yard to her and unpaid**; a shilling at the press on Friday the twenty-fifth for forty copies, which leaves twelve shillings and fourpence; a day on the mole's toe inside the first clause on Monday the twenty-eighth, gross seventeen and the yard's five, which makes five marks and twelve shillings and fourpence; **the wage of four out of that on Tuesday the twenty-ninth, which is a line in the yard's own book and not a scene and is read out as a figure on the Friday morning in Chapter 157;** and nothing after that. **The wage column is seventeen lines and sixty-eight marks after the twenty-second of August and eighteen lines and seventy-two marks after the twenty-ninth, with the fifth of March the oldest and staying there. No wage was paid twice, no wage was paid on a day that is not a Tuesday, and the three marks owing to a man of Ashtide have not moved a single mark since the eighth of August.**

**The batch's pressures alternate:** a market wall with nothing on it and a man in a boat at seven in the morning; a form in a doorway and a letter to a city; a day of ordinary work and a Tuesday wage and a debt entered unpaid on purpose; a stranger with a figure of his own and a wall that tells a man where the copies are; a press, a sheet, a public read-out and an error made in public by the man who published it; a reeve's last entry in the open air and a day of a man of the first lay; a mooring two strangers fixed by reading a date off a wall; a man on the steps of a market office for eighty minutes; a whole book on a table and not one line read out of it; and a notice served on a mole's steps that hands an office the party's own clause.

## Volume 03, Batch 0005 summary (Chapters 141–150) — Movement 5, the cutter and the bell-archive. **THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX AND ITS RESOLUTION.**

****29,814 words across ten chapters, after this batch's review repairs, and 29,688 before them** (Ch141 2,666 · Ch142 2,615 · Ch143 2,743 · Ch144 2,704 · Ch145 2,363 · Ch146 2,795 · Ch147 3,640 · Ch148 2,999 · Ch149 3,213 · Ch150 4,085), measured with `wc -w` including headings, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline. **Every chapter is above the floor. Seven are inside the guideline and three are past it — Chapter 147 by 440, Chapter 149 by 13 and Chapter 150 by 885 — and all three ran because a cost could not be dropped.** 147 is the climax: the six conditions, the approach, three calls down, the case half out of the water, the boy in seven fathoms, the choice, nineteen minutes in the water, four people off, and the second-column entry that puts the price of the choice in the yard's own book. 150 is the volume's close and carries the market wall, the sheet, the wage that is not paid, the third column, the chandler's note said out loud as un-re-written, and the whole labor spine of the batch. **The hearing room was used once, on Saturday the twelfth of August. The market wall was used once, on Tuesday the fifteenth of August, in daylight, in a market, on no Monday and on no hearing-room day; the *Vigil's* own two notices are on that wall and neither is this party's use of it.**

**THE BATCH'S TEN CHAPTERS IN ONE PARAGRAPH: a day of work and a Crown notice nailed over the yard's own return; a mole with a glass, a tide-gang man's nine days of watching, and the arithmetic of ten marks the yard does not have; a wage, a boy's failure in the yard's own hand, and a public sentence about being quiet; a grounding in four minutes and a grapnel; a market office on a Friday morning, six things and no seventh, and a second nail; a reeve's last entry and a cost entered in advance before the day; the working day itself, in which a man had five bells half out of the water and let go of the line; four people of the Sill on a mole's steps with a limit they wrote themselves; a man in a coat in the hearing room with a posting and a question in a market that nobody answers; and the close, on a Tuesday morning, with a page under a naval paper and a wage not paid.**

**THE MONEY, CHECKABLE ON THE PAGE: NOTHING in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, nothing in a tin, three marks owing to a man of Ashtide, on Tuesday the fifteenth of August — against nothing in the same bag on Wednesday the twenty-sixth of July.** The chain is **0, 3, 3, 7, 3, 7, 7, 11, 0, 0.** Four days of work at about three or four marks, all of it out of a week that was not working. On Tuesday the eighth: the wage, four marks; two pots of the office's air, four marks; the *Sallow Reach* at six marks named out loud, three paid at the mole and three not — **which empties the bag exactly.** Three wage weeks fall in the batch: the first and the eighth were paid into envelopes; **the fifteenth was NOT PAID and the envelope was not made, and that is the batch's cost and it is in the first column with the hour and the figure against it.** The column goes **fifteen lines and sixty marks – sixteen lines and sixty-four marks** and stops, with the fifth of March the oldest, and eleven envelopes on a bench and not one of them anywhere in twelve weeks.

**FOUR THINGS THIS BATCH DECIDED, EACH FLAGGED AND DISPOSED OF IN `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0005 as written* § 8: the bell-archive and its loss; Captain Holt's defeat and posting with their dates, which came by the writ; how the outer marks were handled, which was by breaking the yard's own sentence and entering the breaking in advance; and the chandler's note, which is NOT re-written and was said out loud in the yard's own hand in nine people's hearing on the fifteenth of August, with its three counts of weeks unmoved and the refusal of the thirty-first of March unreversed.** Full accounting: `state/chapter-summaries.md` § *Volume 03, Batch 0005 (Chapters 141–150)* and `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0005 as written (Chapters 141–150), Volume 03*. Thread-level: `state/open-threads.md` § *Volume 03, Batch 0005 (Chapters 141–150) — as written*.

## Volume 03, Batch 0004 summary (Chapters 131–140) — Movement 4, what the glass is joined to. THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT.

**Movement 4 is written and its turn has landed.** In twenty days a yard of two and a half men put a hand lead's line into a crevice four fathoms down on the second seam's own face and snagged it, worked it up by hand in a westerly, and came back four days later with a ring and brought up a lump the size of two fists and a chip of worked glass the length of a thumbnail out of the tallow of a lead; **made a fourth return on the day out of it, because the clause catches every finding of worked glass in four days and the day of the finding is not counted and the fourth day after the tenth of July is the fourteenth**; found a scarph between two ends cut to each other with nine fastenings in about seven feet at a hand's breadth apart, three chisel blows on every head and the same three blows on all nine, a packing that is not stone or oakum or lime or pitch pressed in from the seaward side over three inches of fouling, and a chip of the same worked glass put into the packing on purpose; **had a conservator of no office, in the harbor about a fortnight and unknown to anybody in this yard, prove six things about the join and refuse the seventh, which is who**; watched a man in the water read a seam for the first time in his life and then supply the age, the number and the hand of it out of his own head, and **say it out loud in a market in about eleven people's hearing, and be wrong in three parts out of four, and be disproved by fouling in two days by a bar laid flat and a scraper and a rule and two men's hands**; pay for that in a day bought for seventeen and half of it done, in a hired diver of another boat who went down because he was told to and is not doing it because of Tuesday, in a woman of the second lay who came up the hill to say the tide was there at seven and at two, and in the woman of this yard who is the reason there is a reader; **spend the one naming of the condition on the twentieth of July, in the second column, in his own left hand, with a reason, breaking his own line of the second of June, and the reason is that it can be argued with**; go into the market office for the first time in five months and find a builder's day-book with six places in it that are the six joints, a weir-book with a fisher's own storm in it, a poor-book with twenty-two children, and a tide-gang column that stops, and lay a string on the shed floor and get **three hundred and fifty-one feet and nine places of which six stand over a hole in a stone wall and three do not**; **decline the word, disown the word *chain*, and refuse in the second column to join the account about the sea to the finding about the glass**; **nail its own Crown attachment up on a market wall over a better printed sheet with no scale on it and no price on it, and tell a printer in a city three lines in advance in the ordinary post that it is coming and that he is to print what it says and not what it is about**; hear a woman of no office disagree with the holder in the open air in forty people's hearing and put her objection on the sheet unsigned and his answer beside it in nine words; **hear her say, before anybody asked her, that a yard that cannot pay her asks her whether she can be useful, and take that as the end of her time on this crew**; and be told, on a mole's steps, in his own words, that **a conservator he cannot pay is a conservator he has to keep asking and asking her to be useful is asking her to be cheap**; watch a reeve enter an examination of a mole's footing with no money behind it and close the third lay for four days, and hear the man who pays that price say the number out loud in a market and not ask for anything; **close the month with nothing in a bag and a wage column of fourteen lines and nine envelopes on a bench and not one of them anywhere**; and hear a man of the third lay say a boat's name out loud in the street — *Hearthwake*, working, with people in her, off the outer marks, one of the nine — **and write it down where anybody can check it, which is a page in a book in a shed on a hill, and not go, and not send anybody, and ask him no question, and say out loud that the outer marks stand where the writ says they are and the party is not going past them this season and has not tried and is not going to, and that that is not nothing and it is not enough and both of those are on the page.**

**Actual word count: 30,890 words across ten chapters** (Ch131 2,899 · Ch132 3,210 · Ch133 2,403 · Ch134 3,526 · Ch135 2,635 · Ch136 3,347 · Ch137 3,149 · Ch138 3,576 · Ch139 2,317 · Ch140 3,854), measured with `wc -w` including headings, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline. **Every chapter is above the floor. Five are inside the guideline and five are past it** — Chapter 140 by 654, 138 by 376, 134 by 300, 136 by 147, and Chapter 132 by 10 — **and every one of the five that ran ran because a cost could not be dropped; 132's ten words are the cost of a required arithmetic repair that struck the pot of air out of that day's account and had to write the reason on the page.** **The hearing room is used NOT AT ALL in this batch.** The market wall is used **once**, on **Saturday the twenty-second of July**, in daylight, in a market, on no Monday and on no hearing-room day, **and the sheet that goes up on it is the yard's own fourth return.** The batch's pressures alternate: a market and a figure, labor and a snag, a bench and a debt and a letter, an office and a return, a conservator, a working day and a public error, a disproof and a word in a book, a press and a wall, a sentence and a departure, a close and a forward pull.

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page: NOTHING in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, nothing in a tin, on Wednesday the twenty-sixth of July — against FOUR marks in the same bag on Thursday the sixth.** The chain is **4, 2, 6, 2, 7, 1, 4, 0** across the seven days money moves. Two pots of air at two marks apiece go out. Three wage weeks fall in the batch — the eleventh, eighteenth and twenty-fifth of July — and each is four marks out of the bag into an envelope, and the column goes **twelve lines and forty-eight marks – thirteen and fifty-two – fourteen and fifty-six**, with the fifth of March the oldest and staying there, and **nine envelopes on a bench and not one of them anywhere.** Three days' work come in: four on the tenth, five on the seventeenth on Edric Pringle's own account at gross seventeen, and three on the twenty-first with nobody in the water. The note is not re-written and its three counts of weeks do not move. **No money came in from any office in this batch and no money went out to any person except three wages and two pots of air, and a shilling changed hands at the foot of the dock for the rival's printed sheet, and that shilling is not this yard's income and is not in the bag and will not be.**

**Five things flagged rather than hidden, and two author-level decisions taken on the page.**

- **THE ONE NAMING OF THE CONDITION WAS SPENT, AND IT IS SPENT IN CHAPTER 137, ON THURSDAY THE TWENTIETH OF JULY, IN THE YARD'S OWN BOOK, IN THE HOLDER'S OWN LEFT HAND, IN THE SECOND COLUMN, WITH THE WORD *A TIDE-SICKNESS* AND WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE. IT IS NOT AVAILABLE AGAIN IN THIS VOLUME OR ANY OTHER, AND THE NEXT BATCH MAY NOT PUT THE WORD IN.**
- **THE BELLWRIGHT WAS NOT ASKED AGAIN. HE IS IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND WAS NOT IN A SHED, A BOAT, A MARKET, A MOLE OR A ROOM, NO DIGIT OF WHAT HE SAID ON THE SECOND OF JULY IS PRINTED IN ANY CHAPTER OF IT, AND THE CHEST WAS NOT OPENED AND WAS NOT BROUGHT DOWN THE HILL. This is not put forward as a virtue: he was not asked because a yard that has undertaken not to ask does not ask, and because the work did not need him.**
- **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, ON THE PAGE, BINDING — THERE ARE FOUR RETURNS AND NOT THREE.** `outline/volume-03.md` lock L5 enumerates three and says they are the only three. **The prompt that authorised this batch qualified that in its own first line and the chapters obey the qualifier and not the bare count: there is no fourth and a chapter may not produce one out of a day that produced nothing, AND THE TENTH OF JULY PRODUCED A FINDING OF WORKED GLASS, WHOLE OR IN PART, ON GROUND NO PARTY CLAIMS, CAUGHT BY THE FIRST LIMB OF THE CLAUSE BY ITSELF. THE CLAUSE'S OWN WORDS REQUIRE IT AND THE COUNTING RULE GIVES THE FOURTEENTH.** Recorded rather than argued; a Volume 03 audit should rule whether the lock's enumeration is superseded by the chapters. **There is no fifth and dated lines stand for the seventeenth, eighteenth, nineteenth, twentieth and twenty-fourth of July.**
- **AUTHOR-LEVEL DECISION, ON THE PAGE, BINDING — THE JOIN'S PHYSICAL FACTS** are set out in full in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0004 as written* § 8. **THE NINE FASTENINGS ARE NOT A MARK OF NINE JOINED LINES, THE NINE PLACES ARE NOT THE NINE HARBORS, AND NOTHING IN THE BATCH IS CALLED ANYTHING.**
- **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the nine places and the three with no joint,** set out in the same § 8. A later volume may place them; this volume may not, and nothing in this batch connects the nine to anything under this town or to a road or to a name.
- **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — what the glass carries.** A conservator of no office has said in a market and in a shed that she cannot read it, that the trade which can costs six marks and two days, and that none of them has six marks. **No reading occurs, no echo is entered, and no chapter of this batch certifies anything a thing carries.**
- **STILL OPEN, EXPLICITLY, AND NOT DECIDED IN THIS BATCH — CAPTAIN HOLT'S POSTING AFTER HER DEFEAT.** Her defeat is in Movement 5 and a posting cannot be decided before the thing it follows. **What Chapters 130 and 140 foreclose, in the yard's own hand, is what a posting may not be, and Chapter 140 puts the same sentence into a market when a man gives it a boat's name. THE CHANNEL IS THE WRIT, RULED ON THE PAGE ON WEDNESDAY THE FIFTH OF JULY, AND APPLIED ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH, AND NO SECOND CHANNEL MAY BE INVENTED.**

---

## Volume 03, Batch 0003 summary (Chapters 121–130) — Movement 3, the chart and the two men who want it

**Movement 3 is written and its turn has landed.** In twenty-one days a yard of two and a half men made a third return on the day and sent it up a coast in a sealed jacket with a copy for a ledger, and did not go and look at five miles of the coast it has published; said out loud on a mole's steps that it could neither produce the harbor authority's chart nor refuse it for anybody, and that the answer was not its to make; watched a reeve buy an uncontested instrument with nineteen winters of a herring skipper's dues and pay the price in a room with a clerk in it, and entered that it was in the room at the officer's request, produced everything, was asked for nothing, offered nothing and entered nothing; let a man from Ashtide nail a printed sheet over its own on a market wall and said out loud that the yard's own slip of eleven names, on that wall since the third of May for a shilling, was the only reason a stranger could print a sheet anybody could write to; **and said out loud, in a market, in forty people's hearing, that a refusal it had published on the seventh of June is the reason a crew with a compressor will be able to do a job it could have done for four marks on the tenth of June, and that the only reason it could not is that there was a boy who could hold a line and he is on a herring boat now.** It heard a coast office answer a fisher's document at last and put three of this harbor's own lines into a list. It answered three quarters of a stranger's question about a bar of gravel out of its own book and **refused, in writing and in public, to make itself able to answer the fourth, and entered the reason: a figure a yard has asked a man to stand behind is a figure the yard has published, and the day it does that it has made itself the man who takes a road off a chart, in its own hand, on its own wall.** It asked a man of fifty one question in a room in a boat, exactly as he said he would be asked, and **did not write down one stroke of what he said, and undertook in its own book never to ask again, and entered that he did not ask for money.** It declined to put a sheet on a wall it could have put a sheet on, and entered that this is the first thing it has written in four months about a thing it did not do.

**Actual word count: 33,731 words across ten chapters after the Batch 0003 review repairs, and 33,612 before them** (Ch121 3,945 · Ch122 3,175 · Ch123 3,842 · Ch124 3,502 · Ch125 3,193 · Ch126 2,886 · Ch127 3,317 · Ch128 3,262 · Ch129 3,129 · Ch130 3,487), measured with `wc -w` including headings, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline. **Every chapter is above the floor. Four are inside the guideline and six are past it** — Chapter 121 by 745, Chapter 123 by 635, Chapter 124 by 302, Chapter 130 by 287, Chapter 127 by 117 and Chapter 128 by 62 — **and every one of the four that ran ran because a cost could not be dropped: 121 is the third return in full, with the finding, both columns, the line of what it is not, the third bare-fact sound entry and a father at the far bench in the same shed; 123 is one hearing room that has to hold a writ, a crew's book, three returns, a paper and a price, and a chapter long because it is one complete room is a different fault from a chapter long because it is two; 124 is the turn and 130 is the close.** The hearing room is used **once**, on **Wednesday the twenty-first of June**, not a Monday, with a clerk in it, and no market wall on that day. The market wall is used **once**, on **Saturday the twenty-fourth of June**, in daylight, in a market, on no Monday and on no hearing-room day, **and the sheet that goes up on it belongs to another crew.** The batch's pressures alternate: a return and a clock, a market and an institution, a room, a rival, a woman and a wall, an office, a stranger's question, a private room, a reckoning, and a refusal to publish.

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page: FOUR marks in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, nothing in a tin, on Thursday the sixth of July — against EIGHT in the same bag on Thursday the fifteenth of June.** The chain is **8, 6, 2, 9, 5, 8, 4** across the seven days money moves. **A pot of air at the yard's own two-mark rate goes out on the nineteenth. A day at the second seam on the *Anna Beth's* own account brings seven in on the twenty-fourth — gross sixteen, nine out of it gone, the yard's seven, which is three more than the four the yard wrote on the mole's steps on the thirty-first of May for the same day of work, the difference being the pot that had gone out of the bag on the Friday and the rope this job did not need. A day at the second seam on a hirer's own account brings three in on the first of July, with nobody in the water.** Three wage weeks fall in the batch — the twentieth and twenty-seventh of June and the fourth of July — and each is four marks out of the bag into an envelope with the week written on it, and the column goes **eight lines and thirty-two marks – nine and thirty-six – ten and forty – eleven and forty-four**, with the fifth of March the oldest and staying there, and **six envelopes on a bench and not one of them anywhere, and the reason is on the page and the reason is that a wage is not a reason to go to a door.** The note is not re-written, its three counts of weeks do not move, five marks of compressor's gland stand on it unpaid and useless. **No money came in from any office in this batch and no money went out to any person except three wages, and a shipwright bought a rival crew's printed sheet for four marks out of her own hand, which is not the yard's money and is not in any column and is not going to be repaid.**

**Four things flagged rather than hidden, and one author-level decision taken on the page.**

- **THE BELLWRIGHT'S ONE QUESTION WAS ASKED, IN A ROOM, ON SUNDAY THE SECOND OF JULY, AND ANSWERED ONCE.** It was put to him in a doorway on the Saturday and not asked in the doorway; it was asked in the cabin of the *Anna Ray* with the door shut, the hatch open, four men present, no office and no seal and no ledger, and the chest not brought down the hill and not opened. **No digit is printed in any chapter of this batch and no figure goes into any column.** His three conditions were agreed in the room and the yard has undertaken in its own book not to ask again, and **that undertaking binds the yard and not the office and not the market and not the boy. NO LATER CHAPTER IN THIS VOLUME MAY ASK HIM AGAIN.** He did not ask for money, said so, and that he did not ask is entered.
- **THE CHANNEL BY WHICH A DISGRACED OFFICER REACHES A MORROW QUAY REEVE IS RULED, ON THE PAGE, WITH A DATE: IT IS THE WRIT.** Chapter 130, in Calder Marr's own left hand, in the yard's own book, on Wednesday the fifth of July. The writ's own last line says the second season is not this office's to give and will arrive out of a city from a man who has never been inside this harbor, and the holder will be told when it arrives and not before. **A man in a coat, of any office, disgraced or not, arrives as a stranger with a paper about that second season, and the yard will read it, will answer it on the day it comes, and will not treat him as a friend or as an enemy.** No new person, office or institution was created to carry it and no later volume may invent a second channel.
- **CAPTAIN HOLT'S POSTING AFTER HER DEFEAT IS EXPLICITLY STILL OPEN AND IS RECORDED AS STILL OPEN IN `state/open-threads.md`.** Her defeat is in Movement 5 and a posting cannot be decided before the thing it follows. **What Chapter 130 forecloses, in the yard's own hand, is what a posting may not be: no posting, appointment, commission, transport or instruction out of any city has put anybody past this harbor line, the outer marks and the bar of four hundred yards off them stand where the writ says they are and are not nearer, and this party is not going past them this season and has not tried and is not going to.**
- **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — the first request for the harbor authority's chart is referred to with no date, on purpose.** Chapter 122 says the yard was not in that room, did not hear it, has it from a man who was, **and has been able to establish no date for it and has not invented one.** Chapters 49 and 50 of Volume 01 record the request and the refusal and give no date.
- **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — two creditor figures held apart.** The cast list gives Sella Brigg and Edric Pringle each eight shillings and tenpence, and each about four hundred feet of her own warp on the bottom of this harbor. **Chapters 124 and 130 give the eight shillings and tenpence to BOTH women and a warp figure to ONE of them, and the two debts are never in one sentence and the two women are never merged.** An audit should rule whether the February event was one warp with two owners or two warps, and if two, where the second four hundred feet is stated.
- **FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED — an end entered on the ninth seam's mooring, the first of September.** It is a gain and it was entered at no cost to this harbor, which is unusual in this volume, and it is recorded so that a later volume does not mistake it for the notice having been lifted. **The notice of the thirty-first of October is not lifted, not narrowed and not altered, and no acknowledgement was entered, and a municipal mooring is not a person, a boat or a lead.**
- **The batch takes no power stage and does not name the condition.** It is on the page as the bare fact twice, with a reason against each line and a count of four against the fourth, and for the first time in another person's hand, **and the one naming Volume 03 is allowed therefore remains available and is owed to Movement 4.**

---

## Volume 03, Batch 0002 summary (Chapters 111–120) — Movement 2, weather and the work that cannot be sold
