**Movement 2 is written and its turn has landed.** In twenty-two days a yard of two and a half men took a second glass storm and let a lump of glass get wetter rather than put a cloth to it, walked out to a harbor authority's sluice three times for nothing, named three recoveries and funded the smallest of the three out loud, refused a chain on ground a notice closes, priced a woman's own warp in front of her and put her name and a figure on a market wall, told a man of fifty-one out of a storm belt that it does not know who keeps a road and has never been there, agreed to be checked by him in the autumn, **did not put the best man in this harbor on a lead and could not write down the reason why**, entered the three nots about a boy of twenty-two in the first column and let the boy take a season's hand in a market, worked a day at the second seam on a herring boat's own account with a hired man in the water and a tender who is not a diver, **brought up a foot and a half of worked glass of nobody's in a net and put it in a crate in the open air and started a four-day clock**, did not go to the mole's toe on the twelfth of June, **and refused thirty marks in nine sentences on the fourteenth, which cost the town the third recovery.**

**Actual word count: 30,356 words across ten chapters as re-measured by the Volume 03 volume audit, and 30,345 before that audit's eleven mechanical repairs** (Ch111 3,464 · Ch112 3,263 · Ch113 2,522 · Ch114 2,739 · Ch115 3,266 · Ch116 3,107 · Ch117 2,534 · Ch118 3,051 · Ch119 2,937 · Ch120 3,473), measured with `wc -w` including headings, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline. **Every chapter is above the floor. Six are inside the guideline and four are past it — Chapter 112 by 63, Chapter 115 by 59, Chapter 111 by 264 and Chapter 120 by 273 — and all four ran because a cost could not be dropped: 111 is a night of weather with a two-column entry written at ten past two in the morning, and 120 is the batch's climax, which is one shed, one man, one paper, and a refusal that has to be nine sentences long and has to say out loud what the yard would take instead. No cost was dropped.** **The hearing room is used not at all: its slate wall comes down bare at eight on Monday the twenty-ninth of May, the fifth of June and the twelfth of June and on no other day, and there is no sitting on any of the three and the clerk has stopped mentioning it. The market wall is used once, on Wednesday the seventh of June, in daylight, in a market, on no Monday and on no hearing-room day.** The batch's pressures alternate: a storm and a rule that costs, a rota, money, method, discovery from outside, a public wall, an emotional consequence, work and a clock, a municipal recovery, and a refusal in a shed.

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page: EIGHT marks in a bag, thirty-one on the chandler's note, nothing in a tin, on Thursday the fifteenth of June — against thirteen in the same bag on Wednesday the twenty-fourth of May.** The chain is **13, 13, 9, 9, 12, 12, 8, 8, 5, 12, 12, 8, 8, 8.** **Three wage weeks fall in the batch — the thirtieth of May, the sixth and the thirteenth of June — and each is four marks out of the bag into an envelope on the bench with the week written on it in Calder Marr's own left hand, because there is nobody else in that shed who writes; the envelopes are in the bench chest and not among the five rolled in oilcloth; none of them is sent anywhere that week and the reason is on the page and the reason is that a wage is not a reason to go to a door.** The column goes **five lines and twenty marks to eight lines and thirty-two marks, the fifth of March the oldest.** A day at the second seam on the pump hirer's own account brings three in; **a day at the second seam on the *Anna Ray's* own account brings seven in, and the pot of air and a hundred fathoms of hemp went out on the Friday, so the whole of that job left the yard four marks and not seven, which is the figure the yard wrote on the mole's steps on the thirty-first of May before anybody knew which of the two numbers was going to be the one.** The note is not re-written, its three counts of weeks do not move, **and a compressor's gland bought on credit in May is fitted to the yard's own pump and is still unpaid and has made no difference, because the thing after the gland is the hose.**

**Four things flagged rather than hidden, and four author-level decisions taken on the page.**

- **A wage that runs to a boy who is off the stone, into an envelope on a bench that is not carried anywhere.** It is consistent with the advance payment of the fifteenth and with the reason Batch 0001 gave for it, it is on the page in three chapters, it does not reduce the forty marks and is not offered as a reduction, and it is carried in `state/continuity.md` § 8 as a proposed canon change flagged rather than applied.
- **The yard's own boat hauled up on the mole's crown above the third seam's mark, and two objects of nobody's living on that crown for five weeks.** That is an application of the second clause and not an amendment of it, and the crown is landward of the third seam's mark and above the ground the first clause closes.
- **A cistern walk and a mooring's gear line on the harbor authority's own page at no figure, in a wharfinger's own hand, against the office's own interest.** The office has now recorded two things it did not pay for and a man who has not been paid for five pay periods. **It is a fact about an office's bookkeeping and it fixes nothing: the light is not on, the repair account is empty, the ninth seam is out of service with no end of it entered, and forty feet of the office's own marline is in the sea.**
- **The man of fifty-one's statement about coming to the mole is placed on Monday the twenty-second, at the top of the mole's steps, at ten in the morning, in the hearing of three men, and entered as a fact the yard learned on a day the yard names.** The card's *in a market on the fifteenth* is on no page and is not written as though a page said it.
- **The February warp and the Friday warp are dated and grounded on the page** — Tuesday the twenty-eighth of February off the mole's face between the first and second seams on a third lay boat's own account, and four feet of warp on a ring at the second seam on Friday the sixteenth of April belonging to a different boat of a different lay — **and the two figures for the bar out of a storm-belt pilot are two and a half and two against the sheet's two and three-quarters and two and a quarter, and the sheet's own figures are unchanged and unresolved and may only be resolved by the man coming back in the autumn.**
- **The batch takes no power stage and does not name the condition.** It is on the page as the bare fact twice, with a reason against it and nobody asking, **and the one naming Volume 03 is allowed therefore remains available and is owed to Movement 4.**

---

## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 100

**VOLUME 02 IS CLOSED.** Movement 5 is written, the volume's climax is paid, and the volume's resolution is on the page. A wharfinger's fire took about four hundred feet of herring warp off the second lay's wharf and the hose with it; a glass storm came on the twenty-sixth of April and is in the second column with a reason against it and nobody may name a cause for it; a man of thirty-one who had gone down to the ninth seam's mooring without a permit and in a half sea was found alive in five fathoms off the inner shoals at eleven minutes, with a shackle in a hand that would not open, and five men were off that mole of whom not one was on the authority's work and a reeve's barge was committed into a half sea because it was the only boat the harbor authority has; **the cost was a compressor's gland, about ten feet of the yard's own delivery hose, the yard's hand lead's line, a claim window of one date that was worked and logged and raised nothing, two days' work and a pot of air three weeks standing that bought on the seventh of April and is now the whole of the air in this harbor.** The crew earned a **provisional writ with a Crown reporting clause attached to it** in the harbor authority's hearing room on Monday the first of May, **four names on it, the fourth being a hired diver who was asked in person, in a shed, and said he was content to have it**, and the yard took the clause and entered its objection unsummarized and said out loud that the act which made it trustworthy at home is the act that cost it in Veyr, **and that both of those are true and neither cancels the other.**

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page: thirteen marks in a bag, twenty-three on the chandler's note, and nothing in a tin, on Wednesday the third of May.** The bag stood at nothing for five days from the afternoon of the twentieth of April, and it is a fortnight of bread on the twentieth that empties it; the tin was opened on the twenty-seventh for a shilling and sixpence of harbor-authority writ fee and emptied on the twenty-eight, **six marks came in on the twenty-eighth from the harbor authority at the yard's own four-mark rate for the claim window, which is the only money this harbor has paid that yard since the first of February, thirty marks came in on the twenty-ninth from four people who each said the cause out loud first and went straight out again — eight to the harbor's bellwright, sixteen to the boy, six onto the note — and the note came down a further four on Sunday the thirtieth out of a hat in a market, straight onto the creditor's page and never into the bag.** **The bellwright's eight marks are PAID and they are not one mark of the yard's own work. All sixteen marks of the boy's wage came out of four men's pockets and not one out of the yard's, and the boy left the fifth of March — the oldest line in the column — in it on purpose, and the column closes four lines deep at sixteen marks, the same figure and the same depth as on the thirty-first of March.** The forty marks of the claim stand whole. **The forty marks to Mara Venn are not paid, the market money did not touch them, and the writ's reporting clause cannot reach her and she says so in a hearing room before anybody is asked.** Eight shillings and tenpence to Sella Brigg stand undiminished and are unpaid, and about four hundred feet of her own warp is on the bottom of the harbor. **The chandler's note is NOT re-written and the yard says so out loud on a market wall, and the three counts of its weeks are twenty-two, thirteen and eleven as last written and none of them has moved since the fourth of January.** Twenty marks of shoal iron remain unsold, and a man of the south lay has offered nineteen for the stack and a shilling for the crown shackle with the saw kerf in it, separately, and the yard has taken neither.

### The active pressures going into Volume 03

1. **A writ with a four-day reporting clock on it, and a first return falling due.** Every finding of worked glass and every raising of material from claimed ground goes to the circuit office of this coast and to the officer of any vessel holding a claim in this harbor, with place, hour, keeper and limits, **and where a finding is disputed the harbor authority may seat one of the two men and take what a person says as it stands.** The writ runs to the first of March next, may be suspended after an inspection for cause by one man with a key and a book, **is not the Second Bell license, is not a charter, and renews nothing**, and its last line says the second season is not the harbor authority's to give and will arrive out of a city from a man who has never been inside this harbor.
2. **The circuit office's fourth request of the sixteenth of March is still unanswered and unrefused**, and the yard's position has changed underneath it: a party with an instrument can now say no inside an instrument.
3. **A lead plug with a number in it in a chest in a shed, described four times by four men who do not agree, in no book.** Two parts of a chain in a lead plug; a clean half-inch drilled hole; a number of which only a two and a bit of another one and a fourth that is not a two are visible; eleven inches under a bell at eight and a half feet. **The number is not written anywhere and the bellwright refused to say what it is, and he is not of this yard.** It is not connected to the plate dug on the mole's toe in February and the two must never be fused.
4. **The notice of the thirty-first stands whole, and a man nearly drowned inside it.** Three lines in the yard's book say he was not lawful there, that the notice did not save him, and that the yard will not say it did and will not say the notice was not worth the paper it is on.
5. **A glass storm, and nobody may name a cause for it.** It came through the water and not off it and it is in all of it.
6. **A bell that needs a boat.** Fouled, **a frame cut through the casting on one side, two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was, and no tongue on the frame at all** (the bellwright's own statement of the first of May, in the first column as a checkable fact; the standing phrase "a tongue half worn through" is withdrawn by the Volume 02 audit and may not be revived), a new rope bought on the eleventh of January, **eight marks and a day of a boat every four or five years, and the yard has told the four of them now so that there is no argument in four years.** The bellwright has refused on the page to make a repair of the chain in the plug or a finding of it, and has not been asked in a room, and Volume 03 inherits that refusal.
7. **The coast road, published in six hands in six boats, half-believed, and not resolved**, with a question about charts on a market wall and a refusal of the word *network* in public.
8. **Labor.** No air, no gland, no hose, a hand lead's line made badly at night, a bag that stood at nothing for five days, a note that went to thirty-three and is at twenty-three, a harbor authority with no boat of its own and a tide gang four men short and a cistern charged on a rota that a man does not walk, and a mole-head light that is not on.
9. **Neave Tarrant, of the third lay, nine winters, out since the twenty-eighth of February, not found**, and the yard has not gone and has asked nobody anything. **A man of fifty-one with a bad wheel who cannot place a sound and is not put on a lead and does not know why.** The *Sea Wren* at the pile. The hard at Ashtide unanswered since the ninth of November. Nineteen winters. The *Kittiwake* on the hard with no cradle.

### The relationship pressure

**Calder and Mara are at the end of five months of refusals and neither of them has broken.** She was asked for a figure for the third time in five months in April and refused identically in principle and newly in detail; he came within four inches of asking her what is in the anchor and did not, and was told by a shipwright that he has asked four people nothing this month. **In the hearing room on the first of May she said, before anybody asked her, that the writ cannot reach her and that she is not of it, and that she has a paper of her own going up the coast on her own account with three clauses about a false seal and a boat and three questions about a chart, and that she is not an assistant and is not going to be one this afternoon.** She holds the forty marks and the sentence of the twenty-second of March. **He did not spend a mark of the market money on her and did not offer and has not been asked to.**

**Calder and Bevin are at their closest and it is a good place and it is a dangerous one.** The boy is owed forty marks and sixteen marks of wage, **has entered a second omission of his own — that he has been to the fishery twice since the twenty-first of March and had his hand open the whole of both times and has not put that down — and called it an omission and entered both omissions in the same breath so that nobody can come in four years and say he hid one and not the other.** His hand is going, the yard said so out loud in a room in front of a clerk of the circuit office, **and he is to be off the stone on the fifteenth of May whatever his own arithmetic says, and the bonesetter's book still says a boat hand in June.** Nobody in that crew asks him one word about it, then or afterwards.

**Calder and Kest are at the end of the volume's own pattern and the reeve has paid six times in his own hand.** The sixth acknowledgement was on the twenty-seventh of April and was the second a party asked for. **The price of the writ is named out loud in a room: the ninth seam's mooring out of service from the first of May with no end of it entered, a tide-gang man stood off to mind a cart, and the statement that the yard is having the best of it.** He committed the office's only boat into a half sea and said what it cost before he did it. **He is not a friend and he is not to be softened, and his last act in this volume was to enter, in his own hand and at the yard's asking, a sentence about the reeve's own greed.**

**Calder and Alden do not move, and the volume closes on the not-moving.** The bench moves under the drip on the twenty-sixth of April and the two of them are in one room for nine hours in a gale and do not speak, and Calder Marr does not go over to him. **No fragment, no report talk, no second story, and no confession.**

### The power state

**Unchanged in rank.** Line Diver with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice in Volume 01 and not used since. **No dive of his own and no entry of anything in Movement 5, no Bellwright, Keelwright, Storm-Reader, Chorus Diver or Openhand stage is taken and none may be.** What the volume paid is **a rescue and a going out, both of them done entirely by the method the volume built** — a reason entered before anybody is wet, two divers named as not of the party each fixing his own limit at the boat, a named tender who is not a diver and a cutoff he can pull without asking anybody, a pot of air read out on a mole in the yard's own words with the word *once* entered, three columns afterward with a reason against every line of the second and the third one carrying two different times, and four read-backs and a fifth man who was not asked. **The condition is not named in Movement 5 and the word is not used anywhere in Chapters 91–100.** The lead was a burn that came back hours after a rope came off a man's hand, in a leg, in five fathoms of water, and it is in the second column with a reason against it and no name attached to it.

### The bodies and the dated positions

- **No air in this harbor, no compressor gland, no delivery hose, and a hand lead's line being made by one man at night.**
- **The pot of air bought on the seventh of April is the whole of the air and is empty.** The pump is at the harbor authority's work and the yard's reason is in the office's book.
- **The ninth seam's mooring is out of service from the first of May with no end of it entered.** The north berth is out and took a sea on the night of the twenty-eighth with fifty fathoms of warp on it where ninety were to lie, and forty feet of the authority's marline is still in the sea. The third lay is full. **Petch is stood off the wall from the first of May to the tenth** and the mole steps will not be cleared in June. **The tide gang is four men short and the harbor authority's cistern is charged on a rota that a man does not walk, and the office has no boat of its own after the evening of the twenty-eighth until the barge comes back.** The mole-head light is not on and will not be. The *Kittiwake* is on the hard with no cradle. The hard at Ashtide is unanswered. Nineteen winters are disputed and unpaid.
- **A wage at four marks a week:** the first four weeks paid, **the ninth, tenth, eleventh and twelfth paid on Saturday the twenty-ninth of April out of the market, four lines and sixteen marks, and not one mark of the sixteen out of the yard's own work; the fifth to the eighth of March remain in the column and the fifth of March is the oldest line in it.** The column closes four lines deep at sixteen marks. The forty marks of the claim stand whole.
- **The bell is on the harbor's own ground in eight and a half feet off the south end**, fouled, with a frame cut through the casting on one side, **no tongue on the frame at all and two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was**, a new rope bought on the eleventh of January, **a delay out of the water off the south end that has not moved since the sixth of December, a going out on the second of May at eight marks and a day of a boat bought at a market, and a bellwright who has refused on the page to make either a repair or a finding of the chain in the plug and has not been asked in a room.** The eight marks are paid. There has not been a going out since the twenty-seventh of February before the second of May.
- **The delayed note: eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, unchanged. Two occasions logged with no call, unchanged. No occasion is entered in this batch**, and the third column of the book says on the page why a figure taken on a flat calm is not an occasion and that a boy who has counted eleven is not going to make it twelve by writing a number. The answer inside the note remains reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.
- **The coast road, the worked glass, the conservator's unsigned finding, the sixth hand-copied copy and the plate in a master's keeping** are all carried forward and all unresolvable inside this volume. **The road is published and half-believed and is not explained, is not named as a network, is not connected to anything under the town, and is not resolved.**
- **The concealment is hidden and the volume closes with it hidden.** The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation; the one false line on the unverified slate is still false and the slate was not moved; **the boy has not asked and has not been asked, and has entered a second omission of his own, and the holder came within four inches of asking the conservator again and did not; and the conservator with no standing has still never been asked and said in a room, unprompted, that the writ cannot reach her.** It was told once, in Chapter 38, and it is not told again.

---

## Volume 02 close — as AUDITED, and the Volume 03 handoff

**Audited 26 September 2026 in `reviews/volume-02/volume-02-close.md`.** That file is the volume audit and it supersedes the five batch accounts of themselves which appear below it. Nothing is owed to Volume 02 and nothing is owed to a chapter that has not been written.

**What Volume 02 answered, on the page, in the order the outline's own resolution requires it.** A provisional writ of salvage working, granted by the harbor authority on Monday the first of May, running to the first of March next, with **four names on it and a Crown reporting clause attached** and a four-day clock that has started. **Eight marks and a day of a boat paid to the harbor's bellwright**, out of four hands in a market and not one mark out of the yard's own work. **Sixteen marks of a boy's wage arrears paid out of somebody else's pocket**, four lines, and the fifth of March left in the column on purpose. **The yard's own debt to Mara Venn not paid, not paid out of the market money, and not reachable by the writ**, and she says so in a hearing room before anybody asks. **A Kest payment that cost somebody who is not the yard**: about four hundred feet of a woman's own warp off her own wharf, a mooring out of service with no end of it entered, and a tide-gang man stood off to mind a cart. **The chandler's note not re-written, said out loud on a market wall on the third of May**, with its three counts of weeks unmoved since the fourth of January. **Six hand-copied copies in six hands of six boats, none of them the yard's, and a refusal of the word *network* in public and a question about a man with a pen on the wall in its place.** **A man found alive in five fathoms off the inner shoals, and the notice that closed his ground is not lifted and is not going to be argued.**

**The volume's central pressure is resolved, and resolved in the opposite direction to the opening.** The leash did not shorten: it lengthened, acquired a name and four persons, and now carries a reporting clock and a Crown signature. **The volume's own sentence for it is in Calder Marr's mouth in the hearing room on the first of May and is the better sentence, and the outline is not amended: the yard is the most reliable crew in this harbor and it has cost a hand lead, a hose and a claim window, and the writ we got for it is a leash with our names on it, and both of those are true and neither cancels the other.**

**Two clauses of `outline/volume-02.md` were discharged in a different form, and both are RULED by the audit.** The **climax**: there was no charter to abandon, because the charter had expired on the thirty-first of March, so there was no official route to abandon; it was one man and not a crew; the glass storm is the twenty-sixth of April and the rescue is the twenty-eighth in an easing north-northeast; the paid official route was **interrupted rather than abandoned**; and **no salvage award was ever claimable, because nothing was raised, and a clerk's box cannot hold the difference between a window that was worked and a window that was missed.** The **resolution**: the coast road is published in six boats and the word *network* is used once, by a rival, and refused in public, and what is on the wall is a question about a man with a pen. **Neither clause is an unspent debt and no later phase may read either as one.**

**Six state-file figures with nothing standing behind them were corrected, and two of them are material:** Batch 0002 is **31,300 words and not 30,995**; the bag stood at nothing for **five days and not seventeen**; **the six marks of the claim window's day rate did reach the bag** on the twenty-eighth; **the bell's tongue is not on the frame** and the phrase "a tongue half worn through" is withdrawn; and **no batch review log cited anywhere in the state files has ever existed** — `logs/` holds only `close.log` and `models.log`, fifteen false citations naming Batches 0001, 0002, 0004 and 0005 are withdrawn, and the authority for every Volume 02 review finding is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 000N review repairs*.

**Ten mechanical repairs were applied in place, in Chapters 62 and 69, all of them the same defect: Edric Pringle is a woman and two chapters called her *he*.** The state files recorded seven wrong pronouns in three places; the true count is **ten, in four places**, and every one is corrected on the page.

**Volume 03 begins here, and inherits by name:** the writ and its four-day reporting clock and the ground it names and the one office that may renew it; the second season as a named absence; the sixth-lay finding, which **does not exist** — the volume's findings are in the second and third lays and no chapter of Volume 02 names a sixth lay; the two Slees, hired on the twenty-eighth of April and not used since; the lead plug in the chest; the glass storm and the ban on naming a cause; the six hand-copied copies in six boats; the question about charts and the refusal of the word *network*; the notice of the thirty-first and the man in five fathoms; **the ninth seam out of service and the eighth-clause plan for the fifth seam still unanswered and still to be reached by a change of politics in an office**; the hard at Ashtide; the coast office's word *capacity* and Elias Voke's statement; the *Sea Wren* at the pile and Noll Tarner; the fourth request of the sixteenth of March; the gland and ten feet of hose the yard owes for air; **the boy off the stone on Monday the fifteenth of May and the bonesetter's June**; the ninth seam's mooring, the north berth, the warp and forty feet of the authority's marline in the sea; the mole-head light and the empty repair account; the cistern on a rota; the fire party's dead hose; and **Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found.** All of it, with twenty-eight locks and the calendar re-derived day by day, is in `outline/volume-03.md`.

---

## Volume 02, Batch 0005 summary (Chapters 91–100) — Movement 5, the rescue and the writ. **VOLUME 02 IS CLOSED AND AUDITED.**

**Movement 5 is written, the volume's climax is paid, and the volume's resolution is on the page.** In sixteen days the Free Keel yard was asked for a pump and had none, lost four hundred feet of a woman's warp and its own hose to a fire, watched a glass storm come through the water and wrote down what it did in the second column with a reason against it, bought an air pot three weeks standing and read its age out loud on a mole before anybody was wet, put five men off that mole, none of them of the authority's work, and a reeve's barge into a half sea with them, **got a man out of five fathoms in eleven minutes and lost a compressor's gland, ten feet of hose, a hand lead's line and a claim window of one date doing it**, took **thirty marks from four people who each said the cause out loud first** and paid it straight out again, **was granted a provisional writ with a Crown reporting clause and four names on it and said in a room that the act which made it trustworthy at home is the act that costs it in Veyr and that both are true**, looked after a bell for the first time since the twenty-seventh of February, copied a coast road in six hands that are not the yard's, and put a tenth thing on a market wall under a naval paper and said out loud that the chandler's note is not re-written.

**Actual word count: 33,327 words across ten chapters** (Ch91 2,791 · Ch92 2,529 · Ch93 2,972 · Ch94 3,211 · Ch95 3,931 · Ch96 3,435 · Ch97 3,978 · Ch98 3,524 · Ch99 3,136 · Ch100 3,820), measured with `wc -w` including headings after the review repair, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline. **Every chapter is above the floor. Four are inside the guideline and six are past it**, the largest Chapter 97 at about twenty-four per cent over and Chapter 95 at about twenty-three; **every chapter past the guideline is one complete room or one complete day, and three of them carry more than one room on that day — Chapter 92 is the shed and the market, Chapter 98 is the net loft, the mole's steps and the shed, and Chapter 100 is the boards and the dock — which is a shape fact and is now recorded as one instead of being denied.** **The hearing room is used twice, once for the office's own ledger on Thursday the twenty-seventh and once for the one sitting on Monday the first of May, and the market wall is used once, on Wednesday the third of May, and no two of the three are the same day.** The batch's pressures alternate: a fire and a warping, a hand and a market, weather, an office and a paper, the water and a rescue, money and four causes, a sitting, a bell, a going out and six hands, and a wall.

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page: thirteen marks in a bag, twenty-three on the note, nothing in a tin, on Wednesday the third of May.** The full chain is in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0005 as written* and in the prose of Chapters 91, 92, 93, 94, 95, 96, 98, 99 and 100. **The bag stood at nothing for five days from the afternoon of the twentieth of April, the tin was opened once and emptied, and the only money this harbor has paid that yard since the first of February is the six marks of the claim window's day rate, which came in on the twenty-eighth and is still in the bag.** The three counts of the note's weeks are unmoved since the fourth of January and the note is not re-written. **All sixteen marks of wage came out of other men's pockets and not one out of the yard's own work, and the boy left the oldest line in his column in it on purpose.**

**Two things flagged rather than hidden.** **The yard is now the most reliable crew in this harbor and it owns no air, no gland, no hose, a hand lead's line it is making badly, a claim window it worked and raised nothing on, and a lease on a reportable paper.** And **the batch makes no canon change**: the only author-level additions are a discipline — the provisional writ is defined on the page and is not a charter and renews nothing, the yard's practice gains a third column for what the boy said when he was not in the boat, and the counts do not move with the calendar, including the rule that a figure taken on a flat calm is a figure and not an occasion. **Two clauses of `outline/volume-02.md` are discharged in a different form than the outline describes, and both are flagged for the audit in `state/continuity.md` § 9 rather than left for it to find: the climax is one man and not a crew, in an easing wind three days after the glass storm, with nothing raised and therefore no salvage award ever claimable; and the resolution refuses the word *network* in public and puts a question about a man with a pen on the wall instead.** **One inherited defect was recorded for the audit and has now been RULED AND REPAIRED by it: Edric Pringle is a woman, and Chapters 62 and 69 called her *his* — the seven occurrences the state files recorded were in fact ten, in four places, and the audit corrected every one of them mechanically in place. See `reviews/volume-02/volume-02-close.md` Part 2, item 1.**


---

## Volume 02, Batch 0004 summary (Chapters 81–90) — Movement 4, the forged seal. **THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT IS PAID.**

*Superseded by the Batch 0005 section above and retained as the record of the position at the end of Chapter 90. The full accounting is in `state/chapter-summaries.md` § *Volume 02, Batch 0004* and in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0004 as written (Chapters 81–90)*.*

**Movement 4 is written and its turn has landed.** In fourteen days the Free Keel yard went into April with no instrument and was refused a job on the mole's toe and took a job on the second seam in the same morning, because the difference between them is the whole of what a harbor's paper is for; did one paid day's work and lost eight days of the pump's hire; **took a coast road out of a working seaman's mouth and put it in its own book in two columns with a reason against the second; asked a reeve for a claim window on one object on one date and got, in exchange, the fifth time the harbor authority has entered in its own ledger that it cannot lift a notice it did not write — the first of the five a party asked for; had a conservator prove a claim seal forged on a mole's crown in daylight, without a diver and without a ship, and say out loud before anybody asked that she cannot say who held the tool or who made the glass; gave the glass back in a market in front of the man who brought it, on purpose, because it had been brought up the hill to be answered and not to be kept; spent a day and two marks checking two marks and a bar of gravel with a hand lead out of another man's boat; and put the ninth thing on a market wall saying in its own four lines that it is not a seal, is not a claim, is not a survey, binds nobody, consents for nobody, may be copied by any man who can read, and that no person is to be asked for anything.**

**Actual word count: 25,683 words across ten chapters** (Ch81 2,647 · Ch82 2,569 · Ch83 2,673 · Ch84 2,456 · Ch85 2,258 · Ch86 2,702 · Ch87 2,579 · Ch88 2,574 · Ch89 2,605 · Ch90 2,620), measured with `wc -w` including headings and re-measured after the review repair, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline. **Every chapter is above the floor and every chapter is inside the guideline** — the first Volume 02 batch to come in that way. **No chapter is two rooms.** The hearing room is not used at all, its slate wall is wiped and named twice, on the tenth and the seventeenth, and the clerk's own page is carried through both.

**The money: three marks in a bag, thirty on the chandler's note, two crowns in a tin, on Monday the seventeenth of April.** **Twenty-four marks of wage unpaid**, in a column with no adjectives, and the eleventh week falls on Tuesday the eighteenth. The forty to the boy, the forty to Mara Venn, the eight to the harbor's bellwright and the eight shillings and tenpence to Sella Brigg all stand whole and unpaid, and a new figure stands in the conservator's own column: **four marks refused in writing by a shop at Ashtide for an opinion she gave in a market.**

**Two things flagged rather than hidden.** **The yard gave up its only claim to a real object on purpose**, in a market, in front of the man who brought it, and what it kept is a wall with a nail in it and two copies. **And the bell has gone four months without a boat**: the going out costs eight marks and a day of a boat, the eight marks are owing, there has not been a going out since the twenty-seventh of February, and the batch ends on the fact that nobody in that yard has said out loud what happens in June.

**The batch makes no canon change.** The only author-level additions are disciplines rather than rules, and **the counts do not move with the calendar** — twenty-two, thirteen and eleven on the note, eleven copies, eleven logged occasions, two empty answers.

**The volume's climax is not begun.** No fishing crew is trapped, there is no glass storm, and no equipment is destroyed. The batch's pull is weather that has been falling since the third of April, a full third lay, a mole-head light that is not on, and a man off a boat asking for a stove.


*The section below is the state of the yard at the end of Chapter 70 and is superseded by the section above. It is retained because the position on the morning of the second of March is a load-bearing part of the record. The Volume 01 material further down this file is audited and has not been altered.*

## Where the story is at the end of Chapter 70

**Movement 2 is written and its turn has landed.** A herring skipper has answered the coast office's word *capacity* with a document of his own, in a hearing record, for nothing, and **the answer is a document the yard did not write and cannot fix** — and a harbor authority's own account of what it has spent since the twenty-eighth of November is read into the same record on the same morning, and both are on their way to a man in Veyr who has never been inside this harbor. **The first of March is a Wednesday and it is on the page, with forty-one boats in the basin, one berth short, the north berth out of service, fifty fathoms of winter warp on a wall that wanted ninety, three tide-gang men stood off, nineteen winters of dues disputed and unpaid, and a hard at Ashtide applied for on the ninth of November and unanswered.**

**The three storm-exposed recoveries are done and each cost a day the crew cannot replace.** The ninth seam's mooring is worked and in service from four o'clock on the seventeenth of February. Ninety fathoms of the harbor authority's tarred marline went onto the north wall on the twenty-seventh and forty feet of it went over the wall head and out into the harbor on a shackle of the authority's own moorings gear, because the authority would not buy iron in the last week of February and the yard accepted that and worked on it. A herring skipper's ring and forty-one feet of warp came up off the same wall on the twenty-eighth, and the skipper asked for the cause to be said by the yard before she said it.

**A rival crew exists and is not a villain.** Noll Tarner of the *Sea Wren*, with Wend Crale and Piet Marrow, fifteen, who cannot read — a Free Keel crew off the slate since November for taking a job at Ashtide without telling his own crew first, which he says in two sentences and does not apologise for. **He asked the question nobody in the Free Keel asks — whose book is the day in, and can a man come and read my dive in your book, and what is it for — and the answer was two columns, the checkable and the not, which is now the yard's written practice.** The Free Keel yard's book is now the book of another crew's work.

**The circuit office's second seal came on Friday the twenty-seventh of January**, which is the twenty-ninth day from the thirtieth of December, and the yard entered the arrival and did not answer and did not refuse, and Kest has since read the whole of it into a hearing record on his own motion. **The office's own paper still says ten copies of the public record and the count is eleven, and nobody in the yard knows whether the office has not been told, has been told and has not corrected it, or has been told and has left it standing, and that not-knowing is now entered as the third of three facts with no opinion in it.**

**Mara Venn has no standing and has said so in a room, twice.** She prices a refusal in the order she is asked, as a figure and not a decision, and she says *do not make me useful*, and she asks for six marks in daylight on the seventh of February and is not paid and does not ask twice, and on the first of March she says in a hearing room, before she is asked, that the room will not get a conservator's opinion out of her because she is named in a copy of the thirtieth of December as a door that has been tried. **She does not break with the Registry in this batch or in this volume, and her break is Volume 4.**

### The active pressures going into Chapter 71

1. **The paper that asks for the book.** The second request is unanswered and the second seal is on it. **Movement 3 is where it is answered and re-asked**, and the answer must be a document that a coast office and a naval officer can both read, and it must not be a refusal, because a dated refusal is an answer and an answer chooses the office's next move.
2. **A record in a man's keeping.** Eleven copies on the yard's count, two offices holding a stale ten, another crew's day in the yard's book, and a fisher's document describing that book going to a coast office. **Nobody has asked who the record is for and the yard still has no answer.**
3. **The first of March is over and the wall is half bare.** The fleet is in, one berth short, the north berth out, the ninth seam a mooring and not a berth, and whatever comes back from Veyr about the second season is a date the yard does not control.
4. **Neave Tarrant, in the third lay, nine winters, out in the blow, not back until Wednesday.** A boy who cannot read said his name in a shed and nobody said anything.
5. **The money.** Eleven marks in a bag, one mark on the note, two crowns in a tin, a wage that starts again on the seventh of March, the air pot a third gone, a pump that will not be fitted before the eighth of March, and forty marks owed to the boy and forty owed to Mara Venn, both whole and both unpaid.
6. **The run under the town**, four hundred feet of ordinary figures, no chart, no forum, and no instrument in this harbor or in Veyr to put it into. Untouched for two movements and it is the volume's opened pressure.

### The relationship pressure

**Calder and Mara have stopped asking each other to be useful, and neither of them has noticed when.** He asked her for a figure on the twenty-seventh of January and did not ask her for a decision, and she said *do not make me useful*, and he has not asked her for anything since. **He did not ask to have her asked in a hearing room on the first of March and noticed that it was not asked and did not say so.** That is the whole of Movement 2's change and it is the change Volume 2 has been asking for: he is a man who now asks for a number instead of a favour and takes a smaller answer without arguing.

**Calder and Bevin are at the closest they have been and it is a bad place.** The boy refuses figures in the yard's own book, refuses a heaving line, refuses to write another man's reason, asks the harbor authority's ledger for the record of a dive he was not in and was not allowed to be in, works out his own wage without being told it is coming, and stands up in a municipal hearing room and says that he did not ask. **And on the twenty-third of February he entered, in his own right hand, that neither of them has asked the other, and that two of them keeping a thing out of a book is twice as safe and half as honest, and that he would rather it was half as honest than twice as safe.** Calder came within four inches of asking and did not.

**Calder and Kest are past the point where a promise has no date on it.** The reeve has now entered the harbor authority inside its own notice four times in his own hand, has spent its repair account on moorings and warp, has stood three men off, and has said in a hearing record that a second season may not exist and that the yard's part in it is nothing. **He is not a friend. He is a man whose promises have dates on them.**

**Calder and Alden do not move.** A man of sixty-one at the far bench, silent since the thirteenth of November, not asked for anything, and Calder does not go. That not-going is the relationship and it is not a plot point and it is not paid off in this volume.

### The power state

**Unchanged in rank. Line Diver with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice in Volume 01 and not used again, and not used in this batch.** No Bellwright, Keelwright, Storm-Reader, Chorus Diver or Openhand stage is taken and none may be. **What the volume has paid is method:** a stated reason entered before anybody is hired and unedited, a limit set by the diver at the boat and entered as the diver's, a named tender who is not the diver, a cutoff the tender can pull, a ground named in the paper, **and the two-column convention — what a man on a mole can check, and what he cannot, with a reason against the second.** Calder is not a faultreader and does not become one; the dressed aperture and the fouling that gives no hour are not referred to in this batch.

**The tinnitus is named again in Chapter 67, for the fifth time since the fourteenth of October**, and Nessa Pike tells him what it is in a year: a hole where a bell used to be that fills in when somebody says the sentence out loud, and a man who does that well has a trade and a man who does it badly has a disgrace, and there is no third thing.

### The bodies and the dated positions

- **The ninth seam's mooring is in service from four o'clock on Friday the seventeenth of February.** A mooring is not a berth. **The fouling on the ground line between the third and the ninth seams is untouched and is entered in a reason in the harbor authority's own book.**
- **The north berth is out of service** and is the one that takes a north-easter. **One berth short on the first of March, with forty-one boats in the basin where the clerk said forty on the Thursday.**
- **Fifty fathoms of the herring fleet's winter warp lie on the north wall where ninety were to lie.** Forty feet of the authority's own marline is in the sea between the wall and the bar.
- **Three tide-gang men are stood off from the twenty-fourth of February until the fourth of March** — Rowlandike, Petch, and Wil Slee, whose brother Tam went down twice on the seventeenth.
- **A wage at four marks a week from the first of February**: the first four weeks are paid, the fourth a week late, out of the boy's own arithmetic, and the fifth and sixth fall in March. **The forty marks of the claim stand whole and are not reduced, and they are named in a fisher's statement going to a coast office.**
- **Sella Brigg's cover went off the slate on the twenty-ninth of October, and her entered third of the iron — a true figure of six marks, eight shillings and tenpence, there being no coin for the two thirds of a penny missing off a third of twenty marks — was paid at the round figure of six marks at the counter on the sixth of February, with the eight shillings and tenpence entered in her name, undiminished, and not paid.**
- **The air pot is a third gone and a quarter of a mark worse than it was in January. The pump is not fitted before the eighth of March and cannot be hired out. The *Kittiwake* is on the hard with her bilge blocked in and no cradle.**
- **The delayed note: eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, and the eleventh of them, Monday the twenty-seventh of February, has a call in it.** The count of occasions logged with no call in them is **two**, the eleventh and the nineteenth of January, and may not be raised. Two further occasions were heard and not entered and neither is an occasion: the mole steps on the evening of Thursday the twenty-third of February, and the hearing room on the morning of the first of March, which is the twelfth occasion since the twenty-sixth of October and is entered nowhere. **The answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.**
- **The concealment is hidden and its second part has grown.** The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation; the false line is still false; the boy has entered that neither he nor Calder has asked; and Mara Venn has still not been asked. **A concealment may be told once, to one person, in a private scene, at a price. It has been told once, in Chapter 38, and it may not be told a second time for nothing, and this volume closes with it hidden.**

## Volume 02, Batch 0003 summary (Chapters 71–80) — Movement 3, the paper that asks for the book

**Movement 3 is written and its turn has landed.** In twenty-nine days the Free Keel yard answered a circuit office with a stated ground and not a refusal, watched a working boat and its pump and its papers go into a lighter on the fourth of March on a form whose ground was its own market wall, walked two days in each direction to enter the ground and be re-asked the same morning, fitted a pump it had been told it could not buy, was told in a hearing room by a naval officer everything about itself that it had published, was asked to strike out a true entry and refused twice for two different good reasons, gave that man a copy of the leaf instead, found its seven copies named in public alongside four it cannot reach, was asked for all eleven, and did not answer, and could not have paid to.

**Actual word count: 26,358 words across ten chapters** (Ch71 2,679 · Ch72 2,527 · Ch73 2,348 · Ch74 2,535 · Ch75 2,333 · Ch76 3,302 · Ch77 2,639 · Ch78 2,356 · Ch79 2,261 · Ch80 3,378), measured with `wc -w` including headings, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline. **Every chapter is above the 2,200 floor. Eight are inside the guideline and two are past it** — Chapter 76 at about three per cent over, the batch's one hearing room, and Chapter 80 at about five and a half per cent over, the batch's one complete day and one room. **No chapter in the batch is two rooms.** Chapter 80 carried the chandler's shop as a second room and was thirteen per cent over; the review repair moved the creditor down to the yard so the day is one room and compressed the chapter to 3,378, and the whole account is in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0003 review repairs*. The hearing room is used once, in Chapter 76, a fortnight after the hearing room of Chapter 70 and not twice in a week, and the batch's pressures alternate: a decision and its price, a confiscation, a journey and a counter, a supply problem paid, an argument, a hearing, a market, labour and a missing man, a refusal, and a ledger.

**The batch's turn is Chapters 75 to 77.** A man whose boat has been taken asks the yard to strike a true entry out of its book, and is refused by the shipwright for the rule and by the holder for the truth, and asks instead for a copy of the yard's own leaf and is given it, and refuses to sign a sentence of Calder Marr's out of politeness. **A naval officer then uses the yard's own record, correctly, in a room it cannot leave, and the count the yard published in November is used against it in the office's own type.** And the yard answers the demand for its eleven copies by publishing the eleven on a wall in its own hand and by declining to ask a single holder to give one up. **A public record is a thing other people can now use, and it cannot be made private again without losing the only protection a poor crew has, and the yard knows that because a rival crew's pump went into a lighter on the fourth of March and a man of the third lay has not come back since the twenty-eighth of February.**

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page:** **five marks in a bag, twenty-seven on the chandler's note, two crowns in a tin, on Friday the thirty-first of March, the last day of the charter.** The full chain is in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0003 as written* and in the prose of Chapters 73, 74, 78 and 80. **The forty marks to the boy and forty to Mara Venn both stand whole and both are unpaid, sixteen marks of March wage are unpaid in a column with no adjectives, the bellwright's eight marks are still owing and cannot be found before October, and Sella Brigg's eight shillings and tenpence is still in her name.** **The note is the biggest it has been all winter because the yard bought a pump on it, and the yard said out loud, at the head of its own dock, to the creditor who came down to it unasked, that it is not going to ask for a fortnight, and gave the reason.**

**Two things flagged rather than hidden.** **The one-season provisional working charter expired at midnight on Friday the thirty-first of March**, in writing, in three places, and **the yard has no instrument at all going into April, and the first of April is a Saturday, and the third lay fills.** And **the batch makes no canon change**: the only author-level addition is a discipline — the circuit office's three seals all carry nineteen words and the second and third differ only in their first clause, and the chandler's note carries three static counts of its own weeks of twenty-two, thirteen and eleven as last written, none of which may be advanced by arithmetic. **No bible file, no outline and no ending is touched, and the review repair touched none of them either** — it corrected five broken weekdays, one false fact on a form, three figures and one piece of meta-fiction, moved a creditor's shop down to the yard so the last chapter is one room, and withdrew two false claims that the state files had made about their own chapters.

---

## Volume 02, Batch 0002 summary (Chapters 61–70) — Movement 2, weather and the capacity of a harbor

**Movement 2 is written and its turn has landed.** In forty-one days the Free Keel yard wrote down what it actually has and found that a list of gear is a list of shortages, took a working pump's owner as a rival and a customer, freed a herring boat's warp on a seam the notice does not close, let a circuit office's second seal stand unanswered and priced it in three figures in a conservator's mouth, got the harbor authority's fourteen marks and paid a boy his first wage in the same hour, worked the ninth seam's mooring from a hundred yards away because a notice kept the yard off the mole, lost forty feet of the authority's own marline to a shackle the authority would not replace, got a herring skipper's ring back out of the same water she lost it in, and heard a fisherman answer a coast office's word in a hearing room with a document the yard did not write and cannot fix.

**Actual word count, re-measured by the Volume 02 audit after the Batch 0002 review repair: 31,300 words across ten chapters** (Ch61 3,024 · Ch62 3,502 · Ch63 3,195 · Ch64 2,752 · Ch65 3,498 · Ch66 3,275 · Ch67 3,062 · Ch68 2,790 · Ch69 2,636 · Ch70 3,566), against `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s 2,200–3,200. **The figure of 30,995 and the per-chapter figures for Chapters 61, 63, 65 and 68 that stood here were measured before the review repair and were never recomputed; the chapters are the authority and the corrected figures are these.** **Every chapter is above the 2,200 floor. Six are inside the guideline and four are past it, the largest by about eleven per cent — Chapter 70, which is the batch's one hearing room.** Batch 0001 was five inside and five past with the largest at fourteen per cent, so **the ratio is better and the largest chapter is smaller. No chapter in this batch is two rooms**, the hearing room is used once and in the last chapter, and the batch's pressures alternate: supply understood, a rival crew, ordinary work, an office, money and a boy, a closure and a record, aftermath, labour in weather, a recovery that pays, and a sitting. **Edric Pringle's pronouns in Chapters 62 and 69 were repaired mechanically by the volume audit on 26 September 2026; the seven wrong pronouns the state files recorded were in fact ten, in four places, and the whole record is in `reviews/volume-02/volume-02-close.md` Part 2, item 1.**

**The batch's turn is Chapter 70.** The coast office asked on the twenty-eighth of November, in four lines, for a statement of the capacity of this harbor to moor the fleet, and **the answer is Elias Voke's, written at his own table on Saturday night, for nothing, in his own hand, read out by him in a hearing room, and it says this harbor cannot moor its fleet.** Beside it in the same record is the harbor authority's own account of what it has spent since the twenty-eighth of November, and the two will be read by the same man in Veyr. **The yard cannot fix either, and neither can the office, and the reeve has said in a room that whatever comes back decides whether there is a second season and the yard's part in it is nothing.**

**The money, and it closes checkable on the page:** four marks in a bag on the twentieth of January, **eleven in a bag, one on the chandler's note and two crowns in a tin on Wednesday the first of March**, and the whole chain is in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0002 as written* and in the prose of Chapters 63, 65, 68, 69 and 70. **The forty marks to Bevin Rook and the forty to Mara Venn both stand whole and are both unpaid, and the boy's name and the yard's debt are in a document going to a coast office.**

**Two things flagged rather than hidden.** **The ninth seam's mooring is worked and in service from four o'clock on the seventeenth of February**, which changes the position it stood in for the whole of Movement 1; a mooring is not a berth, the north berth is still out, one berth is still short on the first of March, and the fouling on the third-to-ninth line is untouched and is now in a reason in the office's own book. **And the batch makes no canon change**: the only author-level addition is a discipline — the reeve's own order that a day-count is carried as a day-count and not as a date, so that *the twenty-ninth* can never be read as the twenty-ninth of January — and no bible file, no outline and no ending is touched.

---

## Volume 01 close summary

**Volume 01 paid everything `outline/volume-01.md` asked it to, and nothing in it is owed to Volume 01.** A Free Keel yard with a claim to a discovery and no instrument to work under ran that pressure through five movements and ten weeks and came out of it with a one-season provisional working charter that runs to the last of March, a retained salvage share, the harbor's eighth and the harbor's share following the yard in, a reeve's own undertaking lifted on named dates, a working bell-buoy core hung on the town's own ground, a fragment holding Oren's first minutes in a custody room under a claim the yard cannot withdraw and will not ask to withdraw, and a public sheet on a market wall in three parts in four hands with the limits written inside the rules.

**The volume's central pressure is resolved, not deferred.** The legitimacy of the first recovery is defensible and the mystery it opens is not answered: the reason for the seizure of the fourteenth of October is on no paper this harbor has ever been handed. The midpoint's finding is **the space cannot have been cleared**, in three steps, on the page in Chapter 47. The new question is opened twice — in the yard's records, where the reeve rules that no line may be drawn between three points, and in Sera Mott's mouth at a mole head, where the answer is not a mystery but a fact about people and about labor: *the ones she knows about stopped, because the men who kept them stopped going out.*

## The audit's rulings, in one place

Every item below was **deferred by a batch and is now ruled**, dated 26 September 2026, and each is written into `state/continuity.md` and `state/open-threads.md` in full. The full reasoning is in `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md`, Part 2.

1. **Ordinary harbor work landward of the third seam is outside the notice's closure** (continuity item 6 / threads 6c). **Confirmed.** It is the only reason the yard has money in Volume 1.
2. **The *Vigil* tender's days on station are carried forward from the page, not computed** (item 7 / 6b). **Confirmed, and the arithmetic is now given:** interpolate from the fourth of November at twenty days, which reproduces all four figures on the page exactly. Never state a day-count no chapter gave.
3. **The hearing room's slate wall is wiped every Monday at eight** (item 8 / 6a). **Confirmed** as an institutional fact. The ledger is the record; the wall is a convenience.
4. **Chapter 38 tells Mara Venn the Oren mark** where the Batch 0004 card asked for the hiding to continue (item 9 / 9). **RULED: the chapter stands and the card is amended prospectively.** The volume closed with the mark still hidden, so the outline's requirement is met; a concealment may be told once, to one person, at a price, and the volume must close with it hidden. The deviation is accepted, not tolerated.
5. **The bellwright's core and the craft of it** (item 10 / 10). **RULED: the bible already permits it and item 10 is CLOSED. No amendment is proposed.** `bible/terminology.md`'s *bell buoy* is defined as a buoy that can *listen for a stable echo*, and the core listened and answered. `bible/power-system.md`'s Bellwright stage is a craft, not a rank, and a harbor's own tradesman is not a stage. The answer inside the note stays reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint. **The six bible files are unchanged.**
6. **The *Aster Gannet* lies under the south end of the mole and the mole was built on her in the year she went down** (item 11 / 11). **Ratified.** It is the only lawful ground on which the compartment could be measured inside the harbor line.
7. **The midpoint's exact finding** (item 12 / 12). **RULED: the three-step form in Chapter 47 is ratified**, and carried into `outline/volume-02.md` as lock L6 with the instruction that any later volume restating it restates all three steps.
8. **The bellwright is unnamed** (item 13 / 13). **RULED: he is named in Volume 02, in Movement 1, in the chapter that carries the first of the going out** — Chapter 55 — **and the name is Corvin Sarn, the harbor's own bellwright of Morrow Quay.** He teaches nobody and is not a Stage 5 mentor.
9. **The two length records** (item 14 / 14 and 7). **The claim that Batch 0005 was worse on length than Batch 0004 is WITHDRAWN.** Batch 0005 is **35,514 words against Batch 0004's 39,536** and past the guideline in seven chapters against nine; the 40,859 first recorded was a `chapter-004*.md` glob that swept Chapter 40 in. The shape judgement stands and the length instruction now lives in `outline/volume-02.md` as lock L22, not only in a state file.
10. **Threads 1–5** (Holt's posting, the first echo's witness, the abandoned Oren lead, Lira Venn's volume, the teaching question). **None decided here.** Each is assigned to the volume that owns it, and none is a Volume 01 defect. **Lira Venn's volume is a series-level allocation owed by a series phase, and until it is made no chapter in any volume may use her as a device.**

## The corrected position, in three lines

- **Two chapter figures were wrong and one of them was a chapter.** The interval to the first of March in Chapter 48 was *eleven weeks and a day* and is now **eleven weeks and four days**; the officer's recalled words in Chapter 39 said *ten feet* and are now **nine feet**, which is what Chapters 28, 31, 33 and 41 all say.
- **One state-file figure had no chapter behind it and is withdrawn.** `state/continuity.md` asserted *twenty-four marks of air bought since the twenty-eighth of October*. The chapters give **eleven** in mid-October, **nine** by the fourth of November, **four** on the thirteenth, **five** in the mole job and **eleven** on the first of December — **thirteen as at the twenty-eighth of November, which is the figure Chapter 45 reads out in a hearing room, and twenty-nine by the first of December.** The chapter is right and the state file was wrong.
- **One claim about a marker file was wrong in the ledger's favour.** This phase was handed the record that `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/` has no `.done` marker. **It has one.** Batches 0001 through 0005 all carry `.done`, bootstrap carries `.done`, and phases 001 and 002 carry `.retired`. **The gap is the ledger, not the markers**, which is a narrower defect than three consecutive batches have recorded.

## Batch 0005 summary

**Batch goal, achieved. Volume 01 is closed.** Movement 5, *the bell-buoy core*: in ten weeks the Free Keel yard writes a six-page purpose with nine lines in it asking for nothing, is caught by its own silence about ground, finds out that the mole was built on the ferry, gets a steel onto the *Aster Gannet's* compartment lawfully with a form from a man in a barge, **is granted a one-season provisional working charter on the 28th of November and pays for it in one herring berth and a mooring on the ninth seam and nineteen winters of dues that are not paid**, **recovers a working bell-buoy core and hangs it on the town's own ground**, **recovers a fragment holding the first minutes of the seizure and surfaces it through a public route at the cost of a specific childhood memory of his brother's voice**, puts the cutter's claim and the plate on the same ground and an unreliable manifest on a public wall **as records with their limits and not as a ruling**, gets the *Vigil*'s attention landed in a room and in a road, and ends the year with the reason for the seizure still undisclosed on no paper this harbor has ever been handed, a bell answering the harbor's tide bell through the ground a hand's width of a second late with a grown man's call in it that nobody can place, and the Oren mark still hidden from every office, from Kest, and from his father.

**Actual word count:** **35,514 words across ten chapters** (Ch41 3,096 · Ch42 2,822 · Ch43 2,983 · Ch44 3,571 · Ch45 4,324 · Ch46 3,921 · Ch47 3,791 · Ch48 3,637 · Ch49 3,248 · Ch50 4,121), measured with `wc -w` including headings, against `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s 2,200–3,200 for an ordinary chapter. **Every chapter is above the 2,200 floor and seven of the ten are past the 3,200 guideline, two of them by about thirty per cent** (Ch45, 4,324; Ch50, 4,121), with Ch46, Ch48, Ch47 and Ch44 between eleven and twenty-two per cent past. **Re-measured in the review-repair pass: the figure first recorded here, 40,859, was a `chapter-004*.md` glob that swept Chapter 40 in from Batch 0004, and the true batch total is 35,514, re-measured after the review repairs.** The corrected comparison is therefore the opposite of the one first recorded, on both measures: **Batch 0005 is 4,022 words shorter than Batch 0004's 39,536 and past the guideline in seven chapters against Batch 0004's nine.** *Better on shape:* the pressure types alternate as Batch 0004's review asked — a bench and a filing, a barge with the door shut, a market wall and a road, an argument on a mole and a claim window, a hearing, two dives, a custody counter, a hearing room and a mole head, and a mole head at slack water — and **no chapter in the batch is two rooms**, which was Batch 0004's specific fault and which cost it its two longest chapters. *Worse on nothing that Batch 0004 did not do worse:* seven chapters over the guideline against Batch 0004's nine, and no chapter in this batch is anywhere near Ch31's 4,963 or Ch40's 5,822. The remaining excess is structural rather than scenic: a movement containing a hearing, a recovery and a custody surrender cannot be compressed without dropping a party or a cost. **Recorded in `state/open-threads.md`'s deferred decisions, item 14, not defended, and carried into the Volume 01 close prompt.**

**The batch's midpoint turn is Chapter 44.** Chapters 41–43 are the batch setting its own problem in public: a purpose that is silent about ground, a reeve who enters that silence in his own margin the night before, a conservator's note dated the same day in the same week that the purpose cannot reach, and a *Vigil* paper that asks for the crew's book and is refused. Chapter 44 is where the batch turns, because the answer to a question the yard has carried since the 27th of October turns out to be a **claim window on a mole face the notice does not close**, and because the yard's own purpose is found wanting and cannot be corrected, so Calder admits the incompleteness in a supplementary paper an hour before a hearing. Everything after that is a crew that has paper: a charter, three named dates, a bell, a fragment, and the cost of a public route.

**Conflicts found and how they were resolved:**

1. **The December calendar was wrong in eight places and could not stand.** The locked grid gives 1 December as a Thursday, so **the 6th is a Tuesday, the 8th a Thursday, the 10th a Saturday, the 13th a Tuesday, the 17th a Saturday, the 20th a Tuesday, the 21st a Wednesday, the 25th a Sunday and the 26th a Monday**, and **1 January is a Sunday, 4 January a Wednesday, 19 January a Thursday and 1 March a Wednesday.** The first draft had the fourth of December as a Friday, the fifth as a Saturday, the 18th as a Friday, the 21st as a Monday, and the last chapter on the 28th, which is a Wednesday and therefore cannot use the clerk's Monday slate-wall wipe. The first repair pass corrected the first five of those and **left three behind — 17 December was still called a Friday, 20 December a Monday, and 25 December a Saturday — and the Batch 0005 review found them.** **All corrected**: the preparation is the first and the second, Bevin finds the core on **Monday the fifth**, the recovery is **Tuesday the sixth**, the public sheet goes up **Saturday the seventeenth**, the custody paper comes **Tuesday the twentieth**, the hearing room is **Wednesday the twenty-first**, the core is hung **Sunday the twenty-fifth**, and the volume ends on **Monday the twenty-sixth of December**, which is a Monday and therefore the day the wall is wiped, and which is **nine weeks and two days** from the first of March, also a **Wednesday**. The first pass's own grid in this file and in `state/continuity.md` had the 17th and the 20th wrong, and a Monday on the 20th with a Wednesday on the 21st was itself impossible; both grids are corrected.
2. **The supplementary paper was dated a Monday that does not exist.** It was first written as filed "on Monday morning, the thirtieth day of November", and November has no Monday on the thirtieth. **It is now filed at ten past ten on the morning of the 28th**, an hour before the room fills, which is also what Kest's Sunday-night entry requires ("in this office by ten o'clock on the morning of the twenty-eighth") and what makes the yard's admission of its own incomplete purpose land in the reeve's hand before the reeve opens his book. Every reference to "the thirtieth" is now "the morning of the sitting".
3. **The mole work and the *Vigil*'s paper were on the same days.** The first draft ran the three-day mole job on the 23rd to the 25th, which is the day the cutter's boat came in. **It is now the sixteenth to the eighteenth, before the door-shut reading of the twenty-second**, which is where Chapter 42 narrates it in the past tense, and it is paid in the road on the Saturday the eighteenth. The paper is the twenty-third, and Chapter 43's line about the chain still lying where the Saturday's work had left it was corrected, because it had the job running on the twenty-third.
4. **The seizure order's date was wrong three times.** Chapters 47, 48, 49 and 50 referred to *the eighth of October* as the seizure; the seizure was **the fourteenth**, and the eighth of October is the *Vigil*'s lodgment, which is a Navy paper and not a circuit one. All four corrected, and the custody-log entry now says the circuit office's first paper to this harbor was the fourteenth of October.
5. **The custody-room bar was attributed to the wrong clause.** The yard is not barred from the Morrow circuit custody room by the second clause of the notice, which is the shed clause; it is barred because **it is not the custodian and is not entered as one**, and because of the notice's handling limit. Corrected in Chapters 47, 48 and 50, and the claim entered on the 13th now says the claimant *is not entered as a custodian*.
6. **The midpoint said the wrong thing, and then the chapter said it in a way that did not follow.** `outline/volume-01.md` states the midpoint as the compartment being *too large to have been empty*, and the first draft had Nessa Pike say the crew had proved the room "could not have been empty", which is the same as saying it was full and would have contradicted the chapter's own reversal. She was changed to *the room cannot have been cleared*. **The Batch 0005 review then found the corrected chain was a non-sequitur as spoken**: the measurement excludes *this case* as the thing removed through *this opening*, which does not establish that the space was never cleared of anything else. **The chapter now carries the two missing steps on the page** — the space lies under a grating in a deck with no other opening, so the hatch is the only way anything has ever gone in or out of it; and this office's own thirteen-year-old wreck file, in a harbor's hand, gives number four as *grating in place, not cleared, contents not removed*. The entry of the 8th, the reason entered in the reeve's book and the boy's read-back all carry it, and the argument is now the chapter's own unused premise. **Still flagged for the volume auditor, not resolved silently** — see the deferred decisions, item 12.

7. **The plate's depth disagreed between two chapters.** The coaming was measured in "about eight and a half feet" in the entry of the 8th of December and in "nine and a quarter feet" at the mole face in Chapter 44. **Nine feet and a quarter at the mole face, eight and a quarter inside the coaming by a lead off a boat, not better than a foot on either figure, and nine feet in the entry** — and the core, which is a different object, is at eight and a half feet in three places and stays there.
8. **The coaming's outside measurement was dated twice.** It was "measured on the sixth from a boat" in the entry of the 8th; the sixth is the core and the outside figure was taken on the **twenty-seventh** in the slop. Corrected in the entry.
9. **The *Vigil* tender's days on station.** Thirty-nine on the 23rd, forty-four on the 28th, seventy-two on the 26th, carried forward from the page as the standing reading lock requires and **not computed from the calendar**; the first draft said forty-six on the 28th, which is the arithmetic of the fourteenth-of-October float date that the continuity lock says not to use. Corrected in Chapters 45 and 50.
10. **British spellings, volume-wide.** The first pass corrected *harbour*, *favour* and *apologise* in four chapters after a scan of the batch. The Batch 0005 review found the same class of slip surviving in Chapters 1, 3 and 37 *outside* the batch and noted that the state files' note covered the batch only, so it would reach publication as a volume-level inconsistency. **A volume-wide mechanical pass has now been run over `chapters/volume-01/`**: *harbour* ×4 (Ch1) and ×1 (Ch3) to *harbor*; *favour* ×2 (Ch37) to *favor*; *colour* in Ch1, Ch5 ×2, Ch22 and Ch24 to *color*; *neighbour*/*neighbours* in Ch28 ×3 and Ch39 ×2 to *neighbor(s)*; *apologising* in Ch3 and Ch4 to *apologizing*; and *summarise* in Ch42 and Ch47 to *summarize*. **A scan of all fifty chapters for the whole British-spelling class now returns nothing.** The ordinary lowercase use of *a system* in Chapter 46 — a bell's answer being an order of things and the opposite of a mystery — is not interface vocabulary and stands.
11. **Interface compliance.** No panels, stat blocks, character sheets, status messages, floating text, quest text, skill trees, level-ups, or software vocabulary. A scan of the ten chapters for `the system|stats|skills?|skill|mastery|status panel|notification|quest|level up|true memory|panel|API|protocol|backend|runtime|latency` returns nothing. **Chapters 41–50 therefore join Chapters 31–40 in containing no occurrence of the word *panel* at all**, and the only message-like objects used are the yard's purpose and supplement, the reeve's margin entry and Sunday entry, the reeve's chart notation and the suspended-instrument warning spoken aloud, the *Vigil*'s three terms and the yard's four-sentence answer, the count, the tender's written reason, the crew's book, the six-line public sheet and the market-wall nail, the custody-log entry with its seal line, the chart notation for the hung core, and a bell. **None of them tells Calder what to do next**; the one place where a document comes nearest to advising him is Kest's Sunday entry, and it is the reeve protecting the yard from the yard's own record, and Calder is told so and told it was not a trick. Standing bans observed: no "System," "stats," "skills/skill," "mastery," "status panel," "notification," "quest," "level up," "true memory." No Nine Harbors, Quietening, Crown Seal, or Crown Keel named, and the Office of Returning Memory is not named. **Ilya Serr appears three times, on a seal line and a custody-log seal line, always as *Magister Ilya Serr, senior Registry examiner*, never in a room and never as anything else.** Captain Veyra Holt is not named anywhere in the batch and the *Vigil*'s officer is on a quay, in a corner, and in a road.
12. **The money chain did not close, and it was off by five.** Chapter 42 read 34 on the board and 15 out and closed the bag at 19, but the bag already carried 5 from the 13th of November, so 5 + 34 − 15 = **24**, and Bevin Rook's readback in the same paragraph — *sixteen in, thirteen out* — matched no figure anywhere. The one chain is now the only chain and is checkable in the prose: the mole job of the **16th to the 18th** pays 34 and spends **twenty** — the harbor's eighth 3, the harbor's share 3, rope and the day's food 3, five marks of air and six on the chandler's note — which closes the bag at **19**; the 23rd's certified copy is entered against the 19 and **paid at the copying room on the 1st**; and the 1st spends **nineteen less three less eleven = five**, which is the arithmetic Chapter 46 already had. The **chandler's note is six marks for the three days of that job and none since**, which is the one figure Ch42, Ch45, `state/continuity.md` and the queued prompt now all carry; the previous three totals (nine in four days, six in four days, nine in eight days) are gone. **The bag is five from the 1st of December to the 26th and nothing has gone into it since the 18th of November**; the two state-file claims that it was *nil from the second of December* are withdrawn, because they contradicted Chapter 46 and Chapter 50.
