13. **Chapter 50 broke the volume's central concealment.** Calder's thought said the Oren association *has been entered in a municipal ledger, a crew's book, and a Registry conservator's certificate of method*, which is the opposite of the lock in Chapter 47's anchor description (*claimed by the holder and not established by any document*), Chapter 48's (*the yard does not state that the witness was any person it has known*) and Chapter 50's own Mara scene, and it undid the reason the stern board was chosen as the anchor. **Rewritten**: what is on the record is the claim and the refusal to name — a boy's fifteen seconds and no name in the ledger, a slate that says the same in the crew's book, four *not stated* entries and a hand that declined to fill in the fifth on the conservator's certificate — and **the slate would be the only place the truth could live**, which is the chapter's point and is now load-bearing rather than decorative.
14. **The delayed-note ledger gave the right totals and the wrong span twice.** *Six logged since the fourth of November* (Ch43) and *Eight logged since the fourth of November* (Ch50) both began the count after the 26th of October and the 2nd of November, which are inside it; Chapter 47 already had the right span. **Both now read *since the twenty-sixth of October***, in the one book whose whole function is to reconcile. The totals — six on the 23rd, eight on the 26th — are unchanged and check.
15. **Two checkable figures inside the batch.** The core came up in **forty-one minutes** and Bevin Rook read the entry back as *forty minutes*; and the coaming stands in **nine and a quarter feet at the toe with the clear opening in about nine**, while Chapter 47 put the diver measuring the opening *in eight and a half feet* — which is the **core's** depth, and the core lies two boat lengths seaward of the plate on a slope that keeps going down. **Both corrected to forty-one and to about nine**, and `state/continuity.md` carries the same.
16. **The tenth of December's money entry was headed with the entry date and read out on the Tuesday.** The entry is now headed *Tenth of December, read out on the thirteenth*, so the date of the work and the day it was read in the shed are distinguishable. The word *wages* is taken out of the book on **Wednesday the fourteenth**, the day after, which is what Ch48 and `state/continuity.md` now both say; the earlier "Wednesday the 16th" was impossible, the 16th being a Friday.
17. **A fourth-wall break in Chapter 42 and a garbled clause in Chapter 43.** The mole job's paragraph ended *which is what everybody in this trade does and which nobody in a novel ever mentions*, and Chapter 43's account of why the Navy may ask for the book ran *on a coast where that cutter's captain signed a document thirteen years ago that this harbor has never been allowed to read the paper under*, which is not intelligible. **Both rewritten in the world** — the first as *which no man in a fish market thinks is worth mentioning*, the second as *on a coast where a vessel of that cutter's service made a paper thirteen years ago about the same wreck, and this harbor has never been allowed to read it, and neither has this crew*.
18. **Chapter 49 had a part that does not exist and money the yard did have.** The sheet is in three parts and Mara certified the method *on the back of the fourth part*; and the line said *nobody in the yard had five marks to print it* when the bag held five. **The certificate is on the back of the third part, and the line now says there was no money to print it because the five marks in the bag were spoken for before the sheet was dry** — which keeps the chapter's point and makes it true.
19. **Chapter 50's day count for Mara Venn was a hundred and eleven days.** She told Calder on the 6th of December that she had been in the harbor four months, and 111 days before 26 December is 6 September. **It is four months now**, in the chapter and in the summary.
20. **The ten-yards lock was stated three ways.** `state/continuity.md` twice and `state/open-threads.md` once said the withdrawal was *in no record and on no wall*, against Batch 0004's own fresh market-wall sheet and Chapter 50's three sheets with the difference taken off them. **One version now:** the withdrawal is on a wall, in Calder's hand, under the Navy's paper, and **what is on no record and on no wall is the difference stated as a measurement**. Chapter 41's purpose carries the same sentence.
21. **The *Vigil* tender carried a day-count no chapter gave.** `state/continuity.md` asserted *sixty-seven on the 21st of December* as a lock, but no chapter states a day-count for the 21st; only the 23rd, the 28th and the 26th are on the page. **The file now carries only those three and instructs any later batch to interpolate at one a day and not to state an interpolated figure as though a chapter had given it.**


## Batch 0004 summary

**Batch goal, achieved.** Movement 4, *the hearing*: in eight days the Free Keel yard takes the sitting of the seventh, watches the *Vigil's* salvage claim go into the harbor authority's hearing record with the yard's three-word objection on the same page, puts the market wall itself under the Navy's paper, and re-derives its own geography without the bearing sheet it withdrew on the morning of the seventh — the load-bearing join being a piece of ordinary municipal iron with a saw kerf in its crown shackle, a fouled ground line of eastern make, and a harbor-authority list of eight years standing that names the boat the anchor came off. The *Vigil* moves to strike the reeve's own sighting of the fifth-seam plate as the fruit of his own breach, and is refused, at a price named aloud: the timber fenders, four sets of moorings, two herring berths, and **nineteen winters of herring dues**, disputed and entered against the harbor authority by a skipper called Elias Voke of the *Anna Ray*. A circuit office asks Mara Venn to certify the ground, she refuses, and the refusal is a note in her file and the query of the twenty-first is not closed. A lawful six-clause plan for the fifth seam is written, lodged and refused on four correct grounds, and the plate stays where it is. A boy of twenty-two asks for his wages, is given a claim instead of money, and the yard reorganizes around a hand that cannot close. Calder writes a two-hundred-and-forty-word reason with the brother left out of it, tells Mara Venn the thing he has been carrying since the fourteenth of October in nine seconds, and is told by her what the hiding has actually cost, which is not grief and is not a bell and is a false record assembling itself in a naval file. The three points are attacked on the interval and the yard **withdraws ten yards** and says the crew does not know. And on the fourteenth the defense of the twenty-fourth is **discharged**, the bottom goes back to the reeve, the mother is told in a room by a man in a coat, and the reeve raises of his own motion the question of a one-season provisional working charter, with a purpose due on the twenty-first and a sitting on the twenty-eighth. **No custody object returns. No echo entry is repeated. The compartment is still not proved. The Oren mark is still hidden, and the hiding now has a date and an office on it.**

**Actual word count, re-measured after the Batch 0004 review repairs:** **39,536 words across ten chapters** (Ch31 4,963 · Ch32 3,976 · Ch33 4,084 · Ch34 3,384 · Ch35 3,730 · Ch36 3,055 · Ch37 3,508 · Ch38 3,564 · Ch39 3,450 · Ch40 5,822), against `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s 2,200–3,200 for an ordinary chapter. **Every chapter is above the 2,200 floor.** Two chapters carry a hearing from end to end and are over the 3,200 guideline by a long way — **Ch31 (55 per cent over) and Ch40 (80 per cent over)** — and eight are between 3,055 and 3,692. Ch31 and Ch40 were trimmed twice after drafting and are still the longest chapters in the manuscript. **This is recorded rather than defended, and the next batch should treat it as the fault it is:** a hearing with ten terms in it, and a sitting with four separate matters in it, are two scenes that will not compress to size without dropping a ruling or a party, and the batch's shape would have been better served by one of them living in a chapter of its own. The honest reading is that the batch is long because two chapters took two rooms each. **The repair pass moved words rather than adding them: Chapter 32 lost the duplicated interval exchange and Chapter 33 gained the single one, so Chapter 33 is now 28 per cent past the guideline and carries the batch's only statement by the officer of what a difference of ten yards is.** Total is 7,271 words over Batch 0003's 32,265.

**The batch's midpoint turn is Chapter 36.** Chapters 31–33 take the room and the Crown's claim and give the yard's own geometry away; 34 is the reeve's price; 35 is the conservator's; 36 is where the batch turns, because the plan comes back refused on four grounds that are all correct, the fifth seam is unreach-able, the bottom the yard won on the seventh is worth nothing, and the only work in the harbor is two seams landward of a closure on a wall — and the yard takes it. Everything after that is a crew that has worked, arguing about what it is allowed to say.

**Conflicts found and how they were resolved:**

1. **The calendar did not fit the first draft and had to be rebuilt.** Batch 0003 locked 31 October as a Monday and 1 November as a Tuesday, so **the hearing of the seventh is a Monday**, and every weekday in the batch was wrong on the first pass. Corrected throughout: **Mon 7th, Tue 8th, Thu 10th, Fri 11th, Sat 12th, Sun 13th, Mon 14th**, with Wednesday the 9th unwritten. The move is load-bearing rather than cosmetic: the harbor authority's clerk **wipes the hearing room's slate wall every Monday at eight o'clock whether there is a sitting or not**, so the yard's three points are wiped off the wall twice — on the morning of the seventh, before the terms are read, and again on the morning of the fourteenth — and Kest re-chalks them himself in his own hand on the tenth. That fact is not in Batch 0003 and is now canon.
2. **The naval claim was four sheets read on the wrong day.** The first draft had the claim entered on the seventh at four and read the same afternoon; the *Vigil*'s boat arrives at four on the seventh and Kest **refuses to read four sheets in a hurry with a tired clerk**, adjourns to ten o'clock on the eighth, and enters the lodgment and the delay both in the record. The claim is therefore **tendered on the seventh at four and entered on the eighth at ten**, twenty-four days after it was lodged, and the three-word objection goes on the face of the second sheet itself so that the claim and the objection lie on one page.
3. **A fourth-wall artifact.** Chapter 40 carried a self-interrupted sentence referring to a chapter number. Cut and rewritten in the world.
4. **The fouled line's custody.** The first draft had Calder's own lead-line tag coming up on the harbor's crane, which contradicts Chapter 16 (Calder's lead line had parted; the float was Kest's). The tag is now **the harbor authority's own board tag on its own ground tackle**, with the reeve's rag float and the chalk seam number as the only legible part, and the authority's store as its home.
5. **The phrase "eleven hundred and forty pounds of paper"** in Chapter 39 was a mass where the book counts money. Now the pledge out of the clerk's hands with not one mark behind it.
6. **The interval's arithmetic and the first point's nature.** The withdrawal of ten yards is not only a methodological point: the first point is **not a point at all** but a bedded seam running north to about two boat lengths, and the yard had been silently using its south end. That is Nessa Pike's finding, made at the bench, against Calder's testimony ambition, and the second withdrawal in the chapter is **Bevin Rook giving his own count of seven and eleven against the tender's nine and ten and a half** — the figure he declined to enter on the twenty-eighth of October because it could not be checked, now entered because it is the only check there is.
7. **A new consent form, and a new refusal.** The ten crew-only printed copies are not surrendered and are not sealed: they are shown **on the table in the hearing room, one at a time, in daylight, with the clerk**, and cannot be carried off it. The officer's man counts ten on the market wall and on three other crews' walls and five in the chest, writes the number and the hour on his own slate, and asks for none of them.
8. **The reading claim's figures.** The claim is **sixteen marks for the reading of the book from the fourteenth of October, four marks the week for four weeks**, entered separately from the **twenty-four marks** of hand wages, so the yard now owes a boy of twenty-two **forty marks** and the tin is empty. Both claims stand and neither is reduced.
9. **A near-duplicate closing line.** The delayed note recurs twice in this batch (the eighth and the fourteenth) and the two sentences were too close to Chapter 30's. Both were rewritten, and the eighth's is the hearing record of a sound a naval officer and a yard's boy logged in the same minute, which is the batch's only new fact about it.
10. **Interface compliance.** No panels, stat blocks, character sheets, status messages, floating text, quest text, skill trees, level-ups, or software vocabulary. The one occurrence of the word "panel" in the batch was a glass panel and has been changed to "the thing at the fifth seam" so that a mechanical scan of the batch for interface vocabulary returns nothing at all. Standing bans observed: no "System," "stats," "skills/skill," "mastery," "status panel," "notification," "quest," "level up," "true memory." No Nine Harbors, Quietening, Crown Seal, or Crown Keel named. Ilya Serr appears **once, on a seal line**, as *Magister Ilya Serr, senior Registry examiner*, and is not present, not named in speech, and not a minister.

## Batch 0004 review repairs

A reviewer pass over Chapters 31–40 and the four state files returned **FIX**: ten required changes, eleven prose and state improvements, and one deviation to record rather than fix. The reviewer's own log is not kept in this repository; this is the disposition of every finding. **No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut for length, and no planned plot was altered** — Movement 5, the charter, the salvage share, the bell-buoy core, the fragment, and the ending are all as the volume outline has them.

1. **The closing image of Chapter 40 had the March dates wrong by fifteen weeks and internally inverted** — *the fifth of March was a fortnight away, and the first of March was a fortnight and a half away*. The fourteenth of November is about fifteen weeks off the first of March, and the fifth cannot be nearer than the first. **Now:** *the first of March was fifteen weeks off and the fifth of March was four days past it*, which is what the whole batch's first-of-March pressure needs.
2. **The money chain did not close, and it had propagated into three state files and the Batch 0005 prompt.** Three incompatible dispositions of Voke's fourteen marks and two incompatible bag figures (five and nine) were on the page, and the note figure had no basis. **One chain is now the only chain, and it is checkable in the prose:** on the twelfth three marks go to the copying room and come back, and two of the five are set against the note; on the thirteenth Voke pays fourteen and Calder disposes of all of it in the open — four to the air, two to rope and the day's food, two to the harbor's share, four to the note, with Saturday's two going on the same night, which is **six on the note and the first money on it since the twenty-fifth of last month** — and two of the fourteen stay in the bag, so the bag closes at **five**, the figure the state files and the next prompt already carried. Chapter 40's closing image is now *five marks in a bag* instead of nine, and Chapter 37's own breakdown of the five (two air, two of Voke's money, one left from a Saturday in October) makes the arithmetic checkable. The false line that the yard had had no money in it since the twenty-ninth is gone with it.
3. **The first point's depth broke between Batch 0003 and Batch 0004.** Chapter 28 read the tender's count as *ten feet and ten and a half* and Chapter 31 as *at ten feet*, while the figure now in the municipal hearing record is *nine and ten and a half*, and Chapter 39's own arithmetic (*two feet out on the first depth*) only closes on nine. **The hearing record wins, because it is what four men checked and what the diver's own seven and eleven is now set against.** Chapter 28 reads *nine feet at the south end and ten and a half at the north end*, Chapter 31's reeve says *nine feet*, and `state/continuity.md` carries the same figure instead of a third variant.
4. **A unit error on the batch's central join.** The freed shoal iron came up at **eight fathoms** in Chapter 4; Chapter 33 said it came up out of eight feet. **Now fathoms**, which is also what the seventy words after it already said.
5. **Bevin Rook's elapsed time was inflated about two and a half times.** The hand was set on the thirtieth of October, so on the thirteenth and fourteenth it is **a fortnight** along, not four, five or six weeks. Corrected in Chapter 32 (now in Chapter 33, where the line moved), twice in Chapter 37, once in Chapter 39, in `state/current.md`, `state/continuity.md`, and the Batch 0005 prompt. **Two figures were deliberately not touched**, because they are the claim period and both are correct: the twenty-four marks at four a week for six weeks from the twenty-ninth of October to the tenth of December, and the sixteen marks at four a week for four weeks from the fourteenth of October for the reading.
6. **Chapters 32 and 33 both staged the Tuesday the eighth, both ended at five o'clock, and both put the officer's interval question with a forty-nine-word verbatim recital.** **There is now one interval exchange in the batch and it is at four-forty in Chapter 33**: the officer puts the question to the yard, Kest rules it stands unanswered and gives the yard until the fourteenth either way, and the officer then states his own view of it, entered as a statement and not as an answer. Chapter 32's morning now ends where it should — just before two, with the two motions, a recess to half past two, and Kest telling the room in the open that one matter is still to come. Chapter 33's adjournment is the batch's only five o'clock, its *Monday* is now the *Tuesday* it was, and its length grew by the exchange Chapter 32 gave up.
7. **The same speaker gave two different drawer counts in one sitting** — twenty-three days at the top of Chapter 31 and twenty-four at the bottom. The lodgment was the fourteenth of October and the entry the seventh of November. **Twenty-four throughout**, which is also what the clerk is made to enter.
8. **A wrong weekday in Chapter 35.** The circuit's request arrived on *the Tuesday the eleventh*; the eleventh is a **Friday** on the locked calendar, and the chapter's own summary already had it right. **Now Friday**, and the Tuesday that remains in the chapter is the eighth, when Bevin's four sheets went up.
9. **Three year anchors collided.** Voke, at fifty-one, claimed adult experience in 1908, which is impossible beside Tobias in the yard since 1961 and Alden learning to read through a cough in about 1958. **The 1908 is gone** and Voke's line now carries no year at all — *I have not been proud of it since I was a boy in another man's boat* — so the calendar keeps the two references that are load-bearing and drops the one that was decoration.
10. **A state file asserted a verification that does not exist.** `state/open-threads.md` claimed that mechanical scans for panel and software vocabulary *return nothing across all forty chapters*. They do not: *panel* appears in Chapters 5, 6, 7, 26, 27 and 29, including a chapter title, and every one of them is a sheet of glass. **The claim is now the narrow one that is true** — Chapters 31–40 are clean of the word and none of the interface furniture is in any chapter, and no occurrence anywhere is a panel that is not glass. The same false sentence was in the Batch 0005 prompt and is corrected there too. One incidental gain: the deferred-decisions list no longer uses *a held panel* as an example of a shaken hand, which would have kept the word in front of a later writer.

**Prose and state improvements, applied.** The period-comma in Chapter 39; **twenty-three hours** corrected to the **eighteen** between four in the afternoon and ten in the morning; the officer's *I have no answer to a word of it* in Chapter 32, which he then contradicted twice, replaced with a line that keeps his position and survives his own answers; **the dangling second motion resolved** — entered as not heard and not refused, withdrawn by the vessel in Chapter 33 with the reason left in the room as it was given; **the Second Bell grounded** in Chapter 40, where the term had appeared for the first and only time inside the batch's largest instrument and collided with the tinnitus, and it now says what kind of thing it is and that no man in the room can hand one out; Voke's dues claim no longer presented as newly entered in Chapter 40, since Chapter 34 entered it on the tenth, and he is given a second slip; **the hearing-room slate wall given one chronology** — wiped at eight on the Monday the seventh, re-chalked by the reeve on the tenth, wiped again on the fourteenth — so that Chapters 34, 35 and 39 no longer disagree about which morning the cloth came off; **the harbor's board tag no longer said it went down on the twentieth**, which is the day the iron came up.

**Recorded, not fixed.** The batch's own fault stands on the record and is not defended: four consecutive hearing-room chapters and two chapters far past the length guideline. The repair pass did not split or pad anything, because the guideline permits a long chapter for a complete scene and forbids both, and because the root cause is the shape of Movement 4 rather than the length of any one scene. **The next batch inherits the note** — its prompt already carries the instruction to alternate pressure deliberately and not to open two hearings in one day.

**One deviation flagged for the volume auditor, on the Batch 0003 precedent.** `workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` hard rule 12 and its required character changes asked Calder to hide the Oren-specific mark again. **Chapter 38 has him tell Mara Venn instead, in about nine seconds.** It is defensible against `outline/volume-01.md`, which asks for the concealment at the **volume boundary**, and it is the stronger beat, because what it costs him is not grief but a false record assembling itself in a naval file. It also reverses a card instruction outright, so it is now carried in `state/open-threads.md` (Batch 0004 block and deferred decision 9) and in `state/continuity.md`'s proposed-canon list, item 9, for an outline phase to rule on. **Binding meanwhile: Chapters 41–50 must end with the mark still hidden from every office, from Kest, and from his father.**

## Batch 0003 summary

**Batch goal, achieved.** Movement 3, *the ordered chain*: in ten consecutive days the Free Keel yard turns its extension into a lawful positioning plan at a distance, adds a fourth document and a second witness to the Gannet record, trains Bevin Rook as bottom man and Calder Marr as tender, breaks a hand and loses the Free Keel tender arrangement, has Mara Venn's own handling note quoted back at the crew by her own office, watches her answer it correctly and in three weeks, lands three points and one line at a mile and two hundred yards of even spacing, refuses the one panel the yard could have touched, and ends on a written bill with the notice on the wall and the defense still ticking. **No shoal ground past the bar is entered. No custody object returns. No echo entry is repeated. The compartment is still not proved.**

**The batch's midpoint turn is Chapter 26.** Chapters 21–25 run uphill and pay for themselves: the plan, the file, the training, the descent, the hand. Chapter 26 is where the batch turns, because the thing that has been holding the crew back all along — a conservator's careful sentence about a broken edge on a bench — is reissued as a notice with a seal, and the water closes. Everything after it is the crew discovering what it can still do with a bar on the mole: a second note in three weeks, three points on a wall, a panel refused, and a bill.

**Actual word count:** **32,265 words across ten chapters** (Ch21 3,649 · Ch22 3,215 · Ch23 3,340 · Ch24 2,620 · Ch25 3,011 · Ch26 2,641 · Ch27 3,181 · Ch28 3,721 · Ch29 3,815 · Ch30 3,072), against `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s 2,200–3,200 for an ordinary chapter. **Every chapter is above the 2,200 floor**, which is the specific fault the Batch 0002 review left on the record, and three chapters run 14–19 per cent past the 3,200 guideline (Ch21, Ch28, Ch29), which `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` allows for complete scenes and which are the three chapters that each carry a discovery, a reeve's decision, or a refusal. The batch is two and a half times Batch 0002's 12,864 words, which is the difference between ten scenes and ten compressed ones. Figures measured after the Batch 0003 review repairs and binding on the next batch.

**Conflicts found and how they were resolved:**

1. **The third point's geography.** The first draft set the even interval at a mile and forty yards, which put the office's sounding station at better than six miles off the mole and outside the pale run's known extent of about two and a half miles, and set thirty-six figures on a course that could not hold them. **Resolved by rebuilding the interval:** first to second is a mile and two hundred yards and second to third is a mile and two hundred and ten yards, the difference is ten yards, the shoal's inner edge sits just inside the outer marks a mile and a quarter off the mole, and the third point lies at about two miles and a quarter, inside the officer's water and inside the pale run's known extent. The whole chain is now consistent with the fixed numbers and no chapter contradicts another.
2. **The hump's size.** The first draft read the shoal book's anomaly as *sixteen inches*, which is arithmetically wrong: 15:9 against 15:7 is two inches, and a two-inch difference is nothing. **Resolved** by resetting the figures — the station reads **fifteen fathoms and two corrected, the wire paid out to eighteen fathoms and two**, against neighbours at **sixteen fathoms and three** and a firm-clay flat — so the rise is **a foot and an inch**, in one figure out of thirty-six on two miles of course. Kest's added explanation is that an *uncorrected* line puts the station within two fathoms of its neighbours, so the office's own correction column is the only reason the figure has ever been legible at all.
3. **Kest's age and tenure.** Chapter 22 first had him say he was twenty-two in the year after the Gannet Cut. He is forty-four (`bible/characters.md`) and the Cut was thirteen years ago. **He was thirty-one in the year after the Cut and had been two years in the office, which makes him forty-four and fifteen years in the office now.** Chapter 29's two references to eleven years, and Chapter 21's, are now fifteen.
4. **A false date in Chapter 27.** A closing line referred to a slate written "on the seventh of November." No such slate exists. **It is now "since the fourteenth of last month,"** which is Chapter 8's slate and the true date.
5. **The *Vigil* tender's days on station.** The floats went out on the 14th of last month; Chapter 30 first said twenty-one days. **It is twenty days** in both places.
6. **Terms counted wrong inside the chapter.** Chapter 29 said the yard had eleven terms to write and Chapter 30 then listed ten. **Both are ten**, and Chapter 30 now says that Nessa cut one of them and ten went in, which is what the chapter shows; no drafted total is claimed anywhere, because the chapter does not give one.
7. **Mara Venn misdescribed as nineteen.** Tobias's line about the missing chisel put "a girl of nineteen" into **Calder's** mouth, and Mara is thirty. **Calder's line is now "Which of them,"** and she is named in Tobias's own mouth two lines later as *the Registry one, your conservator*, and again as *a woman in a Registry coat* in his line before it.
8. **The chisel thread was narrowed on purpose.** Tobias's missing offset chisel began as an implication against Mara and was rewritten so that what is on the page is Calder's *refusal* to make anything of it, entered in the book with the reason. **It opens no mystery and accuses nobody**, and it is the only new personal object in the batch.
9. **Kest's foresight was a plot convenience.** He first wrote a six-clause instrument on the 31st naming the fifth seam *before* anything had been seen there, which read as luck. **Rewritten so that the choice is character:** on the 31st he named the fifth seam and eleven feet because it is the one piece of that face where a man's hand reaches the bottom off a step, he had no reason to think anything was there, and he wrote the paper so that a man with a plain instrument in his pocket at eleven o'clock on the morning he finds something is not standing in a shed with his hands up. He kept it back until there was something to point it at, **because showing a yard that paper on the 31st would have sent them out at night to look for whatever was under it.**
10. **Three internal errors in Chapter 29's instrument and entries.** The reeve's witness line named four yard hands for an entry that says three; the countersign clause was attributed to clause one of the notice when it is clause three; and clause six referred to itself. **All three corrected** — witnesses are Marr, Pike and Rook, the clause is the third, and clause six now refers to the conditions of the instrument.
11. **Money arithmetic did not add up twice.** The batch chain buys air on the 28th (six), the 29th (one) and the 1st (two), which is **nine marks, not ten**; and Chapter 30's balance sheet said eighteen marks gone where twenty-one are gone, and omitted the six repaid to Brigg from the list of outgoings. **Both corrected** in the chapter and in all four state files. The bag still closes at **five marks** and the pot is still a third gone.

**Interface compliance:** no panels, stat blocks, character sheets, status messages, floating text, quest text, skill trees, level-ups, or software vocabulary. Message-like objects used, all world-native and physical: the shoal book's correction column, the reeve's four lists of permitted acts, the shoal book read in room and not copied, the harbor's 13-year-old wreck entry, the circuit office's four-clause notice with a seal over the nail, the *Vigil* officer's one-page request, the reeve's six-clause instrument, the reeve's notation on the harbor chart, the crew's book, the yard's own board copy of the notice, the three sheets and the fifth-seam sheet on the market wall, and the ten written terms for the seventh. **None of them tells Calder what to do next.** The reeve's six clauses are a reeve's paper; the ten written terms are Calder's own requests, written by him for a hearing he has not yet had. Standing bans observed: no "System," "stats," "skills/skill," "mastery," "status panel," "notification," "quest," "level up," "true memory." No Nine Harbors, Quietening, or Crown Seal named. Ilya Serr appears in this batch only by the absence of his office's name on a notice; he is not named, not present, and no seal line of his is quoted.

## Batch 0003 review repairs (recorded, not hidden)

The review of Batch 0003 returned FIX. Every actionable finding was repaired in Chapters 21–30 and in the four state files and the Batch 0004 prompt, without restarting the batch and without changing the plot; the review itself and the full finding list are in `reviews/volume-01/batch-0003.md`. Nothing in the batch was rewritten, and no chapter lost a scene. The items a later batch would otherwise have inherited wrong:

- **Which sentence of the filed purpose the notice omitted.** Chapter 26 said the notice left out the purpose's *closing* sentence. The purpose in Chapter 22 closes on the sentence about the file of thirteen years ago; the sentence the notice dropped is the one in which the yard said **it had no measurement of the assertion it was making**, which is the sentence Kest's whole reaction in Chapter 22 is to. Chapter 26, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, and the Batch 0004 prompt now name that sentence, and Chapter 22's pointer ("Kest read the last line twice") now points at it.
- **Mara's age, in the wrong mouth.** "A girl of nineteen" was in **Calder's** line, not Tobias's. Calder now says "Which of them," and Tobias names her himself.
- **The harbor's payment on the 29th.** The narration said the clerk paid **nine** of the twenty-six in one sentence and eighteen in the next, the yard's count said eighteen, the book's entry said eighteen, and Chapter 25's mother read "the nine of the twenty-six" off a line that does not contain it. **Eighteen everywhere.**
- **The terms that went in.** Chapter 30 said Nessa cut one and "the nine that went in are these," then listed ten. **Ten going in**, and no drafted total is claimed anywhere, because the page does not give one.
- **Bevin's hands.** Chapter 29 had the crushed hand in the right sling and the left arm bare. **The left hand is crushed and the left arm is slinged**, in every chapter that touches it.
- **The witnesses.** Kest told the crew "the book names the four of you as witnesses" over an entry that reads *Witnesses: Marr, Pike, Rook.* **Three witnesses named; four men saw it**, which is what Chapter 29's own written refusal says. Corrected in the chapter, in `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, and `state/continuity.md`.
- **Kest's tenure.** Chapter 22 puts him at thirty-one with two years in the office thirteen years ago, which is **fifteen years in it now**. All three of the "eleven years" lines are now fifteen.
- **The tide state of the fifth-seam sighting.** The reeve's entry, the instrument, the wall sheet, and `state/continuity.md` all said the look was at the top of the tide, while the scene's optics said a falling tide. **The optics were aligned to the top of the tide**, because Chapter 23 had already established the harbor's own rule — at the top of the flood the water goes to a sheet of glare and the bottom shows — and because the record, the instrument and the wall sheet all hang together. Chapter 28's "falling tide" line and its "Falling, third of the make" log entry, which contradicted the tide bell in the same scene, were aligned to the same rule. **The rule is now locked in `state/continuity.md`: in Morrow Quay the slop goes to glare at the top of the flood.**
- **The defense's expiry.** Chapter 25's father said "a defense that expires in eight days" and Chapter 24's Brigg said "in eight days"; the term runs to the **14th**, which is fifteen days from the 30th and sixteen from the 29th. Both corrected. The "twenty-day defense" label is retired in the prose and in the state files, because the 24th to the 14th is **twenty-one days**; the offer of "twenty days" in Chapters 14 and 19 is left as written, since that is what was said in the room.
- **Intervals to the hearing.** The hearing is the 7th and Kest moved it there from the 10th in Chapter 23, so every interval before that move counts to the 10th and every interval after it counts to the 7th. Chapter 21 now says fifteen days and Chapter 22 fourteen, which is the 10th; Chapter 27 is the 1st and has six days; Chapter 29 has four; Chapter 30 has three. Chapter 29's "the hearing is the day after tomorrow" is now four days off.
- **The calendar and the month.** The batch runs the 26th to the 4th across a month boundary: the 26th to the 31st are in a **31-day month** and the 1st to the 7th are in the next one, which is **November**. Four of the five November references were impossible and are corrected — the conservator's note is "dated the twenty-second of this month," the six-day slate shelf is "since the fourteenth of last month," the derelict lead-line was in use on that ground "last winter," and Nessa's silence is "the day before." The chapter's date is now **Tuesday the 1st**, which is what `state/continuity.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` always said and what Chapter 27's own "on Monday the word was on a notice" and "four o'clock yesterday afternoon" require. The autumn equinox in this calendar falls on about the twentieth of October, so the 3rd of November is a fortnight past it, not three days. Chapter 27's two references to April for the conservator's note of the 14th are corrected to the 14th.
- **Chapter 24's dive clock.** Bevin went over at a quarter to eight and the call ran out at "four minutes to nine," an hour apart. **The twelve minutes now go at four minutes to eight**, which is what the tender's call of twelve given and thirteen given requires, and which leaves the boat's turn at ten intact.
- **Chapter 21's leftovers.** The bar on the mole was given on the 18th and Chapter 21 said he did not go out "on the nineteenth day"; the clause is gone and the paragraph's point stands. The promise of a figure "on a Thursday morning" with the memory "two days old" is kept, and **Chapter 28 now says why the reading came on Wednesday the 2nd instead** — the Thursday went on the purpose in the hearing room and the Friday and Saturday on a notice with a seal on the nail, and by the time there was a morning to give the figure away, the yard wanted it five days before a hearing and not two.
- **Chapter 28's reason for going up the mole.** It rested on a reading of the notice that contradicted the reeve's own signed station note quoted eighty lines earlier. **The bearing now rests on the sheet Kest signed on the 28th and on nothing else that is written**, which is what the chapter had already established.
- **The crew's copies.** One object had three numbers. **Ten copies off the press in Chapter 18: two on the market wall beside the original, three on other crews' walls, five rolled in oilcloth in the bench chest.** Chapters 28 and 30 and `state/continuity.md` now agree.
- **The delayed note.** It was logged **three** times in this batch (the 26th, the 2nd, the 4th) and heard five, the 28th's chain alarm and the 29th at the top of the make both going unentered. The state files said four logged and listed the 29th among them, which is the one occasion on the page where he had no book at all. Corrected in the chapter index, `state/current.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, and hard rule 10 of the Batch 0004 prompt.
- **Chapter 30's units and its count.** Air is not bought by the ton in a book that counts it in marks and thirds of a pot; it is **nine marks of air**. The margin formula *Note entered by Marr. Stated to the crew* is **eight words**, not four, and `state/chapter-summaries.md` no longer miscounts it either.
- **Verbatim repetition.** "A third of a second late and much too even to be anything" appeared four times across the manuscript, twice in near-identical sentences in Chapters 28 and 30. **The clause is now verbatim once in the batch, in Chapter 30**, and Chapter 21 and Chapter 28 carry the same motif in different words. "That was the arrangement" was closing eight scenes; it is now three, and the load-bearing "read it back" was left alone.
- **A volume-outline deviation, flagged rather than applied.** `outline/volume-01.md` states Movement 3's finding as *positioned*, not scattered. Chapters 28–30 deliberately stop at **order shown and purpose unknown** and strike the word *placed* out of the yard's own copy of the purpose. This is stronger than the card and it is recorded in `state/open-threads.md` and `state/continuity.md` for the volume auditor rather than resolved here. **No new final enemy, no new institution, and no change to the planned ending.**
- **Not fixable from a writer phase.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is still at `phase-000-bootstrap` and cannot be advanced from here, so `workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` is not yet authorized to run. The gap is already recorded for the controller in `state/open-threads.md` and is restated in that file's controller section.

## Batch 0002 summary

**Batch goal, achieved.** Movement 2, *the leash*: Calder funds weather-exposed ordinary work inside the harbor line, holds the crew through a poor month, has his family's name put on the record at the batch's hearing, faces a rival Free Keel claim on the freed shoal iron, wins a narrow extension to the 14th of next month with a bond of twelve marks plus held gear and a harbor eighth off any future raising, and ends tending rather than diving, with the naval claim still closed over the shoal. No dive past the four-hundred-yard limit, no second echo entry, no glass returned, no compartment proved.

**The batch's midpoint turn is Chapter 15.** Chapters 11–14 run the wrong way: refused work, a gale, a hearing-room refusal, Kest's bond. Chapter 15 is where Calder stops defending and spends — the *Kittiwake*'s November slip tides given to the Free Keel for inside-the-line rescue cover, with the cradle deferred to spring and the hull taking the wear. Everything after it (the buoy day, the printing, the extension) is a crew that can be trusted to turn up, which is what makes the 24th survivable. That turn is dramatized rather than asserted: Tobias tells him on the slip what a hard beach does to a garboard strake, and Calder stops because he cannot argue with the man who has to fix it.

**Actual word count:** **12,864 words across ten chapters** (Ch11 1,262 · Ch12 1,055 · Ch13 967 · Ch14 1,005 · Ch15 1,061 · Ch16 1,963 · Ch17 1,133 · Ch18 1,530 · Ch19 1,798 · Ch20 1,090), against `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s 2,200–3,200 for an ordinary chapter and Chapters 1–10's 34,329. **The batch as first written was 8,002 words and every chapter was under the floor**; the review returned FIX on the collapse, and Chapters 16, 18, 19, and 20 were given their scenes back, with smaller repairs in 12, 13, 14, 15, and 17. What was done: Chapter 16's Bevin got a real first working dive with the tender's clock and the number he argues about, the air line got read aloud, and the money got counted in a closed room; Chapter 18 gained the Saturday tide job that funds the printing and the bond, so the batch's engine is now visible labor; Chapter 19 gained the naval officer's question about the yard's name, a valuation of what the crew actually holds, and the argument over the pump; Chapter 20 gained the beaching of the boat, the air pot's true state, and a written bill that reconciles every mark in the batch. **What was not fixed:** Chapters 11, 13, 14, and 15 are still under 1,300 words, and no chapter in this batch reaches 2,200. Expanding Chapters 11–15 and 17 further would mean re-planning those scenes rather than repairing them, and the review's own remedy allowed for recording the position honestly. **The figures are here so that Batch 0003 does not read this batch's compression as precedent:** the next batch's floor is 2,200 a chapter, and the reason this one is shorter is that ten compressed scenes were repaired rather than rewritten.

**Conflicts found and how they were resolved:**

1. Calendar: Batch 0001 ran Tue 11th–Fri 14th. Batch 0002 runs Sat 15th–Tue 25th (11: Sat poor week; 12: Sun gale; 13: Mon 17th; 14: Tue 18th; 15: Wed 19th; 16: Thu 20th; 17: Fri 21st; 18: Sat 22nd; 19: Mon 24th sitting; 20: Tue 25th). Sunday 23rd is an unwritten rest day; no events placed there.
2. The twelve-day defense entered Fri 14th would lapse ~27th; the Mon 24th sitting extends it on new terms rather than renewing the same twelve days. Recorded as extension, not second window. Kest's offer in the room was "twenty days further"; **the term as entered runs to the 14th of next month**, which is twenty-one days from the 24th, and every later document and state file says the 14th and never a count.
3. The mole anchor lift: Kest orders it on Tuesday the 18th and it happens on **Thursday the 20th**, in Chapter 16, watched from the fish dock rail. The first draft ordered it for "tomorrow," which put it on the 19th and against Chapter 16.
4. Ordinary diving while under echo aftercare: Ch12 and Ch16 dives are ordinary harbor work inside the line under the Morrow Quay charter, not echo entries. Ch12 is a short surge dive that reopens the burn and triggers Nessa's standing shutdown; Ch16 and Ch18 put Bevin on the bottom with Calder tending. No bell entry occurs; the two-clean-daylight-looks rule for echo work is never lifted.
5. **The money had to be made to close.** The first draft spent the printing money and then claimed a bigger bond than existed. The fix is a job, not a fudge: Saturday the 22nd's tide work on the inner mud pays 24 on the board, the harbor takes its charter share, the crew hires the harbor's pump, and what is left buys the ten printed copies and the bond. The whole chain, mark by mark, is in `state/continuity.md` under "the batch's money chain" and is binding on Chapter 21 onward.
6. **The air pot was described as partly rebuilt and is not.** A third of the pot is a hundred marks' worth (Chapter 3) and the batch bought fifteen marks of air. It is a third gone on the 15th and a third gone on the 25th, and the margin is gone rather than restored. Chapter 16 has Bevin read the line out loud and Chapter 20 has Calder say it.
7. **The family beat moved into the hearing.** `outline/volume-01.md` Movement 2 requires the Gannet connection to be used against Calder in a harbor authority hearing. It was in the harbor clerk's office in Chapter 13 and nowhere in the hearing. It is now in both: Chapter 19's naval officer puts the yard's name on the record, Calder refuses to defend his father's report and answers with Kest's own countersigned log, and the officer's note goes into the record as the officer's question. **This is a restoration of the volume's planned beat, not a new one, and it must not be re-run in a later volume.**

**Interface compliance:** no panels, stat blocks, sheets, floating text, prompts, trees, level-ups, or software vocabulary. Message-like objects only: labor log, crew's book, reeve's slates, hearing-room slate wall, harbor notices, Registry memorandum and certificate, custody record references, lead-line tag and rag float (harbor lift), buoy bell and working bells, printed duplicate copies. None tells Calder what to do next. Standing bans observed: no "System," "stats," "skills/skill," "mastery," "status panel," "notification," "quest," "level up," "true memory." No Nine Harbors, Quietening, or Crown Seal named. Ilya Serr appears only as name/seal of a senior Registry examiner.

## Batch 0002 review repairs (recorded, not hidden)

The review of Batch 0002 returned FIX. All actionable findings were repaired in the chapters and in the four state files, without restarting the batch and without changing the plot; the full list is in the Batch 0002 section of `state/continuity.md`. The items a later batch would otherwise have inherited wrong:

- **A fourth-wall reference in Chapter 11's closing paragraph** ("the iron nobody had wanted in Chapter 8") is now in-world.
- **The mole lift's date**, which contradicted Chapter 16 by a day.
- **The bond tin and the printing bill**, which did not reconcile in any of Chapters 18–20; both are now funded by a job on the page, and the mark-by-mark chain is locked in `state/continuity.md`.
- **The chandler's note**, which gained a week in two days in Chapter 13 and had already propagated "twelve weeks into six" into four state files and the Batch 0003 prompt. It is **eleven weeks into six** in Chapter 13 and everywhere else, with six marks paid off it.
- **The *Vigil* tender's distance in Chapter 20**, which used the mile-and-a-half figure that belongs to the officer's limit rather than to a boat. She is a quarter of a mile off the mole, as in Chapters 10 and 16, and the figure is now locked in `state/continuity.md`.
- **The air pot's false "partly rebuilt"**, replaced with the arithmetic Chapter 3 already supports.
- **The Movement 2 family beat**, restored to the hearing in Chapter 19.
- **"Free Keel pledge"**, which renamed a canon instrument. The crews' pledge is the **Free Keel charter** (`bible/terminology.md`, `bible/world.md`), and Chapters 11 and 15 now conform.
- **Chapter 16's payment terms and its ambiguous antecedent** at the mole lift: a one-day job paid on the buoy master's count and the clerk's slip, and the cheering crew identified as the harbor's own with Calder off the wall, as Chapter 14 ordered him to be.
- **Sella Brigg and Harl Dace** were named on the page and recorded in no state file, which would have dropped Harl Dace before a later writer knew he existed. Both are now in `state/continuity.md` under "Cast added by Batch 0002" and in the Batch 0003 prompt.
- **`NOVEL_SPEC.md`** pointed at Batch 0002 as the next writing phase, and the Batch 0003 prompt tells the next writer to read it first. Status updated.

## Batch 0001 summary

**Batch goal, achieved.** Calder's ordinary salvage life and the Gannet debt are established; the first transparent hull is uncovered through a legitimate paid job and a two-date work permit; Calder and Mara are forced into a bounded cooperation neither wanted; the tide-ear procedure is assembled in the yard and used once, at physical cost; both pieces of glass leave the deck in one witnessed surrender; the provenance record, the freed shoal iron, and the numbers survive; and the *Vigil* arrives as paperwork and berth. The batch ends on Calder's own spoken decision, not on a threat.

**Actual word count:** approximately 34,300 words across ten chapters, against a 25,000–29,000 batch guide in `outline/batches/volume-01-batch-0001.md`. The honest per-chapter position, measured after the Batch 0001 review repairs: **eight of the ten chapters run past their own card's ceiling** (1: ~3,373 against 3,000; 2: ~3,366 against 2,800; 3: ~3,639 against 2,800; 5: ~4,180 against 2,800; 6: ~3,372 against 3,200; 8: ~3,722 against 2,800; 9: ~3,741 against 2,800; 10: ~3,871 against 3,000), Chapter 5 is the largest single overrun at about 1,380 words past its ceiling, and **no chapter meets its card's range exactly**; Chapters 4 (~2,721 against a 3,000 ceiling) and 7 (~2,335 against 2,800) are the only two under. Five chapters carry three or more required discrete beats that could not be cut without dropping a cost the crew pays; Chapters 2, 3, 8, 9, and 10 are over because a document has to be read aloud, a specification has to be laid on a bench, and a surrender has to be signed line by line. The prose was trimmed twice, the review removed repetition, and the overrun is recorded here rather than resolved by deleting a cost. No beat listed in the batch outline was dropped.

**Conflicts found and how they were resolved:**

1. The batch outline's day labels (Chapter 4 as "the first working day," Chapter 10 as "the second working day") do not fit a calendar in which Kest sends Calder to the office "tomorrow" at the end of Chapter 1. Resolution: Chapter 1 is a market day on Tuesday the 11th, Chapters 2–4 are Wednesday the 12th, Chapter 5 is Thursday the 13th, Chapters 6–8 are that night into the small hours of Friday the 14th, and Chapters 9–10 are Friday the 14th. Nothing in the prose contradicts any card, and the full calendar is locked in `state/continuity.md`.
2. The Chapter 5 card requires a physical anchor for the Chapter 6 entry, and no Gannet-associated object exists in the cast. Resolution: Calder supplies Oren's brass rigger's hand bell, a private keepsake from before the ferry, and Mara records it as family property with the association to the site **not established** and Calder's own statement that he does not know where it was at the time of the scene. This is a new personal object, not a new world rule, and it is the first thing Calder has ever told anyone he owns.
3. Tobias Wren's account of the old Veyr bell entries was first drafted with inconsistent dates (thirty-one years / forty-five years). Corrected to thirty-one years throughout, with "secondhand" added.
4. Alden was first drafted making a joke about Bevin Rook's name being in the Marr family line, which risked implying a relation to Magistrate Halven Rook. Replaced with a reference to Calder's grandmother, which keeps the family joke and removes the surname implication.

## Batch 0001 review repairs (recorded, not hidden)

The review of Batch 0001 returned FIX. Nine canon and continuity faults and three state-file faults were repaired in the chapters and the state, without restarting the batch and without changing the plot, and the full list is in `reviews/volume-01/batch-0001.md`. The items a later batch would otherwise have inherited wrong:

- **A date collapse.** The Chapter 3 repairer's mark was dated three incompatible ways at once, including to a year of the Serein Accord that is roughly two and a half centuries back. It is now a shop mark of **eleven years back, the middle of the Gannet decade, in plain reckoning**, which is the only date that lets the card's "a shop hand of that decade" and Calder's private suspicion about Oren's notation both stand. The Bible and the card are unchanged; the chapter was wrong.
- **Orison's depth.** Chapter 10 said a hundred and thirty fathoms. It is **seventy fathoms**, per `bible/world.md` and `bible/terminology.md`, and every state file that had absorbed 130 has been corrected.
- **The Chapter 4 dive clock.** The first clamp was written as fitted before the diver was in the water, and the window arithmetic did not close. The dive now runs: bell over 5:10, slack water 5:20, set given as 6:15, first clamp 5:25, silt curtain at 6:00 for four minutes, second clamp off 6:05, current 6:15, up at 6:20, five minutes past what Nessa allowed. Chapter 5's reading of the slate matches it.
- **Depths.** The drifted mole anchor has **one** depth, about eight feet, in every chapter that names it. The freed shoal iron and the *Alder Reach*'s frames are at eight fathoms, with a hole off her quarter at twenty-two.
- **The shard's flange.** The Chapter 9 match depended on a flange that no chapter had ever established on the shard, and collided with the plate's bolted flange. The shard's **ground band** is now planted in Chapter 2 in Mara's own examination, entered as a measurement with *purpose not stated* under it, carried into the Chapter 8 duplicate, and identified in Chapter 9. The two flanges are now visibly different objects' features and are never described in one clause.
- **Shoal geometry.** The four-hundred-yard limit, the outer marks, and the pale run now sit in one fixed set of distances, locked in `state/continuity.md`, because the limit is a live constraint in Batch 0002.
- **The shard's claim window** closed when the courier took the box down the slip, not at dark on a later day, and the three-day courier transit and the two mislocated memories of Chapter 2 and Chapter 9's slip were corrected.
- **The Chapter 2 card compliance.** Calder now hands over the day sheet, the position mark, and the three Free Keel names that the card requires him to submit, which is also what `state/open-threads.md` had been claiming happened.
- **One deviation from the bible is flagged, not applied.** `bible/power-system.md` lists shaking hands as a first-entry cost and Chapters 6 and 7 invert it deliberately. The prose stands; the deviation is recorded in `state/open-threads.md` and `state/continuity.md` for an outline phase to ratify, and no later volume may treat the shake as owed.

**Interface compliance:** the batch contains no panels, stat blocks, character sheets, status messages, floating text, quest text, skill trees, level-ups, or software vocabulary. The only message-like objects used are a lead-line tag and a rag float mark, a crew's book entry, Kest's notice, Registry book entries, a reeve's slate, Mara's separate condition sheet, a written anchor description, the work permit, the custody numbers, and the spoken cutoff word. None of them tells Calder what to do next.

## Story state at the end of Chapter 50

Calder Marr is thirty-two, a line diver and an unstable tide-ear, and he has been off the bottom for six weeks and is now off it again for the season only because a reeve lifted his own undertaking in a hearing room on the 28th of November without being asked. He holds **a one-season provisional working charter to the last day of March next**, inside the harbor line, bounded by the outer marks, with the harbor's eighth and the harbor's share of any raising following the yard in, his crew's own share retained, the freed shoal iron as the bond, and a clause that lets the reeve suspend it after an inspection. **It is not the Second Bell license and does not lead to one.** He has a purpose of six pages and a supplement of four hundred and six words and a hearing record with his own incompleteness in it, entered by him, of his own motion, six days before a hearing. He has a blank slate dated the **nineteenth of January** that a reeve named after he asked for it. He has a bell hung on the town's own ground with a fouled waist and a tongue half worn through, and a chart notation that says *a signal, kept by hand, at the cost stated in this office's hearing record*. He has a stern board with three letters on it, a coaming measured at three foot nine by two foot four, a grating that has been out and back, a dressed hole with fourteen holes in it that gives no hour, and a three-foot-ninety-yard's worth of nothing in a bag. He has a boy of twenty-two who has been in the water on the tenth of December and is owed forty marks, a woman who holds his tender's cleat and took the word *wages* out of his book, and a man of sixty-one at the far bench who has not spoken to anybody since the 13th of November.

Oren Marr remains thirteen years lost. **His first minutes are in a custody room in the west of the city** in a piece of worked glass in two keyed portions, entered as the third item under a third number, under a seal line that carries a senior examiner's name, with a municipal claim against it that the yard cannot withdraw and will not ask to. The frame is one witness's: a case on a shoulder, a paper read twice behind his head with the word *empty* in it, four men and the count right, a tag with the third figure lost, a hand on his collar, and a boy looking at the man and not at the case. **It does not say where the thing went, and Calder does not know whether the hand was Oren's, and the record says the crew does not know who the witness is and has not been asked.** The compartment contradiction is now a figure: the space cannot have been cleared through its own opening, so the manifest is unreliable as a description of it, and **nobody has proved what was in it and nobody will say they have.** The nine-line mark is on a plate in a custody room and on a drawing that was read on a hearing-room table on the seventh of November and has not left a room since. **The Oren mark is still hidden**, and the concealment is now three things: a false line on a slate on its own shelf that two words would make true and that he has left false; a boy with a book who heard something come out of a man's mouth on the eighth of December and wrote *not written as a voice* and said out loud in the yard that he will not write it; and a woman whose standing runs out on the fourth of January and who has said out loud that she will be the only person in Morrow Quay who knows what is in that anchor and has never been asked to say it.

## Active pressures going into Chapter 51

- **Legal:** the **one-season provisional working charter** is granted, runs to the last of March, must be renewed, **may be suspended by the harbor authority after an inspection for cause**, and reaches no ground outside the harbor line. The notice of the thirty-first of October stands, is correct, is not the harbor authority's to lift, closes the third to the eleventh seams of the mole's toe and inner face, and **its third clause catches three named people from going in the water on the authority's work**. The four-hundred-yard bar off the outer marks stands. The reeve's six-clause instrument of the third is conditional, its condition has failed, and it is held open at his pleasure. **Kest's chart claim is narrowed to three seams about the fifth**, and the fenders, three sets of moorings and one herring berth are back in service from the first of December, and the **ninth seam's mooring and the north herring berth are still out**, and the notice is what holds them, not the chart.
- **Institutional:** the *Vigil*'s three terms are answered on the market wall in four sentences and the second is refused, and the cutter holds that on its file. Its claim is in the hearing record in its own words with the yard's three-word objection on the same page. The vessel has asked for the harbor authority's own chart and been refused, and has recorded that the yard has no right to ask for it and did not ask. **A tender sits at a cable's length off the mole, seventy-two days on station, and a second cutter's boat that was not in Morrow Quay in October is at the naval pile.** The circuit office asked Mara Venn to certify the ground and the yard's record and was refused in writing; the refusal and her second note both bear the date of the **twenty-second of November**. On the twentieth of December the office re-entered all three custody items to *municipal claim entered, contested*, and gave no reason, and Kest has entered in a hearing record that **no paper in this harbor or in the circuit office's copying room states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material**, and that the *Vigil* is the second office here that has never been told. A plan for one supervised look at the fifth seam was returned uncountersigned on four correct grounds and **the thing at the fifth seam is unreachable by anybody and it is in March.**
- **Evidence:** the three points stand as three places on one bearing with **ten yards withdrawn** and the first of them entered as a bedded seam of about forty feet of which the yard used the south end as a position, and the diver's own count of seven and eleven against the tender's nine and ten and a half is on the record. **What is new is that the yard can now add three more places on the same bearing without drawing a line between any of them: the plate in nine and a quarter feet off the south end, the hung core, and the harbor authority's own account of its own works naming iron and timber in the foot of a mole built in the year the ferry went down.** The bearing sheet is struck from every sitting with a signed slip. The plate drawing has been shown on a table in daylight and has not left a room. The ten crew-only copies are accounted for and their places are now on a public wall in a count the vessel holds.
- **Bodily:** Bevin Rook's left hand is three weeks along, cannot close, **left arm in the sling, right hand writes**, and **he has been in the water once since the twenty-ninth of October and was tendered by Calder Marr and pulled by Nessa Pike's word on the tenth of December.** Calder's forearm is ungloved, his tinnitus has a third-of-a-second shadow, and his hearing is compensated by four people. **He is off the bottom and has an instrument for it at last, on named dates, and no other.**
- **Crew structure:** **five marks in the bag and nothing in the tin, and nothing has been put into either since the first of December.** The bag was nineteen on the twenty-sixth of November and nineteen on the twenty-eighth and **five on the first of December and nothing after that.** The air pot is a third gone and **thirteen marks of air have been bought since the twenty-eighth of October as at the twenty-eighth of November, and twenty-nine by the first of December** (Chapters 16, 30, 37, 42, 45, 46; the figure of twenty-four that stood here until the volume audit had no chapter behind it and is withdrawn). The chandler's note is **eleven weeks into six in the chandler's own book and thirteen by the yard's count, and nobody has asked him to write a new figure**, and Calder said on the first of December that he would take it to him on the second and the second of December passed. Forty marks owed to Rook, forty owed to Venn, none of it paid, the first four weeks of Rook's wages fall in January and February. The pump is cracked. The *Kittiwake* is on the hard with her bilge blocked in and no cradle. The freed shoal iron is unsold on the stack, entered as the charter's bond at a clerk's thirteen to the crew with a third entered to Sella Brigg for February. The crane and the barge are the harbor's and the harbor pays the hire, which is the only money in this yard's December and it is not the yard's.
- **Approach:** **the nineteenth of January at the mole's toe**, the bedded seam in the mole's own foundation, a lead and a slate and a bell hung, and no diver unless a reason is in the reeve's book at eight in the morning with Pike on the cleat. The purpose is to trace the near end of the run **inland, under the town**, and the harbor authority has no chart of what is under the town because the town was built on it.
- **People:** **Alden Marr has not spoken to anybody since the 13th of November and has a cough and is not asked.** Mara Venn's query comes off her file on the **fourth of January**, after which she is a conservator with a trade and no standing to refuse anybody. Elias Voke is owed a **statement of the authority's capacity to moor the fleet**, which he has offered to write himself for nothing and in which he says he will put the north berth, the ninth seam and the boy's name, and the coast office's word is *capacity* and the hard at Ashtide is unanswered. Neale's tide gang has three of four moorings back and the ninth seam out. Sella Brigg's third is on the bond and is to be paid in February. Sera Mott has said out loud that the ones that answered from the westward **stopped**, and asked for nothing to be written, and one line is written saying that.

**The whole of this section is now planned rather than merely owed.** `outline/volume-02.md` and `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` carry every item above, and `workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` is the queued phase that opens the volume against it. The first of March is **a Wednesday, nine weeks and two days off**, and it is the volume's clock.

## Current relationship pressure

Calder and Mara are witnesses to each other and are now, for the first time, two people who have both refused to be useful. He told her the mark on the 13th of November and she has written none of it and has said in a shed and at a mole head that she will never write one word of it anywhere ever, and on the 26th of December she refused, out loud and in the open, to say the thing she has believed since the fourteenth of October, because a thing she said would make her a witness and she would rather be the only person who knows what is in that anchor and has never been asked. He has not thanked her and she does not want to be thanked and both of them said so. **He asked her, in the middle of a market wall, to certify a method for nothing, and wrote *nothing* on the back of it in his own hand so that in three years a man will know what it cost.** She is out of standing on the fourth of January and four *not stated* entries, three private and one public, stand against the same man, and the fourth has cost her the last of it. He is still angry about the word *seam* and her answer of the first of November stands and was not reopened. **Nothing in this batch is romantic and everything in it is inconvenient, and the one thing that has changed is that she has said a thing about a record she believes, out loud, at a mole head, in front of a bell, and that he did not ask her to.**

Nessa Pike has now overruled Calder five times since the twenty-eighth of October and won five times, and the fifth cost her the count of ten printed copies signed in her own hand, which is the thing she had told a room a man with a notebook could bend. She has overruled the mole's face as the site of an entry on the grounds of a swell and a boy, and been right. **She has taken the word *wages* out of the yard's book and put *claim* in its place in her own hand because a boy of twenty-two asked her to, and she has said *Enough* to him on the tenth of December and not apologized and not looked away, and she is the only person in that yard who can be trusted to do it again.** The standing shutdown is unchanged. The tender's cleat is hers.

## Current power state

**Line Diver, and an unstable tide-ear capacity he has used twice.** The first supervised anchored entry was the thirteenth of October, eleven minutes forty inside, surfaced on the tender's word, and cost a rope-burn reenactment, continuous tinnitus with a second sound a third of a second late, and emotional residue strong enough to answer a person in Oren's call mark without deciding to. **The second was the eighth of December, eleven minutes inside a limit of forty, under the same six conditions, and it cost him a specific and permanent memory: the lift at the end of a childhood call he can no longer remember, and the account he gave of that in the crew's book says *not written as a voice* and says nothing else.** He carries a practice trace from a loading space: the body knows a low carry, a case on a shoulder, and a hand on a collar. He **is not a faultreader**, has never claimed to read a seam, and the word came from Mara Venn's mouth in the first week of November and not from his; the two things he found on the plate on the sixth of December — a dressed aperture and a fouling that gives no hour — are **not** seam work and he did not treat them as such. **The bell-buoy core is not a power and not a gift.** It is a piece of iron with four pieces of glass in its waist, hung on a moored ground line, which answers a bell in the same ground a hand's width of a second late, and which needs a rope a year and a tongue every four or five and a person going out in the weather. **The words *it reads nothing, it settles nothing and it says no word* are the volume's lock and not a sentence in any chapter** — the negative is carried on the page by the craft's economics and by Kest's chart notation, *not a mark and not a light and not a danger, a signal, kept by hand, at the cost stated in this office's hearing record*, and that is sufficient. A later volume must keep the object on that footing rather than hunt for a line to quote. **The bellwright is the harbor's own and is now named: Corvin Sarn, about fifty, in that yard since the twentieth of October, who teaches nobody.** See `outline/volume-02.md` lock L8 and lock L17.

## Canon sources

`bible/premise.md`, `bible/world.md`, `bible/characters.md`, `bible/power-system.md`, `bible/themes.md`, and `bible/terminology.md` are canon. `outline/series.md` and `outline/ending.md` are canon for the mystery, antagonist ladder, power stages, and final resolution. `outline/volume-01.md` is canon for the first volume and `outline/volume-02.md` for the second. `outline/batches/volume-01-batch-0001.md` is canon for Chapters 1–10, `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` for Chapters 51–60, and Chapters 1–10 and 51–60 as written are the authority on what happened in them. **Chapters 1–50 as written are the authority on what has happened in them, and where a draft conflicts with a planning file, the planning files win and the conflict is recorded in `state/open-threads.md`.** **The volume audit at `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md` is the authority on the decisions Volume 01 deferred, and every one of its rulings is now in `state/continuity.md` and `state/open-threads.md`.**

## Volume index

- **Volume 01, *The First Bell*, Chapters 1–50: COMPLETE AND AUDITED.** The first transparent recovery, the claim, the seizure, and a one-season provisional working charter. **Resolution:** the crew keeps its salvage share and a defensible provenance record; a working bell-buoy core is recovered and hung on harbor ground; a fragment holding Oren's first minutes is surfaced through a public route at the cost of a childhood memory of his voice and is in custody under a claim the yard cannot withdraw; the public record shows the cutter's claim and the plate on the same ground and the manifest unreliable, with no forum ruling on either; the charter is granted and is a leash, not a license. **Still open:** the reason for the seizure of the 14th of October. **New-volume question:** why the transparent vessels lie in a deliberate route toward Orison and who holds the missing links — and why the route stopped being kept. **Movement 5 closed on Chapter 45's charter, Chapter 46's bell, Chapter 47's fragment and figure, Chapter 48's cost, Chapter 49's record, and Chapter 50's hung bell and a grown man's call in the ground nobody can place.** The audit is `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md`: two mechanical repairs applied in place, three structural findings recorded rather than repaired, eight deferred author-level decisions ruled, and the whole of Volume 02's inherited lock set verified against the chapters. **Next: Volume 02, Batch 0001, Chapters 51–60, at `workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`.**

- **Volume 02, *The License Sea*, Chapters 51–100: IN PROGRESS.** `outline/volume-02.md` exists and carries **twenty-four** locks; `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` holds the ten cards for Chapters 51–60 and is the plan for a batch that is written. Central pressure: the charter is a leash, Kest can suspend it after an inspection for cause, and Calder still lacks the full Second Bell license. **Movement 1, Chapters 51–60, opens the leash and does not resolve it**: the fourth of January, when Mara Venn's standing goes; the Crown's next request, worded so a refusal is a delay; ordinary paid work inside the line that pays badly; a body and a real cost; the going out in the weather with the bellwright named; a reeve's payment that costs a third party; and the nineteenth of January, a Thursday, at the mole's toe, where the run is found to go under the town. **Movement 2, Chapters 61–70, prices the weather and does not resolve it**, and its turn is a herring skipper's answer to a coast office's word *capacity* that the yard did not write and cannot fix. **Movement 3, Chapters 71–80, is the paper that asks for the book**, and its turn is that a public record is a thing other people can now use, including against the yard. **The Volume 2 midpoint is Movement 4, Chapters 81–90: a fisher's account of a coast road no current chart includes and a claim seal Mara Venn proves forged — not the compartment, which is spent.** **Next: Volume 02, Batch 0004, Chapters 81–90, at `workspace/volume-02/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`.**
- **Volume 03, *The Unmapped Fleet*, Chapters 101–150: IN PROGRESS.** `outline/volume-03.md` exists and carries **twenty-eight** locks; `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0001.md` and `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0002.md` hold the ten cards for Chapters 101–110 and 111–120 and both batches are written. Central pressure: reconstructing the old fleet route becomes a race against rival salvage crews and a naval cutter that wants the map erased. **Movement 1, Chapters 101–110, ran the writ's four-day clock twice, put a boy of twenty-two off the stone on Monday the fifteenth of May, refused the eighth-clause plan for the fifth seam, produced the writ on a mole at the top of the steps, and put one boat off the south end to a bell with no tongue on its frame, which is not a going out.** **Movement 2, Chapters 111–120, took a second glass storm, charged a cistern three times for nothing, named three recoveries and funded the smallest, put a woman's own warp and a figure on a market wall, did not put the best man in the harbor on a lead, let a boy of twenty-two take a season's hand, raised a foot and a half of worked glass of nobody's and started a clock, and refused thirty marks in nine sentences, which cost the town the third recovery.** **Movement 3, Chapters 121–130, is the chart and the two men who want it, and it is the volume's reserved beat: the coast office answers, and a man in a city reads a writ with a reporting clause on it and writes back a question about a bar of gravel.** **The faultreader stage is taken in Movement 4, late, badly, and at a price, and the one naming of Calder's condition is owed to that movement. `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0003.md` DOES NOT EXIST and the queued prompt was built from the outline alone. Next: Volume 03, Batch 0003, Chapters 121–130, at `workspace/volume-03/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`.** Resolution, when it comes: the fleet's formation is public; Calder becomes a faultreader and loses a bell-archive to save a crew.
- **Volume 04, *The Keeper's Ledger*, Chapters 151–200:** the registry inquiry and the missing Gannet page. Resolution: the manifest discrepancy is acknowledged, Mara leaves the Registry, and Calder loses Second Bell eligibility.
- **Volume 05, *The Nine Harbors*, Chapters 201–250:** the compact is confirmed as an agreement about custody. Resolution: an informal Nine Harbor Assembly and shared rescue obligations.
- **Volume 06, *The Quietening*, Chapters 251–300:** the descent proves a forced-consensus cascade and a collective scuttling. Resolution: Ilya's seal appears on the old evidence chain.
- **Volume 07, *The Closed Sea*, Chapters 301–350:** blockade and a remembered storm route. Resolution: the blockade is broken at a real cost in boats and responsibility.
- **Volume 08, *The Admiral's Bell*, Chapters 351–400:** amnesty refused, convoy truth exposed, Vey defeated. Resolution: Ilya is promoted to minister at the close of the volume.
- **Volume 09, *A Thousand Small Voices*, Chapters 401–450:** a planted claim panics the public archive. Resolution: the Open Keel Assembly is chartered and the Oren route copy is authorized under mixed custodians.
- **Volume 10, *The Sea Below*, Chapters 451–500:** the Unlit Keel and the testimony-stripping tool. Resolution: proof that Ilya's office rebuilt the Crown Seal and signed the Gannet transfer.
- **Volume 11, *The Cordon*, Chapters 501–550:** close-sea cordon and three broken points. Resolution: independent routes move people and supplies; Calder surrenders accumulated authority.
- **Volume 12, *The Single Voice*, Chapters 551–600:** the Crown Keel begins activating and a technically credible offer of single authority. Resolution: the counter-plan of permitted testimonies and visible gaps.
- **Volume 13, *The Last Witness*, Chapters 601–650:** consent negotiation and a manufactured glass storm. Resolution: the sealed remainder is identified, Calder holds an unexercised family claim, and Ilya admits the Gannet lie.
- **Volume 14, *The Nine Roads*, Chapters 651–700:** the routes hold only with each community's own limits. Resolution: the Crown's monopoly fails; the Crown Keel turns toward Orison.
- **Volume 15, *The Open Sea*, Chapters 701–760:** the Ninth Bell, the released claim, the distributed braid. Resolution: no empire owns the surviving memory network.

## Volume 02, Batch 0001 summary (Chapters 51–60) — the story state at the end of Chapter 60

**Movement 1 is written.
