The leash is open and it is not resolved.** In sixteen days the Free Keel yard watched a conservator lose the last of the standing that made her useful, read a second request from the circuit office that is worded so that a refusal is a delay, took the one piece of paid work anybody offered at a bad rate, was told by a mother and by a bonesetter the same thing about a boy in a different tone, went out in a boat in a fine day to look after a hung bell and found out what keeping a signal costs, hurt that boy on a chain on a date the reeve named, understood a supply problem instead of announcing one, was granted a date and a ground by a reeve who said the price out loud and paid for it out of a third party's winter, gave a copy of its public record to a man it did not owe, and went out on the nineteenth of January at the top of the flood with a lead and a slate and no diver and found that the run does not stop at the mole.

**Actual word count, re-measured after the Batch 0001 review repairs: 31,769 words across ten chapters** (Ch51 3,660 · Ch52 2,804 · Ch53 2,361 · Ch54 3,206 · Ch55 3,033 · Ch56 3,641 · Ch57 3,523 · Ch58 3,608 · Ch59 2,766 · Ch60 3,167), measured with `wc -w` including headings, against `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s 2,200–3,200. **Every chapter is above the 2,200 floor. Five of the ten are inside the guideline and five are past it, the largest by fourteen per cent — Chapter 51, which was the largest before the repairs and is still the largest after them.** As first recorded, before the repairs, the batch was **30,632 words, six chapters inside the guideline and four past it (Ch51 3,645 · Ch56 3,218 · Ch57 3,434 · Ch58 3,377), the largest Chapter 51 at about fourteen per cent over**; the per-chapter figures and the total in that first record were exact and the summary sentence that went with them was right in substance. **The Batch 0001 review's own corrected count — *seven inside, three past, the largest by six point eight per cent* — was wrong, because it dropped Chapter 51 at 3,645, which is fourteen per cent over the guideline, and so reported three chapters past the shape when four were.** The excess is not two rooms in any chapter: **no chapter in this batch is two rooms**, the hearing room does not appear at all, and the one institutional room in the batch is the copying room in Chapter 52, which is a single room with a road outside it. **The repairs added about eleven hundred words, almost all of it in Chapters 56 and 58, where the reeve now has to say out loud that his own office has been working inside a closure his own notice created.** That is a cost the volume needed and it is on the reeve and not on the crew.

**The batch's midpoint turn is Chapter 55, the going out.** The chapters before it are the yard learning the price of its own position — a figure of nineteen on a note it had believed was six, three marks for a date, a bad rate on a wall, and a mother's question about a plate. Chapter 55 is where the batch turns, because the bell stops being a discovery and becomes a job with a man's name and a recurring bill: Corvin Sarn is named, the cost is stated in his own trade's terms, a share of the day is offered and refused, and **the bell's behaviour gets a mechanism that is not a mystery and costs nothing to keep — a thing only speaks while somebody keeps the weather working on it.** Everything after it is a crew in the water in weather that is wrong for it, and it pays. **The batch's climax is Chapter 60**, where the day is stopped at a figure the yard did not go out for, and the leash is named as a leash.

**Conflicts found and how they were resolved, recorded rather than defended:**

1. **The calendar in the planning files was wrong in three places and the chapters were right throughout.** *The fourth, eleventh, eighteenth and twenty-fifth of January are Mondays* cannot be true in a grid where the fourth of January is a Wednesday; *nine weeks and two days, eighty-one days* gives a figure that is wrong for its own weeks; and Movement 1's *the first of March … is eleven weeks off* is **eight weeks** on the fourth of January. **The correct Mondays are the 2nd, 9th, 16th, 23rd and 30th, and nine weeks and two days is sixty-five days, and 1 February is a Wednesday as 1 March is.** The chapters ran on the corrected grid and the consequence on the page is that the hearing room's slate wall was last wiped on **Monday the second of January** and goes again on **the ninth**, so a chalked figure survives five days. **On the Batch 0001 review all three planning-file lines were corrected in place** — `outline/volume-02.md` lock L1 and Movement 1, and `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` — with the correction and its date entered in the outline, and the batch's own record of having found two of the three and missed the third is corrected with them. **The queued Batch 0002 prompt's own calendar list was also corrupt and is now the measured grid.**
2. **The card for Chapter 51 asked for a Monday wipe on the fifth of January, and the fifth is a Thursday.** The card's intent — that a figure on the board is a figure for a week and not a day — is honoured with the correct date.
3. **The ninth seam's mooring.** The Chapter 58 card requires a fourth set of moorings on the authority's own account, and the fourth set in Kest's own words is the ninth seam's, which changes a position the volume opened on. **The change is written into the chapter and into Chapter 60 rather than smoothed**, the mooring is bought back but **not worked**, the stone having been shifted four inches on the twelfth and the shackle not freed, and **the north berth stays out, one berth stays short on the first of March, and the nineteen winters stay unpaid.**
4. **The diver in Chapter 56 is a hired man, not the yard.** The card asks for a boy on the fall who is hurt; it also has to be lawful, and the notice of the thirty-first names Calder, Pike and Rook in its third clause. **A body in the water on the authority's work is barred to all three of them**, so the diver is Tam Slee of the *Grey Widow*, hired by Elias Voke on his own account, and the boy is on the fall and puts the arm already in the sling into it. This makes the chapter's argument sharper rather than weaker: the three best men on that ground are stood on a wall because of a document.
5. **The chandler's note's week count.** The lock gives *eleven weeks in the chandler's book and thirteen by the yard's own count* as standing figures, and the arithmetic does not advance them across five weeks. **The chapter resolves it by making the yard's count an unrecorded figure in Calder's head**, which is exactly the kind of unchecked number this book is about: the boy enters *the fact that the number is in his head* in the book and refuses to enter the number, and the chandler's own new figure of **twenty-two weeks** is the one that is written down. No figure was silently adjusted to make a week fit.
6. **Interface compliance.** No panels, stat blocks, character sheets, status messages, floating text, quest text, skill trees, level-ups, or software vocabulary. **The ten chapters contain no occurrence of the word *panel***, and a mechanical scan for the standing banned terms returns nothing. The only message-like objects are the crew's book and its read-backs, the reeve's book and chart, the hearing room's slate wall, the market wall, the copying clerk's counter, the chandler's ledger, the *Kittiwake*'s register, the bonesetter's writing, the notice of the thirty-first with its seal, the second request with its second seal of delay, the reason of two hundred and sixty-nine words, and a bell. **None of them tells Calder what to do next**, and the one document that comes nearest to advising anybody is the second request, and it is an instrument of an antagonist office.
7. **No new antagonist, no new institution, no new final enemy, and the ending is untouched.** Ilya Serr, Halven Rook and Captain Holt do not appear anywhere in the batch, on paper or otherwise. No bible file was edited. **One addition and no change: the harbor authority's breach of its own notice of the thirty-first is entered three times in the reeve's own hand and is now `outline/volume-02.md` lock L24 — a cost on a man who already exists, and not a new institution.**

## Batch 0001 review repairs

A reviewer pass over Chapters 51–60, the four state files, `outline/volume-02.md`, the Batch 0001 card file and the queued Batch 0002 prompt returned **FIX**: twelve required changes, eight prose and state improvements, and three items of planning-file residue. The reviewer's own log is not kept in this repository; the full disposition of every finding is in `state/continuity.md` under **Batch 0001 review repairs**, and this section is the short form. **No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut for length, and no planned plot was altered** — Movement 1, the leash, the fourth of January, the going out, the reeve's price, the body, the nineteenth and the ending are all as the volume outline has them, and `outline/ending.md` was not touched.

**The four that would have cost the next batch:**

1. **The first of February was a Tuesday in four chapters and in a state file, and is a Wednesday** — 1 March is a Wednesday and a February is four weeks. Chapter 54's own derivation was wrong as well and is now *four weeks takes nothing off a week at all*, with the boy's second check off the Mondays added. **The ten chapters behind you are all dated on one grid and the queued prompt's own grid was corrupt and is now the measured one.**
2. **Five marks were deducted twice in Chapter 57** — the week's food went *onto the note and not out of the bag* while the bag fell by nine. **The five is entered on the creditor's page and paid out of the bag the same afternoon, and the chain now reads 13 − 4 = 9, less the five the boy carried down, = 4, with the note at 18 − 5 = 13.** February's squeeze turns on this figure.
3. **The hand crane was paid twice, by two parties, at two rates.** **The second hire is the harbor authority's** — four marks for two days out of its own repair account — and the yard's itemisation of the fourteenth no longer carries it. The yard's own hand-crank on the north wall is a different hire, out of the rope money.
4. **The harbor authority was working inside a closure its own notice created, under its own instruments, and nobody noticed.** **Now Kest notices, three times, in his own hand**, and it is `outline/volume-02.md` **lock L24**: the reason of the twelfth carries the admission and Calder puts the reeve's own November ground to him in the room; the claim window of the seventeenth names ground the same notice closes and Kest says so in his barge; and the line he writes under the reason on the nineteenth says it again. **No chapter may work that ground without the acknowledgement, and the acknowledgement is never free.**

**Also on the page, and each of these was checkable:** Chapter 52's office arithmetic (*six* of the fourteen days gone, not eight) and the clerk's *fifteenth day* and *twenty-ninth day* now given as dates — **the thirteenth and the twenty-seventh of January**, so that the *twenty-ninth* can never again be read as the twenty-ninth of January; the delayed note's no-call count, which the log supports **twice** and not three times, now said the same way in both chapters and in all three state files; Chapter 60's *nine and a quarter, the figure of the twenty-seventh of November, off the coaming*, which is the ***Aster Gannet's* ferry plate beyond the eleventh seam** and not the bedded seam — the south end is **nine feet** on the tender's count with Rook's own seven, the north end **ten and a half** with his own eleven, and the two objects are two objects again; Chapter 60's *a boy who had said … he would be a boat hand in June*, which is **the bonesetter's sentence and never his**; Chapter 55's *fourteen less three this morning was two*; and Chapter 53's hand-crank, which is now inside the itemised nine.

**One number the review did not flag and that is now true: the reason for the nineteenth is two hundred and sixty-nine words as printed and not two hundred and eleven**, and the sentence the boy puts his finger on is eleven words long, which is the length of the clause.

**Prose and state, in place.** The slate wall in Chapter 51 is blank *since eight on Monday the second*, which is what makes Bevin's *five days* true; the chandler's twenty-two weeks is *on my own count*; the tide gang in Chapter 60 no longer claims the eighteenth of January; the brother of Tam Slee in Chapter 58 is a fact Kest entered rather than a clause attached to the wrong man; the wrist is seven days old and the wage *starts* on a Wednesday in a fortnight; three duplicated two-word lines, one near-identical money-beat sentence and the closing beat that ran three times are now one of each. **Planning-file residue is closed: `outline/volume-02.md` lock L1 and Movement 1 and `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` are corrected in place with the correction and its date in the outline, and the batch's record of having found two of those three and missed the third is corrected with them.**

**Where the review's own arithmetic was wrong, and it is recorded rather than adopted:** the review reported *seven chapters inside the guideline, three past, the largest by six point eight per cent*. **That dropped Chapter 51, which was 3,645 and fourteen per cent over.** The true position before the repairs was **six inside and four past**, and after them, with the reeve's admissions added, it is **31,769 words, five inside and five past, the largest Chapter 51 at fourteen per cent over.** The batch's original summary sentence was right in substance; its per-chapter list is now the re-measured one.

## Story state at the end of Chapter 60

Calder Marr is thirty-two, a line diver with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice, and he is **off the bottom for a reason that is a document**: the notice of the thirty-first names him in its third clause and stops him, Nessa Pike and Bevin Rook from the water on the harbor authority's own work. He holds a one-season provisional working charter to the last of March which one man may suspend after an inspection, for cause; a claim window for one date, the nineteenth, naming a look and not a diver; a reason of two hundred and sixty-nine words in a municipal book with no purpose in it; an entry in the crew's own book recording the omission and why; four hundred feet of soundings along a bedded seam that goes under a town; **and no paper of any kind that will accept a route.**

Oren Marr remains thirteen years lost. **His first minutes are in a custody room in the west of this city** in a piece of worked glass in two keyed portions, entered as the third item under a third number under a seal line, with a municipal claim that cannot be withdrawn and that the yard will not ask to withdraw. **The Oren mark is still in no record, in no office, in no book, and not in Kest's chart notation**, and the batch closes on the three parts of the concealment intact and one of them wider than it was: **the false line on the unverified note is still false; a boy with a book has entered an omission in his own right hand because the reason has a hole in it; and four men in a boat have agreed not to ask the question.** **A concealment has been told once, to one person, in a shed, at a price, and it was not told again in this batch, and it may not be told again for nothing.**

## Active pressures going into Chapter 61

- **Money and a date.** **Four marks in a bag and nothing in the tin**, the note at **thirteen**, forty marks owed to the boy, forty owed to Mara Venn, the first four weeks of a wage at four a week falling on **the first of February**, a third of Sella Brigg's iron in **February**, and **fourteen marks from the harbor authority on the first of February** for a day of work on the nineteenth. **The same month has to hold the wages and the first money the yard has ever been owed by an office, and the forty to the woman are not paid and may not be paid out of it.**
- **The second seal.** The circuit office's request of the thirtieth of December is unanswered. **The fourteen days run from the date of the paper and not the date of receipt; six were gone when the request was entered on the fifth of January, the fourteenth is the thirteenth of January, and the clerk's *fifteenth day* and *twenty-ninth day* are day-counts — the second seal is owed on the twenty-ninth day from the thirtieth of December, which is the twenty-seventh of January, not the twenty-ninth of January.** A second seal is a delay and not a refusal, and the thing being taken away is the public record, one copy of which is already in a man's keeping on a market stall and cannot be recalled.
- **The first of March.** A Wednesday, **six weeks less a day from the boat on the nineteenth**. Forty herring boats, the **north berth out of service** and the one that takes a northeast blow, the ninth seam bought back and **not worked**, three tide-gang men lost in the last week of February, nineteen winters disputed and unpaid, and a coast office in Veyr still waiting for a statement of the capacity of a harbor to moor a fleet that Elias Voke has offered to write for nothing and the harbor authority has not written.
- **The run under the town.** Four hundred feet of ordinary figures — **nine feet at the south end of the bedded seam, which is the tender's count of the twenty-eighth of October and Rook's own count of it is seven; ten and a half at its north end, and his own count of that is eleven; seven and three-eighths at the mole's root; and five and a quarter sounding landward under a stone quay** — the bottom coming up landward under a stone quay, and no chart and no forum and no form. **Nine and a quarter feet is a different object at a different part of the mole and is not one of these figures.** **Calder stopped the day at twenty past eleven and the work will not be done again until somebody is found who can be asked.** **All of it was sounded on ground the notice of the thirty-first closes, under a claim window this office had no power to write, and the reeve has entered that fact twice.** Movement 2's three storm-exposed recoveries have to be funded before they can be attempted, and the one thing the yard has found is a thing it cannot ask about.
- **The body.** A boy of twenty-two with a crushed hand and a crushed wrist four months after a hand, in a sling, with a bonesetter's word that has changed and will not promise June, entered for a wage on a day the yard has named, and whose mother has been told the truth twice in a month and is to be told nothing in a room. **The only rescue cover the yard has belongs to a crew that has not been asked and may be.**
- **The supply.** A pump that cannot be worked and therefore cannot be hired out; hired gear at a mark a tide; a boat on the hard with no cradle; the air pot a third gone; and the harbor's crane and barge and the harbor's pump all on the authority's own account, which is the only money in this yard's winter and is not the yard's.

## Relationship pressure going into Chapter 61

**Calder and Mara are two people who have both refused to be useful to each other and who have now been shown what that costs.** She has no standing left, a certificate of method is **six marks and three weeks**, and she is named in a circuit paper as a door that has already been tried, which makes her valuable and simultaneously unuseable. He asked her a question he had no right to ask and paid nothing and did not resent her and did not thank her. **Nothing in this batch is romantic and everything in it is expensive.** He has started asking people what they can do instead of what he wants, and has been given a smaller answer, and has not argued.

**Calder and Kest have reached the point the volume promised: the reeve's own spending can be spent against him.** Kest has named a price, said it out loud before granting the thing, paid seven marks of the harbor's own cost for a day of work that was not done, refused to pretend the boy's wrist is his to pay, read a reason back that he did not edit, added a line under it, and named a ground in his own hand and read the naming back. **And he has now entered, three times and in his own hand, that his own office has no power to lift the notice of the thirty-first and has worked inside it anyway** — on the permit of the eighth, on the reason of the twelfth, and on the ground named on the seventeenth and again in a line under the reason of the nineteenth — and he has said out loud that he wants the yard to be holding it as well as he is, because he has been the only man in this harbor holding things since the thirty-first of October and it has made him a man who decides things alone and writes them down afterward. **He is not a friend. He is a man whose promises have dates on them, there is one owing on the twenty-seventh of January and one entered for the first of February, and he has just put his own hand in a file in Veyr on purpose.**

**Calder and Bevin have moved from a boy who is owed forty marks to a boy who has entered his own wage on a day he can name, in his own hand, for less than he could have asked for, and who has entered an omission nobody asked him for, and who found the sentence in a reason that the three men who wrote it had not seen.** He is still not a boat hand and June is still a sentence in a bonesetter's book.

**Calder and Alden do not move.** A man at the far bench, not asked for anything, not asked anything, and Calder does not go. **That is the relationship and it is not a plot point and it is not paid off in this volume.**

## Power state going into Chapter 61

**Line Diver, with an unstable tide-ear capacity used twice and not used in this batch, and one permanent memory gone. No stage is taken and no rank changes. Not a faultreader, and does not become one.** What changed in this batch is method, and it is all in other people: a reason entered before anybody goes in, by hand, with a clerk present, unedited; a tender who is not the diver; a limit on the slate; a cutoff that can be pulled without asking anybody; a ground named in the paper; a claim window for a look and not a permit for a diver; **and four people in a boat who know why.** **The bell is not an instrument and the mechanism given to it in Chapter 55 is a mechanism, not a power**: it answers because the weather works on it, the delay is a hand's width of a second, and it is a bill. **The tinnitus is named once in this volume, in Chapter 57, and may not be allowed to vanish silently again before the volume closes.**

## Required updates after each batch

A writer phase that completes a batch updates these same files and does not set phase status:

- `state/chapter-summaries.md`: one entry per chapter, two to five sentences each, plus the compact volume index.
- `state/continuity.md`: new canon facts, changed relationships, power costs and gains, discovered world rules, and any proposed canon change.
- `state/open-threads.md`: promises opened, promises closed, and threads carried forward.
- `state/current.md`: the story state at the end of the batch, so the next batch starts from the right place, plus the batch summary.

## Batch 0002 review repairs (recorded, not hidden)

A reviewer pass over Chapters 61–70, the four state files, `outline/volume-02.md` and the queued `batch-0003/PROMPT.md` returned **FIX**: nine required fixes and three prose improvements. The reviewer's own log is not kept in this repository; the full disposition of every finding is in `state/continuity.md` under **Batch 0002 review repairs**, and this section is the short form. **No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut for length, and no planned plot was altered**, and `outline/ending.md`, `outline/series.md` and all six bible files were not touched.

**The six that would have cost the next batch:**

1. **Sella Brigg's third was four figures in one chapter and none was a third of twenty marks.** There is no sum of coin that is a third of twenty marks. **She is owed a true six marks, eight shillings and tenpence, was paid the round six, and the eight shillings and tenpence is entered in her name and is not paid.** The bag still goes from twenty-two to sixteen.
2. **The going out's eight marks had no account** in a column the next two chapters and the volume's closing figure depend on. **It is now entered under a rule and not paid**, and it is a debt like the other two.
3. **Four impossible weekday-and-date pairings** in Chapters 61, 67 and 70, one of them contradicting Chapter 62 on the day the reason reaches the harbor authority's book. All corrected; the anchor is 1 January a Sunday, confirmed 43 times across the two volumes.
4. **The queued prompt's calendar was corrupt from 18 March on**, and the charter's end date was a Wednesday that is a Friday. **The charter runs to Friday the thirty-first of March.** The grid now carries a true provenance line and an instruction that a chapter outranks the grid.
5. **The February calendar paragraph in `state/continuity.md` asserted and denied the same fact in one breath.** Rewritten in full.
6. **The unentered bell occasions were not the three the state files named.** The prose has **two** — the mole steps on Thursday the twenty-third of February, which Chapter 67 says in terms was not an occasion, and the hearing room on the first of March. **There was no occasion on the eleventh of February and there is no collar anywhere in Chapters 61–70**, and both state files and the queued prompt now carry the two.

**Also fixed:** Voke's statement was dated Saturday the twenty-eighth of February in two state files and the twenty-eighth is a Tuesday — the chapters leave it *Saturday night*, the twenty-fifth. `state/current.md` and `state/open-threads.md` both reported a marker that does not exist; `workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/.done` is present and both claims are withdrawn. `state/phase-ledger.json` is genuinely stale and is left for the controller, no marker was written, and no file reports the marker set as evidence that any batch is unwritten.

**Two transcription errors found while re-deriving the money, not raised by the review:** the continuity table gave the twenty-seventh of February a gross of twenty-two where the chapter reads **twenty-six**, and Chapter 68's money reading contradicted its own column about the hired hand's second day. **No figure moved.** The chain re-derives cleanly to **eleven marks in the bag and one on the note** at the end of Chapter 70, which is where the queued prompt and the next writer start.
